
# The navigation study — where the desk lives

> A design investigation, dated 2026-09-10; the panel ran 2026-09-09/10. It changes no code and schedules nothing. It answers one brief, quoted in full below, and ends in one recommendation (§4), not a menu. Nothing in it is built without a mockup the owner has seen and his written word on the rulings it reverses (§6). The shell's revs 54–61 are unverified on a device, so everything below stands on drawings until `build.sh` and `drive.sh` have run.

**The brief** (owner, verbatim):

> I'm not fully convinced by the navigation area yet. Right now, we have Note Tabs as a global navigation space accessed from the main bar, and while the Note List visually feels like a destination/place, it's really more of a feature accessed independently from the panel alongside the open notes. The overall structure also feels visually close to Obsidian, which I'd like to avoid. The interface should feel distinctive and have its own visual language, rather than feeling like a variation of an existing note-taking app. Suggest different ways to approach the interface structure and navigation model while keeping the core concepts of: the existing views; the Note Tab system; the ability to work with multiple open notes. I'm especially interested in approaches that rethink the relationship between the global navigation, open Note Tabs, and views. Explore several distinct directions rather than just minor variations of the current layout. Maybe our system can't get much better... it's just a bit vague in places and doesn't feel 100% natural.

Three sentences in that brief carry the study. *"The Note List visually feels like a destination/place, it's really more of a feature accessed independently from the panel alongside the open notes"* is a correct reading of the code: §1 shows that the list and the open notes are two surfaces with two doors on two edges, and that the view called Notes is also the only place a document can be drawn at all. *"Visually close to Obsidian"* is not an accident: copying Obsidian was the directive of 2026-07-30, and half of what remains is the owner's own later ruling — §2 lists every lift by file and line so the reader knows which are structural and which are his. *"Maybe our system can't get much better"* is answered by §1's finding: the vagueness is in which surface owns the document, which is a question of state and not of pixels; polishing cannot reach it, and one three-file change can (§4.3).

**What the panel was.**

1. **Three readers**, from the tree and the design docs, asked for evidence and not opinion. One read the current model (`Chrome.swift`, `Plane.swift`, `Panel.swift`, `Bar.swift`, `Tabs.swift`, `Navigate.swift`, `Desk.swift`) and produced §1. One read what the code says it lifted from Obsidian Mobile, what Obsidian Mobile (1.4–1.8, the era in the owner's clips) actually is, and eleven axes on which an app can differ from it; that is §2. One read every ruling from the 0.2 constitution to rev 61 in chronology — what still stands, the hard constraints, the product tests, the owner's taste — verbatim.
2. **One diagnosis**: twelve named vaguenesses with their evidence, the one fault under them, what is good and must survive, and eighteen questions any redesign must answer.
3. **Eight designers**, each from a different seed — a root surface, a container idiom, an axis, the product's own furniture, the platform's own shapes — each asked for the five sections the owner named (the navigation concept; how note tabs and the note list are represented; how the six views fit; why it is not Obsidian, item by item against §2; the trade-offs) plus three wireframes, a chrome inventory, what survives in code and what is thrown away by file and line, answers to the diagnosis, and what is conceded.
4. **Three judges**, one lens each, scoring 1–5 with must-fixes: product fit, against `design/what-liv-is-for.md`, `interface.md`'s surviving laws and the rulings; the hand and the head — one-handed reach on a 390pt phone, tap counts, mode errors, gesture collisions, whether a manual-less person has the map by the end of day one; distinctiveness and cost — whether every lift in §2 is actually gone or only renamed, which app it now resembles, how many of the 47 Swift files (26.8k lines) move, and whether a one-week simulator slice proves the idea.
5. **A revision round**: every designer answered its must-fixes and re-checked its file and line claims against the tree. Several first-draft claims were wrong and are corrected in each direction's *changed after critique* section. The scores in §3 are of the first drafts; the notes under the table say what moved.

**How to read it.** §1 says what is broken and why it is not a polish problem. §2 says what "like Obsidian" means in this code, so any direction's claim to have left it can be checked. §3 is the scorecard and the judges' cross-cutting notes, quoted. §4 is the recommendation: what to mock up first, what to do with the good parts, the cheapest slice that can go on the simulator, and what not to do, by name. §5 is what stays whatever is chosen. §6 is the questions only the owner can answer. Then the directions, strongest first, each in its designer's own words — rendered from their structured text, not rewritten — and an appendix for the one retired as a near-duplicate. Rulings are quoted, never paraphrased. Line numbers are the tree's on 2026-09-10 and will drift.

**What it is not.** Not a plan of record, not a build, not a device measurement. As in the furnishing study, n = 0 users; every judgement about reach, legibility and feel is a designer's or a judge's until a mockup is on the owner's phone and a `drive.sh` check has been broken on purpose and then made green.


## 1. What is vague today, and why polishing will not fix it

Read from the code on 2026-09-10, not from memory. Line numbers are `shell/ios/Sources/*`.

### 1.1 Twelve vaguenesses, named

1. **Notes is two things wearing one name — a view, and the container of documents.** Tap the panel's *Notes* row and you get a list. Open a note from Today, a search hit, a `[[link]]` or a notification and the lit row in the panel says *Notes* too, though you never chose it; tap that lit row and, unlike Today or Calendar, it does not do nothing — it throws you out of the note onto the list. Cause: the desk has no state of its own. A document renders only while `state == .notes`: `openDoc` is *the active tab, but only while state == .notes* (Chrome.swift:184), `openDocument` sets `state = .notes` wherever you stood (:853), and `DeskHost` picks the editor or the list by `openDoc == nil` (Desk.swift:48–66). So one view is at once a peer root, the only render surface for the desk, and the list of all notes — and `goToRoot` had to special-case it (:731–738). The code says it in prose: *"A document is a different SURFACE wearing the view's name"* (Chrome.swift:720–726).

2. **The list is reached from the panel, the documents from the bar — two doors on two edges to the same room.** All your notes: top-left, Library → Notes. Your open notes: bottom-right, Open [n]. Both are lists of notes, both open notes, both end in the same editor, and neither mentions the other — the owner's own sentence in the brief. Cause: on 2026-08-17 global STATE went to the panel and global ACTIONS to the bar (Panel.swift:150–151), but *which document am I on* is state, and it lives on the bar as a number. Notes.swift:14–16 rationalises it — *"One is the shelf, the other is what is on the desk"* — a distinction never shown to anyone. Bar.swift:71 calls the box *"the only door to the tabs"*.

3. **"Open N" counts a container nobody was told exists.** Standing in Calendar with three notes open, the bar reads *Open 3*. Calendar is on screen and open in every ordinary sense and is not counted; nor is the Notes list; nor a record card open on top of you. Tap the box and Calendar is not among the cards. The a11y label says *"3 documents open"* while the printed word says *Open* — the code knows the count is narrower than the word (Bar.swift:85–87, 108–111). The `.position` tab case still exists as *"the stale half of a sentence"* (Plane.swift:47–70); the grid draws `desk.liveTabs` only.

4. **The word "Notes" means three surfaces, and the word under + is the only thing on screen that changes with the view.** *Notes* is the panel row, the list's hero and the state a document is drawn in. Meanwhile + reads *Note* in Notes, Inbox and Everything, *Task* in Today and Tasks, *Event* in Calendar — so the one thing that changes when the view changes is not the view's name. Cause: `Feature` is one enum doing three jobs — a root you stand in, a filter over kinds (`Feature.makes`, Navigate.swift:44–50), and, for `.notes` alone, the desk's render mode.

5. **Three routes to the same note, three different backs.** Library → Notes → row; Open → card; Search → hit. Same screen every time; then ‹ from the list route is lit and does nothing visible (it lands on `.state(.notes)` with the note still active); from the switcher route it returns to the view you stood in; cold, it is dim. The panel route also clears the way-back stack on the way in. Cause: two navigation systems overlap — the panel's `go`/`goToRoot` (replace state, clear both stacks: Chrome.swift:698–706, 746–750) and the desk's `openDocument` (push `here`, set state: :841–853), with `here` computed at :760–762 in a way `land` cannot tell apart from "the note is active in Notes".

6. **‹ is a document-history key wearing a browser's glyph in a browser's slot.** Today → Calendar → Tasks through the panel: three moves, ‹ dim. Open one note: ‹ lit. Only `openDocument` pushes (:852); every panel move, workspace switch and list return clears (:701, :748, :681). The bar was copied from a browser's set (Bar.swift:10–15) and Navigate.swift:103–108 wired › to *"the job every browser gives it"* — but the job given to both keys is narrower than a browser's.

7. **The root-of-view rule is a patch, and its two branches do different things.** Tap the row of the view you are in: in five views nothing changes; in Notes the document is deselected but not closed — it vanishes from view, stays counted in *Open*, and is reachable only through the numbered box. `goToRoot` (2026-09-09) exists because `go` preserved the active tab and drew a document where the owner expected a list; the fix deselects (`setActive(nil)`, :746–750 — *"the same thing on screen and a different thing underneath"*) rather than giving the desk a state. Chrome.swift:708–738 is a justification for a special case; Panel.swift:203–214 admits *"It used to be the ONLY half that did that."*

8. **Positions and documents are two remembered things the user never sees as different.** Leave Calendar on March, open a note, come back: March. Leave a note for Calendar, come back to Notes through the panel: the note is gone from view yet still "open". `spots` (one token per Feature, invisible) versus `desk.tabs` (entity ids, counted, aged onto a shelf after 21 days, gridded). Plane.swift:91–110's *"A document is plural; a tool is singular"* is a sound engineering rule with no on-screen expression; ios.md §51 states *"A POSITION SURVIVES, A DOCUMENT DOES NOT"* as a fix, not as something the UI teaches.

9. **+ stays where you stand or moves you, depending on the kind it happens to make.** + in Today makes a task and shows a half-height card over Today; + in Notes makes a note and moves you into a full-screen editor (state → `.notes`, a tab minted, the way-back pushed); + in Inbox makes a note — which is also *"a capture the Inbox lists"* (Desk.swift:684) — and moves you out of Inbox. Records open as cards (Option C, `openAsCard`, no state change); documents open only inside Notes (`createNote` → `adoptCapture` → `openDocument`). The key is labelled by kind, not by consequence.

10. **The switcher is a full-screen mode, not a place, and the only door back to an open note from five of six views.** From Calendar, the note you were editing is reachable only by Open → grid → card: no ‹ (cleared by the panel), no row, no chip. The grid covers the screen; Done returns you to Calendar, not to a note; the Inactive shelf is a mode inside the mode. Because documents render only under `.notes`, a switcher pick must both pick a tab and change state (`show` → `openDocument`, Chrome.swift:549–554 — the 2026-09-09 fix for *"when selecting a note from tabs when not inside Notes, nothing happens"*); Tabs.swift:60 is a `fullScreenCover`.

11. **The panel's selection fill answers "which state" while what you see answers "which surface".** Library shows *Notes* filled while you read a note, while you look at the list, and after a workspace switch that landed you somewhere you did not choose (`adopt` sets `state = .notes`, :678–690). It shows *Today* filled while a record card covers Today. Panel.swift:133 says it outright: *"Nothing in here is about the currently open note."* The app's primary menu cannot say where you are in the half of the app that is the desk.

12. **The Inbox is a queue and the Notes list is a shelf, drawn as six equal rows.** A time dashboard, a document container, a decision queue, a calendar tool, a kind list and a kind-agnostic list are one flat `Feature.inOrder` (Navigate.swift:33) with a monochrome glyph each. Today's footer moves you to Inbox by `go`; the panel by `goToRoot`. Inbox ignores the workspace lens by rule (today-inbox-study, 2026-08-05); Notes obeys it. A user cannot tell from the panel which rows are queues, which are lists, and which is where the documents live. The 2026-08-17 ruling *"each state should be treated equally"* is applied to the doors, not to what they are.

### 1.2 The fault under all twelve

The app has three navigable things and only two of them have a place in the model. The PANEL is a menu of six Features and the only global "where am I". The DESK is an app-wide set of open documents whose only presence is a number in a bar key and a full-screen grid behind it. The VIEWS are six roots rendered under those six Feature states. The desk has no state of its own, so a document can only be drawn by borrowing the Notes state (`openDoc` = active tab AND `state == .notes`) — and Notes must therefore be, at once, one of six peer views, the only render surface for the desk, and the list of all notes.

Every vagueness above falls out of that one overloading: the panel lights *Notes* when you are on the desk (11); the list and the grid compete for "get me back" (2); `go` and `openDocument` are two navigation systems that clear and push one stack differently, so ‹ is a document-history key pretending to be a browser's (5, 6); `goToRoot` had to be invented to make a Notes tap mean the list by deselecting the document instead of by naming it (7); positions live in `spots` and documents in `desk.tabs` and nothing on screen expresses the difference (8); + is local or navigating by which container the kind it makes happens to live in (9). The repo diagnosed the shape once already — kinds-are-peers.md:115–124: *"a note is somewhere you ARE, and a task is somewhere you VISIT … one kind got the main surface and the rest got a drawer"* — and one desk (2026-08-29) fixed the storage half of that without the rendering half. The desk became global in the model and stayed local to Notes on screen.

This is why polishing cannot reach it. No amount of glass, wash, radius, glyph or word under a key can make a three-way relationship crisp when one of the three vertices is defined as a property of another. The vagueness is in which surface owns the document. That is a question of state, and it is answerable in three files (§4.3) before any pixel moves.

### 1.3 What is good and must survive

Every direction was told to keep these or to say, as a reversal, why not.

1. **One desk, app-wide** (`DeskPlanes`, 2026-08-29): a document opened from Today, Search, a link or a notification is the same tab everywhere; the same entity is never two tabs; the count is identical in every view; persisted per workspace with every older key folded and never deleted. The right storage model, and the reason this is a rendering question and not a data one.
2. **"A document is plural; a tool is singular"** (Plane.swift:91–110): one position token per tool in `spots`, restored on arrival. Better than Obsidian's panes-hold-anything; it needs a face, not a change.
3. **Records are cards, never tabs** (Option C, Record.swift, `openAsCard`): a task or event rises over where you stand, closes nothing, writes immediately, minimises to one pill; `TabShape.of(row)` is the one decision; records are swept off the desk on first snapshot. Liv's one genuinely non-Obsidian container and the strongest thing in the app.
4. **Capture never asks**: `liv_capture_at` from the share extension spool, `liv://capture?text=`, Search's Create row and a bare +; a share is saved first and not opened; Route asks WHERE in one tap. Product-correct by what-liv-is-for and to be protected from any navigation change.
5. **Cards from the edge their button is on** (`LivMenu`, `livSheet(from:)`): one menu recipe, one row recipe, every question in the app's own bottom card, no system popovers, no confirmation in front of a reversible verb.
6. **The glass bar as the surface's own foot** (Desk.swift:81–104, 2026-09-08): retires under the keyboard and on scroll, travels with the desk, never retires for an empty desk. The material and the mechanics are right even where the keys' meanings are not.
7. **The six views' contents** — Today's day strip, LATE and Done piles and captured-today footer; Inbox's Route with the clerk's *Work?* chip and Tidy; Calendar timeline-first with the month as a card; Tasks' chips and groups; Everything's All/Upcoming/Unfiled; Notes by log recency, all of it. Each a good surface; the fault is in how they are related.
8. **The panel's mechanics** — drag from anywhere, no close button, the wash takes the closing touch, counts through the lens, the door widens rather than turns accent. Keepers wherever a panel survives; what the rows mean is the open question.
9. **The workspace lens and saved filters as pickers** over existing furniture (rule 5): never a typed query; the core the only parser; Inbox exempt by rule.
10. **`LivReturns`** as a typed, capped stack with `here` computed in one place (Chrome.swift:760–762): the machinery for a real history exists; only its push/clear policy is wrong.
11. **The harness**: `drive.sh` asserting geometry and rendered text across 23 subcommands; `goto notes` refusing a document as an answer; Notes required to list more rows than open tabs. Any redesign can be measured by it on day one.
12. **The furniture as signature** (direction A, 2026-09-06): the six areas in Liv's pen, nameless rows named by kind, the foot counting the unsorted pile, empty states naming the areas, no saturated pixel added. The identity work is done one level below navigation and survives any restructuring above it.

### 1.4 The eighteen questions put to every designer

Each direction answers them in its *Answers to the diagnosis* section; §4 settles the ones the recommendation settles; §6 holds the ones only the owner can.

1. Does the desk have a state of its own you can stand in, or is a document forever drawn by borrowing Notes?
2. When a document is on screen, what does the app's "where am I" indicator say, and is *Notes* the honest answer?
3. Is *Notes* the name of a view, of the container of open documents, or of the list of all notes — and which of the other two gets its own name and door?
4. Are the Notes list and the switcher grid two surfaces or one, and if one, which dies (rule 6)?
5. What does the number in the bar count, and can the user be shown that thing rather than told "documents" by an a11y label?
6. Is ‹ the undo of the last navigation of any kind, or of the last document open only — and if the latter, why does it wear a browser's glyph in a browser's slot?
7. Why does the panel's `go` clear the stack while the desk's `openDocument` pushes, and can one policy serve both?
8. Is "tap the row of the view you are in → its root" a rule the user can see, given it is a no-op in five views and a deselect in the sixth?
9. What on screen tells you a tool resumes where you left it while a document is only "open", and should those be two visible states or one?
10. Is Open [n] a destination, a mode, or the desk itself — and should the grid be full-screen if it is the only way back to a note from five of six views?
11. Does + stay where you stand or move you, and should its word say the kind it makes or the thing that will happen?
12. Are the six rows peers, or a dashboard, a container, a queue and three lists — and should the panel show which is which?
13. Where does a note opened from Today, Calendar, Search or a notification belong when you are done with it: back where you came from, on a desk you must visit, or nowhere?
14. Given the owner has rejected a bar of view keys, one-list-three-arrangements, views-as-tabs and the grid-as-root, what shape makes tasks and calendar central while keeping notes separate and records as cards?
15. Should the "where am I / go somewhere" control live in the thumb zone or stay top-left as the drawer toggle that is the most Obsidian pixel on screen?
16. Why does a workspace switch land on Notes when the app launches on Today, and which of those is home?
17. Which of the two doors to a note survives, or what single indicator makes both honest about the same desk?
18. Can any of this be settled without a mockup the owner sees first, and which `drive.sh` assertion proves the answer on a device?


## 2. What "like Obsidian" means here

The resemblance is not an accident, and not all of it is a fault. The directive of 2026-07-30 was *"starting by copying Obsidian's interface and interaction patterns as a baseline"*. 2026-08-22: *"Remember surface style will be like Obsidian (sizes, text, color except their purple)"* and *"Workspace and Settings at the bottom. See my Obsidian screenshot. Replicate the style."* 2026-08-23: *"the bottom bar having the same button set you'd expect in a browser or Obsidian — literally."* Then 2026-09-06: *"the ui is pretty stale and looks like a bad obsidian clone. how can it be made personal?"* — answered by direction A, which changed the furniture and not the structure — and now the brief, which names the structure. So the question is not whether there is Obsidian in the code (there is, by file and line, below) but which pieces are structural — the ones a person means when they say "it looks like Obsidian" — and which are editor idioms and palette that the owner asked for and that any notes app shares.

### 2.1 Obsidian Mobile, in the era of the owner's clips (1.4–1.8)

No home surface: it opens on the last tab or, with Daily Notes, on today's note; an empty tab reads *"No file is open"*. A header over the note — top-left the sidebar toggle, the title small in the centre, top-right a tab-count square and an ellipsis — that retreats on a downward scroll. A LEFT DRAWER (edge swipe or the toggle) with Files / Search / Bookmarks / Tags across its top and, pinned at its foot, the vault name with a switch chevron and a settings gear; it slides over the note, which dims. A RIGHT DRAWER (edge swipe, no button — phone users cannot find it) with Outline / Backlinks / Properties. A BOTTOM BAR, the page's own fill, five bare glyphs and no words: back, forward (drawn dead almost always), quick switcher, new note, and a calendar-box with today's date that opens the daily note; it hides on scroll with the header. A TAB SWITCHER: a full-screen two-column grid of card previews, an ✕ per card, a plus, *"N tabs"* and Done; tabs persist per vault; a vault switch swaps the whole workspace. An ellipsis menu of grouped cards. An editor with an inline title that scrolls with the body, a properties block behind a chevron, a toolbar above the keyboard, tap-follows-link. A quick switcher that creates on no match. Lucide line icons, neutral greys, one purple. Navigation by folder, search, link and bookmark, and by time only through the daily-note key. The unit of "open" is a tab of one file; a view pane is also a tab. **Documents are where you stand; everything else is a drawer you visit.**

### 2.2 The lifts, by file and line

| Element | Where, and what the code says | Kind | Owner-ruled? |
|---|---|---|---|
| The left drawer = the library: a menu of six doors, edge-swiped from anywhere; the three-zone model (library / desk / item) | Panel.swift:1–9, 129–133; PanelDrag.swift; ios.md rev 2 *"the body is always the Desk … the Obsidian idioms"*; Theme.swift:232–238 (numbers from a ChatGPT clip; *"Notesnook, Obsidian agree"*); the rows are Notesnook's | **Structure** | Yes — 2026-08-15/17/22 (the library is a place; state left, actions below; Notesnook rows) |
| The top-left sidebar toggle in a 44pt circle; ••• top-right | Desk.swift:191–235; Glyph.swift:573–615 `PanelMark` (*"a nearly square plate … with one rounded bar sitting inside its left edge"*) | **Structure** — the single most Obsidian pixel on screen | The circle, yes (2026-08-28); the slot, by inheritance |
| The panel foot: workspace name, count line, gear | Panel.swift:265–270, 310–311, 454–456 (*"Obsidian's foot says how big the vault is; Liv's says how much is unfiled"*) | Structure | Yes — 2026-08-22 *"Replicate the style"* |
| The bottom capsule: five keys in a browser's order and proportions; insets and disabled grey measured off `bar-and-buttons-dynamic-hiding.MOV` | Bar.swift:10–15; Theme.swift:475–518 (sideInset 42, endInset 34, disabledInk 0.30 *"31% of its enabled ink"*, height 57 *"measured off Obsidian for bare glyphs"*) | **Structure** | Yes — 2026-08-23 *"literally"*; the words under keys are Todoist's (2026-09-05) |
| The numbered box: Obsidian's fifth-key shape with the tab count where Obsidian puts the date | Bar.swift:79–83; Glyph.swift:209–219 `LivGlyph.day(n)` | Structure (the shape) | Yes — *"Just have tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box"* |
| The forward key — Obsidian draws it dead; Liv wired it | Navigate.swift:103–107 | Structure (the slot) | Yes |
| The tab switcher: full-screen cover, two-column card grid, ✕ per card, dashed new card, *"N open · Done"* footer — *"Obsidian's, verbatim in structure"* | Desk.swift:1–3; Tabs.swift:60–63, 143–276 (the Inactive shelf is Chrome for iOS's) | **Structure** | The grid, yes (2026-08-23); its being a full-screen mode inherited from 2026-07-29 |
| One desk of tabs per workspace = tabs per vault; the switcher hanging from the foot | Plane.swift:91–110; WorkspaceSwitch.swift:21–23 | Structure (storage right; slot copied) | Storage: team, 2026-08-29 |
| Chrome that hides on scroll (bar and top doors) | Chrome.swift:37–41 *"owner's clips, 2026-08-20 — Obsidian"* | Idiom (Safari's too) | Yes |
| Back/forward over a history stack — *"the most Obsidian thing in the app"* | Navigate.swift:86–140 `LivReturns`; kinds-are-peers.md:51 | Structure | The keys, yes; the policy is §1.1 item 6 |
| The inline title inside the editor's scroll view | Editor.swift:1035–1036; EditorText.swift:709–713 | Editor idiom | Yes — 2026-08-01 |
| Tap follows a link, long-press places the caret | EditorText.swift:1538–1539 | Editor idiom | Yes |
| Find-or-create search (the quick switcher) | Search.swift:9–11 | Idiom | Yes (eval §4.3) |
| Rows grouped on raised cards with a quiet label; the row press highlight | Settings.swift:38–42; Rows.swift:150–153, 186–191 | Idiom (Apple Notes', ChatGPT's as much) | Yes — the 2026-08-20 clips |
| Monochrome line glyphs; neutral greys with one accent | Glyph.swift:187–192; ios.md:557–559 | Palette | Yes — *"except their purple"*; now measured (2026-08-30) |
| The editor parity checklist *"copied from Obsidian mobile"* | editor-study.md:28–61 | Editor idiom | Yes — 2026-07-30 |
| The navigation model benchmarked against Obsidian's | ios-m1-eval.md:31 *"matches Obsidian's best-in-class navigation"* | The reason it converged | — |

Already un-copied, and to be kept that way: the record card (a task or event rises as a half card over where you stand — Obsidian has no object that is not a file); the right drawer (properties became a card, 2026-08-29); launch on Today (Obsidian opens on the last document — though `adopt(workspace:)` still lands on Notes, Chrome.swift:683); *"Untitled"* refused — a nameless row says its kind (Kit.swift:704–710); kind colour in mixed lists and the six areas drawn (Obsidian colours nothing); the forward key alive.

### 2.3 What has to change

The table sorts itself. Palette and editor idioms are the owner's own rulings and are what any notes app shares; changing them would be a re-skin, and the changelog already says why that fails (2026-09-06: *"a collage of other people's apps has no hand of its own"*). Chrome-hides-on-scroll is Safari's as much as Obsidian's. What makes a person say "Obsidian" is five structural things, and all five are in the navigation area:

1. **The left drawer of doors with the toggle top-left** — the three-zone model in words (Panel.swift:1–9). Every direction but one deletes it.
2. **The tab switcher as a full-screen mode behind a numbered box** — the grid the owner asked to keep, in the container he did not.
3. **The five-key capsule** with ‹ › in a browser's slots doing a narrower job than a browser's. The owner ruled it literally, so it is his to keep or change (§6); what a direction may not do is keep the pixels and claim the resemblance is gone.
4. **Two answers to "get me back"** — a recents list (Notes' root) and a tab grid — the most Obsidian-shaped duplication still standing (§1.2).
5. **The assignment of the two axes**: documents are where you stand, everything else is a drawer you visit. This is the fact kinds-are-peers.md diagnosed (*"one kind got the main surface and the rest got a drawer"*) and the one the owner named on 2026-08-16: *"Calendar, Tasks, etc. should be like a central part of the app and not just additions to an Obsidian clone."*

The references reader's eleven axes say the same from the other side: given direction A's constraints — no saturated pixel added; serif, bundled fonts, area tiles and textures refused — Liv's distinctiveness has to be structural: which surface is the root, whether there is a drawer, where documents live, how many persistent chrome elements and where, what the thumb reaches. A palette or glyph pass on the current shape is a re-skin of Obsidian by definition. And any new shape must say why it is not the three the owner has already judged on sight: the four-floor bar (*"a precursor to a sloppy version of clickup"*), one-list-three-arrangements (*"the app is a mess now. revert the whole thing"*), views-as-tabs (Obsidian's own pane model, argued down) — and the grid-as-Notes-root, which hid 126 of 134 notes and is not to be retried.


## 3. The scorecard

Three judges, three lenses, 1–5 each; a 5 was defined as rare and none was given. The scores are of the first drafts; the revision round that followed answered the must-fixes, and the notes under the table say what moved. Totals are arithmetic, not a verdict — §4 weighs them.

| Direction | Product fit | Hand & head | Distinct & cost | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Now & Library (`two`) | 3 | 4 | 4 | **11** |
| Band & Desk (`axes`) | 3 | 4 | 4 | **11** |
| The Spine (`spine`) | 4 | 3.5 | 2 | 9.5 |
| Rooms and a Pocket (`rooms`) | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 9.5 |
| Six Rooms, Six Lenses (`areas`) | 3 | 2.5 | 3 | 8.5 |
| The Line (`field`) | 2 | 3 | 2 | 7 |
| Native Shapes (`native`) | 2 | 3 | 2 | 7 |
| The Desk (`desk`) | 2 | 2 | 2 | 6 |

Rankings, per judge. Product fit: spine, areas, two, axes, rooms, native, field, desk. Hand and head: axes, two, spine, rooms, field, native, areas, desk. Distinctiveness and cost: two, axes, areas, rooms, desk, spine, field, native. `desk` is last on every lens.

**What the revision round changed** (each direction's own *changed after critique* section has the full list; the scores above were not re-issued):

- `two` deleted every gesture (the title-band swipe was inside a `UITextView`; the place swipe collided with Today's strip and Calendar's pager); made the Deck the existing grid in a card instead of a strip; measured the Calendar demotion at ~68pt rather than ~110; drew NOW at 25 unrouted; kept + on NOW as a task (2026-08-28); made a lens change push onto ‹.
- `axes` withdrew the name strip (caption-size clipped names behind a fade — *"too small"* ×3 and *"just ugh"*) and the body swipe; kept the owner's five keys with ‹ › re-meant as one step along the row you are in, the destination's name under each; replaced velocity-dependent drops with one remembered value; named slice 0 — the invisible three-file change.
- `spine` made the `edited` civil stamp a prerequisite of its Notes lens rather than a nicety; withdrew a wrong claim about `liv_snapshot_window_at` (it widens only the recurrence window); fixed the ribbon (scroll and tap, never swipe); added the undated fold at today's head; drew a fresh box; and put the ribbon — the bottom strip removed on 2026-08-23 — as its one question up front, with a fallback.
- `rooms` made chips glyph-only with the active one named at label size; moved the Hall key beside +; kept drag-anywhere as the way to the hall; made + name the surface you see; walled the held record off from the chips.
- `areas` moved the address out of the bar (measured: it did not fit) to a chevron title top-left; left the bar untouched, Forward included; restored the 2026-08-05 exemption for Today and Inbox; made the grid a card; dropped every new gesture.
- `field` put words on all six chips; named the desk chip; made Return open the first hit; put ‹ in the Line; moved Search's list back to top-down.
- `native` checked the deployment floor (iOS 17.0 in both plists) and removed every iOS 26-only piece; moved + and Search to the thumb through the existing capsule; restored Everything as a named place.
- `desk` made the wall on Today the cold-launch frame, one three-key bar, the tool picker a card, the wall at the ruled width — and conceded that its axis assignment is Obsidian's, renamed.

**The judges' cross-cutting notes, quoted.** Written on the first drafts; where a revision answered a point (the Deck strip, the name strip, the swipes, the pill in the bar) the list above says so. The recommendation in §4 follows where the three converge.

Product fit:

> What the whole field is missing: nobody but `areas` makes the six areas — the thing what-liv-is-for calls "the product" — a place you can stand in; seven directions still map the app by kind or by container and leave the furniture as a chip, so 'arrive furnished' is answered by empty-state prose in every one of them. None addresses the first open of an empty box as a navigation problem (`spine` and `desk` are the worst offenders: a ruler of empty rules, a blank sheet). Every direction but `native` deletes the panel and seven of eight reverse 2026-08-23 (the browser bar, the numbered box, the grid, Forward) — the owner said keep the tab system, and the field has quietly agreed among itself that he was wrong; that needs to be put to him as one question, not eight footnotes. Four directions alter the six views he said to keep (`desk` drops Notes, `native` demotes Everything, `two` merges Today and Inbox, `spine` turns Today into a key). Nobody proposes measuring the retrieval rate the furnishing study calls the only metric, and every proposal stacks on revs 54–61, which are unverified on a device. Near-duplicates: `desk` and `axes` are one design with the axes swapped (which is ground, which is pulled up) — `axes` is the product-correct orientation and `desk` can be retired. `rooms`' pocket, `spine`'s ribbon, `native`'s strip, `two`'s Deck and `field`'s '3 open' chip are five drawings of 'give the desk a face at the sill', and the field should pick one (the ribbon/strip, which names the document, over a count). `areas`' address pill and `two`'s Now│Library pill are the same control. `two` and `spine` share the Today+Inbox merge, and `spine` does it better because the pile lands on a day rather than in a section. Combinations worth drawing: `spine`'s stream as the root, `two`'s global/lensed distinction made explicit on it (NOW = the spine at today, unlensed; the lenses wear the workspace), `areas`' room chip as the filing door on every sheet and record card (one tap, visible, reversible — filing after thinking, never before), and `rooms`' rule that + always rises over where you stand. That composite honours reach (one +, rises in place), pile (visible per day, filed where it sits), go back (ribbon for open, month card and Search for old, an `edited` stamp for 'touched'), furnished (areas on every row and every chip), kinds as peers (same day, same row grammar), records as cards, one desk — and reverses only the three rulings the owner would have to re-rule for any of these eight.

Hand and head:

> What the whole field is missing, on this lens: (1) Nobody measures the thumb. Seven directions say 'thumb zone' and none draws the reach arc on a 390×844 frame — far-left sill keys (rooms), split pills at caption size (areas), and a top-right + (native) all pass as 'thumb' in prose. (2) Nobody designs the KEYBOARD-UP state, which is where a notes app spends most of its time: every direction keeps 'the bar retires under the keyboard', so inside a note with the keyboard raised there is no navigation at all in any of the seven — the same cul-de-sac the 2026-08-17 ruling forbade for views. (3) Six of seven delete ‹ › and replace back with a gesture (edge swipe, drag from anywhere, drop, flick) with no visible key on day one; only 'two' and 'spine' keep a single ‹ with a single meaning, and they are the two where the back stack stops lying. (4) A new collision class appears everywhere at once: a horizontal element in the bar that BOTH scrolls and swipes — areas' pill and Open key, axes' strip, spine's ribbon, field's Line, rooms' well, two's title band (worst: inside the text view). Each needs one rule: chips scroll and taps switch, or a fixed handle swipes. (5) Only 'rooms' answers vagueness 9 (+ never navigates); everyone else keeps 'a note fills the screen, a task is a card' and labels the key by kind — the lens's 'same key, two consequences' remains the field's standing defect. (6) Velocity- and height-dependent outcomes (axes' slow-vs-flick, three-meaning Open key; desk's spatial back) are mode errors dressed as physics.
>
> Near-duplicates: 'axes' and 'two' are the same skeleton — a place pill or strip at the left of the bar, a desk that rises from the bottom over the place, Notes as the floor/lens — differing in whether the places are five paged tools or two rooms with four lenses. 'desk' is 'axes' upside down (notes are the ground, tools are the wall) and is the weaker orientation because it re-affirms notes-primary. 'areas' and 'rooms' share a word and nothing else: areas makes the room a filter mode, rooms makes it a screen. 'spine' and 'two' both merge Today+Inbox into the launch surface; spine goes further and merges everything into time.
>
> Combinations that would score higher than any single entry: take 'two's' place model (Now / Library, the one distinction a user can feel, printed as two words at the thumb) + 'axes'' desk-from-below with the shelf under the fan (the open/all relationship made visible) + 'rooms'' creation rule (+ always rises over where you stand, never navigates) + 'spine's' ribbon or 'native's' strip as the persistent, TITLED face of the desk (a number is never as good as a name) — and keep 'two's' single-meaning ‹. Drop every bar-element swipe; keep one drag (the place swipe, vetoed in the pager) and make the pill the taught door. That composite resolves 1–12 including 9, keeps everything at the thumb, keeps a visible back, and asks a first-day user to learn exactly two words and one direction.

Distinctiveness and cost:

> What the whole field converges on, and nobody says: five of eight directions (areas, two, rooms, spine, native) put the desk on screen as a horizontal strip of open-document chips or cards in the thumb zone above or in the bar — the exact element the owner removed on 2026-08-23 ('Not a tab "bar", remove it'). That is the one question the panel should put to him before any direction is drawn further, because every direction's 'the desk gets a face' claim depends on the answer, and the answer is one sentence from him, not a mockup. What the field is missing: (1) a mockup of the BAR alone — six of eight delete Forward, four delete Back, three delete the numbered box, and nobody has measured the replacement text (address pill, name strip, chips) at caption size against 'things are too small' ×3; the owner's most literal ruling ('a browser's set, literally') is reversed by most directions without a single drawn bar to rule on. (2) The cheapest fix under every direction is the same three-file change nobody proposes as a first slice: give the desk its own state (`shown`/`height`/`raised` on DeskModel, `openDoc` without the `.notes` guard, DeskHost choosing the document layer by that state), delete goToRoot/showList, and keep the current chrome. That is Chrome.swift + Desk.swift + Panel.swift's row handler, provable with the existing `goto notes` and `desk` checks in a day, and it resolves vaguenesses 1, 5, 7 and 11 before any chrome is touched — mockup-first is the house rule for VISIBLE UI, and this change is invisible. (3) Nobody costs the harness honestly: every direction rewrites tour/panel/bar/grid, and revs 54–61 are still unverified on a simulator; the first build.sh + drive.sh run should precede all of this. (4) Nobody keeps the record card AND makes it the app's opening idiom for documents except `two` (layers at three heights) and `rooms` (record chip in the pocket); the reader called the card 'Liv's one genuinely non-Obsidian container, under-used as an identity' and most directions leave it as an exception. Near-duplicates: `two` and `areas` share the chrome (pill in a five-slot capsule, cards from the bottom, deck strip, Open box kept) — areas is two plus a rooms axis the owner did not ask for; `rooms` and `native` share a skeleton (a full-screen hall of six tiles is a tab bar drawn as a screen; the pocket is Music's strip); `axes` and `desk` are mirror images (which axis holds the documents), and `axes` is the one that inverts Obsidian's assignment while `desk` keeps it. Combinations worth drawing: `two`'s Now/Library places with `axes`' desk sheet at two heights (the fan pinned as the shelf's first band) and `rooms`' chip well as the strip IF the owner re-rules the strip; `areas`' room chip on a document's head is a cheap add to any winner; `field`'s Line is a good component inside `two` or `axes` and a poor root. `spine` should be kept as the reference for what 'the day is the home' means literally and not built until the `edited` stamp and the window cost test exist. If one direction goes to the owner as a mockup this week it should be `two`, with `native` built beside it as the control because it costs almost nothing and makes the distinctiveness question concrete on the same simulator.


## 4. The recommendation

Written to be acted on, not chosen from.

### 4.1 Mock up `two` — Now & Library — with the desk from `axes`, and nothing else first

**The shape, in one paragraph.** Two places, named as two words on one glass pill at the thumb: NOW (Today's date, day strip, LATE, timeline and Done, with Inbox's CAUGHT and SUGGESTED folded in above them; global, never lensed; the launch surface; + makes a task dated to the shown day) and LIBRARY (everything you own — Everything · Notes · Tasks · Calendar as one `LivSegment` under the lens's own hero band and a workspace line; it wears the workspace and says so). No panel, no top-left door, no top chrome on a place. A document is never a place: it is a layer that rises over whichever place you stand in and lays down onto exactly that place; a record is the half card it is today; the open documents are the grid the owner already has — `TabSwitcher`'s body, content and order unchanged — raised from the Open key as a card at half height over the place, which stays visible and washed above it. The bar is [Now│Library] · ‹ · Search · + · Open [n]: one ‹ with one meaning (the previous surface of any kind — every change of the screen's name pushes; only a workspace switch clears), › gone. That is `two` as revised.

**Taken from `axes`, two things.** First, the Notes lens pins the open documents as its first band above the recency list, with the Inactive fold between them, so the subset (open) is visibly the head of the whole (all), and the Deck card's foot row — *"Notes · 134"* — names the list it is a subset of. This is the direct answer to the sentence in the brief: the list stops being a destination that happens to sit beside the open notes and becomes the floor they stand on; and it makes the 2026-08-24 mistake (grid as root, 126 notes hidden) structurally impossible. Second, a drag down at a document's scroll top is the gesture twin of ‹: it lays the document down onto the surface it was lifted from, it works with the keyboard up (it commits the text, as a system sheet does), and it is the only navigation gesture in the app. Every other swipe in the field is deleted, so the judges' collision class — a bar element that both scrolls and swipes — does not exist here.

**Taken from `rooms`, one rule.** + never navigates. A task rises as a card; a note rises as a layer from the sill, vertically, in the same family of motion as the card and the Deck (record half, deck half-to-tall, document full); the lateral easing (`LivMotion.nav`) is reserved for a place replacing a place. The word under + is the kind of the surface you see — *Note* over a raised note — never of something hidden beneath it. Vagueness 9 is not resolved by this (a note is still full-height and a task half; that is a ruling), but the label and the consequence stop disagreeing.

**Taken from `areas`, one chip.** The area chip in the head of every document layer and every record card, tapping to the six areas as the Route card. One tap, visible, reversible, after thinking. It is the only place in this recommendation where the furniture reaches an open thing, and it costs no navigation.

**Why this and not `axes` whole.** The two are one skeleton — a place control at the left of the bar, a desk that rises from the bottom over the place, Notes as the floor — and the choice is which places: five paged tools (`axes`) or two words with four lenses (`two`). Four reasons for the two words. (1) The global/lensed split is the one distinction a user of this app can feel — Today and Inbox already ignore the lens by rule (2026-08-05) — and `two` turns the rule into architecture and prints it as two words; the pile lands on the first screen every launch, which is the product's second test made visible without a visit. (2) The diagnosis's complaint about ‹ was about its meaning; `two` keeps the meaning (a browser's) and changes the pixels, while `axes` keeps the pixels and gives ‹ › a third meaning (spatial, no history) — §1.1 item 6 with a different label. (3) `axes` re-labels three of five bar words on every page and prints truncated titles under ‹ › on a document; *"too small"* ×3 and *"tiny and subtle"* will land there, and its band pager rides the surface that has lagged three times. (4) `two` composes from bodies that exist — Now.swift is Today's sections plus Inbox's, Library.swift is a segment over four existing views, the Deck is the existing grid in a `livSheet` — and is the one direction whose first slice can be on the simulator with 17 of `drive.sh`'s 23 subcommands keeping their assertions.

**What it does not do, said plainly.** It does not put the six areas on the map — `areas`' bet, deferred: a room that stamps what you make in it is filing before thinking, the July finding, and browsing by area cannot be judged until retrieval is measured. It does not make time the root — `spine`, the best product answer in the field and the least buildable (4.5). It keeps the capsule and the numbered box, two of the literal lifts in §2.2, because the owner ruled both by name; if he wants the box redrawn, `LivGlyph.day(n)` is one drawing. Its nearest living relative is Apple Music's *Listen Now | Library*, and the mockup must show on the same artboard what separates them: one pill in a bar of actions, not a tab bar; one list under four lenses, not categories that push; everything you open rises over a place, not only a player; a day with a pile you empty, not a feed. If NOW reads as a dashboard and Library as "the real app", it has degraded into Notion mobile's home-plus-drawer, and the owner will see that before anyone argues it.

**The reversals it needs — four sentences from the owner, not eight footnotes** (§6, 1–4). (1) Today and Inbox share one scroll, and Notes, Tasks, Calendar and Everything become lenses of one place — a change to the six equal roots of 2026-08-17. (2) The full-screen switcher becomes the same grid in a half-height card over the place — the container of 2026-08-23, not the content. (3) › is deleted — one key of the *"browser's set, literally"*; the fallback is a one-slot pill naming only the other place. (4) The library stops being a pushed place (2026-08-15/23) and its remaining rows — workspaces, saved filters, New filter, Trash, and the foot the owner pointed at — live in a card from the Library's workspace line. Everything else ruled stands: launch on Today, records as cards, one desk, positions per tool, the bar as the surface's foot, capture never asks, no query language, + makes what the place holds in one tap.

### 4.2 What to do with the good parts

Leave them. `DeskPlanes`, `spots` and `Positions.swift` are untouched; `Record.swift` and Option C are untouched and gain a chip; the six view bodies are re-hosted, not rewritten; `LivMenu`/`livSheet(from:)` host two more cards; the glass bar keeps its material, metrics and mechanics and loses one key; `LivReturns` keeps its type and gains the one policy it should have had; Search, the editor, the share extension, `Routes.swift`, `Box.swift` and every `liv_*` call are as they are. No core, services or FFI line moves — this is a shell rendering change, which is what §1.2 said it was. The one keeper that goes is the panel's push-and-wash, which goes with the panel; its wash idiom moves to the Deck card. The furniture (direction A) is the identity, and the composite adds no pixel to it.

### 4.3 The cheapest first slice — and it needs no mockup

Every one of the eight directions begins with the same three-file change, and the cost judge named it: give the desk a state of its own. In `Chrome.swift`, a `shown` (the document on screen) beside `state`; `openDoc` becomes the active tab iff `shown`, and the `state == .notes` guard at :184 goes; `openDocument` stops writing `state = .notes` (:853) and `adopt(workspace:)` stops landing on Notes (:683); `goToRoot` (:731) and `showList` (:746) are deleted, because a view can no longer be wearing a document — the panel's row handler sets `state` and lays the document down. In `Desk.swift`, `DeskHost` chooses the document layer by `shown`, never by `openDoc == nil`. Nothing in the chrome moves. Two behaviours change, both in the direction the owner has already asked for: the lit panel row under a document is the view you opened it from, never *Notes*; and ‹ from a note opened off the list lands on the list instead of doing nothing. It resolves vaguenesses 1, 5, 7 and 11 under today's pixels; it is provable on day one with the existing `goto notes` (refuses a document), `desk` (the count across six views) and `history` (a switcher pick from Today lands on the note, back = Today) checks — break one on purpose first; and it is the foundation the visible slice stands on. It needs one sentence from the owner (§6, 8), not a mockup, because it designs no visible UI. It is also the hedge: if he reads 4.1 and decides *"maybe our system can't get much better"*, this alone has removed four of the twelve.

Before it: the first `build.sh` + `drive.sh bar/panel/tour/chrome/grid/history/routes/spool` run on revs 54–61, which are unverified on a device. Nothing in this study is trusted on top of an unverified rev.

### 4.4 The visible slice — mockup first

Five artboards, drawn before a line of the visible change is written and shown beside the current app: NOW at 25 unrouted captures (the number that sank Quick Capture — the hard case, not the easy one; CAUGHT capped at three rows with a *"22 more"* fold); LIBRARY › Calendar with the timeline's first hour measured against today's (~68pt lower — the 2026-08-16 *"central part of the app"* test in points); LIBRARY › Notes with the open band pinned above the recency list; the Deck card raised over NOW, once with three cards and once with eight; a document layer over NOW with the area chip in its head and the pill naming the place beneath. One control artboard: `native`, beside the four-floors screenshot with its five differences labelled — drawn, not built, because it is the shape the owner has judged on sight and a drawing costs nothing.

After his word on the four reversals, the build: the bar pill; `Library.swift` (a `LivSegment` over the four existing bodies under a hero band and a workspace line); `Now.swift` (Today's sections and Inbox's); the existing `TabSwitcher` body in a `livSheet` at .medium/.large; the workspace card gaining the panel's filter, Trash and foot rows; `Panel.swift`, `PanelDrag.swift` and `PanelMark` deleted; `spots` re-keyed by lens with the old `Feature` keys folded and never deleted (the Plane.swift:304–360 precedent). About 18 of 47 files, most of them deletions or re-hosting; no view body, editor file, `Plane.swift`, `Positions.swift`, `Record.swift` or `Box.swift` changes. `drive.sh`: `tour` becomes now → library×4 → document; `panel` becomes `place` (the pill's rendered text, then the other word tapped and the surface marker changed); `grid` becomes `deck` (the card stops at the medium detent with the place's marker still rendered above it; the count equals the cards); `desk` and `bar` are rewritten; `lens`, `facets`, `rows`, `chrome`, `workspace`, `history`, `routes`, `spool`, `areas`, `vault`, `create`, `cycles` and `quiet` keep their assertions with new gotos. Every new check is broken on purpose once before its green is trusted.

### 4.5 What not to do

- **Do not build `desk`.** It is `axes` upside down — documents as the ground, tools on a wall you slide in — which is Obsidian's assignment of the axes with the words changed, and it says so itself. Last on every lens. Retired to the appendix.
- **Do not build `spine` now.** It is the best product answer in the field — one stream of days, the six views as subtractive lenses, a task and a note on the same day in one row grammar, capture landing where you live — and the least buildable: six view bodies rewritten into a section builder, three position tokens deleted, an owner-gated FFI stamp (`edited`) as a prerequisite, 30+ of 47 files, and a ribbon that is the strip removed on 2026-08-23. Keep it as the reference for what *"the day is the home"* means taken literally. If the shape in 4.1 is judged not distinctive enough, this is the next thing to draw — after the `edited` stamp exists and retrieval can be measured — not `rooms` or `areas`.
- **Do not lead with `areas`.** Its address title is the best "where am I" control in the field and the only one no reference app has; but it adds an axis the owner did not ask for and re-runs the experiment the furnishing study retired in July — a room stamps what you make in it, which is filing after walking, not after thinking — and it pays off only for the person who walks into rooms deliberately. Take its chip. Revisit rooms as places when a retrieval rate says people navigate by area.
- **Do not build `rooms`.** The hall costs two full-screen slides through a menu for the most frequent act in the app; there is no history; and the pocket is the removed strip unless the owner reads it as Safari's pill. Its one unique answer — + never navigates, the surface you see decides the kind — is in 4.1.
- **Do not ship `native`.** It is the rejected four floors with the library in a sheet and + reachable only through a hand-built strip; it has no hand of its own and admits it. Draw it as the control; do not spend a week of deletions on a shape the owner has already judged.
- **Do not make `field` the root.** A search field as the app's face contradicts the furnishing study's best-supported finding (people navigate to where they put things; better search does not change that), and its Tray is the four-floors bar with words. The Line — find-or-create as a standing field — is a component worth remembering for Search later, not a navigation model.
- **Do not put a persistent strip of open-document chips at the sill** in any direction until the owner has re-ruled 2026-08-23 in writing (§6, 5). Five directions drew one; the shape in 4.1 does not need it.
- **Do not polish.** No glass, wash, radius, glyph or word under a key changes which surface owns the document (§1.2).


## 5. What stays, whatever is chosen

Every direction keeps these; the recommendation touches none; a future direction that touches one must say so as a reversal and get the owner's word.

1. **One desk, app-wide** (`DeskPlanes`): entity tabs, one per entity, per workspace, the inactive predicate at 21 days, every older persistence key folded on read and never deleted. The storage is right; only its face changes.
2. **A document is plural, a tool is singular**: one position token per tool (`spots`, `Positions.swift`), restored on arrival, never a list.
3. **Records are cards, never tabs**: `TabShape.of(row)` is the one decision, `DeskModel.open` the one door; a task or event rises over where you stand, closes nothing, writes immediately, minimises to one pill; a saved tab that turns out to hold a record is closed quietly.
4. **Only documents are ever on the desk.** No view, lens or place is a tab; views-as-tabs (Obsidian's panes) stays refused.
5. **The desk gets a state of its own.** No direction keeps the Notes hijack; §4.3 is the floor under all of them.
6. **Launch on Today** — *"planning is what this one is for"* (2026-08-18) — and a workspace switch never lands on Notes.
7. **The Notes list contains every note** the lens admits, by log recency — *"a surface named after a thing has to contain it"* — and the harness keeps asserting it lists more rows than there are open tabs.
8. **Today and Inbox ignore the workspace lens** (2026-08-05); Calendar, Tasks, Everything and every count wear it. Whether stated as a rule or drawn as two places, it holds.
9. **Capture never asks**: a catch from the share sheet or `liv://capture` is saved first through `liv_capture_at`, stamped as a workspace stamps it today, and not opened; Route asks WHERE (the six areas), never what type; no inline parsing (2026-08-09, *"Do not propose it again"*).
10. **One + at the thumb** that makes what the place holds in one tap and offers the menu on a hold; never a per-view floating key, never a *New* button in an empty state.
11. **The bar is Liquid Glass, worded, and the surface's own foot**: it retires on scroll and under the keyboard, never for an empty desk; its material and metrics live in `Theme.swift`.
12. **Every question in the app's own bottom card** (`LivMenu`, `livSheet(from:)`), from the edge its button is on; no system popover, no stock control, no confirmation in front of a reversible verb.
13. **No query language a person can see** (rule 5): workspaces and filters are pickers; the core is the only parser.
14. **The furniture is the signature** (direction A): the six areas in Liv's pen, nameless rows named by kind, the unsorted pile counted, no saturated pixel added, no serif, no bundled font, no textures. Distinctiveness above it is structural.
15. **Every size and colour in `Theme.swift`; every `liv_*` call in `Box.swift`**; no core, services or FFI change for any navigation direction except `spine`'s `edited` stamp, which is owner-gated and failing-test-first.
16. **The harness discipline**: `drive.sh` asserts geometry or rendered text, never whether a view is mounted; a green is trusted only after an assertion was broken on purpose; the first run on revs 54–61 precedes every slice.
17. **Mockup-first for visible UI, the owner's written word for every reversal**, rulings quoted and not paraphrased, no time estimates, one thing at a time.


## 6. Questions only the owner can answer

One sentence each. The first four are the reversals §4.1 depends on; nothing visible is built before they are answered in writing.

1. May Today and Inbox share one scroll (NOW), with Notes, Tasks, Calendar and Everything as four lenses of one Library — a change to the six equal roots of 2026-08-17?
2. May the full-screen tab grid become the same grid, content and order unchanged, in a half-height card over the place you stand in — the container of 2026-08-23, not the content?
3. May › go, given every move now pushes onto ‹ and a place pill wants its slot — or does the *"browser's set, literally"* stand, with a one-slot pill naming only the other place?
4. Does the library stop being a pushed place (2026-08-15/23), its workspaces, filters, Trash and the foot you pointed at moving to a card from the Library's workspace line?
5. Is a visible, titled face for the desk at the sill (a pill naming the active note, as `native` and `spine` draw it) the strip you removed on 2026-08-23, or Safari's pill — judged with three chips and with eight?
6. Is Calendar as a lens, its timeline starting ~68pt lower under the Library's head, still *"a central part of the app"* (2026-08-16), or does Calendar need a door of its own?
7. Does + on NOW make a task dated to the shown day (2026-08-28) or a note?
8. Slice 0's one visible word: under a document, the lit row — later the pill — names the view you opened it from, never *Notes*; yes?
9. Do the open notes sit as a pinned band at the head of the Notes list (the recency order broken once, above the fold), or only as an *Open* mark on their rows?
10. Does the area chip belong in the head of every document and record card as the filing door, or does filing stay behind the properties key?
11. Is the `edited` civil stamp — an additive, optional wire field, failing-test-first in the settled zone — wanted at all, since it gates `spine` and any "touched" order in Notes?
12. Can the first `build.sh` + `drive.sh` run on revs 54–61 happen on a device before the mockup review, since nothing in this study is verified on one?
13. Should the retrieval rate — the share of sessions that open something older than three days, the furnishing study's one metric — be instrumented before the visible slice ships, so this change and the deferred `areas` and `spine` bets are judged by a number rather than on sight?
14. If NOW reads to you as a dashboard and Library as "the real app", is that the end of `two`, and is `spine` — after the stamp — the next thing to draw?


## 7. The directions, strongest first

### 1. Now & Library

*Liv has exactly two places — NOW (the day, the pile you caught, the clerk's questions; global, never lensed) and LIBRARY (everything you own, seen through four lenses that wear the workspace) — named as two words on one pill at the thumb; a document is never a place but a layer that opens over whichever place you stand in, and the open documents are the grid you already know, raised as a card over the place instead of a screen that hides it.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE NAVIGATION CONCEPT. Today the app has six roots in a drawer, a desk that renders only by borrowing the Notes root, and a bar key that opens a full-screen grid. This direction collapses that to two levels with one rule each.

LEVEL 1 — TWO PLACES. You are always standing in exactly one. NOW is Today and Inbox folded into one scroll: the date, the area line, the 7-day strip, CAUGHT (Inbox·Route), SUGGESTED (Inbox·Tidy), LATE, the day's timeline, DONE. NOW ignores the workspace lens, as Today and Inbox already do by rule (2026-08-05). LIBRARY is everything you own: one lens row — Everything · Notes · Tasks · Calendar — over the four existing bodies. LIBRARY wears the lens and says so on its second line ("Work · Urgent ▾"); NOW does not and never shows a lens word. That one visible difference is why there are two places and not one: the 2026-08-05 exemption stops being a rule to remember and becomes the architecture.

THE LIBRARY'S HEAD, measured. Two bands and a line, never more: (1) the hero band (44pt) is the LENS's own name — "Everything", "Notes", "Tasks" — or, on Calendar, the day title with its chevron, Today pill and ‹ › exactly as Calendar.swift:217-260 draws it now (the day title is already the hero at LivType.hero); (2) the workspace line (~24pt, caption, ink tier down, tap → the workspace card); (3) the lens row, one LivSegment (44pt, Kit.swift:194). The word "Library" is printed only on the bar's pill. Against today's Calendar (hero band, then the timeline), the timeline's first hour lands ~68pt lower (44 + 24), not the ~110 the first draft claimed, and Everything/Notes/Tasks pay the same 68. That is the whole price of Calendar as a lens, in points; whether it passes the owner's 2026-08-16 "central part of the app" test is his call, and the day view — 7-day strip plus timeline — is on the first screen at launch.

MOVING BETWEEN PLACES: the pill, only. The bar's first element is a double-width glass pill reading "Now │ Library", the current word in full ink and the other one tier down; tap the other word to go there, tap the current word to scroll to the top. There is NO horizontal place-swipe: NOW's 7-day strip and all-day band (Today.swift:510) and Calendar's pager already own that axis, and a gesture that silently fails on two of six surfaces is a mode error, not an accelerator. PanelDrag.swift is deleted, not repurposed. No drawer, no top-left door, no top chrome on a place: the surface runs to the clock and the hero is the first thing on it.

LEVEL 2 — LAYERS over a place. Three things open over the place you stand in, and none changes the place: (a) a DOCUMENT (note or file) slides in laterally as a full-screen layer with its own foot (the same bar); ‹ drops it back onto the place; (b) a RECORD (task/event) rises as the half-height card it is today and minimises to the pill; (c) the DECK — the open documents — rises from the Open key as a CARD (.medium by default, drag to .large) holding the same two-column grid the numbered box opens today, over the place, which stays visible and washed above it. Search, properties, history, the workspace card, menus and the route card are covers exactly as now. Three heights of one idiom: record half, deck half-to-tall, document full.

THE DESK HAS ITS OWN STATE. `DeskModel` gets `place: Place` (.now | .library), `lens: Lens` (Library's, persisted as a spot) and `shown: UInt64?` (the document on screen). `openDoc` is `shown` with no state guard. Opening a document sets `shown`, never `place`. That single change removes the Notes overloading: Notes is a lens, the Deck is the container, the document is a layer, and nothing wears a name it does not own.

WHAT "OPEN" MEANS. The Deck holds documents only — `DeskPlanes.desk` unchanged. Places are not "open": there are two and they always exist. So the count on the bar equals the number of cards in the grid it raises, and the a11y label ("3 documents open") and the printed word finally agree.

WHAT THIS REVERSES, put as questions, not footnotes: (1) the six equal roots (2026-08-17) become two places and four lenses — Today+Inbox merge; (2) the full-screen grid (2026-08-23 "as they appeared when you clicked the numbered box") becomes the same grid in a card; (3) › is deleted (2026-08-23 "a browser's set, literally"); (4) the library as a pushed panel (2026-08-15/23) becomes a place beside the day. Each has its one-line fallback in the open questions. Nothing else ruled is reversed: + still makes what the place holds (a TASK on NOW, as on Today), records are cards, one desk, launch on the day, filters are pickers, capture never asks.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** HOW NOTE TABS AND THE NOTE LIST ARE REPRESENTED. They get different names, different doors, different shapes, and stop competing for "get me back".

THE NOTE LIST is the Notes lens of the Library: every non-trashed, non-archived, non-record row the lens admits, by log recency — `NotesList` verbatim, mounted under the Library's head. It is a way of looking at what you OWN. It knows one fact about the desk: a row whose entity is on the Deck carries the word "Open" in its fact tier (a fact drawn only where it changes, 2026-09-02). "Notes" now means one thing — this lens — and is printed in one place, the lens row, plus the hero band while the lens is lit. It is never the state a document is drawn in.

THE NOTE TABS are the Deck: the one app-wide set of open documents (`DeskPlanes.desk`, unchanged in storage, per workspace, aged onto the Inactive shelf after 21 days). It is a way of looking at what you are WORKING ON. Two doors, no gestures:

1. The Open [n] key raises the DECK as a card from the bottom: the existing `TabSwitcher` grid — two columns, anchored to the bottom so the newest is at the thumb, the New-note card bottom-right, ✕ on each, the active card marked, "Inactive n ›" swapping in the shelf with its "Close all" — hosted in the app's own sheet at .medium (about four cards) and draggable to .large (what the full screen showed, minus a washed band of the place at the top). The place stays visible above it, washed 50%; a tap on the wash or a pull down drops it (the record card's grammar). This keeps the owner's word on the SHAPE ("tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box") and changes only its HOST: the grid no longer hides the place you left, so "which note was I on" is answered without leaving where you are. A card tap opens that document as a layer over the place and drops the Deck.

2. A document is opened onto the Deck by any row tap, search hit, [[link]], notification, `liv://entity` or capture, through `DeskModel.open` — the one door, unchanged. Same entity, same card, never two.

There is NO swipe that walks the Deck. The first draft put one on the document's title band; that band is a `UITextView` (EditorText.swift:715) where a sideways drag is caret placement or selection, and a navigation gesture inside a text view is a mode error shipped into the editor. Walking from one open note to the next is Open → card, two taps, as it is today.

Closing: ✕ on a card, or "Move to Trash" from •••. Dropping a document layer (‹, or tapping the place pill) does NOT close it — it stays on the Deck. So "nothing is closed" (2026-09-09) holds, and the user can now see the difference between "dropped" (the place is on screen, the count unchanged) and "closed" (the count fell).

What this deletes: `goToRoot` and `showList` (a document is not in a view, so there is no "tap the view you are in → its root"), the `state == .notes` guard on `openDoc`, `Feature.notes` as a root, the `fullScreenCover` host of the switcher (App.swift:267 — its grid, `TabCard`, `InactiveTabs` and `LivTabs` survive), and the rule that a workspace switch lands on Notes (it lands on the Library, whose second line is the workspace).

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** HOW THE SIX VIEWS FIT.

```
PLACE  NOW   (global — never lensed; the launch surface; the + makes a Task dated to the shown day)
  ├ date hero + area line ("Work 2 · Home 1 · 25 unfiled") + 7-day strip   ← Today's, verbatim
  ├ CAUGHT n · "Route all ›"   3 newest rows, then a "22 more ›" row that unfolds in place   ← Inbox·Route
  │     row tap → route card (six areas, then "Not a note…"); "Work?" chip files in one tap; swipe left → trash
  ├ SUGGESTED n  (fold)                                   ← Inbox·Tidy (leading circle accepts)
  ├ LATE n  (fold)                                        ← Today's LATE pile
  ├ the day's timeline: tasks + events                    ← Today's list (Calendar's day view, which Today already draws)
  └ DONE n  (fold)                                        ← Today's Done pile
PLACE  LIBRARY   (wears workspace + saved filter; the + makes what the lens holds)
  ├ hero band: the lens's name — or, on Calendar, the day title ▾ · Today · ‹ ›   (Calendar.swift header, unchanged)
  ├ "Work · Urgent ▾" → the workspace card, from the top edge:
  │     workspaces · saved filters (one toggled) · New filter · Trash · foot: workspace name, unfiled count, Settings gear
  │     (Panel.swift's rows 235-292 minus the six view rows, re-hosted in WorkspaceSwitch.swift)
  ├ lens row: Everything · Notes · Tasks · Calendar   (LivSegment; one spot each; the last lens used is restored)
  │   ├ Everything: All / Upcoming / Unfiled pills; the list         ← Everything.swift
  │   ├ Notes: the list by recency; rows on the Deck say "Open"      ← Notes.swift
  │   ├ Tasks: filter chips, completes groups; rows → card           ← Tasks.swift
  │   └ Calendar: the timeline; month grid behind the title          ← Calendar.swift, Month.swift
LAYERS over either place (never change the place)
  ├ Document (note / file)     — full-screen, own foot; ‹ drops it; stays on the Deck
  ├ Record card (task / event) — half sheet; swipe-down → pill; one pill
  ├ Deck (Open n)              — the grid, in a card at .medium / .large
  └ Search cover, properties card, history card, route card, create menu, workspace card, Settings, camera
```

Six views become two places and four lenses: Today + Inbox → NOW; Everything, Notes, Tasks, Calendar → the Library's lenses. The `Feature` enum's six cases become `Place { now, library }` and `Lens { everything, notes, tasks, calendar }`. `Feature.inOrder` becomes `Lens.inOrder`; `Feature.makes` becomes `Lens.makes` (note / note / task / event) and `Place.now.makes = .task` (the 2026-08-28 rule, kept).

The positions rule ("a document is plural; a tool is singular", Plane.swift:91-110) survives and gains a face: `spots` is keyed by `Lens` plus one `now` entry (`NowPosition` = day, doneExpanded, lateOpen, caughtExpanded, suggestedExpanded — `TodayPosition` and `InboxLens` folded; the old `Feature` keys are read and folded, never deleted, as Plane.swift:304-360 does for v2). The Library remembers the lens you left it on, so "Library" from NOW lands on the Calendar month you were browsing with the pill lit — the on-screen expression the diagnosis said positions never had.

The "kinds are peers" question gets a third answer that is neither four floors nor three arrangements: four ways of looking at ONE list, in one place, under one head — not four places on a bar. A task and a note are peers because Everything shows them together and Notes/Tasks are the same list narrowed; Calendar is the same list on a clock.

The empty box on first open: NOW with no rows is Today's empty state, which already names the six areas (2026-09-06); the pile section is absent, not empty (a fact drawn only where it changes).

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** WHY IT IS NOT OBSIDIAN, against the resemblance list item by item — then the nearest living relative, named.

1. "LEFT DRAWER = THE LIBRARY" (Panel.swift's three-zone model; Obsidian's drawers, Notesnook's rows, ChatGPT's geometry, Obsidian's foot). GONE. No drawer, no toggle, no wash-the-desk-and-push. Panel.swift, PanelDrag.swift and PanelMark are deleted. The library is a PLACE beside the day, reached by a pill at the thumb. The panel foot (workspace + count + gear — "Obsidian's shape, which the owner pointed at", 2026-08-22) survives as the foot of the WORKSPACE CARD: same three things, inside a card that comes from the top edge when you tap the workspace line. The owner's "replicate the style" applied to a panel; the argument to him is that a place cannot be Obsidian's drawer if there is no drawer, and the foot he liked is still there where the workspace is.

2. "TOP-LEFT SIDEBAR TOGGLE + TOP-RIGHT ELLIPSIS" (the most Obsidian pixel on screen). The top-left door is gone from every surface. A place has NO top chrome — the first thing under the clock is the date or the lens's name. A document keeps Properties + ••• top-right because that is the note's furniture, not the app's; the ••• is Anytype's and Apple's as much as Obsidian's.

3. "BOTTOM CAPSULE (copied, then relabelled)". The capsule stays — the owner asked for it by name, twice. Its key set changes where it was literal: the ‹ › pair, "the most Obsidian thing in the app" (kinds-are-peers.md:51), the one Obsidian draws dead, becomes the Now│Library pill and one ‹. No reference app has a two-word place pill in a glass capsule. › dies (rule 6: forward was dim almost always and nothing pushes onto it now). Measured: the capsule is 306pt wide on a 390 screen (LivBar.sideInset 42), five slots of 58.8pt today; the pill takes two (117.6pt) and "Now │ Library" at caption 14 medium is ~100pt — it fits with margin. The one-slot fallback ("Library" alone, 48pt in 58.8) fits on width and names only a destination.

4. "THE NUMBERED BOX (copied shape, new meaning)". RETAINED ON PURPOSE, and said so: the owner ruled the box by name ("Just have tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box", 2026-08-23) and asked for its word (2026-09-05). Its shape is Obsidian's fifth key; its meaning is Chrome's and Safari's tab count; what it opens is neither — a card over the place. If the owner wants the glyph replaced, `LivGlyph.day(n)` is one drawing.

5. "TAB SWITCHER AS A CARD GRID (copied verbatim)". The grid survives — it is the owner's ruling — but its HOST changes: a card over the place at half height, not a full-screen mode. Obsidian's grid is a screen; this is a sheet in the record card's family.

6. "ONE DESK OF DOCUMENT TABS PER WORKSPACE (Obsidian's tabs-per-vault)". Kept in storage. But Obsidian's tab is where you STAND and everything else is a drawer you visit; here a document is a LAYER over a place and the places are where you stand. That inversion is the structural difference the repo diagnosed (kinds-are-peers.md:114-124). Nothing is somewhere you are except NOW and LIBRARY; a note and a task both open over them, one full-height, one half.

7. "CHROME THAT HIDES ON SCROLL" — kept; Safari's and Mail's as much as Obsidian's, and asked for from a clip.

8. "LANDING — a recents list AND a tab grid as two answers to 'get me back'". Resolved by naming them: the Notes lens is what you own (rows marked "Open"); the Deck is what you are working on, and it no longer hides the place. Two lists remain, visibly not the same question.

9. "MONOCHROME GLYPHS / NEUTRAL GREYS / INLINE TITLE / FIND-OR-CREATE / TAP-FOLLOWS-LINK" — untouched; surface and editor idioms, and direction A (2026-09-06) already put Liv's identity in the furniture below them.

10. "THE RECORD CARD — Liv's one genuinely non-Obsidian container, under-used as an identity." Promoted to the grammar: EVERYTHING opens over a place — record half, deck half-to-tall, document full. Obsidian has no layer that is not a file in a pane.

THE NEAREST LIVING RELATIVE is Apple Music: "Listen Now | Library" with a segment under the Library title.
