The judges are right that the skeleton rhymes, and the mockup has to show what separates them on the same artboard as the pill, so here it is: (a) Music's two words are two of five tabs on a tab bar; Liv's are one pill in a bar of actions, and there is no third tab to add — a third place is the direction's failure mode, named; (b) Music's Library is a list of categories that push screens; Liv's is one list under four lenses, and a lens change is a position move under a fixed head, not a push; (c) in Music nothing rises over a tab but the player; in Liv everything you open rises over a place — a note as a full layer with the place named on its bar, a task as a half card, the deck as a card — and that layer grammar is drawn on screen 3; (d) Music's Listen Now is a feed; NOW is a day with a pile on it that you empty, and a + that makes a task dated to it. What remains shared is a two-word toggle at the bottom, and the direction's own risk stands: if NOW reads as a dashboard and Library as "the real app", it has degraded into a home tab plus the old views in a pill row — Notion mobile's shape. The test is whether a user can say what NOW is for without the word "home".

What Obsidian has that this does not: a drawer, a file tree, a daily-note key, a right drawer, a workspace that restores the last document at launch, "No file is open". What this has that Obsidian does not: a first screen that is a day with a pile on it, a lensed library named as such, and the rule that a document never owns the screen's identity.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- WORSE: Calendar is demoted from a named root to a lens. Measured: its timeline starts ~68pt lower than today (a 44pt LivSegment lens row plus a ~24pt workspace line under the same hero band), and browsing a month is Library → Calendar instead of a panel row. Everything, Notes and Tasks pay the same 68pt. Mitigation: the day view (7-day strip + timeline) is on NOW at launch; the Library remembers the Calendar lens so a calendar-first user is one pill tap from the month with the lens lit. Not mitigated: someone who wants the month as their home has none, and the owner's 2026-08-16 test may fail on sight.
- WORSE: NOW is long, and the pile is at the top of every launch. Drawn at 25 unrouted (the number that sank Quick Capture): CAUGHT shows its three newest rows and a "22 more ›" row; with SUGGESTED and LATE folded, the timeline's first row lands at ~550pt of 844 and about four rows of the day are visible above the bar. The pile cannot be hidden, by design — the product test says it must not build up — but it will grate before it motivates, and the owner may want the fold closed by default.
- WORSE: a note from the first screen costs a hold. + on NOW keeps the standing rule and makes a TASK dated to the shown day (2026-08-28); a note is hold-+ → Note or Search → Create. The catch reflex (what-liv-is-for's first test) is served from outside the app by the share sheet and liv://capture, both of which land at the top of CAUGHT — but a one-tap note from the launch screen does not exist. Flipping it is one line in `Place.makes`, and it needs the owner's word.
- WORSE: no place-swipe at all. The pill is the only door between the places, so the whole app has one drag fewer than today (the panel drag is gone and nothing replaces it). A user who expects to swipe between two side-by-side places will find nothing, and the lateral slide (LivMotion.nav) will promise a gesture the app does not have.
- WORSE: the Deck at .medium shows about four cards where the full-screen grid showed six to eight; the fifth is a drag to .large away. The card keeps the owner's grid but not its size, and a card over a washed place is a different picture from "tabs as they appeared before". If he wants the full screen back, the card is pinned at .large — the model does not change.
- WORSE: forward is deleted, against 2026-08-23's "a browser's set, literally". It was dim almost always, but it existed. If the owner wants › back, the pill shrinks to one slot naming only the OTHER place — measured to fit (48pt in 58.8) but it then reads as a key to somewhere rather than a statement of where you are, and the hero has to carry "where am I" alone.
- WORSE: saved filters move from a panel row to a row in the workspace card. Tap count is unchanged — the panel was door + row, this is line + row — and the active filter's NAME is now printed on the Library's second line, which the panel never showed on the desk; but a filter is behind a card instead of in a list you can see while you scroll, and Trash and Settings go with it (card + row, the same count as panel + row).
- WORSE: a document layer over NOW hides the day; over the Library it hides the list. Already true today. What is new is that the place underneath is remembered and the pill names it, so you know what ‹ will show. Nothing on the document itself says which place is under it except that pill.
- WORSE: every lens change pushes onto ‹ (one policy: any change of the screen's name pushes). So ‹ after Notes → Tasks → Calendar walks back through the lenses before it reaches NOW — a browser's behaviour, and honest, but a user who expected ‹ to mean "out of the Library" gets three taps.
- WORSE: the harness. `tour`, `goto`, `panel`, `bar`, `grid` and `desk` are rewritten (`panel` → `place`, `grid` → `deck`); the other seventeen of drive.sh's 23 subcommands keep their assertions with new gotos. `liv://today|inbox|notes|tasks|calendar|everything` and `-desk.boot` remap so old links keep working, but the harness is a day before a single green means anything, and revs 54–61 are unverified on a device underneath all of it.
- WORSE, structurally: two places is a bet that the global/lensed distinction is the one a user needs to feel. If he does not feel it, the model degrades into a home tab plus the old six views in a pill row — Notion mobile's shape, the named enemy — and its nearest relative, Apple Music's Listen Now | Library, is what the panel will see first.
- BETTER: no top chrome on a place, one pill for "where am I / go there" at the thumb, one ‹ with one meaning that can never be lit over nothing, one word for Notes, no gesture that collides with a text view or a pager, the panel's whole selection-fill problem deleted rather than fixed, and the count on the bar equal to the cards in the card it raises.
- BETTER: the catch loop is visible for the first time — a share from Safari, a liv://capture, a hold-+ note or a Search-created note all land as the top row of CAUGHT on the launch screen with the clerk's "Work?" chip beside it; one tap files it. Today that note vanishes into the Notes state and reappears only if you go to Inbox. Today's "N captured today · Route them" footer is deleted because the thing it pointed at is on the same screen.

**Wireframes.**

*NOW — cold launch with 25 unrouted captures (the hard case)*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                    ▪▪▪  │
│                              │
│ Wednesday 10 Sep             │
│ Work 2 · Home 1 · 25 unfiled │
│  M   T  [W]  T   F   S   S   │
│  8   9  10  11  12  13  14   │
│                              │
│ CAUGHT 25        Route all › │
│ ○ Call the plumber     Home? │
│ ○ arxiv.org/abs/2409…  Work? │
│ ○ Milk, eggs, bread          │
│   22 more                  › │
│ SUGGESTED 2                ▾ │
│ LATE 2                     ▾ │
│ ○ 09:00 Standup notes   Work │
│ ○ 11:30 Dentist       Health │
│ ○       Renew passport       │
│ DONE 4                     ▾ │
│                              │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │[Now│Library] ‹   ⌕  +  [3]│ │
│ │              Back Srch Task Open
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
No top doors, no scrim band: the date is the first thing under the clock. The area line and 7-day strip are Today's verbatim (the unfiled count now lives here, where the panel foot had it). CAUGHT at 25: the three newest rows, then a "22 more ›" row that unfolds in place (NowPosition.caughtExpanded); the header's count and "Route all ›" are the whole pile's. Row tap → the route card (six areas, then "Not a note…"); the clerk's guess rides the row as "Home?" and one tap files it; swipe left trashes. SUGGESTED is Inbox·Tidy folded. LATE, the timeline and DONE are Today's. Measured: with these folds the timeline's first row is at ~550pt of 844 and four rows of the day sit above the bar at 778. NOW never wears the workspace lens, so no lens word appears. The bar: the Now│Library pill (current word full ink, other one tier down; tap the other word to go, the current one to scroll to top), ‹ Back (dim here: nothing beneath), Search, + (makes a TASK dated to the shown day — the 2026-08-28 rule; hold for Note/Task/Event/File/Scan), Open [3] (raises the Deck card). Marker liv.surface.now.

*LIBRARY — Calendar lens, lensed by Work · Urgent (the measured demotion)*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                    ▪▪▪  │
│                              │
│ Thu 11 Sep ▾    ‹  ›  Today  │
│ Work · Urgent ▾              │
│ Everything Notes Tasks [Cal] │
│                              │
│  9 ┃ Standup            Work │
│ 10 │                         │
│ 11 ┃ Dentist          Health │
│ 12 │                         │
│ 13 │                         │
│ 14 ┃ Review draft       Work │
│ 15 │                         │
│ 16 │                         │
│ 17 │                         │
│ 18 │                         │
│                              │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │[Now│Library] ‹   ⌕  +  [3]│ │
│ │              Back Srch Event Open
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
The hero band is Calendar's own header, unchanged (Calendar.swift:217-260: day title at LivType.hero with the chevron into the month card, the Today pill, ‹ › stepping a day). Under it, two things the Calendar does not have today and every lens pays for: the workspace line ("Work · Urgent ▾", caption, ink tier down; tap → the workspace card from the top edge: workspaces, saved filters, New filter, Trash, and the foot with the unfiled count and the Settings gear) and the lens row (LivSegment, 44pt; the lit pill is restored on arrival — a position survives). Together ~68pt: the first hour is at ~190pt from the top against ~120 today. On Everything the hero band says "Everything" and its All/Upcoming/Unfiled pills follow the lens row; Tasks its chips; Notes nothing. The + makes what the lens holds (Event here, on the shown day). A block tap raises the record card; a tap on empty grid creates. There is no place-swipe: the timeline's pager owns the horizontal axis and nothing fights it. Marker liv.surface.library; the lens is asserted from the lit pill's text.

*DOCUMENT layer over the Library, with the Deck card raised at .medium*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                 (⚌) (•••)│
│                              │
│ Kitchen rebuild              │
│                              │
│ Budget is 14k. Ask Sam about │
│ the [[Plumber quote]] before │
│ Friday. Tiles: matte, not    │
│ gloss.                       │
│░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░│
│╭───────────── ▬ ─────────────╮
││ 3 open           Inactive 2 ›│
││ ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  │
││ │Plumber   │  │Trip plan │  │
││ │quote     │  │          │  │
││ │NOTE     ✕│  │FILE     ✕│  │
││ └──────────┘  └──────────┘  │
││ ┏━━━━━━━━━━┓  ┌ ─ ─ ─ ─ ─┐  │
││ ┃Kitchen   ┃    New note    │
││ ┃rebuild   ┃  │          │  │
││ ┃NOTE     ✕┃  └ ─ ─ ─ ─ ─┘  │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │[Now│Library] ‹   ⌕  +  [3]│ │
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
Above the wash: a document layer — the title is the note's first line (its own UITextView) and scrolls with the body; top-right the Properties key and •••; NO top-left door. The bar is the layer's own foot: the pill reads Library (the place beneath — tap it and the layer drops onto the Library with the note still open); ‹ drops the layer or steps back along a [[link]] chain; Open [3] raised what you see below. Below the wash: the DECK — the existing two-column grid, anchored to the bottom (newest at the thumb, `defaultScrollAnchor(.bottom)` survives), the active card outlined, ✕ on each, the dashed New-note card bottom-right, "3 open" and "Inactive 2 ›" on its head row (the shelf swaps in with its Close all) — in the app's own card at .medium, drag up to .large. Tap the wash or pull the grabber down to drop it. Card tap → that document replaces this layer (pushing this one onto ‹). This is the layer grammar on one artboard: a full layer (the note), a half card (the deck; the record card is its sibling), a place beneath both, named on the pill. Markers liv.surface.document and liv.overlay.deck.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- THE BAR (persistent on every place and every document layer; the surface's own foot; one glass capsule, LivBar.height 66, sideInset 42; retires under the keyboard, under a LivMenu and on scroll; never for an empty Deck): [Now│Library] double-width place pill (two slots, 117.6pt on a 390 screen; current word full ink, other one ink tier down) · ‹ Back (dim when the stack is empty) · Search · + with the word of what it makes here (Task on NOW; Note/Task/Event by lens in the Library) · Open [n] (LivGlyph.day(n), retained on purpose; raises the Deck card). Five elements, six slots' width, every one with a word.
- TOP CHROME ON A PLACE: none. `LivTopScrim` only (the words run under the clock). No door, no gear, no floating key.
- TOP CHROME ON A DOCUMENT LAYER: top-right Properties key (slider) and ••• Note actions, in 44pt glass circles, retiring with the bar on scroll; top-left nothing. The band under the clock belongs to these two keys and goes when they go.
- THE LIBRARY'S HEAD (scrolls with the surface): hero band 44pt — the lens's name, or Calendar's own header (day title ▾ · ‹ › · Today); the workspace line ("Work · Urgent ▾", caption, ink tier down); the lens row (LivSegment, 44pt, four pills). Then the lens's own row if it has one (Everything's slices, Tasks' chips, Calendar's timeline directly).
- THE DECK (summoned, from Open [n]): the existing two-column TabCard grid, bottom-anchored, New-note card bottom-right, "n open" and "Inactive n ›" on its head row, in the app's own card at .medium / .large with a grabber, the surface in front washed 50% above it; the bar stays under it.
- RECORD CARD (summoned): the half-height .medium/.large sheet with grabber, over any surface; MINIMISED PILL above the bar, one at a time.
- WORKSPACE CARD (from the Library's workspace line, from the top edge): workspaces · saved filters · New filter · Trash · foot (workspace name, unfiled count, Settings gear). PROPERTIES CARD, HISTORY CARD (from the document's keys), SETTINGS (from the card's gear), SEARCH COVER (from the bar), CAMERA COVER, CREATE MENU (hold +), NOTE-ACTIONS MENU (•••), ROUTE CARD (a CAUGHT row), TRASH-CONFIRM MENU — all `LivMenu`/`livSheet`/system sheets exactly as today.
- GONE: the library panel and its door (PanelMark), the desk push/wash/rounded-corner card under a panel, the panel drag recognizer (PanelDrag.swift entirely — no gesture replaces it), the panel foot as a foot (re-hosted as the workspace card's foot), per-view ViewCounts (only the unfiled count survives, on NOW's area line and the card's foot), the › Forward key, the full-screen switcher host, Today's "Route them" footer, the Inbox hero and its Route|Tidy segment, the word "Library" as a heading (it is on the pill only).

**Gestures.**
- Tap the other word of the Now│Library pill → switch place (LivMotion.nav, lateral; drops a document layer if one is up, keeping it on the Deck). Tap the current word → scroll the place to its top. This is the only door between the places — there is no place-swipe.
- Tap a row / block / search hit / [[link]] / notification → `desk.open(id)`: a document rises as a full layer over the place (pushes `here`); a record rises as the half card (pushes nothing, closes nothing).
- ‹ on the bar → the previous SURFACE: the place or document beneath, or the previous lens of the Library. The document you leave stays on the Deck. Dim when the stack is empty (cold launch on NOW).
- Tap Open [n] → the Deck card rises to .medium over the surface in front (a place or a document); drag it to .large; tap the wash or pull the grabber down → it drops. Card tap → that document as a layer; ✕ → close; New note card → a new note layer with the caret in it.
- Tap + → makes what the surface holds in one tap: a TASK dated to the shown day on NOW (the 2026-08-28 rule, kept) and in Tasks; an event on the shown Calendar day; a note in Everything and Notes (opens as a layer with the caret in it). Hold 0.45s → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text). Unchanged.
- Swipe a record card down → minimise to the pill above the bar; tap the pill → restore; ✕ → drop. One pill. Unchanged.
- Tap the Library's workspace line ("Work · Urgent ▾") → the workspace card from the top edge (a card comes from the edge its button is on): workspaces, saved filters, New filter, Trash, and the foot with the unfiled count and the Settings gear.
- Tap a lens pill → that lens, restoring its spot, and pushes the lens you left onto ‹. Tap the lit pill → nothing (a lens has no root; its position row is its state).
- Tap a CAUGHT row on NOW → the route card (six areas, then Not a note… → Task / Event / Link); tap its "Work?" chip → filed in one tap; swipe left → trash; tap "22 more ›" → the section unfolds in place and the position remembers it.
- Scroll down 44pt on any place, layer or list → the bar retires (and a document's top keys); scroll up or reach the top → home. With the keyboard up the bar retires (2026-08-02) and there is no navigation until it goes — unchanged, and named as a limit below.
- Nothing horizontal is a navigation gesture anywhere in the app: the 7-day strip, the all-day band and the Calendar pager scroll their own content and nothing else listens on that axis.

**Capture.** CAPTURE lives in exactly the places it lives today, and every catch lands in one visible spot: the top of CAUGHT on NOW, which is the launch surface.

1. + TAP on NOW: a TASK dated to the shown day, in the half card with the caret in the name (Today's behaviour, the 2026-08-28 rule; `Place.now.makes = .task`). Not a note. The first draft flipped this to a note and the judges were right that it reverses half a standing ruling; it is one line and it is his line.

2. A NOTE from NOW: hold + → Note, or Search → the Create row. Either way `box.createNote` → `workspaces.stamp` → `requestFocus` → `desk.open(id)` → a document layer slides over NOW with the caret in an empty editor. ‹ drops the layer: NOW is back and the note is the top row of CAUGHT with the time as its fact and, once the clerk has looked, a "Work?" chip. One tap on the chip files it; one tap on the row asks WHERE. Today's "N captured today · Route them" footer is deleted because the thing it pointed at is on the same screen.

3. + TAP in the Library: the lens's kind — a note in Everything and Notes (as in 2, but the layer is over the Library and ‹ returns you to the list), a task in Tasks (card, caret in the name, due today 09:00), an event in Calendar (card, on the shown day).

4. + HOLD anywhere: the New menu, unchanged.

5. SHARE SHEET: unchanged mechanics (UIKit extension → App Group spool → drained at foreground through `liv_capture_at`, stamped, NOT opened). Where it lands: NOW's CAUGHT, top row, on the next foreground — and NOW is the launch surface, so a share made in Safari is the first thing on screen when Liv opens. This is the strongest single argument for merging Inbox into the first screen: the share loop closes without a navigation.

6. liv://capture?text= → `catchText` → saved and (unchanged) opened as a document layer over whatever place is in front; a bare liv://capture → a new note layer. liv://capture/photo → the camera cover; Done opens the last shot as a layer.

7. SEARCH find-or-create → `liv_capture_at` → opened as a layer over the place you searched from; the cover closes.

8. Calendar empty-grid tap, Duplicate note, editor [[ → unchanged.

The instant after every capture: either a layer or a card is on screen with the caret in it (you asked to write), or nothing changed and the thing is at the top of NOW's pile (you asked from outside). No form, no grammar, no question.

**Back, links and catches.** BACK. One stack, one policy: EVERY change of the screen's name pushes `here`, and only a workspace switch clears. `LivPlace` becomes `.now`, `.library(Lens)` and `.document(UInt64)` (`.state(Feature)` goes). `here` = `.document(shown)` if a layer is up, else `.library(lens)` or `.now`. A lens change pushes like everything else — the first draft exempted it, and two kinds of "move" with two back behaviours is the mode error the diagnosis was written against. So ‹ means one thing: the previous SURFACE. From a document opened off a list → that lens, list intact. From B opened by a link in A → A. From B picked in the Deck while reading A → A. From Library·Calendar having come from Library·Notes → Notes; from Library having come from NOW → NOW. From NOW on cold launch → dim. There is no case where ‹ is lit and nothing visible changes, because two `LivPlace` values never describe the same pixels — which is exactly what `.state(.notes)` broke. › is deleted; `LivReturns` keeps `push`, `stepBack`, `cap` (20) and `clear`; `stepForward`, `forwards`, `next`, `forwardCount` and `goForward` go.

The Now│Library pill is not back: it always names the place beneath and always lands there (dropping a layer if one is up, keeping it on the Deck; it pushes the layer onto ‹ like any surface change).

liv:// ROUTES (Routes.swift remapped, no host dropped): liv://today and liv://inbox → stand in NOW (inbox unfolds CAUGHT); liv://notes|tasks|calendar|everything → stand in the Library on that lens; liv://entity/<id> → `desk.open(id)` over whichever place is in front (a document layer, or a record card); liv://capture… as in the capture flow. A cold launch parks the route as now.

NOTIFICATION tap → `desk.open(id)` over the front place; a tapped reminder for the document already shown short-circuits as now.

SHARE-SHEET CATCH → the spool → NOW's CAUGHT; it never navigates.

WORKSPACES AND FILTERS: the lens is a property of the LIBRARY only, and the Library's second line says it. `workspaces.setActive` → `desk.adopt(workspace:)` swaps the Deck and the spots (per-workspace keys unchanged), clears the stack, closes covers, and lands in the LIBRARY on its remembered lens — never on Notes, never on NOW (NOW is the same in every workspace, so landing there would show no change). Saved filters live in the workspace card under the workspaces, one toggled at a time, "New filter" after them, Trash after that, and the foot (workspace name, unfiled count, Settings gear) — Panel.swift's rows minus the six views. The active filter is the second word of the Library's lens line. NOW ignores both, as Today and Inbox do today; Search is lensed as today and says so with its LensChip.

A RECORD opens the way it does today: `desk.open` on a record shape raises the half card over the front surface — a place, a document layer, or the Search cover — closes nothing, edits write as made, swipe-down minimises to the one pill. A record is never on the Deck; the first snapshot's sweep stays.

**Survives in code.** Plane.swift `DeskPlanes` (481 lines): the one app-wide `desk` of `DeskTab`s and the per-workspace persistence keys survive verbatim; `spots` is re-keyed by `Lens` + one `now` entry with a fold that reads the old `Feature` keys and merges the Today + Inbox tokens into `NowPosition` — folded, never deleted, on the precedent of the v2 fold at Plane.swift:304-360. `live`, `inactive`, `open(entity:)`, `close`, `closeInactive`, `setActive`, `dropRecordsAndStrangers` unchanged.; Chrome.swift `DeskModel` (1090 lines): `open(_:as:)`, `openAsCard`, `openDocument` (minus `state = .notes` at :853 and the `returns.push` policy unchanged), `focus`, `surfaceCleanup`, `recordCard`/`minimiseRecord`/`restoreRecord`/`dropMinimised`, `adopt(workspace:)` (landing changed from `.notes` at :683 to `.library`), `createHere`/`createSomething`/`createNote`/`createRecord`/`adoptCapture`, `requestFocus`, `contextDay`, `cards`, `chromeAway`/`chromeHomeAgain`, `endEditing`, `LivTopScrim`. `LivReturns` (Navigate.swift:97-146) keeps `push`/`stepBack`/`cap`/`clear`.; Positions.swift: `EverythingLens`, `TasksPosition`, `CalendarPosition` verbatim; `TodayPosition` (:111) + `InboxLens` (:37) merge into `NowPosition`.; Record.swift (RecordCard, RecordBody, MinimisedRecordPill), Detail.swift, History.swift, Links.swift, Search.swift (cover and picker mode, LensChip, facets, find-or-create), Camera.swift/Scan.swift, Trash.swift, Settings.swift, Menu.swift (LivMenu, livSheet(from:), LivEdgeSheetHost — the Deck card is one more client), Rows.swift, Kit.swift (LivSegment is the lens row), Glyph.swift (minus PanelMark), Theme.swift (every token; new: `LivPlacePill` metrics, `LivDeck` detents; deleted: `LivPanel`).; WorkspaceSwitch.swift (346 lines): the card gains the saved-filter rows, New filter, Trash and the foot from Panel.swift:235-292 and is presented from the Library's workspace line from the top edge; `ViewCounts.foot` (Panel.swift:457) survives as the card's foot line.; Today.swift's body (hero at :220, areaLine at :245, day strip, LATE/timeline/DONE) and Inbox.swift's Route list, Tidy list, route card and kind card — re-composed into Now.swift as sections with a three-row fold on CAUGHT; Everything.swift, Notes.swift (NotesList, :31), Tasks.swift, Calendar.swift (its header at :217-260 becomes the Calendar lens's hero band), Month.swift bodies unchanged and mounted under Library.swift's head.; Tabs.swift (712 lines): `TabSwitcher`'s grid, `TabCard`, `InactiveTabs`, `LivTabs` (the 21-day predicate), `defaultScrollAnchor(.bottom)` (:118), the New-note card bottom-right, "Nothing open" — all survive; only the host changes from `fullScreenCover` (App.swift:267) to a livSheet-style card with .medium/.large detents, and the footer's Done goes (the wash and the grabber are the way down).; Bar.swift (160 lines): the capsule, `key(...)`, `slot(...)`, the glass, the word-under-glyph form, `tabKey` → Open; two slots replaced by the place pill; the a11y label "n documents open" now matches what the key shows.; Desk.swift (1078 lines): `DeskHost` structure (surface + LivTopScrim + surfaceFoot overlay + ignoresSafeArea), `EntityTabBody` (:967), the top-right keys, `noteMenu`/`noteVerbs`/`trashNote`/undo chip, `createMenu`/`createHere`/`createNote`. The ZStack at :48-71 picks by `desk.shown` then `desk.place` instead of `openDoc`/`state`; `PanelMark` at :223 goes.; Surface.swift markers: liv.surface.document, liv.overlay.properties/history/settings/trash/workspace unchanged; liv.surface.now, liv.surface.library, liv.overlay.deck added; liv.surface.notes and liv.overlay.library/tabs retired. drive.sh: 17 of 23 subcommands (boot, check, surface, tap, chrome, areas, routes, rows, create, lens, facets, vault, cycles, quiet, workspace, history, spool) keep their assertions with new gotos; `tour` becomes now → library×4 lenses → document; `panel` → `place`, `grid` → `deck`, `desk` and `bar` rewritten; each new assertion broken on purpose before its green is trusted.; Routes.swift (the `.view(Feature)` case remaps to `.now` / `.library(Lens)`), Notify.swift, Catch.swift/Spool, ShareExtension, Box.swift (no new liv_* verb: the model is a shell rendering change and touches neither core nor FFI), Workspace.swift (lens, stamp, filters, activeFilterId), Outbox.swift, Files.swift, the whole editor (Editor/EditorText/EditorStyle/EditorCost) untouched — no gesture is added to the text view.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift (461 lines: LibraryPanel, the six view rows, the fade mask, the pinned foot as a foot — its filter/Trash rows and `ViewCounts.foot` are re-hosted, the per-view counts are not), PanelDrag.swift (280 lines, entirely — the latch, the pagerZone veto, deskInFront, the touch cancellation; no gesture replaces it), PanelMark (Glyph.swift:590) and the library door, `setLibrary`/`libraryShown`/`libraryDrawn`/`panelProgress`/`deskShift`, the desk-as-a-card mask, wash and square-corner logic under a panel, `LivPanel` in Theme.swift, `DeskModel.pagerZone` (:362 — nothing vetoes anything now).; `Feature` as six roots (Navigate.swift:23-60): `inOrder`, `title`, `makes` per feature, `FeatureBody` (:181); replaced by `Place` (2) and `Lens` (4). `Feature.today`, `.inbox`, `.notes` as states cease to exist.; `DeskModel.state` (Chrome.swift:149), the `state == .notes` guard on `openDoc` (:184), `go(_:)` (:698), `goToRoot(_:)` (:731), `showList()` (:746), `here`'s `.state` case (:761), `land(.state)` (:786-791); `LivPlace.state(Feature)` (Navigate.swift:87).; `LivReturns.forwards`, `next`, `forwardCount`, `stepForward` (Navigate.swift:108-135); `DeskModel.forward`/`goForward` (Chrome.swift:766-778); the › key (Bar.swift:54).; `TabSwitcher` as a `fullScreenCover` (App.swift:267) and `switcherShown` as a cover flag (it becomes the Deck card's presentation state); the grid's `+ | N open | Done` footer (the wash is the way down); the Inactive shelf's slide-from-trailing host (it becomes a swap inside the card).; Inbox.swift as a surface: the hero, the Route|Tidy lens row, the "this list ignores the lens" prose line, `InboxLens` as a spot; Today.swift's "N captured today · Route them" footer (:358-361) and its `desk.go(.inbox)`.; The heroes of Everything, Notes and Tasks as their own surfaces' headings (the Library's hero band prints the lens's name once); the rule that adopt(workspace:) lands on Notes; the `-desk.boot library|grid` flags (App.swift:477; replaced by `place` and `deck`); the drive.sh checks `panel` (with `check_library`, `panel_open`, `panel_closed`) and `grid` as written.; DeskTabContent.position (already legacy, Plane.swift:70) and the v2 per-view plane fold can go in the same change if the owner accepts a one-way migration of pre-2026-08-28 desks; otherwise they stay one more rev. The new `spots` fold follows the same rule: old keys are read, never deleted.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- RESOLVED — 'Notes is two things wearing one name': the desk gets its own state (`shown`); a document is a layer over a place, drawn by nothing called Notes; Notes is a lens and only a lens; `goToRoot`'s special case is deleted rather than justified.
- RESOLVED — 'The list from the panel, the documents from the bar — two doors on two edges': the list is a lens in the Library (what you own; rows on the Deck say "Open"); the Deck is the grid in a card from the bar (what you are working on) that keeps the place visible. Two lists remain, named, shaped and placed as two different questions.
- RESOLVED — 'Open N counts documents but not the place you stand in': places are not open (there are two and they always exist); the count equals the cards in the card the key raises; the a11y label and the word agree.
- PARTLY RESOLVED — 'Notes means three surfaces, and the + word changes': Notes means one thing. The word under + still changes (Task on NOW; Note/Task/Event by lens), co-located with a lit pill or a hero that explains it, but it still changes.
- RESOLVED — 'The panel row and the Open key lead to a note by routes with different side effects': one push policy (every change of the screen's name pushes; only adopt clears), so ‹ after any route lands on the surface you left, and no two `LivPlace` values describe one screen.
- RESOLVED — '‹ is a document-history key wearing a browser's glyph': ‹ is the previous surface of any kind (place, lens or document); › is deleted rather than kept dim.
- RESOLVED (by deletion) — 'The root-of-view rule is a patch': there is no 'tap the view you are in'; a place pill re-tap scrolls to top, a lens pill re-tap is a no-op, and a document is never 'in' a view to be deselected from.
- PARTLY RESOLVED — 'Positions and documents are two remembered things the user never sees as different': a lens visibly resumes (its pill lit, its position row where you left it) and a document visibly persists (in the Deck, marked Open in the list). Nothing on screen says WHY one ages after 21 days and the other never does.
- NOT RESOLVED — '+ stays where you stand (a card) or moves you (a layer) depending on kind': a note still opens a full layer and a record a half card. Mitigated by the layer being over the place (one ‹ back) and by NOW showing a catch at the top of CAUGHT on return; the asymmetry of container by kind is a ruling (2026-08-07/08) this direction keeps.
- RESOLVED — 'The switcher is a full-screen mode and the only door back to a note': the Deck is a card over the place, the place stays visible, and the place pill names what is beneath a document. It is still the only door to a note not in the list you are on — two taps, as today — and there is deliberately no swipe.
- RESOLVED (by deletion) — 'The panel's fill answers which state while the screen answers which surface': no panel; the place pill and the surface's own hero say where you are, and a document layer says nothing false about the place beneath it.
- RESOLVED — 'Inbox is a queue in the product but a view in the panel; Notes a shelf but a view': the queue is a section of NOW (global, first screen); the lists are lenses of the Library (lensed); the difference is the two places, drawn as the two words on the bar.
- ANSWERED for the redesign's own list: the desk has its own state (`shown`); what the panel says when a document is on screen (no panel; the pill says the place beneath); Notes is a lens and the container is the Deck; the list and the grid are two surfaces with two jobs and the grid's full-screen host dies; the number counts the cards of the card it opens; ‹ undoes any change of the screen's name; one push policy; the root rule is gone; a lens resumes visibly, a document persists visibly; the Open key is a layer not a mode; + says the kind (the consequence still differs by kind — not answered); the six rows become two places and four lenses and the panel that made them peers is gone; a note opened from anywhere belongs on the Deck and under ‹; the shape is not four floors (two words, not four view keys), not three arrangements (four lenses over one head in one place), not views-as-tabs (nothing is a pane), not grid-as-root (the Notes lens lists everything); the where-am-I control is at the thumb; after a workspace switch the app lands on the Library because the lens is the Library's; both doors survive with distinct jobs; mockup-first — this document is the mockup, and `drive.sh tour` rewritten to now → library×4 → document is the proof.

**Changed after the judges.**
- DELETED the title-band swipe that walked the Deck. The judge's collision is real in the tree: the title is its own `UITextView` (EditorText.swift:715) and a sideways drag there is selection or caret placement. No gesture replaces it; walking the Deck is Open → card, two taps, as today. The editor files are now untouched by the direction.
- DELETED the horizontal place-swipe and PanelDrag.swift with it. Three judges asked for pill-only or a per-lens veto list; NOW's 7-day strip and all-day band (Today.swift:510) and Calendar's pager already own the axis, so a veto list would be prose. The pill is the only door between the places; nothing horizontal navigates anywhere.
- THE DECK IS THE GRID IN A CARD, not a horizontal strip. The judges' cross-cutting note is right that five directions drew 'a strip of open documents at the sill' — the element the owner removed on 2026-08-23 — and that the grid-as-card fallback should be the drawing, not a footnote. The existing `TabSwitcher` grid, bottom-anchored with New note bottom-right, survives verbatim; only its host changes from `fullScreenCover` (App.swift:267) to a card at .medium/.large. This keeps the owner's word on the shape and changes only what it hides.
- MEASURED the Calendar demotion instead of guessing it: the Calendar lens keeps its own header as the hero band (the day title is already at LivType.hero, Calendar.swift:229), and the word 'Library' is printed only on the pill. What the lens pays is a 44pt LivSegment row (Kit.swift:194) plus a ~24pt workspace line: ~68pt, not ~110. The Library's head is two bands and a line on every lens, never more; the gear left the hero row for the workspace card's foot.
- DREW NOW AT 25 unrouted, the number that sank Quick Capture: CAUGHT shows its three newest rows and a '22 more ›' row; with SUGGESTED and LATE folded the timeline's first row is at ~550pt of 844 and four rows of the day are visible above the bar. Screen 1 is now the hard case, not the easy one.
- REVERTED + on NOW to a TASK dated to the shown day (the 2026-08-28 rule). Two judges named the reversal; it is the owner's line to flip, so the default keeps the ruling and the note-alternative is an open question. The capture flow is rewritten around it: a note from NOW is hold-+ or Search, and the share sheet and liv://capture remain the one-screen loop.
- MADE A LENS CHANGE PUSH onto ‹ like every other change of the screen's name. `LivPlace` is now `.now | .library(Lens) | .document(id)`; one policy, no exception, and the open question about it is closed.
- RE-COUNTED the saved-filter cost: the panel was door + row (two taps) and the workspace card is line + row (two taps); the first draft called it a regression and the judges took it. The active filter's name is now printed on the Library's second line. The workspace card is Panel.swift's rows minus the six views — filters, New filter, Trash, and the foot with the unfiled count and the gear — so Trash and Settings keep the panel's tap count and the 2026-08-22 foot survives inside the card.
- NAMED THE NEAREST RELATIVE (Apple Music's Listen Now | Library) and drew what separates the two on the same artboard as the pill: one pill in a bar of actions vs a tab bar; one list under four lenses vs categories that push; everything rises over a place vs only the player; a day with a pile vs a feed. The Obsidian-shaped Open box is stated as retained on purpose because the owner ruled it by name.
- MEASURED THE BAR: capsule 306pt on a 390 screen (LivBar.sideInset 42), five slots of 58.8pt; the pill takes two (117.6pt) and 'Now │ Library' at caption 14 is ~100pt; the one-slot fallback ('Library', ~48pt) fits on width and is drawn in prose as the › fallback.
- COSTED THE HARNESS BY NAME: 17 of drive.sh's 23 subcommands keep their assertions; `tour`, `goto`, `panel`→`place`, `grid`→`deck`, `desk` and `bar` are rewritten; the `spots` migration folds the old Feature keys and never deletes them (Plane.swift:304-360 precedent); the first `build.sh` + `drive.sh` run on revs 54–61 precedes any of this.
- PUT THE REVERSALS TO THE OWNER AS FOUR QUESTIONS (six views; the grid's host; ›; the library as a place) with a one-line fallback each, instead of eight footnotes.

**Conceded.**
- Calendar is demoted from a named root to a lens, and its timeline starts ~68pt lower than today. The number is smaller than the first draft's but it is not zero, and the owner's 2026-08-16 'central part of the app' test may fail on sight regardless of the day view being on NOW at launch.
- Today and Inbox merge, which alters the six views the owner said to keep (2026-08-17) and removes Inbox as a named place. Nothing of Inbox's content or `liv://inbox` is lost, but the ruling is reversed and needs his word.
- The pile is on the launch screen every time. At 25 unrouted it takes the top ~490pt and the day is four rows above the bar. This is the product test made visible, and it will grate.
- Forward is deleted, against 'a browser's set, literally' (2026-08-23). The fallback is drawn and less legible.
- The full-screen grid becomes a half-height card. The grid is the owner's, the host is not, and 'as they appeared before' is not exactly met — the card shows about four cards where the screen showed six to eight.
- The numbered Open box keeps Obsidian's fifth-key shape and the capsule keeps Obsidian's proportions. Both were the owner's rulings by name; the direction retains them and says so rather than redrawing them unasked.
- Its nearest living relative is Apple Music's Listen Now | Library, and if the global/lensed distinction is not felt the model degrades into a home tab plus the old views in a pill row — Notion mobile's shape. The mockup argues the difference; only the owner's reaction can prove it.
- A note from the launch screen costs a hold or a search; the one-tap catch from NOW is not there because + keeps making a task. The share sheet and liv://capture carry the catch reflex instead.
- There is no swipe anywhere for navigation — not between places, not along the Deck. The app gains one fewer gesture than today and a lateral slide that no drag can drive.
- The asymmetry of container by kind (a note fills the screen, a task is a card) stands, and the + key still says the kind rather than the consequence — vagueness 9 is kept as a ruling, not resolved.
- Nothing on screen explains why a document ages onto the Inactive shelf after 21 days while a lens position never does; the two are made visible but not explained.
- Inside a document with the keyboard up there is no navigation at all, as today (2026-08-02); this direction does not design the keyboard-up state.
- The whole thing stacks on revs 54–61, unverified on a simulator, and the harness needs a day of rewriting before a green means anything. This is a mockup; nothing here is proven on a device.

**Open questions.**
- THE ONE QUESTION FOR THE OWNER FIRST: the Deck is the grid you ruled (2026-08-23), hosted as a card over the place instead of a full screen — do you want it half-height (four cards, drag to .large) or pinned at .large (the full grid with a washed band of the place above)? The model is the same either way; only a detent changes.
- Do the six views stay six (2026-08-17)? This direction merges Today and Inbox into NOW so the pile is on the launch screen. If the six must stay, the fallback that keeps two places is NOW = Today alone with CAUGHT as its first section and Inbox's Tidy behind SUGGESTED — which is the same screen with one fewer word.
- Should + on NOW make a task (kept, the 2026-08-28 rule) or a note (the catch reflex, what-liv-is-for's first test)? One line in `Place.makes`; needs his word.
- Is deleting › acceptable, given 2026-08-23 asked for the browser's set literally? If not, the pill shrinks to one slot naming only the other place — measured to fit, less honest about where you are.
- Does Calendar as a lens survive the 2026-08-16 test ('a central part of the app and not just additions'), now that the cost is a number (~68pt lower, two taps from NOW unless the Library was left on it)? If it fails, the fallback that keeps two places is a Calendar row on NOW under the 7-day strip that opens the month card — not a third place.
- Should CAUGHT's fold on NOW be open (three rows + '22 more') or closed by default at some count, so the day is never below the pile? The position remembers either; the product test argues for open.
- Does the Library's hero band as the LENS's name read as one place with four views, or as four screens with a segmented control — Apple Music's Library? The alternative that keeps the model is the word 'Library' as the hero with the lens name demoted to the pill only, which costs the Calendar its day title as hero.
- Saved filters as rows in the workspace card (equal tap count to the panel) versus a chip row under the lens row (one tap, but a fourth row of head on every lens — the density the owner named). The 2026-08-11 ruling is satisfied either way.
- Migration of `spots` from Feature keys to Lens + now keys — folded, never deleted — and whether the pre-2026-08-28 `.position` fold can finally go in the same change.
- The keyboard-up state: with the bar retired there is no navigation inside a note while typing, as today (2026-08-02). Is a Done/‹ on the keyboard toolbar (Bear's) wanted, or is that the cul-de-sac the owner accepts inside a document?

### 2. Band & Desk

*Sideways is what you are DOING (five tools on one band, walked by the bar's ‹ › which wear the names of the tools either side); up from the bar is what you have OPEN (the desk, a row of note cards standing on the shelf of every note); a note rises from below to fill the screen and drops back to exactly where it was lifted from — no drawer, no switcher screen, no history stack.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE MAP. The app is two rows and a floor that you hold in your hand. The TOP ROW is the band: Inbox · Today · Calendar · Tasks · Everything, one screen each, in a line with ends (no wrap). You launch on Today, one page in from the left end. The BOTTOM ROW is the desk: your open documents (notes and files), pulled UP from behind the bar as a row of cards over whatever tool you stand on, never instead of it. Under the desk is the floor — the SHELF: every note by recency, the Notes view, reached by pulling the desk all the way up. A card is lifted off the desk to fill the screen; dragging it down puts it back exactly where it was lifted from. That is the whole structure: left/right = which tool or which card; up = toward your notes (open ones first, all of them beneath); down = back out. Nothing else is a place. Records (tasks, events, people) float over whatever is on screen as cards, exactly as today; Search is the bar key and only the bar key; the workspace/filter lens is a pill at the top-left that opens a card from the top. There is no library panel, no tab switcher screen, no back stack.

THE STATE. `DeskModel.state` stops being a Feature and becomes a Stand: `band: Feature` (never `.notes`), `height: .closed | .fan | .shelf | .lifted`, and `liftedFrom: Height` — the one remembered value that makes 'down undoes the lift' deterministic (it is a single value, not a stack). `openDoc` = the active tab's entity iff `height == .lifted`. The desk (DeskPlanes: tabs + activeTabId, per workspace) and the tools' position tokens (spots) survive verbatim; a document is drawn by its own height, never by borrowing the Notes state. `openDocument` becomes `lift(entity)`: mint-or-reuse the tab, record `liftedFrom = height`, set height = .lifted. `go(feature)` becomes `page(to:)` on the band; `goToRoot`, `showList`, `LivReturns`, `LivPlace`, `here`, `land`, `goBack`, `goForward`, `switcherShown`, `libraryShown` are deleted.

THE BAR — HIS PIXELS, LIV'S MEANING. One glass capsule at the foot of every surface (its own foot, 2026-09-08 — kept), the five keys of 2026-08-23 in the browser's order and Todoist's form (2026-09-05), every key with a word: ‹ › ⌕ + [n]. What changes is only what ‹ › MEAN: they walk THE ROW YOU ARE IN, one step, and the word under each is the NAME OF WHERE IT GOES — on a tool 'Inbox' under ‹ and 'Calendar' under ›; with the desk up or a document lifted, the titles of the cards either side ('Q4 plan', 'Trip to…'). At a row's end the key dims (LivBar.disabledInk, kept) with no word: Inbox has no ‹, Everything no ›. No history is kept; ‹ never means 'undo'. The screen names where you are (every surface has a name since 2026-09-02; Today keeps its date; a document its title); the bar names where you can go. The numbered box is kept as the Open key and the only door to the cards ('tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box' — they appear, at half height, over where you stand); it gains one drawn state: FILLED while the desk is up, meaning tap lowers it. `+` makes what the row holds (Task / Event / Note; Note whenever the desk is up or a document is lifted); hold = the New menu, unchanged. The bar retires on scroll, under the keyboard, and never for an empty desk — all kept.

THE BAR IS THE ONLY HANDLE. The capsule is the one thing you drag: sideways pages the row you are in (continuous, latch at |dx| > 18 && dx > 2|dy|, flick or 50% commits — PanelPanRecognizer's own logic, retargeted from the window to the bar's rect); upward raises the desk (|dy| > 2|dx|); with the desk up, downward on the bar lowers it. Nothing on a tool's body pages the band: Today's day strip, Calendar's pager and month grid, Tasks' chip row and every row's swipe-to-trash keep their gestures untouched, and the pagerZone veto is deleted with the from-anywhere drag that needed it.

THE DESK. Tap Open, or drag the bar up: the app's own sheet rises BEHIND the bar to half height, no grabber (the grabber is the system card's mark; the desk's handle is the bar you just pulled). One horizontally scrolling row of cards (TabCard, unchanged: title, excerpt lines, kind footer) at the grid's card width — two full cards and a sliver, the active card scrolled to the leading edge with an accent ring; ‹ › step along them. Beneath the cards a row 'Notes · 134' with an up-chevron. Above the sheet the tool is washed 50% and takes only the tap that lowers the desk (the panel wash's rule, moved). Tap a card → it lifts to fill the screen. Swipe a card up → it leaves the desk (its ✕ stays as the tap door). Pull further, or tap 'Notes · 134' → the sheet rises to full: the SHELF — the same card row pinned as its first band, then a folded row 'Inactive · 4' (LivTabs.days, the Today-LATE fold idiom) whose unfold shows the stale cards and ends with a full-height 'Close all' row, then every note the lens admits by recency (NotesList's body, unchanged; open notes are not repeated). The Notes view, and its root is always the whole list. An empty desk skips the half stop: pulling up lands on the shelf, whose empty hint points at `+`.

THE LIFTED DOCUMENT. Full screen, the editor as today (title scrolling with the body, properties key and ••• top-right, nothing top-left). ‹ › wear the titles of the cards either side and step along the desk without leaving full screen. A [[link]] to a note inserts the target immediately to the RIGHT of the current card and lifts it, so ‹ reads the source's title — a chain of links is a row of adjacent cards and back is one tap (Safari's open-beside-parent rule; `DeskPlanes.open(entity:after:)`, one insertion, covered by a self-check). A link to a task or event raises a record card over the document. Drag down at the scroll top → the document drops to `liftedFrom`: to the tool if the desk was closed, to the fan if it was up, to the shelf if you came from the shelf. One outcome, no velocity, no threshold — down undoes the lift. It works with the keyboard up: the drag commits the text and lowers the card, as a system sheet does. Tap Open → the fan rises and the document shrinks onto it as the active card.

THE LENS. Top-left, a glass pill reading the active workspace or saved filter with a chevron.down: 'All ⌄', 'Work ⌄'. Tap → a card from the top (livSheet(from: .top), the edge rule): All, the workspaces, the saved filters, New filter, then Trash and Settings, with the old foot's line (workspace · N items · M unfiled, ViewCounts) at its head. This is WorkspaceSwitcher's body plus the library's remaining rows; the panel's six view rows are the band itself. Switching a workspace swaps the desk and spots (per workspace, unchanged), lowers everything to `.closed`, and stays on the tool you are on — it no longer lands you in Notes.

COLD LAUNCH. Today, height closed, desk as saved (the box reads n), band position restored from the workspace's stand key (default Today). The first snapshot's record-and-stranger sweep runs as today.

BUILD ORDER. Slice 0 is invisible and needs no mockup: give the desk its height (Stand), drop the `.notes` guard on `openDoc`, let DeskHost choose the document layer by height, delete goToRoot/showList — Chrome.swift + Desk.swift + the panel's row handler, provable with the existing `goto notes` and `desk` checks. It resolves vaguenesses 1, 5, 7 and 11 under the current chrome. Slice 1 (the visible change: bar meaning, desk sheet, shelf, lens pill, no panel) goes to the owner as the three screens below first.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS ARE CARDS ON A DESK YOU RAISE FROM BEHIND THE BAR. Each open note or file is one card (its durable identity the entity id, one card per entity — DeskPlanes unchanged). The desk is present on every tool in exactly one place: behind the bar, one tap (Open) or one pull away, at half height, over the tool you are on and never instead of it. The numbered box is still the only door and its count is the count of cards you will see when you tap — no longer a number describing an invisible container. Order on the desk is the order of opening, except that a document opened FROM a document is inserted right after it (Safari's rule for a link opened beside its parent), so link chains are adjacent and ‹ names the source. The bar's ‹ › step along the cards whenever the desk is the row you are in (up, or lifted), so with twelve open notes a card is at most a few taps away and never a mode; at twelve the honest answers are those taps, the shelf beneath (which lists the same notes by recency, so the ones you touched are its first rows) and Search. Stale cards (LivTabs.days) leave the fan and the count and sit in one folded 'Inactive · n' row on the shelf, under the pinned open ones: unfold to revive (tap = lift) or to 'Close all' as a full row — a destructive verb in the open, not a chip at the end of a swipe. There is no Inactive screen and no dimmed tail.

THE NOTE LIST IS THE FLOOR THE DESK STANDS ON. It is the same sheet at full height: the open cards pinned at the top as its first band, the Inactive fold, then every note the lens admits by recency (Notes.swift's body, its sort, its lens rule, its 'more rows than open tabs' guard — all kept; open notes appear once, in the pinned band). It is still the Notes view, still reached by name (liv://notes, 'Notes · 134' under the cards), still lists all notes; what changes is WHERE it lives — on the vertical axis under your open notes rather than as the second row of a menu of six. The two doors on two edges (panel → list, bar → grid) become one door on one edge: the switcher and the list are one surface at two heights, and the subset (open) is visibly pinned to the head of the whole (all). The 2026-08-24 mistake (grid as Notes' root, 126 notes hidden) cannot recur: the root of Notes is the full shelf and the cards are only its first band.

Tapping a row on the shelf lifts that note (a card is minted or reused, `liftedFrom = .shelf`); dragging it down returns you to the shelf. Tapping Open from the shelf lowers to the fan. `+` on the shelf or on the fan makes a note and lifts it.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** Two rows and a floor, no tree.

BAND (top row, equal peers, in a line): Inbox · Today · Calendar · Tasks · Everything. Each is a tool; each remembers one position token (spots — Everything's slice, Inbox's lens, Tasks' filter/groups, Today's day/piles, Calendar's month/day) and visibly resumes it when you page back, which is the on-screen expression 'a position survives' never had. Today and Inbox ignore the workspace lens; the other three wear it — unchanged. The band has ends: Inbox is the left end, Everything the right; ‹ dims on Inbox and › on Everything. `Feature.inOrder` is re-declared as the band's order with `.notes` removed (`Feature.band`); the owner's 'ordered like a day' ruling was written when Notes sat in the list, so the order is his call again. Paging is a tap on ‹ › (LivMotion.nav, the one lateral easing — a surface replacing a surface, as today) or a sideways drag on the bar (continuous; the neighbour in the drag direction is mounted at the latch and torn down if the drag fails; nothing else is kept mounted).

DESK (bottom row): open documents, per workspace, raised over any band position. Not a peer of the tools — the other axis. 'Notes should remain separate' is met literally: notes are never among the tools.

SHELF (the floor): the Notes view — every note by recency, the open ones pinned at its head, the stale ones folded between — under the desk.

FLOATS (no axis): a record card (task/event/person) over whatever is on screen, minimising to the pill above the bar — always raised by a tap (a row, a link, the pill), never by a pull, so the bottom edge's one pull belongs to the desk; the properties and history cards over a lifted document; the Search cover from the key; the lens card from the top-left pill; the New menu from the bottom; the camera. Every float exits by its own control or a swipe and leaves the stand untouched.

Where a document opened from anywhere BELONGS when you are done: on the desk, beneath the tool you were on. Drag it down and you are exactly where you lifted it from; the card stays on the desk. That single value (`liftedFrom`) replaces goToRoot, showList, land, and the pushed way-back.

With the keyboard up (most of a note's life): the bar is retired (2026-08-02, kept), so there is no lateral navigation — by design, the same as today; the one exit is the drag-down at the scroll top, which commits and lowers the card, and the top-right keys stay.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against the references reader's list, item by item:

1. The bottom capsule '‹ › 🔍 + [n] — a browser's, literally' (Bar.swift:10-15): the PIXELS are kept — they are the owner's ruling and his clip — but the two keys that made it a browser's are re-meant. Obsidian's ‹ › drive document history (and its › is drawn permanently dead); a browser's undo the last navigation. Here ‹ › walk the row you are in and wear the NAME of the place they go to, which changes with every page. No app in the reference set (Obsidian, Notesnook, ChatGPT, Todoist, Safari, Things, Arc) prints the neighbouring place-names under its back and forward keys, because none has a row to walk.
2. The numbered box (Obsidian's daily-note shape with a count, Glyph.swift:209): kept as the Open key — the owner chose it — but what it opens is a half-height desk over where you stand, not a full-screen grid, and the box fills while the desk is up. Obsidian's box opens today's note; a browser's opens a grid; this one raises a drawer.
3. The forward key (Navigate.swift:103): not deleted, not history — it is the next tool or the next card, and it is live whenever there is one.
4. Chrome retiring on scroll: KEPT — the owner's own clip and a reading rule, not a navigation shape. Conceded as a shared idiom.
5. The panel foot (workspace name, count line, gear — 'Replicate the style', tabs.md): gone with the panel. The workspace is a pill at the top-left; its card's head carries the old foot's line; Settings is a row in it. No pinned foot anywhere.
6. The panel's surface and metrics (ChatGPT frame-by-frame, Notesnook rows, Obsidian screenshot): there is no panel. The six-row drawer of views — what made Liv 'a drawer-of-doors app' copied from two drawer-of-doors apps — is deleted, and with it the top-left toggle the diagnosis calls the most Obsidian pixel on screen.
7. The tab switcher 'Obsidian's, verbatim in structure' (Tabs.swift:1-3) and the Inactive shelf (Chrome's): the switcher is a half-height row over the tool, never a full-screen mode; there is no Done, no grid, no second mode for stale cards — one folded row on the shelf.
8. One desk per workspace = tabs per vault: the STORAGE stays (it is right); the vault-switcher-in-the-foot slot is gone; a workspace is a lens pill, not a vault.
9. Top-left toggle + top-right ellipsis (Obsidian's header layout): the top-left is the lens pill on a tool and empty on a document. The ••• and properties keys stay — the document's own verbs, not navigation.
10. Edge-swipe-from-anywhere for the drawer: gone. The one drag handle is the bar, on two axes. A horizontal swipe changes WHAT YOU ARE DOING, not which drawer is open, and only the bar does it.
11–13. Inline title, properties chevron idiom, editor parity, tap-follows-link: untouched — owner-directed editor behaviour, outside the navigation area.
14. Find-or-create search: kept, one door (the key) — the pull-down of the first draft is withdrawn.
15–16. Monochrome glyphs, neutral palette: kept — the owner's surface rulings.
17. Back/forward over a history stack — 'the most Obsidian thing in the app' (kinds-are-peers.md:51): the stack is deleted (LivReturns goes); the keys become spatial.
18. 'Untitled' rejected: unchanged.
19. The structural resemblance the repo diagnosed — 'a note is somewhere you ARE, a task is somewhere you VISIT… one kind got the main surface and the rest got a drawer': inverted. Tools are where you stand; notes are what you raise. Obsidian's model is 'documents are where you stand; everything else is a drawer you visit' (refs §10). This is the opposite assignment of the two axes, which is why it cannot be mistaken for Obsidian even with the same editor and the same five keys inside.
20. Landing on Notes after a workspace switch: gone; you stay on the tool.
21. 'Liv keeps BOTH a recents list and an Obsidian-style tab grid as two answers to get me back': merged into one surface at two heights; the grid is gone and the list keeps its rows.
22. The record card, 'Liv's one genuinely non-Obsidian container and under-used as an identity': now the app's pattern — records float, documents rise, tools are the ground; every container is a height.

What it now most resembles: Safari's tab-key-raises-tabs and open-link-beside-parent (idioms, not a shape), and Things' 'a sheet over where you stand'. No reference app pages its top-level surfaces sideways from a bar whose back/forward keys name the neighbours while its open documents rise from behind that bar as a two-height sheet whose floor is the full list. The visual language fits one sentence: sideways is what you are doing, up is what you have open.

Why it is not the two rejected shapes either: the four-floor bar put Find and Open as floors among Today/Calendar/Tasks as five buttons — a ClickUp tab bar; here the tools are pages, the bar has no view keys at all, and the notes are not on the band. One-list-three-arrangements collapsed the views into one list; here each view keeps its own body and position. Views-as-tabs (Obsidian's panes) is refused: nothing but documents is ever on the desk.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- There is no history. From Calendar → Tasks → Search → note, dragging the note down lands on Tasks; nothing takes you to Calendar but ‹. Cross-row history (view, then document, then view) is gone by design. A note reached from two link chains sits in one place on the desk, so its ‹ names whichever neighbour it has now, not the source you came from last.
- The bar is no longer static: the words under ‹ › change with every page and every card. Muscle memory gets the same five positions, but a person who reads the bar has to read it again after each step, and on a document the words are truncated titles ('Trip to…'). This is the cost of naming the neighbours at legal size instead of peeking them.
- ‹ › go dim at a row's end — on Inbox, on Everything, on the first and last card. Two of five tools always show one dead key. It is honest (Obsidian's permanently dead › was the complaint; this one is dead only where there is nowhere to go), but it is a dim key on the launch screen's neighbour.
- Paging the band costs a tap or a bar swipe; there is no swipe-from-anywhere. The owner asked for panels 'dragged in from anywhere' (2026-08-08); that gesture is deleted, and the thumb has to find the capsule. In exchange, Today's day strip, Calendar's pager and month grid, Tasks' chips and row swipes never fight a page gesture, and the 'swiping right opens the panel instead' complaint disappears with its cause.
- Leaving a document to the tool costs one drag (down at the scroll top) or two taps (Open, then the wash). The drag is fast but invisible until learned; Open is the visible door. With the keyboard up the bar is retired (2026-08-02) and the drag is the only exit — the same cul-de-sac the app has today, not a new one, but this design does not remove it.
- The fan shows two cards and a sliver; the grid showed six to eight. With ten open notes, reaching the sixth is four taps on › or a sideways scroll. The shelf and Search are the other answers, and both are one more gesture than the grid was.
- Stale cards are a folded row on the shelf, one pull further than the fan; reviving one is a shelf trip. 'Close all' asks nothing (a tab is device state — kept) but it is now a full row you cannot hit by accident, which is also why it is one screen further away.
- The lens pill is top-left — the one control outside the thumb zone, chosen because it is the rarest. Workspaces, filters, Trash and Settings are demoted from a place (the panel) to a card behind it, and the panel's per-row counts survive only in that card's head line and are seen less. The 2026-08-15 'the library is a place' ruling is reversed; 'a place is a panel, a description is a card' has no panel left.
- Two things still rise from the bottom: the desk (behind the bar, no grabber, the bar its handle, raised only by the bar or Open) and a record card (a system sheet with a grabber, over the bar, raised only by a tap). They share an edge but not a gesture and not a chrome; a first-day user may still read the desk as a sheet and try to grab it by a grabber it does not have.
- 'Everything' at caption 14 medium measures ~73pt; a five-slot capsule at 393pt gives ~72pt per slot and ~68pt at 375pt. The ‹ › words either truncate the longest tool name or the capsule's slots are unequal (‹ › wider). Either is a drawn decision the owner must see, and 'too small' ×3 forbids shrinking the word.
- The band pager is a new continuous gesture over the app's heaviest views. Tap-paging costs what today's panel row costs (one lateral easing); the bar SWIPE needs the neighbour mounted at the latch and Calendar (1811 lines, lag reported three times) is a neighbour of Today. drive.sh cannot see frame rate; a profiler run on a device is the gate, and until it passes the swipe may have to ship as tap-only.
- The band scales worse than a list: five names fit; an eighth view would not. That is a real limit and a deliberate one — the furniture is fixed and few.
- Inserting a link-opened note beside its source changes 'order = open order' in one case and is a one-line insertion in DeskPlanes; it is shell state (UserDefaults), not the box, so rule 2's cost test does not apply, but the fold of older desk keys must still read old orders unchanged — a self-check in suites.sh, not a cost test.
- Reverses standing rulings that must be named to the owner before a line of the visible slice is built: ‹ › as the way-back stack (2026-08-23), the library as a pushed place with Notesnook rows and Obsidian's foot (2026-08-15/17/22), the full-screen switcher (2026-07-29, 2026-08-31 'tabs begin at bottom' — honoured better by a sheet from the bottom, but not the same thing), the drag-from-anywhere panel (2026-08-08). Keeps the bar's key set and the numbered box as the only door (2026-08-23), every word under a key (2026-09-05), and every product ruling: records are cards, tabs hold documents, one desk, positions per tool, capture never asks, no query language, launch on Today, the Notes root lists every note, dense, one-handed.

**Wireframes.**

*Today on the band (cold launch)*

│                                 │
│ Tuesday 9 September             │
│ Work 2 · Home 1                 │
│  M   T  [W]  T   F   S   S      │
│  7   8   9  10  11  12  13      │
│                                 │

│                                 │
│ 2 captured today · Route        │
│                                 │

││Inbox Calendar Search Task Open││

The 2026-08-23 capsule, unchanged in shape: five keys, every key a word at caption (the size the owner set on 2026-09-05). ‹ wears 'Inbox' and › wears 'Calendar' because those are the tools either side of Today on the band; tap to page (one lateral easing), or drag the capsule sideways to page continuously. The screen names where you are (Today's date, as ruled); the bar names where you can go. Drag the capsule UP → the desk rises. The lens pill top-left ('All ⌄') is the only other chrome; it opens the workspace/filter card from the top. Nothing on the body pages the band: the day strip, the rows and the row swipes keep their gestures. Tap + → a task due today rises as a record card with the caret in its name (unchanged). Today sets contextDay and parks its day/piles as it does now. drive.sh: liv.surface.today the one surface; `band` walks ‹ › to all five markers.

Reached by tapping Open or dragging the bar up. The sheet is the app's own, rising BEHIND the bar, which stays as the surface's foot; there is no grabber — a grabber is the record card's mark, and the desk's handle is the bar. Today above is washed 50% and takes only the tap that lowers the desk. Cards are TabCard as it exists (title, excerpt lines, kind footer) at the grid's card width: two and a sliver, the active one (Kitchen reno) at the leading edge with an accent ring; scroll the row sideways or tap ‹ › to step — their words now name the cards either side (Q4 plan is off-screen left). The Open box is FILLED: the desk is up, and tapping it lowers the desk. + reads Note and makes one, lifted. Tap a card → lift; swipe a card up → close. The row 'Notes · 134' (tap, or keep pulling) takes the sheet to full height: the SHELF — this card row pinned at the top, a folded 'Inactive · n' row, then every admitted note by recency (NotesList's body). An empty desk skips this half stop and lands on the shelf. drive.sh: liv.overlay.desk at half with liv.surface.today still the one surface (`desk`); liv.surface.notes at full with more rows than open tabs (`shelf`).

height == .lifted; openDoc is the active card; the editor is EntityTabBody unchanged (title scrolls with the body; properties key and ••• top-right; nothing top-left — the 2026-08-24 ruling holds, down is the way out). ‹ › wear the titles of the cards either side and step along the desk without leaving full screen. Tapping [[Tiles quote]] inserts that note as a card immediately to the RIGHT of this one and lifts it, so its ‹ reads 'Kitchen reno' — back is one tap. A link to a task raises a record card over the note. Drag down at the scroll top → the note drops to where it was lifted from (Today, since the desk was closed when a Today row opened it; the fan if it was up; the shelf if it came from a shelf row) — one outcome, no flick/slow rule; it works with the keyboard up and commits the text on the way down. Tap Open → the fan rises and this note shrinks onto it as the active card (the box fills). + makes a new note as a card immediately to the right and lifts it. The properties and history cards, the ••• menu and the trash-with-undo chip are unchanged; trashing the lifted note drops to `liftedFrom`. drive.sh: liv.surface.document (`lift`: tap a card → document marker; drag down → the tool's marker again).

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- THE BAR — one Liquid Glass capsule (LivBar material, height 66, endPad, sideInset kept), the surface's own foot on every tool, on the desk sheet and on a lifted document; retires on scroll, under the keyboard, under a LivMenu; never for an empty desk. Five slots, the 2026-08-23 order, every key with a word at caption: ‹ (word: the previous place on the row you are in, dim at an end), › (word: the next), SEARCH, `+` with the kind it makes here, OPEN with the numbered box (LivGlyph.day(n), live count) which is drawn FILLED while the desk is up. The capsule is the one drag handle: sideways pages the row, up raises the desk, down lowers it.
- THE LENS PILL — top-left on tools and on the shelf, 44pt glass, the active workspace or filter's name + chevron.down ('All ⌄'); opens the lens card from the top. Absent on a lifted document (nothing inert on screen).
- PROPERTIES KEY and ••• — top-right on a lifted document only, 44pt glass circles, unchanged.
- LivTopScrim — status-bar band on every surface; its band leaves with the pill on scroll-away. Unchanged.
- THE DESK SHEET — the app's own sheet behind the bar at two heights (fan / shelf), NO grabber (the bar is its handle), the 50% wash over the tool at half; not a system sheet. Replaces the library panel's push/wash and the switcher cover.
- THE 'Notes · N' ROW — the fan's floor row, the tap door to the shelf.
- THE 'Inactive · n' FOLDED ROW — on the shelf only, between the pinned open cards and the list (the LATE-fold idiom); unfolds to the stale cards and a full 'Close all' row.
- MINIMISED RECORD PILL — above the bar, one at a time, unchanged.
- FLOATS (not persistent): record card, properties card, history card (system sheets, grabber); Search cover (from the key); lens card, New menu, ••• menu, route card, trash-confirm (LivMenu / livSheet from the edge their button is on); camera cover.
- GONE: the library door (PanelMark), the library panel and its foot, the tab switcher cover, the Inactive screen and its 'Back to tabs' band, the 'Nothing open' state, the dashed New-note card and switcher footer, the from-anywhere panel drag and the pagerZone veto.

**Gestures.**
- Tap ‹ or › in the bar → one step along the row you are in: the previous/next tool on the band, or the previous/next card when the desk is up or a document is lifted. The word under each key names where it goes; at a row's end the key dims with no word.
- Drag the bar sideways → the same, continuously (PanelPanRecognizer retargeted to the capsule's rect: latch at |dx| > 18 && dx > 2|dy|, cancels touches under the finger, flick or 50% commits). Nothing on a tool's body pages the band; Today's day strip, Calendar's pager and month grid, Tasks' chips and every row swipe keep their own gestures, and the pagerZone veto is deleted.
- Drag the bar up (|dy| > 2|dx|), or tap Open → the desk rises to half height (the fan) over the washed tool. Keep pulling past the fan, or tap 'Notes · N' → the shelf at full height. With the desk up, the Open box is filled and tapping it lowers the desk; dragging the bar down does the same.
- On the fan: tap a card → lift it; swipe a card up → close it (its ✕ stays as the tap door); scroll the row sideways → move along the desk. Tap the washed tool above → lower the desk. Drag the shelf down → stops at the fan, then closes.
- In a lifted document: drag down at the scroll top → the document drops to where it was lifted from (`liftedFrom`: tool, fan or shelf). One outcome; no velocity rule. Works with the keyboard up (commits the text, lowers the card). Tap Open → the fan rises with this document as its active card.
- Tap the lens pill (top-left) → the workspace/filter card from the top; drag it up or tap its scrim to close.
- Tap + → make what the row holds (task in Today/Tasks, event on the shown day in Calendar, note on Inbox/Everything/fan/shelf/document). Hold + 0.45s → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text). Unchanged.
- A record card is raised by a tap (a row, a link, the minimised pill) and never by a pull — the bottom edge's one pull belongs to the desk. Swipe the card down → it minimises to the pill above the bar; tap the pill → it returns; ✕ → dropped. Unchanged.
- Chrome: 44pt of downward scroll retires the bar and the top pill; scrolling up or reaching the top returns them. Under the keyboard the bar retires. Unchanged.

**Capture.** `+` lives where it does now — fourth key of the bar, always present, labelled with the kind the row makes — and what happens the instant after is what the vertical axis is for. TAP on Today/Tasks: a task (due the day you look at, 09:00) rises as a record card over the tool with the caret in its name; on Calendar: an event on the shown day, the same card. TAP on Inbox, Everything, the fan, the shelf, or a lifted document: liv_create_note_at, stamped with the workspace's cells, a card minted on the desk (appended; or inserted right after the current card if you were in a document), `liftedFrom` recorded, and the note LIFTS — the same rise the fan makes, so you see where it came from — with the caret at its start. Drag it down and it is a card on the desk, the tool you were on is back, and (Desk.swift:684 unchanged) it is the capture Inbox's Route lists until it is filed. HOLD `+`: the New menu, unchanged (File → fileImporter, last pick lifted; Scan text → camera, Done lifts the last shot). SEARCH's Create row: capture, stamp, lift. SHARE SHEET and liv://capture?text=: unchanged and deliberately not opened — the extension spools, the app drains at foreground, liv_capture_at, stamp, and the thing waits in Inbox; the only visible effects are Inbox's own Route count and the new row at its top if Inbox is the tool on screen. Bare liv://capture → a new note lifted, as `+` Note. The tab switcher's dashed 'New note' card and its footer `+` die with the switcher; the fan needs no New card because the bar's `+` is under your thumb while the fan is up and reads 'Note'.

**Back, links and catches.** BACK IS A DIRECTION, NOT A STACK. Down leaves a document, to exactly where it was lifted from (`liftedFrom`, one value); down lowers the desk; ‹ steps back along the row you are in — the previous tool on the band, or the previous card on the desk, which for a link-opened note is its source. LivReturns, LivPlace, here, land, goBack, goForward are deleted; the bar's ‹ › keys survive with one spatial meaning and a name under each. There is nothing to clear on a page or a workspace switch because nothing was pushed. 'Back' from a note opened from Today lands on Today; from a Search hit while on Calendar, on Calendar; from a shelf row, on the shelf; from a [[link]], on the source by one tap on ‹. Re-opening the document already lifted (a reminder tap) does nothing, as today.

LINKS AND ARRIVALS. liv://today|inbox|calendar|tasks|everything → lower everything (height closed, floats cleaned — surfaceCleanup survives) and page the band there; liv://notes → the shelf at full height; liv://entity/<id> → a record raises a card over whatever is on screen, a document lifts over the current stand (`liftedFrom` = the current height); a notification tap does the same; unknown hosts still do nothing; a payload only on capture. A share-sheet catch lands in Inbox, saved and not opened, as today.

WORKSPACES AND FILTERS. The lens pill (top-left) shows the active workspace or saved filter by name; its card lists All, workspaces (edit/trash in the row's context menu, New workspace… inline form), saved filters (toggle), New filter (the filter form), then Trash and Settings, with the workspace's count line at its head. Choosing a workspace swaps the desk and spots (DeskPlanes per workspace, unchanged), closes every float, lowers to height closed and stays on the current tool — it never lands in Notes. A filter narrows Calendar, Tasks, Everything, the shelf and search; Inbox and Today are exempt by rule. Nothing here types a query; the core stays the only parser.

RECORDS. Unchanged and load-bearing: TabShape.of decides, DeskModel.open is the one door, a record rises as a half-height system card over the tool, the fan, the shelf or a lifted document, edits save as made, swipe-down minimises to the one pill, Trash clears both, records are swept off the desk on the first snapshot. Records never become cards on the desk, and a record card is never raised by a pull.
