**Survives in code.** DeskModel and DeskPlanes (Plane.swift): one desk per workspace, tabs + activeTabId, spots per tool, every persistence key ('desk.v3.<ws>', 'desk.spots.v3.<ws>') and the fold of older keys, open(entity:) reuse rule (gains an `after:` insertion point for link-opened notes), park(feature, at:), hasStrangers / dropRecordsAndStrangers, close, LivTabs.age / isInactive / days predicate and liveTabs / inactiveTabs (the shelf's folded row reads them).; Positions.swift tokens — untouched; the band makes 'a position survives' visible.; DeskModel.open(_:as:) / TabShape.of / openAsCard / recordCard / minimiseRecord / restoreRecord / MinimisedRecordPill — Option C verbatim. openDocument (Chrome.swift:841) survives renamed as lift(_:) minus its returns.push and state = .notes; adoptCapture, show(_:) (routed through lift), surfaceCleanup — every arrival still closes every float.; All six view bodies: Today.swift, Inbox.swift (Route/Tidy, route card, clerk chip), Calendar.swift + Month.swift (minus their pagerZone publishing at Calendar.swift:1093-1095), Tasks.swift, Everything.swift, Notes.swift (NotesList becomes the shelf's list body under the pinned cards). Navigate.swift's Feature enum, makes, title, glyph, FeatureBody; inOrder re-declared as the band order without .notes.; Bar.swift entire in shape: BottomBar's five-slot HStack, key(_:_:spoken:on:hold:action:), slot(_:glyph:), the Todoist word-under-glyph recipe, the box glyph LivGlyph.day(n), LivBar.disabledInk (now used at a row's end), the simultaneous long-press on +; the two ‹ › keys re-wired from desk.goBack/goForward to desk.step(-1/+1) with their words from the row's neighbours; the box gains a filled state in Glyph.swift.; Chrome.swift's retire/return mechanics (chromeAway, livHidesChrome, LivChromeScroll), the keyboard watch (Chrome.swift:900-925) and Desk.swift's surfaceFoot (the bar as the surface's own foot, Desk.swift:357-398), LivBar/LivTheme/LivMotion tokens — nothing typed raw.; PanelDrag.swift's PanelPanRecognizer — the window-level latch, cancelsTouchesInView, mayStart/mayClaim, flick/50% commit — retargeted: mayStart limited to the bar's rect, and the latch gains the vertical axis for raising/lowering the desk.; Tabs.swift's TabCard (title, excerpt lines, kind footer, dead-tab case) becomes the fan card and the shelf's pinned band; LivTabs and livTabsSelfCheck survive; the grid, InactiveTabs and the fullScreenCover go.; Search.swift entire (SearchView cover, chips, facets, find-or-create, picker mode, its own recordCardHost) and its App.swift:273 fullScreenCover — one door, the key.; WorkspaceSwitch.swift's WorkspaceSwitcher body and the filter form → the lens card's content; Panel.swift's ViewCounts (Panel.swift:401) → the lens card's head line; Menu.swift LivMenu / livSheet(from:) / LivEdgeSheetHost; Settings.swift; Trash.swift; History.swift; Detail.swift (properties card, InspectorValueSheet); Links.swift.; Editor.swift / EditorText.swift / EditorStyle.swift / Files.swift (EntityTabBody in Desk.swift:967) — the lifted document is exactly this body; the New menu, createHere, createNote, createRecord, noteVerbs, the flash/undo chip (Desk.swift:622-856) unchanged.; Camera.swift / Scan.swift, FileImport, Catch.swift + Share.swift + ShareExtension, Routes.swift (host table kept, handlers retargeted), Notify.swift, Outbox.swift.; The workspace lens and saved-filter plumbing (Workspace.swift, Furnish.swift), Box.swift (no new FFI verb is needed — this is a shell-only change; Box.swift stays the one file calling liv_*).; Surface.swift markers and drive.sh: liv.surface.today|inbox|calendar|tasks|everything|notes|document keep their names; liv.overlay.desk and .lens replace .library and .tabs; day-one checks are `band` (‹ › to all five markers and back), `desk` (re-aimed: Open raises, the tool stays the one surface, the count holds), `shelf` (full height: liv.surface.notes with more rows than open tabs), `lift` (card → document marker → drag down → the tool's marker); `goto notes` still refuses a document; tour/lens/facets/vault/rows/chrome/bar/create/routes/spool/history/workspace re-aimed; `panel` and `grid` deleted with their surfaces.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift's SidePanel, LibraryPanel, its rows, foot(_:), fade mask and hairline; the PanelMark door glyph; Chrome.swift setLibrary (222), panelProgress (395), deskShift (423), libraryShown (209); Desk.swift goToLibrary / closePanel / panelOffset / claimPanel / settleDrag (412-512), the desk's square-corner mask and 50% wash under the panel (the wash idiom moves to the desk sheet); -desk.boot library|grid.; The from-anywhere panel drag: PanelDragInstaller's window-wide mayStart, Coordinator.pagerZone and startAllowed's zone test (PanelDrag.swift:124-215), Chrome.swift's pagerZone (362) and its publishers in Calendar.swift:1093-1095 / Month.swift / Desk.swift:315.; Tabs.swift's TabSwitcher (64-335) and its App.swift:267 fullScreenCover, its grid, footer, 'Nothing open', the dashed New-note card; InactiveTabs (337-443) and its 'Back to tabs' band; switcherShown (Chrome.swift:165); App.swift:479-482 -desk.boot switcher|inactive; the a11y 'N documents open' phrasing (the word becomes 'N open').; Navigate.swift LivReturns / LivPlace (86-160) and livPlacesSelfCheck (281), -places.selfcheck; Chrome.swift returns (206), here / back / forward / goBack (770) / goForward (775) / land (782), returns.push in openDocument and every returns.clear. The ‹ › KEYS THEMSELVES STAY.; Chrome.swift go (698) / goToRoot (731) / showList (746) and the whole root-of-view special case; the state == .notes guard on openDoc (183-186); adopt(workspace:)'s state = .notes (678); DeskHost's 'EntityTabBody vs NotesList by openDoc == nil' choice (Desk.swift:48-66) — replaced by height; FeatureBody's `.notes: EmptyView()` case (Navigate.swift:187).; Feature.notes as a band member; Today's footer desk.go(.inbox) becomes page(to: .inbox).; The library's per-row selection fill, the 'Trash' and 'New filter' rows as panel rows (they become lens-card rows), the panel foot's unfiled count line (moves to the lens card's head).; The recorded rulings this overturns, to be re-ruled in writing as ONE question: ‹ › as the way-back stack (2026-08-23), the library as a pushed place with Notesnook rows and Obsidian's foot (2026-08-15/17/22), the full-screen switcher (2026-07-29 / 2026-08-31), panels dragged in from anywhere (2026-08-08), 'a switcher pick lands on the note from any view' (2026-09-09 — moot: the desk is on every view). NOT overturned, and named so the owner can see it: the five-key bar 'literally' and the numbered box as the only door (2026-08-23), every key's word (2026-09-05).

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- 1 'Notes is two things wearing one name' — RESOLVED. A document is drawn by its own height (.lifted), never by borrowing a Feature; Notes is one surface (the desk sheet at full height) and is not on the band; nothing on screen says 'Notes' while you read a note.
- 2 'List from the panel, documents from the bar — two doors on two edges' — RESOLVED. One door (Open / the bar pulled up) on one edge to one surface at two heights; the open notes are visibly the first band of all notes.
- 3 'Open N counts an invisible container' — RESOLVED. The count is the cards you see when you tap the box; tools are the other axis and are visibly not cards; the box fills while the cards are up.
- 4 'Notes means three surfaces; the + word changes' — RESOLVED for the name (one surface, one name). The + word still changes per row (Task/Event/Note) — kept on purpose (2026-09-05); what is now uniform is the consequence: a note always rises from below, a record always floats.
- 5 'Same note by three routes, three different backs' — RESOLVED. Every open is the same lift; down always returns to `liftedFrom`; ‹ always names the neighbour; no stack to differ by route.
- 6 'Back key is document history wearing a browser's glyph' — RESOLVED without deleting the glyph: ‹ › keep their slots and lose the stack; each has one meaning (a step along the row) and a name under it saying where. Neither is ever 'lit but does nothing' — dim only where there is nowhere to go.
- 7 'Root-of-view rule is a patch with two branches' — RESOLVED. There is no 'tap the view you are in' at all (the band has no view keys); the shelf's root is always the whole list; goToRoot and showList are deleted.
- 8 'Positions and documents are two remembered things the user never sees as different' — RESOLVED. A tool visibly resumes when you page to it; a document visibly sits as a card on the desk; the two axes ARE the difference.
- 9 '+ is local or navigating depending on kind' — PARTLY. A record still floats and a note still fills the screen, but the note's lift is a move on the axis you reverse with one drag to exactly where you were, not a state change into another view; the label question (kind vs consequence) stays as the owner ruled it.
- 10 'The switcher is a full-screen mode and the only way back' — RESOLVED. The desk is half-height over where you stand, no Done, reachable from every tool and every document by the same key; from a document ‹ › also reach the neighbours without any switcher.
- 11 'The panel's selection fill answers state while the screen answers surface' — RESOLVED by deletion; the screen's own name says where you are and the bar's words say where you can go, and both switch rows when you lift a document.
- 12 'Inbox is a queue, Notes a shelf, presented as peers' — PARTLY. Notes leaves the peer list and becomes the floor, which is the larger half of the confusion; Inbox stays a band tool at the left end beside three lists, distinguished only by its glyph, hero and position. Not resolved, and this design does not claim to.
- The triangle: the desk gets its own state (height), so no vertex is a property of another — and slice 0 does exactly that under the current chrome before any pixel moves. Kinds-are-peers: tools are the ground, notes are raised from beneath — addressed at the level of 'where does a task live relative to a note', not 'are they the same object' (still open).

**Changed after the judges.**
- The NAME STRIP is withdrawn (three judges: caption-size clipped names behind a fade — 'too small' ×3 and 'just ugh'). The bar keeps the owner's five keys instead, and ‹ › become spatial: one step along the row you are in, with the destination's NAME as the word under each key at the size every bar word already has. No fade, no clipping, no peeking, no half-ink; the self-teaching affordance the hand-and-head judge praised survives as legal-size words. This also cuts the reversals: the 2026-08-23 bar and the numbered box are kept, not deleted.
- The BODY SWIPE is deleted (all three: vetoed exactly where the owner reported 'swiping right opens the panel instead'). The bar capsule is the one drag handle for both axes; the pagerZone veto and the from-anywhere recognizer go with the gesture that needed them. Today's strip, Calendar's pager and month grid, Tasks' chips and row swipes are untouched.
- The PULL-DOWN SEARCH door is withdrawn (judge 2: opposite meanings on adjacent surfaces; judges 1 and 3: two doors to one room, cut four times). Search is the key only; pull-down at a scroll top now has one meaning in the app — drop a lifted document.
- The VELOCITY rule (slow → fan, flick → tool) is replaced by 'down undoes the lift': the document drops to `liftedFrom`, a single remembered value, one outcome. It also works with the keyboard up, which answers the cross-cutting note that no direction navigates from inside a note with the keyboard raised.
- The OPEN KEY's three meanings collapse to a toggle with a drawn state: the numbered box raises the desk and is FILLED while the desk is up (tap lowers). From a document it raises the fan with that document as its card — the same meaning.
- TWO RISERS from the bottom edge are told apart by gesture and chrome: the desk has no grabber (the bar is its handle) and is the only thing a pull raises; a record card is a system sheet with a grabber and is only ever raised by a tap.
- The INACTIVE TAIL and its 'Clear' chip are gone (judge 1: a destructive verb hidden in a scroll). Stale cards fold into one 'Inactive · n' row on the shelf (the LATE-fold idiom) with 'Close all' as a full row in the unfold.
- The FAN is drawn at the grid's card width (two cards and a sliver, active at the leading edge) instead of one-and-a-half centred, and what happens at twelve open notes is stated: ‹ › taps, the shelf's recency rows, Search — not 'a stance'.
- The PERFORMANCE gate is turned into a mount strategy and a decision: only the current tool is mounted; a bar swipe mounts the neighbour at the latch; tap-paging costs what today's panel row costs; the swipe ships tap-only until a device profile passes.
- The link-opened insertion is kept but costed honestly (judge 3): `DeskPlanes.open(entity:after:)` is one shell-state insertion covered by a suites.sh self-check; it is not on the box's snapshot or write path, so rule 2's cost test does not apply, and the old-key fold is the thing to test.
- A BUILD ORDER is added from judge 3's cross-cutting note: slice 0 (height on the desk, no visible change, provable with existing checks) before the visible slice, and the reversals collected into ONE written question for the owner instead of footnotes.
- The day-one drive.sh checks are named (`band`, `desk` re-aimed, `shelf`, `lift`) and the lens card's head line takes the panel foot's counts so ViewCounts has a place.
- The 'hero could go' open question is closed: the screen names where you are, the bar names where you can go, so the hero stays.

**Conceded.**
- No history. Cross-row 'back' (Calendar → Tasks → Search → note → Calendar) is gone by design; ‹ is spatial and a note reached from two chains has one set of neighbours. This is the price of one navigation system instead of two.
- The lens pill is outside the thumb zone and the library is no longer a place: 2026-08-15's ruling is reversed and 2026-08-11's 'filters live where you come to change what you are looking at' is met only in the sense that the lens pill IS that place. A regrip for the rarest control is accepted.
- The band swipe cannot be verified by the harness and Calendar has lagged three times; until a device profile passes, paging is a tap, which is one gesture slower than a swipe and no faster than today's panel row.
- Two things still rise from the same edge. They are drawn and raised differently, but a first-day user may still look for a grabber on the desk.
- Keyboard-up is still a cul-de-sac for lateral navigation (the bar retires, 2026-08-02); the drag-down exit is the same one a system sheet has and the only one this design offers.
- Chrome-retires-on-scroll is a shared idiom with Obsidian and is kept as the owner's own ruling.
- Vagueness 12 (Inbox as a queue among lists) is not resolved; Inbox stays a band tool at the left end.
- Vagueness 9 is only partly resolved: + still floats a record and lifts a note, labelled by kind (the 2026-09-05 ruling), not by consequence.
- Four rulings are reversed (‹ › as a stack, the library as a place, the full-screen switcher, drag-from-anywhere), and the visible slice cannot be built before the owner re-rules them in writing. Slice 0 does not need that word; slice 1 does.
- Twelve open notes cost more taps than the grid did; the desk is designed for a few live cards and the shelf for the rest.

**Open questions.**
- THE ONE QUESTION FIRST, in writing, before a canvas: the owner said keep the tab system and the browser's bar; this keeps both in pixels and reverses four things behind them — ‹ › as a history stack, the library as a pushed place, the full-screen switcher, the drag-from-anywhere panel. Does he re-rule those four, or is any of them load-bearing for him?
- Do slice 0 (height on the desk, no visible change, provable with today's `goto notes` and `desk` checks) and the first `build.sh` + `drive.sh` run of revs 54–61 precede the mockup? Both are cheap and neither needs his word.
- Band order: Inbox · Today · Calendar · Tasks · Everything (Today one in from the left, so launch has a ‹ and a ›) versus 'ordered like a day' with Today at the left end (launch with a dead ‹). Feature.inOrder is re-declared either way.
- The ‹ › words at caption 14: 'Everything' measures ~73pt against a ~72pt slot at 393 and ~68pt at 375. Truncate, give ‹ › wider slots than the three keys, or shorten the name? Mock at both widths with 'Everything' as a neighbour.
- Do the ‹ › words carry the lens counts ('Inbox 5') or are counts only on each tool's own hero and the lens card's head line?
- The lens pill: top-left (the app-level corner, its card from the top as the owner suggested on 2026-08-15) or top-right on tools (nearer the thumb, but the corner a document uses for its own keys)? And does Settings deserve a door nearer the thumb than a row in that card?
- Bar swipe on day one or tap-only until a device profile of Today ↔ Calendar with the neighbour mounted at the latch shows no hitch? drive.sh cannot see frame rate and the owner has reported lag on Calendar three times.
- Card close: swipe-up on the fan plus the ✕, or ✕ only.
- A link-opened note inserted beside its source (adjacent chain, ‹ names the source) versus appended (stable order). Kept as inserted; the owner should see both drawn.
- After a workspace switch: stay on the current tool (proposed) or on the workspace's own remembered band position?
- Stale cards folded on the shelf with 'Close all' as a full row: is that his Chrome-for-iOS 'inactive tabs' without a screen, or does he want them on the fan too?
- Whether the mockup's two bottom risers (desk behind the bar, no grabber; record card over it, with one) read as two things on sight — an eyes-on check, not a harness one.

### 3. The Spine

*Liv is one continuous stream of days — today under your thumb, yesterday above, tomorrow below — on which every note, task, event, file and capture hangs off the day it is about (or, if it is about no day, the day you last touched it, with open to-dos always at today's head); the six views are lenses that subtract from that stream, open documents ride as a titled ribbon above the bar, and nothing in the app is a room you go to.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE ROOT IS TIME, NOT A MENU. Cold launch draws one vertical scroll: a sticky heading "TODAY · Thu 10 Sep" with the day's area counts under it (the existing Today count line), then today's things in one column — two folds at the head, "▸ 3 late" (incomplete tasks whose day has passed, the phase-5 predicate) and "▸ 5 undated" (open tasks with no day at all — today's head is where a to-do with no day lives, under EVERY lens that shows tasks, because a reminder that sinks is a product failure, not a trade-off); then the day's events in hour order, tasks due today, notes and files you wrote in today, unrouted captures that arrived today wearing the clerk's "Work?" chip. Drag down and yesterday's section is above (its done pile struck); scroll on and tomorrow, the day after, the week are below. A day with nothing between two days with something is a single rule; a run of them is one rule ("Fri 11 – Sun 13"); beyond the last thing there is nothing to draw — the stream is exactly as long as what is on it, so a fresh box is today's furnished section and no ruler.

WHAT HANGS WHERE — one rule, four cases: an event on its date; a task on its due day (open and undated → today's head; done and undated → the day it was ticked); a note or file on the day you last wrote in it; an unrouted capture on the day it arrived. The first two use `due`, which is on the wire; the last two need `edited`, an optional civil stamp per row that the core can already answer (every transaction is stamped — the History card prints them) and the snapshot does not yet carry. That stamp is a purely additive FFI change, owner-gated, failing-test-first, and it is a PREREQUISITE of the Notes lens, not a later nicety: without it a note can only hang off `created`, which buries an edited-yesterday note under its birth month and loses the recency list that Notes.swift argues, correctly, is the app's best "get me back". The critique was right; the direction now depends on the stamp and says so.

The day is the only PLACE in the app. Where you are is a day (the pinned heading names it) and what you are looking at through is a LENS (the bar's Lens key prints its name — "Everything", "Notes", "Inbox", "Calendar", "Tasks"). A lens only subtracts rows from the same stream; it never rearranges it. The seventh name, Today, is not a lens but a position: the bar's Today key snaps the stream to today under any lens, and at today it re-pins the day's heading (the platform's re-tap-scrolls-to-top idiom), so it is never a dead key. Six names survive, in Feature.inOrder; what changes is that they stop being six peer rooms and become one room seen five ways plus a way home — and that IS a change to one of the six the owner said to keep, listed under conceded and put to him as one question.

Opening a thing keeps the day under you. A note or file slides in from the right as a DOCUMENT surface (LivMotion.nav) with the ribbon and bar still at its foot; ‹ slides it away and the spine is exactly where you left it, same day, same lens, same scroll — never a snap. A task or event rises as the existing record card over the spine and closes nothing. The set of open documents is the RIBBON: a strip of title chips above the bar, ordered by the day each was last touched (DeskTab.lastUsed, already a civil stamp), newest at the thumb end, the active one filled; chips older than LivTabs.days fall off the far end into a "+N older" fold. The ribbon is the desk made visible: it is on the spine and on every document, it counts nothing, and it is the only door back to an open note from anywhere. The numbered box, the full-screen grid, the library panel, the top-left door and the forward key are deleted; the desk finally has a state of its own (`Where = .spine(lens, day) | .document(id)`) instead of borrowing Notes'.

The ribbon is a bottom strip of open documents, and the owner removed exactly that on 2026-08-23 ("Not a tab 'bar', remove it. We're on the phone, not desktop."). This proposal does not pretend otherwise: the ribbon is the ONE question to put to him before any pixel, the same question five of eight directions depend on. If he keeps the 2026-08-23 ruling the spine still stands — the ribbon then appears only ON a document (where it is Safari's own tab strip, a reference he named) and on the spine the desk is the first row of the Lens card ("Open · 3", which opens the same chips as a bottom card). The stream, the lenses, the day heading, the capture landing and the `Where` state do not depend on the ribbon; only "one tap back to any open note from the spine" does.

Why this is the answer to the owner's brief and not a re-skin: Note Tabs stop being a global navigation space behind a key and become a thing you can see at all times; the Note List stops pretending to be a destination and becomes what it always was — a filter ("every day, only notes"), now ordered by the day you last wrote, which is the recency list drawn on a time axis; the views stop being a drawer of six doors and become lenses on the one surface the app is about, the day; and the kinds-are-peers question (2026-08-16) is answered on the surface itself — a task, an event and a note on the same day, in the same row grammar, at the same level — without a tab bar of views, without three arrangements, and without views-as-tabs.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS = THE RIBBON. The one app-wide desk (DeskPlanes, per workspace) is unchanged in the model and gains a permanent face: a horizontal strip of title chips riding directly above the bar, in the thumb zone, present on the spine and on every document, absent entirely when the desk is empty and nothing is minimised (nothing inert on screen). Chips are ordered by DeskTab.lastUsed — newest nearest the thumb; the active chip is filled; each chip is label size (16) on a 44pt touch band (LivRow.touch), never micro-text; a title is cut at two words plus an ellipsis so nine chips are nine readable words, not a scrolling sentence.

ONE AXIS, ONE MEANING (fixed after the critique): the strip SCROLLS horizontally and a TAP switches. There is no swipe-to-switch on the ribbon — the same axis on the same element cannot both scroll and switch, and the critique was right to call that a collision, not a tuning matter. When a document slides in, its chip is scrolled into view at the thumb end; next/previous is a tap on the neighbouring chip, which is always visible beside the filled one. Long-press a chip → the app's bottom card with "Close", "Properties", "Move to Trash" — one LivMenu, subject named. Chips older than LivTabs.days (the existing inactive predicate, unchanged) drop out of the strip into a trailing "+N older" chip; tapping it raises a bottom card listing them by title and age with "Close all" (the Inactive shelf, now a card, not a mode inside a mode). A minimised record (swipe a task card down) becomes the ribbon's leading chip with its kind glyph — one at a time, exactly as the pill is today, in the same strip. Records still never enter planes.desk. A new note (+ under a note-making lens, the create menu, Search's Create row, a [[link]] to a scrap) mints a chip and slides the editor in, as now.

Foot height, honestly: ribbon band 44 + LivBar.gap 4 + bar 66 ≈ 114pt at rest when the desk is non-empty. That is more chrome than today's 66. It pays for itself only because it retires: the ribbon retires with the bar on scroll (existing hysteresis), under the keyboard, and vanishes when the desk is empty. The critique asked for a nine-chip mockup before the owner's "tiny and subtle" correction is applied — that mockup is the first artboard, not the last, and the two-word cut is the answer drawn on it.

What dies: the "Open [n]" key, LivGlyph.day, the full-screen TabSwitcher grid and its InactiveTabs overlay, the dashed New-note card, the "N open" footer, and the 2026-08-23 "tabs as they appeared when you clicked the numbered box" — this reverses that ruling on purpose and needs the owner's word (see concept for the fallback if he keeps it).

THE NOTE LIST = THE NOTES LENS: "every day, only notes, each under the day you last wrote in it." Choose Notes in the Lens card and the same stream shows, under each day heading, the notes and files whose `edited` day is that day and nothing else — monochrome rows, because a single-kind list wears no kind colour (2026-08-18). It contains every note (the harness guard "Notes lists more rows than open tabs" holds trivially), it has no separate screen, no hero, no door in a panel, and it is reached from the same key as every other lens. Its order is the current recency list's order — `edited` day descending, `recency` (the seq) within a day — drawn on the time axis with day headings that are honest by the 2026-09-02 rule (a header only where the printed key is the sort key: here the day IS the sort key). "Get me back to what I was editing" is the ribbon's job; "the one I touched last week and closed" is under last week's heading, two flicks up; "something old" is the month card or Search. What is LOST against the journal reading of v1: a note moves when you edit it (a March note edited today sits under today) — which is exactly what the recency list does now, so nothing gets worse than today, and "a note stays where it was written" is given up (trade-offs). Until the `edited` stamp exists the Notes lens cannot be built honestly and is gated on it; the bare spine can ship before it with notes on `created` and a stated regression.

The Notes lens's + makes a note (word "Note"); the editor slides in with the caret in it; when you slide back the new chip is on the ribbon, the spine is exactly where you were (no snap), and the new row is under today's heading — visible if you were at today, one Today-key tap away if you were not, and on the ribbon either way.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** There is one level, not a hierarchy: SPINE › lens › day › thing.

THE SPINE is the root and the only surface: one snapshot, bucketed by day in the shell. It is what Everything.swift's "All" is today — the whole `everything` list, lensed by workspace — re-sorted from created-desc into day sections by the hang-where rule, past above, future below. Everything's three slices dissolve: All = the bare spine; Upcoming = the sections below today (the same rows Everything.Upcoming shows — dated things in the next seven days, due ascending — in the same order, with day rules between them; `drive.sh spine` asserts the two sets are equal, so nothing is lost, only headings added); Unfiled = a filter row in the Lens card (it already exists as a facet). The workspace lens and any saved filter narrow the spine exactly as they narrow Everything today; the Inbox exemption survives as: unrouted rows ignore the workspace lens in every lens (they have no cells yet to be narrowed by).

FEED AND COST, corrected: the spine is NOT a windowed feed. `liv_snapshot_window_at` widens only the recurrence `occurrences` window; `dated` is always the full set and, per ffi/liv.h, "the shell buckets by day" — which is what the spine does. So there is no new FFI, no prefetch and no paging: the one existing snapshot (with its existing cost tests) is sectioned in the shell, and the occurrence window is re-pointed (`refreshWindow`, as the calendar does per month; the engine caps it at a year) when the scroll leaves the loaded range. The cost that is new is the shell-side section builder, and it ships with a SHAPE self-check in suites.sh in the pattern of EditorCost.swift: doubling the rows roughly doubles the sectioning work, never a millisecond. Mounting: a LazyVStack of day sections, each built by Today.swift's agenda assembler, so only sections near the viewport lay out; a section's folds ("▸ 3 late", "~~2 done~~") are per day. The past is as deep as the box because the snapshot already is.

THE LENSES, in Feature.inOrder, are the five views that subtract: Everything (nothing subtracted; + makes a task due the pinned day — the bare spine is Today extended, so it inherits Today's + rule from 2026-08-28), Notes (notes and files under their `edited` day, monochrome; + makes a note), Inbox (unrouted captures only, by the day they arrived, each row tappable to the existing Route card; the Tidy proposals are a chip in the pinned heading, "4 suggestions", that opens the existing Tidy list as a card; + makes a note), Calendar (events only; the PINNED day is drawn as the existing hour timeline — tap an empty hour to create, the parked month card behind the day heading kept, Notion Calendar's shape kept — and every other day as event rows, so a week under Calendar is a short scroll and only the day you are on is expanded; + makes an event on the pinned day), Tasks (tasks only, keeping the All/status/project chips as a facet row under the pinned heading, TasksPosition as its token; the late and undated folds at today's head as on the bare spine; + makes a task due the pinned day). The undated fold is present under Everything and Tasks — the two lenses that show tasks — and absent under Notes, Inbox and Calendar, which do not; a to-do never sinks out of any lens that could show it.

TODAY is the sixth name and is a position, not a lens: the bar key that scrolls the stream to today under whatever lens is on, and re-pins today's heading when already there. What Today.swift shows now — the LATE fold, the timeline rows, the done pile, the captured-today line — becomes exactly what today's SECTION shows on the bare spine, plus the undated fold; the 7-day strip dies because the spine is the strip.

A DAY is the position. There is one position token for the whole app — the day the pinned heading names — instead of three hidden ones (TodayPosition, CalendarPosition, EverythingLens are deleted; TasksPosition and InboxLens survive as lens facets); the lens changes, the day stays. Tapping the pinned heading raises the existing month card (MonthGridView) to jump anywhere in time; the Today key jumps home.

A THING is opened, never entered: a note or file slides in as a document (its own state, ribbon and bar at its foot, ‹ takes it away); a task or event rises as the record card (unchanged). No lens is ever "inside" another, no view has a root, no row needs a second meaning: goToRoot, showList, go(feature) and the three-branch special case are deleted with the panel.

Workspaces and saved filters: the Lens card (one livSheet(from: .bottom), grabber, hugs content, rising from the key that opened it) lists the five lenses as the existing Notesnook-style rows with counts through the workspace+filter lens and the current lens on a soft fill; then Unfiled, the saved filters (toggle, lit when on) and "New filter"; then Trash; its pinned foot is the workspace button (→ the existing WorkspaceSwitcher, from the same edge), the count line, and the Settings gear — the owner's requested foot, now on a card. Picking a row swaps the lens and keeps the day. Switching a workspace swaps the ribbon to that workspace's desk and snaps the spine to today under Everything: home is always today, which answers "why does a switch land on Notes when launch lands on Today".

A SLICE THAT PROVES IT, named: (1) the invisible three-file change first — `Where` on DeskModel, `openDoc` without the `.notes` guard, DeskHost choosing the document layer by that state, goToRoot/showList deleted — provable with the existing `goto notes` and `desk` checks under today's chrome; it resolves vaguenesses 1, 5, 7 and 11 before any visible pixel and is not mockup-gated because nothing visible changes. (2) Everything.swift's All slice re-rendered as day sections with a sticky heading and the two folds at today's head, under today's panel and bar — proves the section builder, its shape check, the sticky heading, the hang-where rule (with `created` standing in for `edited`, a stated regression) and the row grammar; proves nothing about the ribbon, the Lens card or the bar. Those two are a week; the rest is not.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Argued item by item against the resemblance list, not in general.

THE BOTTOM CAPSULE (copied key set: back · forward · search · + · numbered box): the material stays (it is Safari's, and the owner's), the SET does not. Forward is deleted — the one key Obsidian draws dead and Liv wired; a stream has no forward. The numbered box — the copied fifth-key shape — is deleted with the count it held. In their places sit Today and Lens. Today is a position key on a stream; Obsidian's date key opens a FILE — and, corrected: the core does have a daily-note verb (`liv_open_daily_note_at`) that the shell has never called and this direction never calls; Liv's day is a section that collects typed things, not a page you write into, and the verb stays uncalled. Lens, whose printed word is where you are, is a key no reference app has. Three of five keys change job and glyph; the remaining two (‹, +) are a phone's, not Obsidian's.

THE TAB SWITCHER GRID ("Obsidian's, verbatim in structure"): deleted. Open documents are a ribbon of chips sorted by the day you last touched them — a time-ordered strip is the spine's own idea applied to the desk, and no notes app draws its tabs by date. But a strip of open-document chips at the foot is a tab strip, and honesty requires saying which app it now resembles: Safari's tab strip on iPad and Arc Mobile's, not Obsidian's — and it is the element the owner removed on 2026-08-23. Its distance from those is titles ordered by day with an age fold; its distance from a tab bar is that a lens is never a chip. That is a real resemblance and it is put to the owner as the one question, with the fallback stated.

THE LEFT DRAWER, THE TOP-LEFT TOGGLE, THE EDGE-SWIPE: gone, all three. No drawer exists; the top-left of the spine is empty (only the sticky day heading, which is content); the rightward drag from anywhere is retired and a rightward swipe means one thing, "back from this document". The "most Obsidian pixel on screen" and Obsidian's left third are not restyled — they do not exist. What replaces them is a bottom card of lenses in the thumb zone.

THE PANEL FOOT (workspace · count · gear): kept because the owner asked for it by screenshot twice, but it is now the foot of a card that rises from the Lens key, not the foot of a drawer. That is the one Obsidian slot that survives, and it survives on the owner's explicit word.

ONE DESK PER WORKSPACE: kept in storage and made visible in a way Obsidian's never is.

TOP-RIGHT ELLIPSIS + PROPERTIES KEY: kept on documents only, by standing rulings (2026-08-02, 2026-09-07). On the spine there is no top chrome at all.

CHROME THAT HIDES ON SCROLL: kept for the bar and ribbon (Safari does it too). The sticky day heading does NOT hide — it is the "where am I" and it is content.

INLINE TITLE, TAP-FOLLOWS-LINK, FIND-OR-CREATE, MONOCHROME GLYPHS: kept — editor-level idioms the owner directed; none of them is navigation.

ROOT = LAST DOCUMENT (Obsidian, Apple Notes): Liv's root is today on a stream. The nearest relative is Logseq's journal, and the difference is exact: Logseq's day is a DOCUMENT with blocks; Liv's day is a SECTION that collects typed objects — a task made under Tuesday is a task with a due day, not a bullet on Tuesday's page. Logseq keeps a drawer; Liv has none. Day One and Journal are single-kind feeds with no lenses and no open-document ribbon. Amie is a timeline of two kinds with no documents. Things 3's Anytime is the one idea borrowed on purpose (open undated to-dos at today's head) and it is a fold, not a room. Nobody has "one stream, five subtractive lenses, a ribbon of open documents by day."

THE DIAGNOSED RESEMBLANCE (one kind gets the main surface, the rest a drawer): dissolved structurally. No kind has the main surface; the day has it, and every kind hangs off it in one row grammar. Notes are still separate in the sense the owner requires — a note is the only thing that opens as a document, the only thing that rides the ribbon — while tasks and calendar are as central as notes because they sit on the same day at the same level. It is none of the three rejected shapes: not a bar of view keys (one Lens key), not one-list-three-arrangements (one arrangement, time; lenses subtract, never re-sort), not views-as-tabs (only documents are tabs).

**5. Trade-offs.**
- A note MOVES on the spine when you edit it: it hangs off the day you last wrote in it, so a March note edited today sits under today and March's heading loses it. The recency list already behaves this way, so retrieval does not get worse than today — but the journal quality v1 promised ('a note stays where it was written') is gone, and re-reading a month as written is now Search's or the month card's job, not the scroll's.
- The Notes lens cannot exist before the `edited` civil stamp is on the wire — an additive FFI field, optional in the decoder, owner-gated, failing-test-first, with the core's per-transaction stamps as its source. If the owner refuses the stamp, notes fall back to `created` and the recency surface is lost outright; the direction then fails its own retrieval test and should not be built.
- The deep past is a long scroll. Anything three months old is many flicks away; the month card behind the day heading is the jump, and Search is the other door. The spine adds nothing the recency list did not have here, but it adds day rules between things, which is more distance per flick.
- The ribbon is the bottom strip the owner removed on 2026-08-23; it costs ~114pt of foot at rest when the desk is non-empty (44 band + 4 gap + 66 bar), against 66 today. It retires on scroll, under the keyboard and for an empty desk, and its chips are label size cut at two words — but it is more chrome, it is a tab strip by any honest name, and if he keeps the ruling the spine gets the fallback (ribbon on documents only, 'Open · N' as the Lens card's first row), which makes the note you were in two taps from the spine instead of one.
- With no swipe-to-switch, moving between open documents is a tap on a neighbouring chip, always visible beside the active one; the Safari pill gesture is not offered. One fewer gesture, one fewer collision.
- Two standing rulings and one law are reversed and need the owner's word: 'top-left is ALWAYS the library door' (no door exists), 'tabs as they appeared when you clicked the numbered box' (2026-08-23), and the forward key's job (2026-08-23). And Today ceases to be a view in the card — the owner said keep the six; its name and contents survive but its door moves to the bar. 'Panel should not be full screen' becomes moot.
- A note made under any lens lands under TODAY (its edited day is now), never under the pinned day; a task or event made there lands on the pinned day (due). The spine no longer snaps when the editor slides away — you come back to the day you were on, the chip is on the ribbon, and the row is one Today-tap away — but a user who expects the note to appear under last Tuesday will not find it there.
- The Calendar lens expands only the pinned day as an hour timeline; every other day is event rows. Tap-an-empty-hour-to-create therefore works on one day at a time, and seeing two days' hour grids side by side (a thing the month pager never did either) is not possible.
- Empty stretches inside a populated range are still rules — one per run of empty days. A quiet fortnight in the middle of a busy quarter is one rule, but a sparse box with one thing a week is a column of rules with a row between each; only a fresh box (nothing beyond today) is exempt.
- Two bar words change together when you pick a lens (the Lens word and the + word). Decided: they change only on your own tap, never on scroll or on arrival, so the bar never changes under a still thumb — but a first-day user sees two words move for one action.
- Everything's compact 'Upcoming' list (only dated rows, no headings) is replaced by the sections below today; the same rows in the same order, with day rules added. A user who liked seven rows with no furniture gets seven rows and up to seven rules.
- Two surfaces still open differently on +: a note slides in as a document, a task rises as a card. The spine does not fix vagueness 9; it keeps the standing rulings (records are cards, tabs hold documents) and labels the key by kind, as now.
- The Tasks filter chips and the Inbox/Tidy state remain small remembered facets with no indicator beyond their own chips — narrowed from five hidden tokens to two visible chip rows, not eliminated.

**Wireframes.**

*First open of a fresh box — the spine at today, nothing else*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                    ▪▪▪  │
│                              │
│ TODAY  Thu 10 Sep            │
│ Work · Health · Money ·      │
│ Home · Family · Learning     │
│                              │
│                              │
│          ◌                   │
│    Nothing here yet          │
│  Anything you add lands      │
│  under today — in Work,      │
│  Health, Money, Home,        │
│  Family or Learning.         │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │  ‹    ◉    ◫    ⌕    +   │ │
│ │Back Today Every… Search Task│
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
Added after the critique: the fresh box is the FIRST thing the owner sees, and v1 drew it as a ruler. Now: the stream is exactly as long as what is on it, so a box with nothing beyond today is today's section alone — no past headings (the box has no past), no future rules (nothing hangs there), no ribbon (the desk is empty; nothing inert on screen). Today's heading carries the six areas as its count line (Furnish/LivArea names, zero counts printed as names, not zeros), and the body is the existing furnished EmptyHint (glyph + line + quieter line, no button — 2026-08-20; the bar's + is the one door). The + word is 'Task' (the bare spine inherits Today's + rule: a task due the pinned day). ‹ is dim: nothing has moved. Marker liv.surface.spine with lens trait 'everything'; `drive.sh spine fresh` asserts the heading text, the six names, and that no rule is rendered.

*Cold launch on a lived-in box — the spine at today, Everything lens*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                    ▪▪▪  │
│ ── Wed 9 Sep ───────────── 3 ─│
│ ▪ Sprint notes         Work  │
│ ○ Call plumber         Home  │
│ ~~2 done~~                   │
│                              │
│ TODAY  Thu 10 Sep            │
│ Work 2 · Home 1 · 3 unfiled  │
│ ▸ 3 late                     │
│ ▸ 5 undated                  │
│ 09:00 ▌Standup               │
│ 11:30 ▌Dentist        Health │
│ ○ Send invoice         Work  │
│ ▪ Kitchen plan               │
│ ✂ "chapter 4 of…"    Work? › │
│                              │
│ ── Fri 11 Sep ─────────── 1 ─│
│ 14:00 ▌Review                │
│ ── Sat 12 – Sun 13 ─────────│
│ ── Mon 14 Sep ─────────── 1 ─│
│                              │
│ [Kitchen pl…][Q3 memo]  +2 › │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │  ‹    ◉    ◫    ⌕    +   │ │
│ │Back Today Every… Search Task│
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
One column, past above, future below. 'TODAY Thu 10 Sep' is the sticky heading (pins at the top as you scroll into the day; tap → the month card). Two folds at today's head under every task-showing lens: '▸ 3 late' (Today's existing LATE predicate) and, new after the critique, '▸ 5 undated' — open tasks with no day, Things' Anytime as a fold; they never sink. Marks: ○ task (a place keeps the tick), ▌ event block (washed body, 3pt leading bar), ▪ note (under the day you last wrote in it — 'Sprint notes' was edited yesterday), ✂ unrouted capture with the clerk's 'Work?' chip (one tap files it; tap the row → the Route card). Kind colour allowed: the spine is a mixed list. Yesterday's done pile is struck. Empty days between things collapse to one rule. The ribbon holds two open notes (titles cut at two words) and a '+2 older' fold; it SCROLLS and a tap switches — no swipe. Bar: ‹ Back · Today (LivDayMark from Kit.swift, the same mark the month grid uses) · Lens (word = the lens on) · Search · + (word = the kind this lens makes). Bar and ribbon retire on scroll with the existing hysteresis; the heading never does. No top-left door. Marker liv.surface.spine; `drive.sh spine` asserts the heading text, both fold labels, and that the rows under the seven future headings equal Everything.Upcoming's set.

*A document open over the spine, ribbon at rest*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41              (⚌)  (•••) │
│                              │
│ Kitchen plan                 │
│                              │
│ Measure the north wall       │
│ before ordering the units.   │
│ Ask [[Plumber]] about the    │
│ sink trap.                   │
│ - [ ] call about the sink    │
│ - [x] measure                │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│                              │
│ ●Kitchen pl… [Q3 memo]  +2 › │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │  ‹    ◉    ◫    ⌕    +   │ │
│ │Back Today Notes Search Note│
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
The editor is unchanged (inline title, markdown, [[links]], caret where you left it). Top-right keeps the Properties key and the ••• (secondary verbs), by ruling; top-left is empty. The document is its own state (`Where.document(id)`), not Notes wearing a note. The ribbon shows the active chip filled and scrolled to the thumb end; the neighbour is always visible, so next/previous is one tap; long-press a chip → Close / Properties / Trash in the bottom card. ‹ (or a rightward swipe from the left edge) slides the document out to the spine exactly where it was — same lens, same day, same scroll, no snap, even if this note was just made from + while you were on last Tuesday. The Lens key still prints the lens the spine is under (here 'Notes'); tapping it drops the document and opens the Lens card over the spine. Under the keyboard both ribbon and bar retire (2026-08-02), as now. Foot at rest: 44 + 4 + 66 — the honest cost, drawn. Marker liv.surface.document; `drive.sh ribbon` asserts the filled chip's title, the chip count, and that the ribbon's frame sits above the bar's and leaves the screen with it.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- Status-bar scrim (LivTopScrim) on every surface; the content runs under the clock. Nothing else at the top of the spine — no door, no title bar, no hero.
- The pinned day heading on the spine (weekday and date, 'TODAY' word on today's, the area count line under it — area names alone on a fresh box): content that sticks, not chrome; it never retires; tap = the month card. It is the app's only 'where am I in time'. Under the Inbox lens it carries the 'N suggestions' chip; under Tasks the filter-chip row sits beneath it.
- On a document only: the Properties key (slider glyph, glass 44pt circle, top-right) and the ••• Note actions key beside it — both by standing ruling; top-left empty.
- The ribbon: a horizontally scrolling strip of open-document chips above the bar (label size 16, titles cut at two words, 44pt band, active chip filled, ordered by last-touched day, a '+N older' fold at the far end, a minimised record as the leading chip). Present on the spine and on documents; absent when the desk is empty and nothing is minimised; retires with the bar on scroll and under the keyboard. Foot at rest with a non-empty desk: 44 + 4 + 66 ≈ 114pt. Fallback if 2026-08-23 stands: on documents only.
- The bar: one Liquid Glass capsule, five keys with a word under each — ‹ Back · Today (LivDayMark) · Lens (word = the lens on: Everything / Notes / Inbox / Calendar / Tasks) · Search · + (word = the kind it makes here). Two words change, together, only on a Lens pick. The bar is the surface's own foot (rev 54): retires on scroll with the ribbon, retires under the keyboard, never retires for an empty desk, hidden entirely under a full-screen cover (Search, Camera).
- That is the whole persistent set: two elements on the spine (ribbon when non-empty, bar), four on a document (plus the two top-right circles). Cards (Lens, Record, Route, Properties, History, Workspace, Settings, Trash, New menu, ribbon menus, month) are visits, one LivMenu/livSheet recipe, from the edge their button is on — all from the bottom, except the ••• menu from the top.
- Deleted from the inventory: the library door (PanelMark), the library panel and its wash, the panel drag, the numbered Open key and LivGlyph.day, the Forward key, the full-screen tab switcher and Inactive shelf, the Today 7-day strip and hero, the Notes/Inbox/Everything heroes and slice pills, the minimised-record pill as a separate element (folded into the ribbon).

**Gestures.**
- Vertical scroll on the spine = move through time; the current day's heading pins at the top; bar and ribbon retire after 44pt down and return on scroll up or at the day's head (existing chrome hysteresis). When the scroll leaves the loaded occurrence window, `refreshWindow` re-points it (as the calendar does per month).
- Tap the pinned day heading = the month card (MonthGridView) rises to jump to any day; no panel drag exists to veto.
- Tap the bar's Today key = snap the spine to today under the current lens; when already at today, re-pin today's heading to the top (never a dead key).
- Tap the bar's Lens key (its word is the lens on) = the Lens card from the bottom; pick a row = swap the lens, keep the day; the Lens word and the + word change together, on that tap only.
- Tap a row = open: a note/file slides in from the right as a document; a task/event rises as the record card over the spine (Option C, unchanged); an unrouted capture row = the Route card (areas, then 'Not a note…').
- Tap the clerk's 'Work?' chip on an unrouted row = file it under that area in one tap (unchanged).
- Swipe a row left = trash, with the 5s Undo chip (existing; extended to every row on the spine, which gives Notes the swipe it never had).
- ‹ on the bar, or a rightward swipe from the left edge of a document = back: slides the document out to the spine where you left it; on the spine, undoes the last lens change, day jump or workspace switch.
- Ribbon: the strip SCROLLS horizontally; tap a chip = that document slides in; long-press a chip = bottom card with Close / Properties / Move to Trash; tap the '+N older' chip = the aged documents as a card with Close all. No swipe-to-switch — one axis, one meaning.
- Swipe a record card down = it minimises into the ribbon's leading slot as a kind-glyph chip (one at a time); tap it = the card returns; long-press = drop it.
- Tap + = make the lens's kind: a task due the pinned day (Everything, Tasks), an event on the pinned day (Calendar), a note (Notes, Inbox) — no menu; hold + 0.45s = the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text).
- Tap an empty hour on the PINNED day under the Calendar lens = a draft event block at the quarter hour, released → created and opened as a card (unchanged); other days under Calendar are rows and open as cards.
- Tap '▸ 3 late' / '▸ 5 undated' / '~~2 done~~' = unfold that pile inside the day section (Today's folds, now per day; late and undated only at today's head).
- Tap the Inbox lens heading's 'N suggestions' chip = the clerk's Tidy list as a card (accept on the leading circle, dismiss asks).

**Capture.** Capture never asks, and everything captured lands on a day you can see or one tap from it.

BAR + (tap): the lens decides the kind and the pinned day decides the date. Under Everything or Tasks: a task due the pinned day at the default hour (desk.contextDay is no longer set by views — it IS the spine's pinned day, one source) → stamped with the workspace cells → the record card rises over the spine with the caret in the name; when the card is swiped down the new row is under that day's heading, where you are. Under Calendar: an event on the pinned day → card, same. Under Notes or Inbox: box.createNote → stamp → the editor slides in with the caret in it and a chip appears on the ribbon; when you slide back the spine is exactly where you were — NO snap (fixed after the critique) — the chip is filled on the ribbon, and the note's row is under TODAY's heading (its edited day is now), which is where you are if you were at today and one Today-tap away if you were not. A note made this way is, as now, the capture the Inbox lens lists until it is filed. HOLD +: the existing New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text); File and Scan open the last item as a document, as now.

SHARE SHEET and liv://capture?text=: unchanged mechanics — spooled by the extension, drained at foreground through liv_capture_at, stamped, NOT opened. The instant after: the capture is a ✂ row under today's heading on the spine, with the clerk's "Work?" chip if it has a guess. You see it the moment you come back to the app, without visiting anything, because you live on today. Filing it is one tap on the chip or one tap on the row → the Route card (the six areas; "Not a note…" → Task / Event / Link). Older unrouted captures are under the day they arrived — as a "▸ 2 unrouted" fold at the head of that day's section under the bare spine, or as the whole stream under the Inbox lens (every day, only unrouted). The Inbox stops being a room you must remember to empty and becomes a pile on the day it arrived — the thesis's second test made visible.

SEARCH's Create row: box.capture(typed) → stamp → the document slides in (a chip on the ribbon) → the Search cover closes; under today on the spine afterwards.

CAMERA / SCAN: unchanged; Done opens the last shot as a document, its file row sits under today.

liv://capture (bare): a new note, as + Note.

The one rule every route obeys: a capture appears under the day it happened, is never opened unless you started it yourself, is filed from where it sits, and never moves the spine under you.

**Back, links and catches.** BACK is one key with one meaning: undo the last navigation, whatever it was. LivReturns survives with a new Place enum — `.spine(lens, day)` or `.document(id)` replacing `.state(Feature)` / `.document` — and a push-everywhere policy: a lens pick, a Today snap, a month-card jump, a document open (from a row, the ribbon, a search hit, a [[link]], a notification, a liv:// route), and a workspace switch all push `here` before they move. ‹ pops and lands: `.document(id)` slides that document in (re-minting its chip if it was closed); `.spine(lens, day)` slides any document out and restores the lens and scrolls to that day. Capped at 20, per workspace like the desk. The forward stack (`forwards`, `goForward`, the forward self-checks in Navigate.swift) and the › key are deleted — a stream has no forward, and a key that is dim most of the time is the reference's defect, not a feature. ‹ is dim only on a cold launch before anything has moved. The dead-‹ case of the current model (opened off the Notes list, ‹ lit, nothing changes) cannot occur: the place under a document is always the spine at a day, which is visibly different from the document.

Rightward edge swipe on a document = the same pop (the phone's grammar, and the gesture the retired panel drag used to own).

liv:// ROUTES (Routes.swift, remapped): liv://today → the spine at today under the current lens (pushes); liv://everything|notes|inbox|calendar|tasks → that lens at the current day (pushes); liv://entity/<id> → a document slides in (chip on the ribbon) or a record card rises over the spine, after box.refresh if unknown; liv://capture and liv://capture/photo as above. An unknown host does nothing. A cold-launch route is parked until the spine's closures exist, as now. Every arrival still runs surfaceCleanup (Lens card, menus, properties/history cards, search/camera/settings/trash/workspace closed) so a link never lands behind something.

NOTIFICATIONS: a tap → desk.open(id) → a card for a record, a document for a note, over the spine at wherever it was; ‹ returns to the day you were on.

SHARE-SHEET CATCH: never navigates. It lands as a row under today; the next foreground shows it there.

A [[link]] tap inside a document: pushes `.document(from)` and slides the target in; the ribbon gains a chip; ‹ returns to the source note. A reference chip on the properties card: the same.

Records opened as cards push nothing (they close nothing and cover where you stand, as now); minimising to the ribbon pushes nothing.

WORKSPACE SWITCH: pushes `.spine(lens, day)` of the outgoing workspace, swaps the ribbon to the incoming desk, lands on today under Everything. ‹ takes you back to the previous workspace's day — the one navigation the current app makes irreversible.

**Survives in code.** Plane.swift (481) DeskPlanes / DeskPlane / DeskTab, one desk per workspace, persistence keys and the fold of every older key, dropRecordsAndStrangers — unchanged; DeskTab.lastUsed (already a civil stamp) becomes the ribbon's sort key and age.; Tabs.swift (712) LivTabs (the inactive predicate, `days`, `isInactive`, `age`) — kept as the ribbon's fold rule; TabSwitcher, InactiveTabs and TabCard around it go.; Record.swift (375) RecordCard and RecordBody; Chrome.swift openAsCard / minimiseRecord / restoreRecord / dropMinimised, TabShape.of — unchanged; MinimisedRecordPill (Record.swift:97) is replaced by the ribbon's leading chip.; Chrome.swift (1090) DeskModel as the one owner: open(id, as:), openDocument (minus `state = .notes`), focus, close, closeInactive, newTab/createSomething/adoptCapture, requestFocus/consumeFocus, surfaceCleanup, chromeAway and its hysteresis (scrolled(to:), chromeHomeAgain), the `cards` counter, the -desk.boot flags (remapped), the sweep on first snapshot; `state: Feature` and `openDoc`'s `.notes` guard are replaced by `Where`.; Navigate.swift (396) Feature and Feature.inOrder (as lens order and lens glyphs), Feature.makes (the + word), LivReturns (with the new Place enum and push-everywhere; its forward leg deleted), livHidesChrome.; Bar.swift (160) metrics and mechanics (LivBar in Theme.swift, glass, `key`/`slot`, words under keys, retire rules, disabledInk); Back kept, Forward and Open deleted, Today and Lens added, + re-pointed.; Theme.swift (every size and colour; LivBar, LivRow, LivChip, LivMotion.nav), Glyph.swift (the area glyphs; PanelMark and LivGlyph.day deleted), Kit.swift (LivDayMark reused for the Today key; LivName; EmptyHint and livAreaSentence for the furnished fresh box; StatusRing), Rows.swift (LivListRow — every spine row; livNewFact), Menu.swift (LivMenu, livSheet(from:), LivEdgeSheetHost) — the Lens card and every ribbon menu are built from these.; Today.swift (906) agenda assembly, the LATE predicate and fold, the Done fold, the area count line, the captured-today logic — become the per-day section builder for the spine, gaining the undated fold; TodayDateStrip and the hero go.; Calendar.swift (1811) CalendarView's hour timeline and draft-block create — the pinned day under the Calendar lens; Month.swift MonthGridView — the day-heading jump card; MonthPagerView goes.; Inbox.swift (762) the Route card, kindMenu, the 'Work?' chip, the Tidy proposal rows — the Inbox lens rows and the suggestions card.; Tasks.swift (620) TasksFilterChip row and groups, TasksPosition token (Positions.swift) — the Tasks lens facet row; Everything.swift (237) `rows` lens/sort logic and `trailing` — the section builder's row source; EverythingLens slices deleted.; Search.swift wholesale (cover, facets as chips, find-or-create, picker mode); Detail.swift / Links.swift / History.swift cards; WorkspaceSwitch.swift and Workspace.swift (lens, filters, stamp); Settings.swift; Trash.swift; Camera/Scan; Files.swift FileBody; Furnish.swift; Editor / EditorText / EditorStyle untouched.; Routes.swift and Notify.swift (remapped targets, same parking on cold launch); Catch.swift, Share.swift and the ShareExtension spool — unchanged, still never open what they catch.; Box.swift as the one FFI caller: `liv_snapshot` / `liv_snapshot_window_at` exactly as now (refresh coalescing, `refreshWindow` for the occurrence window); the decoder gains one OPTIONAL field, `edited`, once the additive FFI change exists; EditorCost.swift's pattern for the shell-side section-builder shape check.; Surface.swift markers and drive.sh: liv.surface.document and the overlay markers stay; liv.surface.spine (with a lens trait) replaces the six view markers (Feature.rawValue ×5 + notes); 'tour' becomes 'lens', and 'spine', 'spine fresh' and 'ribbon' are added, each broken on purpose before trusted.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift (461: LibraryPanel, its rows, foot, wash, offset) and PanelDrag.swift (280); the library door PanelMark (Glyph.swift:590); deskShift / panelProgress / panelOut / setLibrary and the travel maths in Chrome.swift; the desk mask, square corners and wash in Desk.swift.; Tabs.swift TabSwitcher (:64), InactiveTabs (:337), TabCard (:554), the fullScreenCover in App.swift:267, the dashed New-note card, the 'N open' / 'Nothing open' footer; the bar's Open key (Bar.swift:98-111) and LivGlyph.day (Glyph.swift:219); the a11y 'N documents open'.; The Forward key (Bar.swift:54), LivReturns' `forwards` / `next` / `forwardCount`, goForward (Chrome.swift:775) and the forward self-checks (Navigate.swift:365-385).; Notes.swift NotesList (the recency-ordered root, 139 lines — its ORDER survives as the Notes lens's order once `edited` is on the wire); Everything.swift as a screen (EverythingLens slices All/Upcoming/Unfiled, its hero); the Today hero and TodayDateStrip; the Inbox hero and the Route|Tidy lens row (Route is the lens; Tidy is a chip); Calendar.swift MonthPagerView as a standalone view (MonthGridView stays as the card).; `state: Feature` as the one state (Chrome.swift:149), `openDoc` guarded on `.notes` (:183), go(feature) (:698), goToRoot (:731), showList (:746), land(.state) (:782) — replaced by `Where = .spine(lens, day) | .document(id)`; the three-branch root-of-view special case; adopt(workspace:) landing on .notes; DeskHost's three-way `if` (Desk.swift:44-66) becomes a two-way one on `Where`.; Positions.swift tokens for Today (TodayPosition: day/doneExpanded/lateOpen), Calendar (CalendarPosition: month/day) and Everything (EverythingLens) — the spine's one pinned day and per-day folds replace them; TasksPosition and InboxLens survive only as lens facets.; MinimisedRecordPill (Record.swift:97; mounted in Desk.swift:393 and App.swift:282) — folded into the ribbon.; desk.contextDay as something views SET (Today.swift:201-203, Calendar) — it becomes the spine's pinned day, derived; desk.pagerZone and the panel-drag veto (Chrome.swift:362, PanelDrag.swift:124-188, Calendar.swift:1093-1095, Month.swift).; The v1 claim that the spine is fed by `liv_snapshot_window_at` in ±7-day windows — withdrawn; that verb widens only the recurrence occurrence window, and `dated` is the full set the shell already buckets by day.; Three rulings, on the owner's word: 'top-left is always the library door', 'tabs as they appeared when you clicked the numbered box' (2026-08-23), and the › key's job (2026-08-23). The 2026-08-17 'each state a root you go to' reading becomes 'each lens a way of seeing the one root'; Today's door moves from the card to the bar.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- 1 'Notes is two things wearing one name' — RESOLVED. A document has its own state (`Where.document`); Notes is only a lens; nothing hijacks it, and there is no lit row to tap twice.
- 2 'The list from the panel, the documents from the bar' — RESOLVED. One door to the lenses (the bar's Lens key) and the desk is always visible as the ribbon on the same edge; the list and the grid no longer compete because neither exists as a surface. (Under the 2026-08-23 fallback the desk is the Lens card's first row — still the same door as the lenses.)
- 3 'Open N counts an invisible thing' — RESOLVED. There is no count; the ribbon shows the documents themselves, the minimised record beside them, and age as distance.
- 4 'The word Notes means three surfaces; the + word changes' — RESOLVED for Notes (one meaning: a lens). The + word still changes per lens by standing ruling, and now the Lens word changes with it — two honest words, changing together only on your own tap.
- 5 'Three routes to a note, three back behaviours' — RESOLVED. Every navigation pushes; `here` under a document is always `.spine(lens, day)`, so ‹ from any route lands visibly somewhere else.
- 6 'Back is a document-history key wearing a browser glyph' — RESOLVED. ‹ undoes the last navigation of any kind; › is deleted rather than left dim.
- 7 'goToRoot is a patch with two branches' — RESOLVED by deletion. No view has a root; the Today key is the one 'go home', means the same thing under every lens, and re-pins the heading when already home.
- 8 'Positions and documents are two invisible kinds of remembered thing' — MOSTLY RESOLVED. Documents are visible on the ribbon; the app's one position is the pinned day, visible in the heading. The Tasks filter chips and the Inbox/Tidy state remain small remembered facets with no indicator beyond their own chips — narrowed from five hidden tokens to two visible chip rows, not eliminated.
- 9 '+ stays or moves depending on the kind' — NOT RESOLVED, deliberately. A note still slides in as a document and a record still rises as a card (rulings 2026-08-06/07/08/16). The key says the kind, and the kind decides. What is fixed: neither outcome moves the spine under you any more.
- 10 'The switcher is a full-screen mode and the only way back to a note' — RESOLVED. The ribbon is persistent and one tap; no mode, no Done. (Two taps under the fallback.)
- 11 'The panel's fill answers which state while the screen answers which surface' — RESOLVED. The panel is gone; the bar's Lens word and the day heading say where you are on the spine, the filled ribbon chip says which document; a record card covers without pretending.
- 12 'Inbox is a queue, Notes a shelf, presented as peers' — RESOLVED in structure. The six flat rooms are gone; unrouted captures sit on the day they arrived with a Route chip, and the Inbox lens is 'only the unrouted'. The queue is visible as a per-day pile rather than a room you must remember. The Inbox's lens exemption survives as a property of unrouted rows.
- THE TRIANGLE — resolved at its cause: the desk has a state of its own, the views are lenses over one surface rather than six render states, and the panel — the vertex that only knew Feature — no longer exists to disagree with the screen.

**Changed after the judges.**
- Undated open tasks: added a '▸ N undated' fold at today's head under every lens that shows tasks (Everything and Tasks), beside the LATE fold — Things' Anytime as a fold, not a room. All three judges called the sinking to-do a product failure, and furnishing-study §4.8 (organisation is reminding as much as finding) agrees.
- The hang-where rule now uses an `edited` day for notes and files ('the day you last wrote in it'), which reproduces the recency list on the time axis, instead of `created` (which buried an edited-yesterday note under its birth month). The `edited` civil stamp — additive FFI, optional in the decoder, owner-gated, failing-test-first — is now a stated PREREQUISITE of the Notes lens, not an 'if daily use demands it'. The journal reading ('a note stays where written') was given up in exchange; listed under trade-offs.
- The feed claim was wrong and is withdrawn: `liv_snapshot_window_at` only widens the recurrence occurrence window; `dated` is the full set the shell already buckets by day (ffi/liv.h). The spine is the one existing snapshot, sectioned in the shell, with a shell-side shape self-check (EditorCost.swift's pattern) and the occurrence window re-pointed on scroll as the calendar already does. No new FFI, no prefetch, no paging; the mount strategy (LazyVStack of day sections) is stated.
- Ribbon collision fixed: the strip scrolls, a tap switches, and there is no swipe-to-switch. The active chip is scrolled to the thumb end so its neighbour is always one tap away. Titles are cut at two words so nine chips are nine words.
- The ribbon's honest cost is now drawn and admitted: ~114pt of foot at rest with a non-empty desk; it resembles Safari's/Arc's tab strip more than Obsidian's grid; and it is the element the owner removed on 2026-08-23. It is put to him as THE one question up front, with a fallback that keeps the spine if he keeps his ruling (ribbon on documents only; 'Open · N' as the Lens card's first row).
- + Note while scrolled into the past no longer snaps the spine: the editor slides back to the day and scroll you were on, the chip is on the ribbon, and the row is under today (its edited day), one Today-tap away. The 'capture never moves the spine under you' rule is added to the capture flow.
- A fresh-box wireframe was added as the FIRST screen: the stream is exactly as long as what is on it, so a box with nothing beyond today draws today's furnished section (six area names in the heading, the existing EmptyHint) and no rules — the ruler is gone. The Lens card wireframe was dropped to keep three screens; its content is described in views_hierarchy and unchanged from v1 except the 'Open · N' fallback row.
- The Calendar lens expands only the pinned day as an hour timeline; every other day is event rows. A week under Calendar is now a short scroll; tap-an-hour works on the pinned day only (trade-offs).
- Today key is no longer ever a no-op: at today it re-pins today's heading (the platform's re-tap idiom). The two moving bar words are decided: the Lens word and the + word change together, only on a Lens pick, never on scroll or arrival.
- Retrieval regressions answered with a checkable claim: the sections below today contain exactly Everything.Upcoming's rows in its order (asserted by `drive.sh spine`); undated tasks are at today's head; month browsing is the month card from the heading. A device-local retrieval-rate measure is raised as a question, not proposed.
- A cheap slice is named in two parts: the invisible `Where` change (three files, provable with existing `goto notes` and `desk` checks, not mockup-gated) and Everything re-rendered as day sections under today's chrome (proves the section builder, folds and heading; proves nothing about the ribbon, card or bar).
- The 'Liv has no daily-note file' claim was corrected: the core has `liv_open_daily_note_at`, which the shell never calls and this direction never calls — the day stays a section, not a note.
- survives_in_code / thrown_away re-grounded against the tree: LivDayMark is in Kit.swift (not Glyph.swift); MinimisedRecordPill is Record.swift:97 mounted from Desk.swift:393 and App.swift:282; the forward leg is `forwards`/`goForward` with self-checks at Navigate.swift:365-385; Month's card is MonthGridView; the five view markers are Feature.rawValue plus `notes` and `document`; the drive.sh subcommand set (23) is cited by name; line counts are the real ones.
- Today ceasing to be a card row is now conceded as a change to one of the six the owner said to keep, rather than argued away as 'six names survive'.

**Conceded.**
- It is not buildable as a one-week slice, and 'distinctive AND buildable' cannot rise above what judge 3 gave it. Six view bodies become one section builder plus per-lens rendering, three Positions tokens go, the state model changes, the bar changes three keys, the panel and the grid go — 30+ of 47 files — on top of revs 54–61 that are unverified on a device. The two named slices prove the state model and the section builder; they do not prove the direction.
- The ribbon is a bottom tab strip of open documents, whatever its sort order, and the owner removed that on 2026-08-23. The direction cannot make it not one; it can only ask, and offer a fallback that costs one extra tap back to an open note from the spine.
- Today stops being a row among six and becomes a bar key. Its name and its contents survive, but the owner said keep the six, and this changes one of them. He has to re-rule it; the direction does not work with Today as a lens (a lens that subtracts 'not today' from a stream whose home is today is a no-op).
- The direction depends on an FFI addition (`edited`) that the settled zone must approve, failing-test-first, with a snapshot-path cost test. Until it exists the Notes lens is unbuildable and the bare spine hangs notes off `created`, which is a retrieval regression the direction knowingly ships in its first slice if built ahead of the stamp.
- Foot chrome grows from 66 to ~114pt at rest when the desk is non-empty. Retiring on scroll and under the keyboard is the whole mitigation; it is still more than any other direction asks the thumb zone to hold, and the 'tiny and subtle' correction may land on the chips regardless of the two-word cut.
- A note moves when edited. The journal quality — re-reading a month as it was written — is gone; only Search and the month card read the past by birth date, and nothing reads it in creation order.
- Vagueness 9 stays open by choice: a note fills the screen and a task is a card, and the + key says the kind rather than the consequence.
- Sparse boxes still show rules between things (one per run of empty days). Only a fresh box is exempt; a box with one thing a week is a ruler with a row on each rung.
- The Inbox lens and the Tasks chips remain remembered facets with no indicator beyond the chips themselves — narrowed, not eliminated.
- Nothing here is measured on a device: not the section builder's cost on a real box, not the ribbon at nine chips, not the sticky heading under the existing chrome hysteresis. Every claim about feel is a drawing until `drive.sh spine` exists and has been broken on purpose.

**Open questions.**
- THE ONE QUESTION FIRST: does the 2026-08-23 ruling ('Not a tab "bar", remove it') stand against a titled, day-ordered ribbon of open documents at the foot? Five of eight directions depend on the same answer; it is one sentence from the owner, not a mockup. If it stands: ribbon on documents only, 'Open · N' as the Lens card's first row, and the spine still holds.
- Three further reversals need his word before any build: no top-left library door (the standing law), no numbered box and grid (2026-08-23), no Forward (2026-08-23) — plus Today's door moving from the six-row card to the bar, which changes one of the six he said to keep.
- The `edited` civil stamp: an optional per-row field on the snapshot, sourced from the core's per-transaction stamps (the History card already prints them), purely additive, failing-test-first, with a cost test on the snapshot path (rule 2). The Notes lens is gated on it. Does the owner want it — and does he want a note to MOVE to the day it was edited (recency, proposed) or stay on the day it was born (journal, refused after the critique)?
- Undated open tasks at today's head under every task-showing lens is now the design (Things' Anytime as a fold). Should done-undated tasks hang off the day they were ticked (edited day, proposed) or vanish into the done pile of today?
- The Calendar lens: only the pinned day expanded as an hour timeline, the rest as rows (proposed after the critique). Is a two-day expansion (pinned + next) worth its scroll?
- Ribbon chip size and count: label size on a 44pt band, titles cut at two words, horizontally scrolling — the first artboard is nine chips open, so the owner's 'tiny and subtle' correction is tried before build, not after. Alternative if it fails: one row of glyph-only chips with the active title spelled out.
- The Lens card carries the workspace foot (the owner's Obsidian screenshot, twice requested). Keep it on the card, or give the workspace a chip in the day heading? Proposed: keep, on the card.
- Does the app remember the lens across launches, or always open on Everything at today? Proposed: always Everything at today — home is one thing.
- Whether the spine keeps kind colour marks (a mixed list by rule → yes, small marks) while the Notes lens is monochrome — the whole app becomes a mixed list and the colour budget should be re-measured against the 0.58% reference.
- Retrieval rate is the metric the furnishing study says outranks everything, and nothing in the app measures it. A device-local count of 'opened something older than three days' per session would be a feature the owner has not asked for; it is raised here as a question, not proposed as work.
- Order of work if he says yes: (0) build.sh + drive.sh on revs 54–61 first — everything stacks on unverified code; (1) the invisible `Where` change (three files, existing `goto notes` and `desk` checks); (2) the mockup: four artboards (fresh box; spine at today; a document with nine chips; the Lens card) on the design canvas; (3) his word; (4) `drive.sh spine`, `spine fresh`, `lens`, `ribbon` written and broken on purpose; (5) Everything-as-day-sections under today's chrome; (6) the `edited` stamp; (7) the rest. Nothing here is verified on a device.

### 4. Rooms and a Pocket
