*Six rooms off one hallway, each keeping its own furniture; the notes you are working on ride in a pocket at the bottom sill — glyphs for the ones you carry, a name for the one you hold up — raised over whichever room you stand in and dropped back; no drawer, no tab grid, no numbered box, no back key.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE HOUSE. The app is a house with one HALLWAY and six ROOMS (Today, Notes, Inbox, Calendar, Tasks, Everything — `Feature.inOrder`, unchanged). You are always in exactly one place: the hall, or a room. The hall is a full screen you WALK to: the room slides out to the right, the hall slides in from the left, one lateral easing (`LivMotion.nav`, 0.22s); nothing is washed, pushed or peeked. Its tiles are the six rooms, one column, each a glyph, a name and one live line that is the room's current state ('4 due · 1 late', 'Kitchen plan · 2 min ago', '3 to route', '14:00 Dentist', '12 open · 1 late', '212 · 67 unfiled'). The hall's title is the workspace ('Home ⌄'): a workspace is a house, and switching houses lands you in the new one's hall. Saved filters are a chip row under the tiles; Trash and Settings are two quiet rows at the hall's foot. Cold launch lands in the Today room (the 2026-08-18 ruling stands). A tile always lands on the room's FURNITURE — its remembered position (`spots`: Calendar's month/day, Today's day, Tasks' chips, Everything's slice, Inbox's lens, Notes' shelf). A room never draws a document.\n\nTHE POCKET. Open documents are things you CARRY. They live in a pocket: a recessed well in the bottom sill, visible in the hall and in every room, holding one chip per carried document in stable insertion order. Chips are GLYPH-ONLY (36pt, the kind's LivGlyph) except one: the document you last held up wears its title at label size (16) beside its glyph, filled with LivTheme.selection while it is raised, outlined while it is dropped. So the well always answers two questions at a glance — how many you carry (the glyphs) and which one is yours right now (the named one) — and never draws a 9-character caption. Tap a shelf row, a search hit, a [[link]], a notification, a liv://entity link, or make a note with + and the note goes INTO the pocket and RISES over the room you are standing in (vertical motion, from the sill). The room is still where you stand; the note is what you hold up. Tap the filled chip, or pull the note down from its top band, and it DROPS back; the room is exactly as you left it. Tap another chip and the two swap in one motion. Long-press the well (0.45s, the + hold's number) and the pocket FANS OUT into a half-height list from the bottom (LivMenu form): every carried document with a ✕, an 'Inactive' section for chips untouched 21 days (LivTabs predicate, unchanged), 'Empty pocket' at the foot. The pocket is per workspace (DeskPlanes, unchanged) and persists across launches.\n\nTHE HOLD. A minimised record (a task or event card swiped down) is NOT a document and does not become a chip: it parks as the record's own round MARK at the well's leading edge, outside the chip run and separated from it by a hairline — one at a time, never persisted, never counted ('Pocket, 3 documents' excludes it), never listed in the pocket card. Tap the mark and the card returns; ✕ in the card or Trash drops it. Same idiom (you are holding it), different shape (a circle among capsules), so the 'a record is never a tab' rulings (2026-08-07/08/16) stay visibly true.\n\nTHE SILL. There is one persistent strip of chrome in the whole app, at the bottom, in the thumb zone, in this order: [Search] [ pocket well ] [Hall] [+ kind]. Hall sits beside + because walking is the most frequent act after making and a right thumb reaches the trailing half without a regrip; Search is the leftmost key because it is a full cover you enter deliberately. A rightward drag ANYWHERE on a room (the existing PanelPanRecognizer, 'dragged from anywhere' per 2026-08-08, dx > 2|dy| latch, the Calendar pager's veto intact) also walks to the hall, so the hall is one thumb-drag away without any key. No top-left door anywhere. No back, no forward, no numbered box. Rooms have no top chrome but their own title; a raised document has the two keys it already has top-right (Properties, •••). The sill is the FLOOR of the house — hoisted to RootView above every surface and below every card; it retires under the keyboard and on a downward scroll as today, and never for an empty pocket (its + is the way out of one).\n\nWHAT + MAKES. The kind under + is the kind of the SURFACE YOU SEE, never of something hidden: a room's kind in a room (Feature.makes, unchanged — Task in Today/Tasks, Event in Calendar, Note elsewhere), 'Note' in the hall, and 'Note' whenever a document is raised, whatever room is beneath. A note made while a note is up enters the pocket after it and rises in its place. This is the one rule that removes the mode error the judges named: the word under + is always about what is on screen.\n\nWHY THIS SHAPE. The diagnosis's fault is that the desk has no state of its own and is drawn by borrowing Notes. Here the desk has a state you can SEE — the pocket — and a verb of its own — raise/drop — orthogonal to the room. `openDoc` stops meaning 'active tab AND state == .notes' and means 'the active chip, while raised'. Notes stops being three things: it is a room whose furniture is a shelf of all notes. The hall stops being a menu that highlights a state: you are either in it or not. And 'get me back' has one answer for documents (the pocket, always at your thumb) and one for tools (the room remembers its furniture), and both are visible.\n\nTHE ONE QUESTION FIRST. On 2026-08-23 the owner ruled a browser's bar with ‹ › and a numbered box as the only door to the tabs, and removed a tab strip ('Not a tab \"bar\", remove it. We're on the phone, not desktop'). This direction replaces the box, the grid and ‹ › with a visible pocket at the sill. It is not the strip he removed — that was a row of titled tabs in the document's header, the desktop idiom; this is one named thing and a few glyphs in a tray among the keys, Safari's pill with neighbours — but it IS a visible row of what is open at the bottom of the screen, and the proposal stands or falls on his reading. Put it to him as one question with the three-chip and eight-chip mockups side by side and the ruling beside them, before anything else in this direction is drawn further.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS = THE POCKET. The one app-wide desk (Plane.swift DeskPlanes: tabs, activeTabId, per-workspace persistence, the 21-day inactive predicate in Tabs.swift LivTabs) survives verbatim; only its FACE changes. A tab is a chip in the well at the bottom sill. Chip form, after the critique: every chip is its kind's glyph alone in a 36pt glass capsule; the ACTIVE tab (the one last raised) alone carries its title at label size (LivType 16, not caption) beside the glyph, truncated at ~12 characters in ~120pt — filled with LivTheme.selection while raised, outlined while dropped. Stable insertion order (a pocket does not rearrange itself); the well scrolls horizontally, always scrolled so the active chip is in view; the last visible chip is clipped by the well's edge, which is the scroll cue (no fade — 2026-09-05). With three documents the whole pocket is visible: two glyphs and one name. With eight, the well shows the name and two or three glyphs and clips the rest; a11y 'Pocket, 8 documents'. Both states are drawn below for the owner to judge against 'too small' ×3 and against the 2026-08-23 strip.\n\nThe well is the switcher: one tap on a glyph swaps documents without leaving the room, so the full-screen grid (Tabs.swift TabSwitcher, 712 lines) and its Inactive sub-mode die. What remains of the grid's job — closing, inactive, empty — is a half-height card raised by LONG-PRESSING the well (0.45s): rows are LivListRow (title; kind · last used in the second voice), ✕ 'Drop' per row, an 'Inactive' section (same threshold, '24d' in the second voice), 'Empty pocket' as the last row (asks nothing, no undo — a chip is device state, as ruled 2026-08-10). Never full-screen; it hugs content like every other card. Gesture rule for the well, one and only one: horizontal scroll moves chips, tap switches, long-press fans out. There is NO upward pull and NO upward flick on the well — the earlier draft's two vertical gestures on a horizontal scroller were the collision class the second judge named, and they are gone. Closing is the card's ✕ only.\n\nA raised document is a LAYER, not a state: it covers the room from under the status scrim to the sill, has a grabber band at its top, Properties and ••• top-right, the title scrolling with the body (unchanged). Pulling it down (or tapping its filled chip) drops it; the room is underneath, untouched. When the chrome has retired on scroll (the sill and the top keys away), the drop affordance is the same as every sheet's: overscroll past the top of the document pulls it down, and any upward scroll brings the sill back with the filled chip in it. It is a surface for drive.sh (liv.surface.document) because the room beneath is off-screen.\n\nTHE NOTE LIST = THE SHELF IN THE NOTES ROOM. NotesList (Notes.swift: every non-trashed, non-archived, non-record row the lens admits, ordered by log recency) is the Notes room's furniture, unchanged in content. It is what the Notes tile ALWAYS lands on — there is no goToRoot special case because a room never draws a document. A row tap = pick the note up: chip in, note up. Notes on the shelf are not 'open'; notes in the pocket are not 'the list'. The two surfaces stop competing because they answer different questions in different places: 'where are my notes?' — the shelf, in a room; 'what am I holding?' — the pocket, at the sill. The shelf never grows a switcher; the pocket never lists what is not carried.\n\nTHE HOLD IS NOT A TAB. The minimised record card (MinimisedRecordPill's rules: one at a time, tap restores, ✕ drops, not persisted) parks at the well's leading edge as the record's round mark, walled off from the chips by a hairline, absent from the count and from the pocket card. It is the pill relocated into the sill, not a record admitted to the desk.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** Depth is exactly two, plus one layer: HALL → ROOM, with a DOCUMENT raised over either (you can raise a note in the hall too; dropping it leaves you in the hall). No room is above another; no room is inside another; the hall is beside all of them, and walking is lateral in both directions. This is the six-equal-roots ruling (2026-08-17) taken literally: a room is a place you walk to, not a state you set.\n\nWAYS BETWEEN ROOMS, stated as one rule so there is no 'sometimes': a KEY walks to the hall (the Hall key, or a rightward drag anywhere); a LINK that names a room by its word walks straight to it (Today's footer 'Route' → Inbox is the only one in code today; a lensed room's LensChip and the Inbox tile's live line are not links). Nothing else moves you between rooms. Cost, measured honestly: Today → Calendar is Hall, then the Calendar tile — two taps, two 0.22s slides (~440ms), and one screen between them that is not what you were working on; today it is one drag with the room in view, then one tap. Hold the Hall key (0.45s) and the six rooms rise as a bottom card (LivMenu, the six glyphs and names) so a frequent trip is hold + tap with no hall drawn — the same tap/hold pair the + already has. That hold-menu is a second door to the rooms and is flagged as such under rule 4; it is the price of a hall that is a screen and not a drawer.\n\nWhat the hall says about each room (the live line) is the ONE thing that distinguishes their natures without adding structure: Today — the date and the day's count ('Wed 10 Sep · 4 due · 1 late'); Notes — the most recent note and when ('Kitchen plan · 2 min ago'); Inbox — the queue ('3 to route', or 'Nothing to route'); Calendar — the next thing ('14:00 Dentist', or 'Free until Friday'); Tasks — the pile ('12 open · 1 late'); Everything — the house's size and the unsorted pile ('212 · 67 unfiled', where the panel foot's count line moves). Every line comes from data the app already walks (ViewCounts, Today's late pile, the calendar window, the unfiled count) and every line changes, so it earns its ink under the 2026-09-02 rule. All six lines are computed in ONE pass over the snapshot (ViewCounts extended, re-homed in Hall.swift) and a self-check asserts the pass count does not grow with the box (rule 2, the EditorCost.swift precedent). Room contents are untouched: Today's strip and piles, Inbox's Route|Tidy and the route card, Calendar's timeline with the month card, Tasks' chips and groups, Everything's slices, the Notes shelf. Today and Inbox still ignore the workspace lens; the others wear it, and a lensed room wears the active filter as the existing LensChip beside its title (tap → drop the lens).\n\nTHE AREAS. The hall's tiles are the six tools, not the six areas; the areas stay the furniture INSIDE the rooms — Today's area count under its date, Route's six-area card, the 'unfiled' half of the Everything line, the empty states that name them. This direction does not make an area a place you stand in; that is `areas`' answer and not this one's.\n\nWORKSPACES AND FILTERS. The hall's title is the workspace name with a chevron; tapping it raises the existing WorkspaceSwitcher card from the bottom (All, each workspace, New workspace…). Choosing one swaps the house: the pocket (DeskPlanes keyed per workspace), the room furniture (spots), the lens — and lands in the new hall, not in Notes. Saved filters are chips under the tiles (SavedViewRow's data as chips, lit when active; '+ New filter' last, opening the same card in filter-form mode); a filter narrows every room but Inbox and Today, as now. Trash and Settings are two rows at the hall's foot, the only rows that are not rooms.\n\nRECORDS. A task or event never enters a room's furniture as a page and never enters the pocket as a document: tapping one raises the RecordCard (half-height, medium/large, edits write immediately) over whatever is in front — a room, the hall, or a raised note. Swipe it down and it parks as the round hold at the well's leading edge; tap it to bring the card back; ✕ or Trash in the card drops it. One at a time (the 2026-08-08 rule), not persisted, never counted as a document, never in the pocket card.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against the reference reader's list, item by item:\n\n1. THE BOTTOM CAPSULE (‹ › 🔍 + [n], measured off Obsidian's clip). Gone. The sill has no back, no forward and no numbered box; it has a magnifier, a well, a doorway and a plus. Three of Obsidian's five keys are deleted and the fourth is replaced by a thing Obsidian has no analogue for. Glass and the word under each key stay (Safari's material, Todoist's form) — neither is Obsidian's.\n\n2. THE NUMBERED BOX (LivGlyph.day(Int), Glyph.swift:219 — Obsidian's daily-note key reshaped). Deleted with the glyph. Nothing in the app is a rounded outline with a number in it.\n\n3. THE TAB SWITCHER, 'verbatim in structure' (full-screen two-column card grid, X per card, plus card, 'N tabs', Done). Deleted. The pocket card is a half-height list raised from a tray — Todoist's card, not Obsidian's grid — and most switches never open it at all.\n\n4. THE LEFT DRAWER = THE LIBRARY, THE TOP-LEFT SIDEBAR TOGGLE, THE EDGE-SWIPE DRAWER, THE PANEL FOOT (vault name + count + gear). All four deleted. There is no drawer. The hall is a screen you walk into; the workspace is its TITLE, not its foot; the gear is a row, not a circle beside the name. The single most Obsidian pixel on screen — the top-left toggle (PanelMark) — is gone, and with it the whole 'three drawers around a note' geometry: rooms have no top-left control at all.\n\n5. THE RECENTS LIST AND THE TAB GRID AS TWO ANSWERS TO 'GET ME BACK'. Resolved by making them different objects in different places: the shelf is furniture in a room; the pocket is what you carry. One is deleted (the grid), the other stops pretending to be a switcher.\n\n6. BACK/FORWARD OVER A HISTORY STACK ('the most Obsidian thing in the app', kinds-are-peers.md:51). Deleted, LivReturns with it. The way back is spatial — drop the note, walk to the hall — and visible, which a stack never is. This is a reversal of 2026-08-23's ‹ › and is listed as one for the owner's word.\n\n7. ONE DESK OF TABS PER VAULT. Admitted: the storage model is structurally Obsidian's tabs-per-vault and it stays, because it is right. What changes is that the desk is EXPRESSED as carried things at the sill — one named, the rest glyphs — rather than a hidden plane behind a number. Obsidian never shows you your tabs while you read.\n\n8. THE TOP-LEFT TOGGLE + TOP-RIGHT ELLIPSIS HEADER. Half deleted: no left key on any surface; the ••• and the Properties key survive on a raised document only (the 2026-09-07 ruling put Properties there; the ••• holds secondary verbs by the 2026-08-02 ruling). A room has no header keys — its title is its only top furniture.\n\n9. LANDING ON NOTES AFTER A WORKSPACE SWITCH. Gone: a switch lands in the new hall. The app has one home and it is not a document.\n\n10. CHROME THAT HIDES ON SCROLL. Kept. It is Safari's and Obsidian's; the owner asked for it twice (2026-08-20, 2026-09-07) and it is mechanics, not structure.\n\n11. FIND-OR-CREATE, THE INLINE TITLE, TAP-FOLLOWS-LINK, THE KEYBOARD TOOLBAR, MONOCHROME LINE GLYPHS, THE PROPERTIES SHEET. Kept, unargued: they are below navigation, most were owner-directed, and the brief is the navigation area.\n\n12. THE RECORD CARD ('Liv's one genuinely non-Obsidian container… under-used as an identity'). Extended, not merely kept: a record you swiped away is held at the sill as its own round mark beside the pocket, so card-and-pocket becomes the app's whole 'open' vocabulary — a document rises from the pocket, a record rises as a card, and either can be held — while the two stay different shapes. Obsidian has one container (a pane) and one axis (files); this has a house, a pocket and a hold.\n\nWhat a user would name it: not 'Obsidian without the purple' — no drawer, no tab count, no header, no history keys — but a Things-shaped home with a Safari-shaped pill for what you are holding, in Liv's own furniture. Its nearest relatives are Things 3's root list and Safari's tab pill; its nearest sibling in the field is `native` (a hall of six tiles is a tab bar drawn as a screen), and the honest distance from `native` is that here the tiles carry a live line and are left behind, not pinned under every room. Axis 6 (chrome count and placement) and axis 7 (what the thumb reaches) are both moved: one bottom strip, zero top-left controls, and the app's 'where am I / go somewhere' control under the right thumb.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- ROOM-TO-ROOM COSTS A HALL. Today → Calendar is Hall, then tile: two taps and two 0.22s lateral slides (~440ms) with a screen between them that is not what you were working on, where the drawer was one drag and one tap with the room staying in view. Mitigations: a rightward drag anywhere walks to the hall (the existing PanelPanRecognizer, Calendar's pager veto intact), and holding the Hall key raises the six rooms as a bottom card for hold + tap with no hall drawn. The hold-menu is a second door to the rooms (rule 4), accepted as the cost of a hall that is a screen and not a drawer. The most frequent navigation act is still more motion than today.
- THE HALL IS A FULL-SCREEN MENU, and on 2026-08-23 the owner said 'Panel should not be full screen!' and on 2026-08-15/17 that the library is a place that PUSHES the desk. The argument: a panel over the desk that covers it is what he refused; the hall is not over anything — you left the room. But the argument is made from the same six rows he has seen as a drawer for a month, and he may read the hall as the panel gone full screen. The push mechanic is reversed; the 'a place you walk to' principle is honoured more literally than the panel did.
- NO HISTORY. Deleting ‹ › means no undo of a navigation you cannot see. Today → Search → note → drop lands on Today (right). But Calendar (March) → Hall → Tasks → 'back to March' is Hall + Calendar (two taps; March survives in spots). And a chain of [[links]] A→B→C→D leaves four chips in the pocket and no 'back' but the chips themselves — drawn below at four deep: the way back to A is one tap on the leftmost of four glyphs, with no name on it until you tap. A pocket is honest about this; a stack was not; but a user who liked ‹ loses it, and this reverses 2026-08-23.
- THE POCKET FILLS UP. Every link you follow, every search hit, every notification tap adds a chip, because that is what a pocket does. The 21-day inactive rule, the card's ✕ and 'Empty pocket' are the only relief. The old model had the same growth hidden behind a number; here you SEE it, which is better and also noisier — with eight documents the well is a scrolling row of glyphs that all look alike except the named one. A 'links replace instead of add' variant is offered to the owner as a switchable rule, not chosen: it would make the pocket lie about what you opened.
- GLYPH-ONLY CHIPS ARE ANONYMOUS. Fixing 'too small' by removing the titles means only the active chip says what it is; the others are the same ▤ glyph three or seven times. The well tells you HOW MANY and WHICH ONE, and the pocket card (long-press) tells you WHAT THEY ARE. That is one long-press more than a titled strip would cost, and it is the direction's answer to the 'tiny and subtle' correction — the name it does draw is at label size (16), larger than any bar word today.
- THE SILL NO LONGER TRAVELS WITH THE SURFACE. The 2026-09-08 ruling made the bar the surface's own foot because a card rose under it; here the sill is the floor of the house, hoisted to RootView above every surface, with card hosts stacked above the sill. The ruling's reason (z-order) is honoured; its letter is reversed eleven days after it was written, and it needs the owner's word.
- WHILE A NOTE IS UP YOU CANNOT SEE WHICH ROOM YOU ARE IN. The room title is covered; only dropping reveals it. After the critique + no longer betrays or contradicts it: + says 'Note' over a raised note and makes a note. The remaining cost is that a user who came from Today and wants a task must drop the note first (one tap) — the same cost as today, where a note fills the screen and + makes a note.
- TOP-LEFT IS EMPTY ON A RAISED DOCUMENT. Dropping needs the filled chip, the grabber band, or an overscroll at the top of the document; there is no chevron key. Discoverability rests on the filled chip reading as pressed and on the band. If the owner wants a key, it is one glass circle, and it costs the 'no top-left control anywhere' claim.
- KEYBOARD UP, NO WAY OUT BUT DOWN. The sill retires under the keyboard (2026-08-02), so inside a raised note with the keyboard up there is no navigation at all — the keyboard toolbar's dismiss is the only way back to the sill. Every direction in the field shares this and this one does not solve it.
- THE HALL IS NOT THE LAUNCH SCREEN. Cold launch lands in Today (2026-08-18), so the house is discovered on the first Hall tap or drag, not on first sight. A first-run-only hall landing was considered and rejected: on an empty box it is a menu of six rooms with nothing in them, which is the worse first screen; Today's empty state already names the areas.
- THE PANEL'S COUNTS LEAVE THE ROOMS. Counts were one drawer-swipe away from any room; now they are in the hall only. The live lines are richer than the old counts but farther.
- THE HALL'S TILES ARE VIEWS, NOT AREAS. 'Arrive furnished' is answered inside the rooms (Today's area line, Route's six areas, the unfiled count) and by empty-state prose, not by the hall. This direction maps the app by tool, as the panel does; it does not make the six areas a place.
- THE BIGGEST JUDGEMENT RISK: the pocket is a visible row of open documents at the bottom of the screen, and the owner removed a row of open documents on 2026-08-23. If he reads it as the strip, the proposal collapses to a numbered box again, which is what it exists to replace. It goes to him as one question with two mockups (three chips, eight chips) before anything else.
- DRIVE.SH CHURN: `panel`, `bar` and `grid` checks die with their surfaces; `hall`, `sill` and `pocket` must be written and each broken on purpose once before its green is trusted; `tour` gains a hall hop between rooms; `desk` (the count across six views) becomes the pocket's a11y count across six rooms and the hall. Revs 54–61 are unverified on a device and must be run first.
- THE KINDS-ARE-PEERS QUESTION IS NOT ANSWERED. Only documents ride in the pocket; tasks and events are still cards and still have rooms of their own. The hall makes the six rooms look like peers; it does not make a task and a note the same kind of object.

**Wireframes.**

*The Hall (workspace 'Home', pocket holding two notes)*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▮▮▮ │
│                            │
│ Home ⌄                     │
│                            │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ◔  Today               │ │
│ │    Wed 10 Sep · 4 due  │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ▤  Notes               │ │
│ │    Kitchen plan · 2m   │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ⊡  Inbox               │ │
│ │    3 to route          │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ▦  Calendar            │ │
│ │    14:00 Dentist       │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ☐  Tasks               │ │
│ │    12 open · 1 late    │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ ≡  Everything          │ │
│ │    212 · 67 unfiled    │ │
│ └────────────────────────┘ │
│  (Work) (Reading)  (+)     │
│                            │
│  Trash             Settings│
│┌──────────────────────────┐│
││ ⌕  ▕▤ ▓▤Kitchen▏  ⌂   +  ││
││Search           Hall Note││
│└──────────────────────────┘│
└────────────────────────────┘
```
Full screen, no scroll on a 6.1" phone (6 tiles × 72pt + title + chip row + foot + sill ≈ 660pt). Title = the workspace, chevron opens the existing WorkspaceSwitcher card from the bottom. Tiles: one column, LivTheme.panel elevation, 8pt gaps, monochrome 24pt glyph (the existing view glyphs), name at body, live line at label/text3 — the ONLY per-room fact, and one that changes. Filter chips lit when active; '+' is New filter. Trash and Settings are rows, not circles. The sill is identical to every room's: Search, the well, Hall (drawn at LivBar.disabledInk here — you are already in it), + reading 'Note' (the hall makes notes). In the well: one glyph chip and the named chip 'Kitchen', outlined (last held, dropped) — a note raised over the hall would fill it. No highlighted row: you are not in a room. Marker liv.surface.hall.

*The Today room — three documents in the pocket, a held task at the well's edge (the three-chip judgement)*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▮▮▮ │
│                            │
│ Today                      │
│ Wednesday 10 September     │
│                            │
│  M   T  [W]  T   F   S   S │
│  8   9  10  11  12  13  14 │
│                            │
│ ○  Call plumber       Home │
│ ○  Q3 budget draft    Work │
│ ○  Run 5k           Health │
│                            │
│ ▸  LATE 1                  │
│ ▸  Done 2                  │
│                            │
│                            │
│ 3 captured today · Route   │
│                            │
│┌──────────────────────────┐│
││ ⌕  ▕○│▤▤▤Kitch…▏  ⌂   +  ││
││Search           Hall Task││
│└──────────────────────────┘│
└────────────────────────────┘
```
The room is Today.swift unchanged: no top-left door, no top-right keys; the title is the only top furniture. The sill, left to right: Search; the well (recessed, LivTheme.panel2); Hall (doorway glyph, word 'Hall') beside +; + with the room's kind word ('Task'). In the well: the HELD task — the round task mark ○, walled off by a hairline, one at a time, tap to bring its card back — then two glyph chips and the named chip 'Kitch…' (label size, outlined: last held, not raised). With three documents nothing is clipped; this is the state to judge against 'too small'. Tapping a glyph or the name raises that note over Today; tapping a task row raises its card over Today (unchanged). 'Route' in the footer is a LINK that names a room and walks straight to Inbox (lateral, hall skipped). A rightward drag anywhere on the room walks to the hall. Marker liv.surface.today; the well is liv.chrome.pocket with a11y 'Pocket, 3 documents' (the held task is not counted).

*A note raised over Today at the end of a four-link chain, eight documents in the pocket (the eight-chip judgement)*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▮▮▮ │
│          ━━━━        ⊜   ⋯ │
│                            │
│ Ove (contractor)           │
│                            │
│ Free from 6 October. Quoted│
│ the floor at 14 m², see    │
│ [[Q3 planning]] for the    │
│ number we agreed.          │
│                            │
│ - [x] send measurements    │
│ - [ ] confirm tiles        │
│                            │
│                            │
│                            │
│                            │
│┌──────────────────────────┐│
││ ⌕  ▕▤▤▤▓▤ Ove ▏  ⌂   +  ││
││Search           Hall Note││
│└──────────────────────────┘│
└────────────────────────────┘
```
Kitchen plan → [[Budget]] → [[Q3 planning]] → [[Ove]]: each link added a chip after the last and raised it, so the chain reads left to right in the well and the way back is one tap per step on an unnamed glyph (the retrace the judges asked to see). Eight documents live in the pocket; the well is scrolled to the raised chip, the three glyphs before it visible, the four before those clipped at the well's leading edge (the scroll cue; scroll to reach them, long-press for their names). The document layer covers the room from the status scrim to the sill; the grabber band (━━━━) is the pull-down handle, and when chrome has retired on scroll an overscroll at the top drops it too; Properties (⊜) and ••• are the existing glass circles top-right; no top-left key. 'Ove' is filled: tap it to drop; tap any glyph to swap. + reads 'Note' — the kind of the surface you see — and makes a note that enters the pocket after Ove and rises in its place. Marker liv.surface.document; a11y 'Pocket, 8 documents'.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- STATUS SCRIM (LivTopScrim, Chrome.swift:1032) at the top of every surface — kept, status-bar height only; there is no doors band because there are no top doors on rooms or the hall.
- THE SILL — one Liquid Glass strip at the bottom, LivBar.height (66, with words), the only persistent chrome in the app. Retires under a keyboard, retires on downward scroll with hysteresis (livHidesChrome, kept), never retires for an empty pocket, sits above every surface and below every card (hoisted to RootView; card hosts stack above it).
- SILL KEY 1 (leading) — Search: magnifier, word 'Search' → the full-screen SearchView cover, unchanged.
- SILL ELEMENT 2 — The pocket well: a recessed capsule (LivTheme.panel2) between Search and Hall, ~150pt wide at 390pt, holding one 36pt glyph chip per carried document (LivGlyph per kind, monochrome, glass) with the ACTIVE one alone carrying its title at label size (16) — filled with LivTheme.selection while raised, outlined while dropped; horizontally scrolling, scrolled to the active chip, clipped at its edges (no fade); at its leading edge, outside the chips and behind a hairline, at most one HELD record drawn as the record's own round mark; empty = the bare recess. a11y 'Pocket, N documents' (the held record not counted).
- SILL KEY 3 — Hall: a doorway glyph drawn in Liv's pen (new, LivGlyph.hall; PanelMark is deleted), word 'Hall', beside +. Drawn at LivBar.disabledInk in the hall (you are already there). Hold 0.45s → the six rooms as a bottom card.
- SILL KEY 4 (trailing) — +: the kind of the surface you see as its word (Task / Event / Note in a room; 'Note' in the hall and over a raised document), tap makes it, hold opens the New menu. Always rises over where you stand.
- A RAISED DOCUMENT'S TOP: a grabber band centred under the status scrim (the pull-down handle), the Properties key (slider.horizontal.3) and the ••• 'Note actions' key top-right in 44pt glass circles — both existing. No top-left key.
- A ROOM'S TOP: its title only (hero for Today/Notes/Inbox/Tasks/Everything; Calendar's day title with the month card behind it). No keys.
- THE HALL'S TOP: the workspace name with a chevron.down (the WorkspaceSwitcher's door).
- CARDS (unchanged recipe): the record card, the pocket card, the rooms card (Hall held), the properties card, the history card, the workspace card, the trash sheet, the settings sheet, every LivMenu — all from the edge their button is on, all above the sill.
- DELETED CHROME: the library door (top-left, PanelMark), the ‹ and › keys, the numbered Open box (LivGlyph.day), the library panel and its wash/push/hairline/foot, the tab switcher cover and its footer, the Inactive shelf band, the minimised-record pill above the bar (it moved into the well as the held mark), the panel-drag latch's 'deskInFront' plumbing.

**Gestures.**
- Tap Hall (sill, the key beside +) or drag rightward ANYWHERE on a room (the existing PanelPanRecognizer, dx > 2|dy| latch) → walk to the hall: the room slides out right, the hall slides in from the left (one lateral easing; the sill stays put). The Calendar pager still vetoes the drag inside its grid.
- Hold Hall (0.45s) → the six rooms as a bottom card (LivMenu); tap one → walk straight there, no hall drawn.
- Tap a hall tile → walk into the room: hall out left, room in from the right, landing on the room's remembered furniture (spots).
- Tap a link that names a room (Today's 'Route') → walk straight to it, lateral, hall skipped.
- Tap a shelf row / search hit / [[link]] / notification → CARRY: a glyph chip slides into the well, becomes the named chip, and the document rises from the sill over where you stand (vertical easing).
- Tap the filled chip, pull down from the raised document's grabber band (>90pt or a fast flick, the LivMenu dismissal numbers), or overscroll past the document's top → DROP: the document sinks into the well; the room beneath is untouched.
- Tap another chip while one is raised → SWAP in one motion (current sinks, the other rises).
- Scroll the well horizontally → move through the chips; the well never responds to a vertical gesture.
- Long-press the well (0.45s) → the pocket fans out into the bottom card; tap a row raises it; ✕ drops it from the pocket; 'Empty pocket' is the last row.
- Tap + → the kind of the surface you see, one tap: in a room its kind (a task or event rises as a card; a note goes into the pocket and rises); in the hall or over a raised note, a Note. Hold + 0.45s → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text), unchanged.
- Swipe a record card down → it parks as the record's round mark at the well's leading edge (one at a time, outside the chips); tap the mark → the card returns.
- Scroll down 44pt in a room, shelf or raised note → the sill retires (with the raised note's top keys); scroll up or reach the top → it returns. Under a keyboard the sill is absent. Never absent for an empty pocket.
- Tap the hall's workspace title → the workspace card from the bottom; choosing one lands in that workspace's hall with its own pocket.
- Tap a filter chip in the hall → the lens toggles; the rooms narrow; a lensed room wears the LensChip beside its title, tap to drop it.

**Capture.** CAPTURE NEVER ASKS, AND NEVER MOVES YOU. The sill's + makes the kind of the surface you see in one tap: in Today/Tasks a task due the day you look at, rising as a record card with the caret in the name; in Calendar an event on the shown day, as a card; in Notes, Inbox, Everything and the hall a note — createNote → stamp → a chip enters the pocket and the empty editor rises over the room with the caret in it; over a raised note, a note — it enters the pocket after the current one and rises in its place, so the word under + is never about a room you cannot see. The instant after: you are still where you stood; the new thing is up; drop it and the room is as it was. This is the one change to + and it removes the 'sometimes local, sometimes navigating' fault: creating always raises something over where you stand. A note made anywhere is still the capture the Inbox lists (Route lists rows with no kind and content, unchanged), so 'file it later' is intact: it waits in Inbox, whose hall tile says '3 to route'. Hold + → the New menu, unchanged (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text); File imports rise as documents, Scan text opens the camera cover, whose Done raises the last shot.\n\nFROM OUTSIDE. The share extension spools; the app drains at foreground (liv_capture_at, stamp) and does NOT open or raise anything — the catch waits in the Inbox room and the Inbox tile's live line climbs by one. liv://capture (bare) = + Note wherever you are; liv://capture?text=&url= = catchText → saved, stamped, and raised over the current surface (as today: a payload you asked for lands in front of you). liv://capture/photo → the camera cover. The Search cover's 'Create \"words\"' row captures and raises the scrap over the room you searched from; in picker mode it links instead, unchanged. Calendar's empty-grid tap → a draft block → an event card, unchanged. The Inbox route card (WHERE — the six areas; 'Not a note…' → Task / Event / Link) is unchanged and is still the only question the app asks after a capture.

**Back, links and catches.** THERE IS NO BACK KEY. Back is the shape of the house: drop the note (down), walk to the hall (a key, or a drag from anywhere), or tap a link that names a room. LivReturns, LivPlace, goBack, goForward, here, land, and the ‹ › keys are deleted; the push/clear policy conflict in the diagnosis disappears because nothing pushes. The three routes to a note (shelf, pocket, search) now leave you in exactly the same situation — the note raised, its chip filled and named, the room beneath — and dropping it always shows the room you were in. What this loses is real and listed in the trade-offs: a Hall→Tasks→Hall→Calendar trip is retraced by hand, and a link chain is retraced by chips. It reverses the ‹ › half of 2026-08-23 and is put to the owner as such.\n\nLINKS. Tapping [[B]] inside raised A: B's chip enters the pocket after A's, B rises and takes the name, A drops to a glyph. Tap A's glyph to return. Four deep (screen 3) the chain reads left to right in the well and the way back is one tap per step; the chips are unnamed until tapped, which is the honest cost. A note opened from a properties-card reference chip or a Links row behaves the same. A note reached from a hall tile's Notes live line ('Kitchen plan · 2m') — the line is a fact, not a door; the tile lands on the shelf.\n\nliv://<view> → walk to that room (dropping a raised note into the pocket first, so the deep link shows the view it names; the room lands on its furniture, never a document — the harness's 'goto notes may not answer with a document' rule is satisfied by construction). liv://entity/<id> → box.refresh if unknown, then carry: a document rises over wherever you are; a record rises as a card. Notifications: the same carry. Unknown hosts do nothing. A cold-launch route is parked until the sill's closures exist, as today. surfaceCleanup survives with fewer members: every arrival closes the search/camera/settings/trash/workspace covers, the properties and history cards, the pocket card, the rooms card and any menu — a link never lands behind something.\n\nWORKSPACE SWITCH → the new hall. Nothing is closed in the old workspace; its pocket is persisted and swapped out (DeskPlanes per workspace, unchanged); the saved filter is dropped as today.

**Survives in code.** shell/ios/Sources/Plane.swift (481) — DeskTab, DeskTabContent, DeskPlane, DeskPlanes, spots, park/position, open(entity:), close, per-workspace persistence and the v1/v2/v3 folds: verbatim. The pocket is this struct with a face.; shell/ios/Sources/Positions.swift (370) — EverythingLens, InboxLens, TasksPosition, TodayPosition, CalendarPosition, LivPosition, livPlanesSelfCheck: verbatim; this is what a tile lands on.; shell/ios/Sources/Tabs.swift LivTabs (lines 30–63: the inactive predicate, days, liveTabs/inactiveTabs ordering) — kept and moved into the pocket card; TabSwitcher (64), InactiveTabs (337), TabCard (554) and the switcher half of livTabsSelfCheck go.; shell/ios/Sources/Chrome.swift DeskModel — kept as the one model, with a smaller vocabulary: `state: Feature` (149) becomes `room: Feature?` (nil = hall) plus `raised: Bool`; `openDoc` (183) = the active tab while raised (no coupling to .notes); openDocument (841) → carry(id) (pocket.open + raise, no state change, no push); show(549)/focus(561) → raise(tab); showList (746) → drop(); go (698) → walk(room); goToRoot (731) deleted; adopt(workspace:) (678) lands on room = nil; open(id, as:) (822), openAsCard (832), adoptCapture (486), minimiseRecord/restoreRecord/dropMinimised (462–475), surfaceCleanup (864), chromeHomeAgain (133), cards (267), pagerZone (362) — kept.; shell/ios/Sources/Navigate.swift Feature (23: cases, inOrder, makes, word), LivCaret, FeatureBody (181), livHidesChrome (218) and LivChromeScroll's hysteresis — kept; LivPlace (86), LivReturns (97) and livPlacesSelfCheck's stack cases deleted.; shell/ios/Sources/Surface.swift — LivSurface (notes, document, the Feature rawValues) kept and gains `hall`; LivOverlay keeps properties, settings, trash, workspace, history and gains `pocket` and `rooms`; `library` and `tabs` go with their surfaces.; shell/ios/Sources/Notes.swift NotesList (31) — kept unchanged as the Notes room's shelf.; Today.swift, Inbox.swift (route card, Route/Tidy, the 'Work?' chip), Calendar.swift + Month.swift, Tasks.swift, Everything.swift — room bodies unchanged; every desk.open call still works (it now means carry); Today's footer `desk.go(.inbox)` becomes the one room-naming link.; shell/ios/Sources/Record.swift TabShape (31), RecordCard (65), RecordCardHost (149), RecordBody (173) — kept; MinimisedRecordPill (97) becomes the held mark at the well's leading edge (same rules: one at a time, tap restores, ✕ drops, not persisted).; shell/ios/Sources/PanelDrag.swift PanelPanRecognizer (27), PanelDragInstaller (113), PanelDragHost (239) — the window recognizer and its dx > 2|dy| latch and pager veto survive as 'drag anywhere to walk to the hall'; the drawer-progress semantics they fed are deleted.; shell/ios/Sources/Panel.swift ViewCounts (401) — the one box walk survives, extended to the six live lines in one pass and re-homed in Hall.swift; LibraryPanel (134) and SidePanel (51) go.; Search.swift (cover, facets as chips, find-or-create, picker mode) — kept; a hit carries.; Editor.swift, EditorText.swift, EditorStyle.swift, EditorCost.swift, Files.swift (FileBody), Detail.swift (EntityInspector, InspectorValueSheet), Links.swift, History.swift — kept; Desk.swift's EntityTabBody (967) becomes the raised layer's body.; WorkspaceSwitch.swift, Workspace.swift (lens, stamp, filters), Settings.swift, Trash.swift, Camera.swift/Scan.swift, FileImport, Routes.swift (with liv://<view> → walk), Notify.swift, Catch.swift/Spool, Share.swift and the share extension — kept; their doors move to the hall's title and foot.; Menu.swift (LivMenu, LivMenuRow, LivGrabber, LivEdgeSheetHost, livSheet(from:), LivMenuHost), Rows.swift (LivListRow, LivRowFact, LivPress, LivCard), Kit.swift (LivDayMark and the rest), Glyph.swift (minus PanelMark at 590 and LivGlyph.day at 219), Theme.swift (LivBar glass, words, height 66, disabledInk 0.3; LivRow; LivType; LivMotion.nav 0.22s; the palette) — kept; Theme gains LivSill/LivPocket tokens (well width, chip 36, name at LivType label) and loses LivPanel (243).; Box.swift — untouched (rule 1: every liv_* call stays there; no new verbs are needed — the pocket writes the same UserDefaults keys the desk does).; App.swift — the search and camera fullScreenCovers (273, 297) kept; the switcher cover (267) goes; RootView hosts the sill above its one child.; drive.sh's discipline — surfaces asserted by marker and geometry; `boot`, `check`, `surface`, `tap`, `goto`, `tour`, `workspace`, `history`, `spool`, `rows`, `routes`, `areas`, `chrome`, `create`, `lens`, `facets`, `vault`, `cycles`, `quiet` survive; `panel`, `bar`, `grid` are replaced by `hall`, `sill`, `pocket`; `desk` reasserts the pocket's a11y count across the six rooms and the hall.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift LibraryPanel and SidePanel (the foot, the fade mask, the hairline, the row builder's `on: feature == desk.state`) — replaced by Hall.swift (tiles over ViewCounts + Feature.inOrder, filter chips, Trash/Settings rows, the workspace title). ViewCounts moves; nothing else in the file survives.; PanelDrag.swift's drawer semantics, and in Desk.swift/Chrome.swift: deskShift (423), panelProgress (395), libraryShown/libraryDrawn (209/217), setLibrary (222), the desk mask with square leading corners, the 50% wash, the wash's touch handling, the library door and its widening mark. The recognizer itself survives as the walk-to-the-hall drag.; Tabs.swift: TabSwitcher, TabCard, the two-column grid, the dashed New-note card, the '+ | N open | Done' footer, InactiveTabs and its band, the switcherShown fullScreenCover in App.swift:267, the -desk.boot switcher/inactive rehearsals (replaced by -desk.boot pocket).; Bar.swift BottomBar as it stands (160 lines): the ‹ and › keys, the Open key and its count, the five-key browser order and the Obsidian-measured insets; rebuilt as Sill.swift (Search, the well, Hall, +).; Navigate.swift: LivReturns, LivPlace, here/land, goBack/goForward, the forward stack, the 'a document opened from the list pushes .state(.notes)' dead-back.; Chrome.swift: goToRoot (731, and its comment), showList's stack-clearing, go's stack-clearing, openDocument's `state = .notes` (853), the 'openDoc only while state == .notes' guard (183–186), the adopt-lands-on-Notes rule, minimisedRecord's pill host.; Desk.swift DeskHost's three-way body (EntityTabBody vs NotesList vs FeatureBody chosen by state and openDoc, lines 48–66) — replaced by room-or-hall plus a raised layer; surfaceFoot (363) as the surface's own foot (the sill is hoisted); the MinimisedRecordPill in surfaceFoot (393).; Glyph.swift: LivGlyph.day(Int) (219, the Obsidian numbered box) and PanelMark (590, the drawer toggle).; Theme.swift: LivPanel (243: width, peek 100, row, the ChatGPT-measured numbers); LivBar.disabledInk's use for ‹ › (kept only for the Hall key in the hall).; Surface.swift: LivOverlay.library and LivOverlay.tabs.; The earlier draft of this direction's two vertical gestures on the well (pull up to fan out, flick a chip up to close) — one horizontal scroller, one vertical gesture class; the long-press is the door, the card's ✕ the verb.; The earlier draft's titled chips at caption size — replaced by glyph chips with one named chip at label size.; The earlier draft's 'the room decides what + makes under a raised note' — replaced by 'the surface you see decides'.; The earlier draft's parked record as a capsule chip and as the first row of the pocket card — replaced by the round held mark outside the chips, absent from the card and the count.; The 2026-08-15/17/23 drawer rulings' mechanics (push, wash, no close button, 100pt peek) — moot without a drawer; and the 2026-09-08 'bar is the surface's foot' letter (its z-order reason is kept by hoisting).; The a11y label 'N documents open' and the word 'Open' — the pocket shows the things instead.; ios.md §51's 'a position survives, a document does not' as a rule that needs stating — it becomes visible structure (furniture in the room, chips in the pocket) and no longer needs a sentence.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- Does the desk have a state of its own? YES — the pocket (chips) and `raised`, orthogonal to the room; a document is never drawn by borrowing Notes. Vagueness 1 resolved.
- What does the panel say when a document is on screen? There is no panel to lie; the hall is a place you are in or not; the room's identity is beneath the raised note and nothing on screen claims otherwise (+ says 'Note', the kind of what you see). Vagueness 11 resolved by removal, with the trade-off that the room name is hidden while a note is up.
- Is 'Notes' a view, the container, or the list? A ROOM whose furniture is the shelf. The container is the POCKET, with its own name and its own place. Vagueness 4 resolved.
- Notes list and switcher grid — two surfaces or one? Two objects, not two doors to one: the shelf (a room's furniture) and the pocket (carried things); the GRID dies (rule 6). Vagueness 2 resolved.
- What does the number count? Nothing — there is no number; the glyphs are the count and the named chip is the one you hold. Vagueness 3 resolved.
- Is ‹ the undo of any navigation or of a document open only? Neither: ‹ is deleted; back is spatial (drop / hall / a chip per link). Vagueness 6 resolved; the cost is no history, and it reverses 2026-08-23.
- Why does go clear the stack while openDocument pushes? Nothing pushes or clears any more; carry and walk are independent verbs. Vagueness 5 resolved.
- Is 'tap the row you are in → its root' a visible rule? It no longer exists: a tile always lands on the room's furniture because a room never draws a document; in the hall you are in no room. Vagueness 7 resolved.
- What shows that a tool resumes while a document is only open? A room keeps its furniture where you left it (visible on arrival); a document is a chip you can see at the sill. Two visible states. Vagueness 8 resolved.
- Is Open [n] a destination, a mode, or the desk? The pocket IS the desk, visible in every room; the fan-out is a half card on a long-press, not a mode; no full-screen anything stands between you and a note. Vagueness 10 resolved.
- Does + stay or move you? Always stays: a note rises like a card does, and the word under + names the kind of the surface you see, so the label and the consequence agree everywhere. Vagueness 9 resolved.
- Are the six rows peers, or a dashboard, a container, a queue and three lists? Six tiles, still peers in structure; the live lines say what each is FOR ('3 to route' vs 'Kitchen plan · 2m'). Vagueness 12 only partly resolved — the difference is spoken, not built.
- Where does a note opened from Today/Calendar/Search belong when you are done? In the pocket, at your thumb, and dropping it lands you back where you came from with no stack.
- What shape makes tasks and calendar central while keeping notes separate and records as cards? Six equal rooms off one hall (no bar of view keys, no three-arrangements page, no views-as-tabs, no grid-as-root); notes separate because only documents ride in the pocket; records stay cards and one can be held beside the pocket as its own mark.
- Thumb zone or top-left? Thumb zone: the app's only chrome is the sill, Hall and + on its trailing half, the hall one drag from anywhere; the top-left has no control on any surface.
- Why Notes after a workspace switch? It isn't: a switch lands in the new hall; launch lands in Today; the hall is the home you pass through, Today is the room the day starts in.
- Which of the two doors to a note survives? Both, as different things: the shelf finds, the pocket holds; the grid dies.
- Can this be settled without a mockup, and which drive.sh assertion proves it? No — mockup first (this is one, in two pocket states); proof: `tour` hopping through the hall lands each room on its own marker; `goto notes` from a raised note answers liv.surface.notes with the chip still present; `pocket` asserts the a11y count and that the filled chip's title equals the raised document's, with three and with eight; `sill` asserts the four elements' geometry in every room and the hall, and that the Hall key's x is in the trailing half.

**Changed after the judges.**
- CHIPS: titled chips at caption size (~9 characters, two visible) replaced by glyph-only 36pt chips with the title on the ACTIVE chip alone at label size (16, ~12 characters) — filled while raised, outlined while dropped. Answers 'too small' ×3 from all three judges; the eight-chip state is now drawn (screen 3) beside the three-chip state (screen 2) so the owner judges both.
- + UNDER A RAISED NOTE: 'the room decides' replaced by 'the surface you see decides' — + reads 'Note' and makes a note over a raised document, which enters the pocket after the current one and rises in its place. Removes the mode error all three judges named (a task made under a note opened from Today).
- SILL ORDER: Hall moved from the far-left to beside +, [Search][well][Hall][+], so the two most frequent keys sit under a right thumb; and the existing drag-from-anywhere recognizer (2026-08-08) is kept as 'drag rightward anywhere → the hall', so no key is needed for the most frequent act. Answers the hand-and-head judge's reach finding.
- ROOM-TO-ROOM: one stated rule instead of 'sometimes': a KEY walks to the hall, a LINK that names a room walks straight there (Today's 'Route' is the only one in code). Added the Hall HOLD → six-rooms card as the frequent-trip shortcut, flagged as a second door under rule 4. Tap counts and motion (two taps, ~440ms) are now stated as measured, not argued away.
- WELL GESTURES: the upward pull to fan out and the upward flick to close a chip are deleted — a horizontal scroller with two vertical gestures was the collision class the second judge named. One rule: scroll moves, tap switches, long-press fans out, the card's ✕ closes.
- THE HELD RECORD: no longer a capsule chip inside the chip run and the first row of the pocket card. It is the record's own round mark at the well's leading edge, walled off by a hairline, uncounted and unlisted — visibly a different thing, so 'a record is never a tab' (2026-08-07/08/16) stays true on screen.
- DROP WHEN CHROME IS AWAY: specified — overscroll past the document's top drops it, and any upward scroll brings the sill back with the filled chip. Answers the third judge's 'grabber under a retiring scrim' finding.
- LINK CHAIN: drawn at four deep (screen 3) with the retrace stated as one tap per unnamed glyph — the honest cost is now visible rather than described.
- LIVE LINES COST: stated as one pass over the snapshot (ViewCounts extended, re-homed in Hall.swift) with a self-check asserting the pass count is flat in box size, per rule 2 and the EditorCost.swift precedent.
- REVERSALS NAMED UP FRONT: 2026-08-23 (the bar, the box, the grid, ‹ ›) is now the first line of the concept and the first open question — one question to the owner, not footnotes; 2026-09-08 (bar as foot) and the 'Panel should not be full screen!' collision are each stated as reversals in the trade-offs.
- SURVIVES/THROWN AWAY re-checked against the tree: line numbers and struct names corrected (LibraryPanel/SidePanel/ViewCounts in Panel.swift; PanelPanRecognizer/PanelDragInstaller/PanelDragHost; LivGlyph.day(Int) at Glyph.swift:219, PanelMark at 590; LivPanel at Theme.swift:243, LivBar.height 66, LivMotion.navSeconds 0.22; App.swift's switcher cover at 267; LivOverlay.library/tabs; drive.sh's 23 subcommands named and the three that die).

**Conceded.**
- The pocket is, structurally, a visible row of what is open at the bottom of the screen, and whether it is Safari's pill or the strip removed on 2026-08-23 is the owner's reading, not an argument this direction can win on paper. If he reads a strip, the direction collapses to a numbered box.
- Room-to-room is more expensive than the drawer: two taps and two lateral slides through a screen that is not your work, against one drag with the room in view. The drag-anywhere and the Hall hold-menu shorten it; they do not make it cheaper than today.
- The hall is a full-screen menu reached laterally, and the owner has said 'Panel should not be full screen!'. The distinction (over the desk vs. instead of the room) is real and may not survive his first look.
- No history: ‹ › are deleted and nothing replaces them but the shape of the house. A Hall→Tasks→Hall→Calendar trip is retraced by hand, and a link chain by unnamed glyphs. This reverses part of 2026-08-23.
- Glyph-only chips are anonymous: only the active chip says what it is, and seven ▤ glyphs look alike. Names cost a long-press. This is the price of not drawing caption text at the sill.
- Under a raised note you cannot see which room you are in; you learn it by dropping. + no longer contradicts it, but the room's name is off screen while you read.
- The sill hoisted above every surface reverses the letter of 2026-09-08 (the bar as the surface's own foot); the z-order reason is honoured, the sentence is not.
- The Hall hold-menu is a second door to the rooms (rule 4), justified only by the +'s identical tap/hold pair.
- The hall's tiles are the six tools, not the six areas; 'arrive furnished' is still answered inside the rooms and by empty-state prose, as today. Making an area a place is `areas`' direction, not this one's.
- With the keyboard up inside a raised note there is no navigation on screen, the cul-de-sac 2026-08-17 forbade for views. This direction keeps 'the sill retires under the keyboard' and does not solve it.
- The hall is not the launch screen, so the house is first met on the first Hall tap or drag. A first-run hall landing was rejected because an empty box's hall is six empty rooms.
- Kinds-are-peers is not answered: only documents ride in the pocket; a task is still a card with a room of its own.
- The harness churn is real — `panel`, `bar`, `grid` die, `hall`, `sill`, `pocket` must be written and each broken on purpose, `tour` and `desk` change — and revs 54–61 are unverified on a device, so the first build.sh + drive.sh run precedes any of this.

**Open questions.**
- THE ONE QUESTION, before anything: on 2026-08-23 the owner ruled a browser's bar with ‹ ›, the numbered box as the only door to the tabs, and 'Not a tab "bar"'. This direction deletes all three for a visible pocket. Does he read the well — one named chip and a few glyphs in a tray among the keys — as Safari's pill or as the strip he removed? Judge with the three-chip and eight-chip screens side by side. Yes or no; the rest follows.
- Chip form as revised: glyph-only chips with the title on the active one at label size. Is the anonymity of the other glyphs acceptable, or does he want names on all and accept the size?
- Should a [[link]] followed from a raised note ADD a chip (proposed: a pocket that tells the truth) or REPLACE the current chip (a pocket that stays small)? Offered as his choice; the four-deep screen shows the cost of adding.
- The hall is a full screen: does he read it as the panel gone full screen ('Panel should not be full screen!') or as a place you walk to (2026-08-15)?
- The Hall hold-menu (six rooms as a bottom card) is a second door to the rooms, mirroring +'s tap/hold pair. Keep it as the frequent-trip shortcut, or accept the hall on every trip?
- Should the hall be the launch screen (the house seen first) or should Today stay the launch room (proposed; the 2026-08-18 ruling)?
- Does a raised document want a top-left drop key after all, at the cost of 'no top-left control anywhere'?
- The held record beside the pocket (round mark, walled off, uncounted, unlisted) versus the pill above the sill as today: is the shape difference enough to keep 'a record is never a tab' visibly true?
- Which of the hall's live lines are right, and is 'Kitchen plan · 2m' under Notes a fact or a second recents door? (Drawn as a fact; the tile lands on the shelf.)
- Reversal of the 2026-09-08 'bar is the surface's foot' letter: the sill is the floor and is hoisted; cards stack above it. Needs the owner's word since it undoes a rev written twelve days ago.
- Keyboard up inside a raised note leaves no navigation on screen (the sill retires, 2026-08-02). Every direction shares this; should the keyboard toolbar carry the filled chip, or is 'dismiss the keyboard first' the rule?
- Kinds-are-peers stays open: only documents ride in the pocket. Should a record be carriable as a document on demand from its card ('later', per kinds-are-peers)? Not proposed here.
- Sill order [Search][well][Hall][+] puts Search at the far-left, the hardest reach; is a full-cover search rare enough to sit there, or should Search and Hall swap and the drag-anywhere carry the hall?

### 5. Six Rooms, Six Lenses

*You always stand in a ROOM (Everywhere, one of the six areas, Unfiled, or a room of your own) and look at it through a LENS (Today, Notes, Inbox, Calendar, Tasks, Everything); the address is printed top-left as a chevron title that is also the only go-somewhere control, the bottom bar stays the browser's five keys the owner ruled literally, a document is a sheet lifted over the place you stand with the room chip in its head as the filing door, the tab grid rises as a card from the bottom instead of covering the screen, and the drawer, its door and the window-level drag are deleted.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE ADDRESS. Every screen has a two-word address, Room › Lens, printed once, as a glass chevron title at the top-left where the library door used to be: "Everywhere › Today", "Work › Notes", "Unfiled › Everything". The room is where you stand; the lens is how you are looking. A document (note or file) is neither: it is a sheet lifted from the room onto the desk and laid over the place you stand, and the address stays printed above it (with the room shortened to its mark, because the sheet's own head prints the room in full). A record (task, event) is, as today, a card over where you stand.

WHERE THE ADDRESS LIVES, AND WHY NOT IN THE BAR. The first draft put the address in the bar as a pill. Measured, that fails: the capsule is 306pt wide at 390pt (LivBar.sideInset 42 each side), five slots of 61pt; a pill wide enough for "Learning › Calendar" at caption size leaves the four remaining keys under the 44pt touch floor, and "Family & Friends › Everything" (29 characters) fits nowhere in a bar. It also broke a ruling the owner made twice: global ACTIONS on the bar, global STATE up top (2026-08-17), and the bar's set is a browser's, literally (2026-08-23, re-affirmed with words under every key 2026-09-05). The address is state, so it goes where state lived: the top-left, as a 44pt-tall glass pill in the band the door circles own, at label size (16), with the room's mark at 24. On a lens the top-right is empty, so ~358pt is available and every one of the 9×6 addresses fits in full; on a sheet the two top-right circles leave ~254pt, so the pill prints the room's MARK, ›, and the lens word, and the sheet's head chip prints the room's name. This is Notion Calendar's chevron title, which the owner attached and which the Calendar already uses for its day: a title you tap to drop the picker for it. The pill retires on scroll with the band and returns on scroll-up, exactly as the door it replaces; unlike the bar, it stays up under a keyboard, so a sheet with the keyboard raised still says where it is and still has a way out.

ROOMS are the product's furniture: Everywhere (the whole house; the launch room; stamps nothing), the six areas drawn in Liv's own pen (Work, Health, Money, Home, Family & Friends, Learning — plus any area the person mints, since areas grow), Unfiled (the hall: everything with no area), and the person's own rooms (today's workspaces). Mechanically a room is what a workspace already is — a lens (a query the core runs: area:Work, no:area, or the workspace's own) plus a stamp (its equality terms) — and the six areas are the rooms the app furnishes without storing anything: they are drawn from LivArea and the box's own area values, their query composed at the seam. What made areas-as-workspaces fail in July was not the machinery; it was that an area was the FIRST question. Here the first room is Everywhere.

LENSES are the six existing views, unchanged in their bodies. Four of them wear the room the way they wear the workspace lens today (Notes, Calendar, Tasks, Everything all call workspaces.admits already); two do not, by the 2026-08-05 ruling that the first draft revoked and this one keeps: Today and Inbox list the whole house in every room, because a thing you must decide about must not hide behind the lens you happen to be wearing. In every lens the room means the same two things: it is what + stamps, and it is where the Route's leading circle files. The Lenses card draws the difference as two groups, "Everywhere" (Today, Inbox) and "In Work" (Notes, Calendar, Tasks, Everything), so the rule is visible furniture, not a footnote.

WHY THE FURNITURE AND NOT THE VIEW TYPE: the app has six kinds and one workspace, and until now the map was drawn by kind with the person's own furniture demoted to a chip in a drawer foot — the Obsidian shape. Inverting it makes the six areas the map: 'where did I put it' is the retrieval question the furnishing study measured people asking (§4.3, §4.7: 56–68% retrieve by navigating to where they put it, 4–15% by search), and it is a question the six areas answer and Notes/Tasks/Calendar cannot. Tasks and calendar become central without a bar of them: they are lenses on the room you stand in, and no kind owns the surface. Notes stays separate: a note is still the only thing that becomes a sheet on the desk.

THE STUDY'S WARNING, KEPT: (1) the launch room is Everywhere and stamps nothing; (2) a room stamps a new thing only when you deliberately walked in, the printed address says so above the thumb, and the stamp is shown on the result as a room chip that clears in one tap; (3) a catch from outside (share sheet, liv://capture, Search's Create) is saved first and never asks, stamped exactly as a workspace stamps it today; (4) Unfiled is a real room with all six lenses, so a person who never files lives in Everywhere, sees no red, is asked nothing, and loses nothing.

THE THREE THINGS THE OWNER NAMED: global navigation = the address title (room and lens); open Note Tabs = the grid the numbered box has always opened, raised as a card from the bottom instead of a cover, sinking to the thumb as ruled; the views = lenses, reached by the title's lens word. The panel, its door, the window-level drag and the per-workspace desk are removed in the same change (rule 6). The bottom bar is not touched.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** TWO THINGS, TWO NAMES, TWO DOORS, ONE HONEST DIFFERENCE.

THE NOTES LIST IS A PLACE: it is the Notes LENS of whatever room you stand in — "Work › Notes" is Work's notes by log recency, "Everywhere › Notes" is exactly today's NotesList. It is reached through the address (title → lens word → Notes), it keeps its screen name, and it lists ALL the room's notes, so 'a surface named after a thing contains it' holds and the grid-as-root regression cannot recur. It never shows which notes are open; being open is not a property of a place.

THE DESK IS A CARD, AND IT IS THE GRID THE OWNER ASKED FOR: the bar's numbered key (word "Open", count = live tabs, unchanged) raises the Desk card from the bottom — the existing TabSwitcher body (TabCard grid, the Inactive shelf, the "3 open" footer, the New-note card bottom-right) hosted in the app's own livSheet(from: .bottom) at .large instead of App.swift's fullScreenCover. The cards begin at the bottom where the thumb is (2026-08-31), the room stays visible in the band above the card, and "just have tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box" (2026-08-23) is kept in content and order; the only change is the container, from a cover to a card, and that one change is put to the owner by name. Tap a card → that sheet is laid over the room and lens you are standing in (no room change, no lens change); ✕ closes; the card dismisses by a pull or a pick; the Inactive shelf unfolds in the same card with Close all, asking nothing. The Open key wears a filled state while a sheet is on screen: the desk is 'up'.

A SHEET ON THE DESK: a document renders as its own state — address.doc — over the room's lens, never by borrowing the Notes state. Its head carries the ROOM CHIP (the area's mark and name, or a quiet "Unfiled"), which is the filing door: tap → the six areas as a bottom card (the Route card, at home on every document and every record card). The top-right glass keys (Properties, •••) stay. Tapping the title's lens word, or ‹, lays the sheet back down: it stays open on the desk, the lens draws. So the two doors to a note are honest about two different things: the Notes lens is where a note LIVES; the Desk card is what you have LIFTED. One is a place at the top; one is a shelf at the thumb.

ONE DESK FOR THE BOX: the desk no longer swaps per workspace (a workspace is now a room, and a room is a place you walk to with your sheets). DeskPlanes keeps its shape; the persistence key becomes desk.v4 (one plane), with every desk.v3.<ws> key (Workspace.swift:333) folded on first read and never deleted. Spots stay keyed per Feature — 'a tool is singular' survives verbatim: Calendar is on March in every room.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** ROOM › LENS › [SHEET | CARD]

ROOMS (the Rooms card, dropping from the title; one walk of the box per render gives every room a count through any saved filter): Everywhere · Work · Health · Money · Home · Family & Friends · Learning · any minted area · Unfiled (drawn only while its count differs from Everywhere's — a fact drawn only when it changes) · a rule · the person's own rooms (today's workspaces, with their emoji) · New room… · foot: Trash · Settings. Two groups, one word: a room is a place with a stamp. The furnished six stamp area:<name>; Unfiled stamps nothing and lenses no:area; a person's room stamps whatever its query's equality terms say, as a workspace does now.

LENSES (the Lenses card, dropping from the title; counts within the current room; order = Feature.inOrder, unchanged), drawn as two groups: "Everywhere — Today · Inbox" and "In <room> — Notes · Calendar · Tasks · Everything". Each lens body is today's view file; the four in the second group take the room's predicate at the same query seam where they already take the workspace lens (workspaces.admits; the core parses area:Work, no:area, and ANDs any saved filter on). In Everywhere every lens is exactly today's view.

What each lens means inside a room: Today — the whole house's day (LATE, the day, Done, the area-count line, '3 captured today · Route them'), by the 2026-08-05 rule; + here makes a task stamped with the room. Notes — the room's notes by recency. Inbox — the whole pile in every room (Inbox.swift's stated safety rule, unchanged); standing in an area, each Route row's leading circle files it INTO THAT ROOM in one tap and its chip reads the room with a question mark, the clerk's own guess shown when it differs; in Everywhere and Unfiled the circle asks WHERE, as now. Calendar — the room's events on the shared month/day. Tasks — the room's tasks with the same chips. Everything — All / Upcoming / Unfiled slices, the Unfiled slice hidden in an area room (as now when a workspace stamps an area) and answered by the same no:area query the Unfiled room uses (one test, rule 4).

Positions: one per lens, room-independent. Documents: one desk, room-independent. Records: cards, room-independent. The only per-room state is the room itself.

Cold launch: Everywhere › Today. Walking into a room keeps the lens (the 2026-08-18 home is the day; adopt(workspace:) landing on Notes goes). Opening a sheet keeps the room (a thing opens over where you stand).

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against the references reader's list, item by item:

LEFT DRAWER = THE LIBRARY, the PANEL FOOT, the TOP-LEFT SIDEBAR TOGGLE, the EDGE-SWIPE FROM ANYWHERE: deleted. There is no drawer, no push/wash of the desk, no window-level recognizer. The top-left pixel is no longer a toggle: it is a printed address, wider than the circle it replaces, and tapping it drops a card for the half you tapped — Notion Calendar's chevron title, not Obsidian's hamburger. The vault-name-count-gear foot is gone; Trash and Settings sit in the foot of the Rooms card.

BOTTOM CAPSULE (five keys, back/forward/search/plus/box) and THE NUMBERED BOX: KEPT, on purpose, and owned. The owner ruled this set literally (2026-08-23) and asked for the words under it (2026-09-05); it is his bar now, not Obsidian's, and its fifth key counts documents where Obsidian's opens a date. What made the bar read as copied was the drawer it sat under and the grid it opened as a mode; both change. If the owner wants the bar to change, that is his one-sentence call, and this direction does not need it.

TAB SWITCHER AS A CARD GRID 'verbatim in structure': the grid stays (his word: as it appeared before) but stops being a mode — it is a card with the room still showing above it, the way Safari's tab grid is not and a Todoist bottom card is. Obsidian's switcher takes the screen; this is a shelf raised at the thumb.

ONE DESK PER VAULT / WORKSPACE = VAULT: the desk is one per box, and a workspace is a room you walk into carrying your sheets. Obsidian swaps the whole workspace on a vault switch; Liv does not.

NAVIGATION AXES (Obsidian: folder tree, search, link, a daily-note key, bookmarks): Liv's axes are rooms × lenses. There is no tree, no folder, no daily note, no bookmark. Time (Today, Calendar) and kind (Notes, Tasks) are ways of looking, never places; the places are the areas of a life, which no reference app in the list (Things' Areas are user-made containers of projects, not lenses over every kind) draws this way.

'DOCUMENTS ARE WHERE YOU STAND; EVERYTHING ELSE IS A DRAWER YOU VISIT': inverted. A place is where you stand; a document is a sheet lifted onto it, and the place stays printed above the sheet.

THE STRUCTURAL RESEMBLANCE kinds-are-peers diagnosed ('one kind got the main surface and the rest got a drawer'): in a room, notes, tasks, events and captures are equal contents and the lens is the only thing that changes; no kind owns the surface. Tasks and calendar are central without a bar of them.

LANDING and the recents/grid duplication: the recency list is a lens (a place) and the grid is the desk (a shelf); they no longer compete for 'get me back' because one lists what lives here and the other what is lifted, and both say so in their names.

WHAT STAYS COPIED, deliberately: the five-key bar (his ruling), chrome retiring on scroll (Safari's as much as Obsidian's), the inline title (2026-08-01), find-or-create in Search, monochrome line glyphs, ••• at the top-right of a document, the record card (Liv's own). The reader's axis 10 says distinctiveness must be structural: the drawer, the toggle, the drag, the mode-switcher and the type-map are the structural items on the list, and every one of them is changed.

WHICH OTHER APP IT NOW RESEMBLES: Notion Calendar (a chevron title that drops its picker) crossed with Todoist (labelled bar, bottom cards). It stands apart from both in the one thing neither has: a printed place with two axes, one of which is the person's own furniture.

WHY IT IS NOT THE THREE REJECTED SHAPES: not 'four floors' — the bar carries no view keys; not 'one page, three arrangements' — the six views are untouched bodies; not views-as-tabs — a lens is not a tab and the desk holds only documents; not grid-as-root — the Notes lens lists every note in the room and the grid is a card the numbered box opens.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- THE TAP TABLE, HONESTLY. Capture: 1 tap (+), unchanged. Re-open the note you were in: ‹ (1 tap) or Open → card (2), unchanged or better. Reach the note list: title's lens half → Notes (2 taps, at the top) vs door → row today (2 taps, at the top) — equal. Switch lens: 2 taps at the top, equal to today. Switch room: 2 taps at the top — a new cost, because rooms did not exist. Switch both: 4 taps — the worst case, and it did not exist. File a thing: chip → area (2 taps from any sheet or card) vs properties key → area field → value today (3+). Nothing got worse for the three acts the owner named; crossing the grid is the new cost.
- THE GO-SOMEWHERE CONTROL IS AT THE TOP, NOT THE THUMB. It sits exactly where the panel door sits today, so reach is unchanged, and the pill is 4–7 times wider than the 44pt circle it replaces; but the diagnosis's question 15 asked for the thumb zone and this answers no, on the strength of the owner's own 2026-08-17 separation (state up, actions down) and the measured bar. A regrip to change place stays.
- A ROOM STAMPS WHAT YOU MAKE IN IT. + in Work › Notes makes a Work note with no question. The alternative — + never stamps, the chip offers — loses the note you just made from the very list you made it in (Work › Notes would not contain it), which is the vanish bug the Inbox safety rule exists to prevent. So the stamp stays, printed twice (the title above, the chip on the result) and cleared in one tap. It is still filing-after-walking rather than filing-after-thinking, and it needs the owner's word.
- FILING BECOMES THE MAP, SO A WRONG FILING IS A WRONG ROOM. The study measured 57% of ordinary captures as having no defensible area; a dentist note filed under Health is not found by walking into Family. Everywhere is home and finds everything; the chip refiles in one tap; Search is unchanged. But browsing by area exposes the ambiguity the lens chip used to hide, and the study said to measure retrieval before trusting this. It is the seed's own bet.
- THE ROOM NARROWS NOTHING IN TWO OF SIX LENSES. Standing in Learning, Today and Inbox list the whole house (the ruling kept), so the printed room does only two things there: it stamps + and it answers Route. The address is therefore weaker in Today than in Notes, and the Lenses card's two groups are what explain it — visibly, without a grey sentence, but a first-day user will notice that 'Learning › Today' shows their dentist.
- THE INBOX'S LEADING CIRCLE CHANGES MEANING BY ROOM. In Everywhere it asks WHERE; in Work it files into Work. One control, room-dependent — a smaller mode error than the one the first draft had (a different LIST per room), kept because it is the direction's best answer to the pile: the question is answered by standing somewhere. The row's chip prints the answer before the tap, which is the mitigation.
- THE ADDRESS IS PRINTED ONLY WHILE THE TOP CHROME IS UP. It retires on scroll with the band, as the door does today; on a long list the app says where you are only after a scroll-up. The bottom bar's + word still tracks the lens, so the kind you would make is always visible; the room is not.
- THE DESK CARD IS STILL A CHANGE TO THE SWITCHER. The grid, order, shelf and footer are as they appeared; the container is a card, not a cover, so the room shows above it and a pull dismisses it. That is one sentence for the owner, and if he says cover, the direction survives unchanged with the cover.
- NO DRAG REPLACES THE PANEL DRAG. Every bar swipe and lens-paging swipe from the first draft is dropped (three horizontal gestures in one bar was a collision, and rule 4 says the card is the one door). The panel's from-anywhere drag — a big-target gesture people found — is lost with the panel, and only the system pull on a card remains.
- WORKSPACES BECOME ROOMS, AND SOME ARE ODD ROOMS.
