A room is defined as a place with a stamp, and a tag-only workspace is a room whose stamp is a tag; the Rooms card draws the furnished six and the person's own as two groups. The word 'room' stretches over a saved query. Existing boxes keep their workspaces; nothing is deleted.
- THE COUNTS ARE ONE WALK, CHECKED IN SWIFT. PlaceCounts replaces ViewCounts with one pass over the decoded snapshot filling a rooms × lenses table; it ships with a launch-flag self-check asserting the shape (double the rows, roughly double the time). That is a Swift-side shape check in suites.sh, not a Rust cost test — the walk is on the shell's side of the snapshot, where no Rust test can see it.
- THE NOTE-MAKING + STILL LIFTS A SHEET. + in a Notes/Inbox/Everything lens lifts a full sheet while + in Today rises a half card; the container still changes with the kind (vagueness 9), unresolved by design (a note is a sheet, a task is a card, by the 2026-08-07/08 rulings). What improves is that the address under either never changes.
- NO drive.sh CHECK PROVES THE ADDRESS YET, and revs 54–61 are unverified on a device. Two checks are specified (address, room) and must each be broken once on purpose before their green is trusted; they sit on top of unverified work.

**Wireframes.**

*Cold launch — Everywhere › Today*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                 ▂▄▆ ▮ │
│ ╭────────────────────╮     │
│ │◎ Everywhere › Today│     │
│ ╰────────────────────╯     │
│ Today · Tue 9 Sep          │
│  M   T  [W]  T   F   S   S │
│  7   8   9  10  11  12  13 │
│ Work 2 · Health 1 · 3 unf. │
│                            │
│ LATE · 3                 ▸ │
│ ○ Pay dentist        Money │
│ ○ Call Anna         Family │
│ ○ Draft brief         Work │
│ ────────────────────────── │
│ ○ Dentist 14:00     Health │
│ ○ Read ch. 4      Learning │
│ ○ Buy lamp            Home │
│ ○ Untangle notes           │
│ Done · 2                 ▸ │
│ 3 captured today · Route › │
│╭──────────────────────────╮│
││  ‹    ›    ⌕    +    ▢3  ││
││Back  Fwd Search Task Open││
│╰──────────────────────────╯│
└────────────────────────────┘
```
The address is the top-left glass pill, 44pt tall in the band the door circles own, label size, the room's mark at 24 — where the panel door was and four times as wide. Its left half (room) and right half (lens) are two buttons, each dropping its card from the top. Today's own hero is unchanged (its date, the day strip, the area-count line); the room is not repeated in it, because a fact is printed once. Rows wear their area as the second voice, so in Everywhere the map is visible in the list. The bottom bar is exactly today's: Back, Forward, Search, + labelled with what it makes here (a task; unfiled, because the room is Everywhere), Open with the live count. No drawer, no door, no drag.

*Rooms card dropped over Work › Notes*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                 ▂▄▆ ▮ │
```

Tapping the title's room half drops the Rooms card from the top (a card comes from the edge its button is on; Menu.swift already has the top recipe, grabber at the foot). Rows are LivMenu rows: the furnished rooms in Liv's pen with counts through any saved filter, the current one on a soft fill, Unfiled shown only while its count differs from Everywhere's; a rule; the person's own rooms (today's workspaces, their emoji) and New room… (the WorkspaceSwitcher form: Name, Area, Tags); at the foot Trash and Settings. Picking a row walks into that room keeping the lens; the sheets on the desk come along. The Lenses card is the same recipe from the title's right half, its rows in two groups — 'Everywhere: Today · Inbox' and 'In Work: Notes · Calendar · Tasks · Everything' — with counts within the room and the saved filters as toggles at its foot. The bar is untouched under the card.

*A sheet over Work › Today with the Desk card raised*

The sheet lies over Work › Today. On a sheet the title prints the room's mark, ›, and the lens (the two top-right circles leave ~254pt, which the longest full address does not fit), and the sheet's own head prints the room in full as the ROOM CHIP — the filing door: tap → the six areas as a bottom card; on an unfiled note it reads a quiet 'Unfiled'. Top-right keeps Properties and •••. The Desk card is the existing grid — TabCard, the Inactive shelf, the '3 open' footer, New note bottom-right — raised as a .large card from the bottom instead of a cover, cards beginning at the thumb, the active sheet outlined, the room still showing above. A pick lays that sheet over the same room and lens; ‹ or the title's lens word lays the sheet down and Today draws with the sheet still counted. The bar retires under the card as under every LivMenu.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- STATUS SCRIM (LivTopScrim) — every surface runs under the clock; unchanged.
- THE ADDRESS TITLE — top-left, in the band the top chrome owns, replacing the library door: one glass pill 44pt tall (the door circles' height), the room's mark at 24, then the room name, ›, the lens name at label size (16); its left half and right half are two buttons; on a sheet it prints the room's mark, ›, and the lens word only. It retires on scroll with the band and returns on scroll-up (the door's mechanics); it does not retire under a keyboard. Its accessibility label is the rendered address ('Work, Today') so drive.sh can read it. Nothing else is ever top-left.
- ON A SHEET (document): two top-right glass circles — Properties and ••• — unchanged; the ROOM CHIP at the head of the sheet (the area's mark and name, or 'Unfiled'; the filing door).
- ON A LENS: nothing top-right. The lens's own hero is unchanged (Today its date and day strip; Calendar its day title; Notes, Tasks, Everything their screen names; Inbox its Route/Tidy tabs). The room is not repeated in the hero.
- ON A RECORD CARD: the same room chip in its head beside the name field; the card, its grabber and its minimised pill above the bar are unchanged.
- THE BOTTOM BAR — unchanged: one Liquid Glass capsule, five keys with words — ‹ Back, › Forward, Search, + with the word of what it makes here (Task / Event / Note), Open [n]. One addition: the Open key wears a filled state while a sheet is on screen. Retires on scroll, under a keyboard, under any LivMenu; the surface's own foot (2026-09-08); travels with nothing, now that nothing pushes the desk.
- CARDS (all LivMenu / livSheet, from the edge their button is on): the Rooms card and the Lenses card from the top; from the bottom the Desk card (the grid at .large), the New menu, the Route (six areas) card, the record card, the properties card, the history card, Settings, Trash; the note-verbs menu from the top under •••. The workspace card and the full-screen tab switcher are removed; their contents live in the Rooms card and the Desk card.
- FULL-SCREEN COVERS that remain: Search and Camera. The Desk is no longer one.
- MARKERS: liv.surface.<lens> stays per lens body; liv.surface.document stays for a sheet; the title's label carries the rendered address; liv.overlay.rooms, liv.overlay.lenses and liv.overlay.desk replace liv.overlay.library, liv.overlay.workspace and liv.overlay.tabs; liv.overlay.properties, .settings, .trash, .history unchanged.

**Gestures.**
- Tap the title's room half → Rooms card from the top. Tap its lens half → Lenses card from the top. There is no swipe on the title, no pull, no paging: the two cards are the one door each (rule 4).
- No new horizontal gesture anywhere. Rows keep their own leading/trailing swipes (Inbox, Tasks); the month grid keeps its pager; the editor keeps text selection; nothing in the bar swipes. The first draft's three bar swipes and lens paging are dropped.
- Tap + → makes the lens's kind, stamped with the room (nothing in Everywhere/Unfiled): a task or event rises as a card with the caret in the name, a note lifts as a sheet with the caret in it. Hold + 0.45s or a11y 'More' → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text), unchanged.
- Tap Open [n] → the Desk card (the grid, from the bottom). Tap a card → that sheet over the current room and lens; ✕ → close; pull the card down or pick → dismiss; tap the Inactive shelf → it unfolds in the same card; Close all asks nothing.
- Tap ‹ → pop one step of the single history (room change, lens change, sheet lifted, sheet laid down, record opened from a link). Tap › → un-retrace, browser semantics: a new move drops the forward stack. Both keys unchanged in the bar.
- Tap the title's lens word while a sheet is on screen → the sheet is laid down (stays open) and the lens draws. Tap a row in any lens → a document lifts as a sheet, a record rises as a card, over the same address.
- Tap the room chip on a sheet's head or a record card's head → the six-areas card (Route); one tap files; the chip rolls to the new room; the address does not change.
- In an area room's Inbox, tap a Route row's leading circle → files it into the room you stand in (the row's chip already printed the answer); in Everywhere and Unfiled the circle asks WHERE, as now.
- Swipe a record card down → minimises to the pill above the bar, one at a time; tap → restore; ✕ → drop (unchanged).
- Scroll down 44pt on any lens or sheet → bar, title and top glass keys retire; scroll up or reach the top → they return (unchanged mechanics). The bar retires under a keyboard and under every LivMenu; the title does not retire for a keyboard.
- No edge swipe, no window-level panel drag, no top-left door: those gestures are deleted, so a rightward drag on a list or in the editor means what iOS says it means.

**Capture.** THE BAR'S + (the one visible create door, always present, unchanged in the bar): makes what the lens holds — task in Today/Tasks (due the day you look at), event on the Calendar's shown day, note elsewhere — and stamps it with the ROOM: area = the area you stand in; a person's own room presets its cells exactly as its workspace does now; Everywhere and Unfiled stamp nothing. The instant after: a record rises as a card over the same address with the caret in its name (unchanged), its head chip reading the room; a note lifts as a sheet over the same address with the caret in it, its head chip reading the room or 'Unfiled', Open ticks up by one, the address above it unchanged but for the room's mark. One tap on the chip clears or changes the stamp. A note made in Everywhere or Unfiled IS the Inbox's capture (kinds empty, content print non-zero) and appears in the Inbox in every room until routed. Hold + → the New menu, unchanged, every kind stamped the same way.

FROM OUTSIDE — share sheet (spool, drained at foreground), liv://capture?text=&url=, Search's Create row, the camera's Done: saved first through liv_capture_at, stamped as a catch is stamped today (the active room's stamp, which for the launch room is nothing), never opened, never focused. This is the current rule unchanged; the first draft's 'a catch lands Unfiled' is withdrawn because it would silently change what a person who lives in one room gets. Where it is: the Inbox's Route at the top in every room, with the clerk's 'Work?' chip; Unfiled › Everything if it has no area; Search under 'captures'. Today says '3 captured today · Route them' → the Inbox, same room.

ROUTING: in Everywhere › Inbox and Unfiled › Inbox the Route card asks WHERE with the six areas, as now. In Work › Inbox the question is already answered by where you stand: the row's chip prints 'Work?' (or the clerk's own guess when it differs, so a guess is never hidden by a room), and the leading circle accepts it in one tap. A capture routed to 'Not a note…' → Task / Event / Link, unchanged. Filing is a tap on furniture; it is never asked at capture; nothing asks twice.

**Back, links and catches.** ONE HISTORY, BOTH KEYS. LivReturns keeps its shape (typed, capped at 20, with its forward stack) but its element becomes the Address {room, lens, doc?} and its policy becomes: EVERY move pushes — a room change, a lens change, a sheet lifted, a sheet laid down, a record opened from a link — and nothing clears except a box change; a new move drops the forward stack, as a browser does. `here` is computed in one place as the current Address. ‹ pops one address and lands on it exactly: landing on an address with a doc re-lifts that sheet (re-minting the tab if it was closed); landing without one lays the sheet down but never closes it. › retraces the pop. The dead ‹ after opening from the Notes list disappears because 'Work › Notes' and 'Work › Notes + sheet' are different addresses. The two navigation systems (the panel's `go` clearing, `openDocument` pushing) become one verb, `desk.go(to: Address)`, with `open(id)` deriving the address (same room, same lens, doc = id) and `openAsCard` untouched. Re-opening the sheet already on screen is not a step (unchanged). Because every move pushes, Forward is useful for the first time instead of dimmed for most of its life.

LINKS AND ARRIVALS: liv://today|notes|inbox|calendar|tasks|everything → Everywhere › that lens (a lens address, never a sheet). liv://entity/<id> → box.refresh if unknown, then open: a document lifts as a sheet over the address you are at (or Everywhere › Today on a cold launch), a record rises as a card; the address is pushed first so ‹ returns. liv://capture (bare) → a new note lifted over the current address, stamped as + stamps; with a payload → catchText, saved, not opened. liv://capture/photo → camera, unchanged. A notification tap → the same open. Unknown hosts do nothing. Every arrival still runs surfaceCleanup (Rooms/Lenses/Desk cards, menus, properties, history, search, camera, settings, trash closed) so nothing lands behind a card. liv://<area> is not added now; it is one line when the owner wants it (a Room in the route table).

WORKSPACES AND FILTERS: a workspace is a room of the person's making, listed in the Rooms card under the furnished ones with its own emoji, edited and trashed from a context menu, made by 'New room…' (Name, Area, Tags — the WorkspaceSwitcher's form, kept). Walking into it pushes an address, keeps the lens, keeps the desk, closes the cards; nothing lands on Notes. A saved filter is a toggle at the foot of the Lenses card, ANDed onto the room and lens at the seam by the core's parser; the lens chip in Search names 'Work · Overdue' as it names the lens today. Search is global by default and shows the room as a constraint chip that can be dropped, so standing in Work does not hide the rest of the house from a search.

**Survives in code.** Bar.swift — the whole file: five keys, Forward included, words under keys, LivBar metrics in Theme.swift; the one change is a filled state on the Open key while a sheet is on screen.; Plane.swift DeskPlanes — the desk (tabs, activeTabId, lastUsed) and spots per Feature, unchanged in shape; persistence moves from Workspace.swift:333's desk.v3.<ws> keys to one box-wide desk.v4 key, the v3 keys folded on first read and never deleted (the same fold Plane.swift:304-360 already does for v2).; Positions.swift and every lens's position token (Today, Calendar, Tasks, Everything, Inbox) — 'a tool is singular' stays verbatim; positions are room-independent.; Tabs.swift — TabSwitcher's body, TabCard, InactiveTabs, the LivTabs inactive predicate, liveTabs/inactiveTabs, close/closeInactive, the '3 open' footer and the bottom-right New-note card: hosted in a livSheet(from: .bottom) instead of App.swift:267's fullScreenCover.; Record.swift, openAsCard, the minimised pill, TabShape.of — the record card is untouched and gains a room chip in its head.; The six view bodies — Today.swift, Notes.swift (NotesList), Inbox.swift, Calendar.swift/Month.swift, Tasks.swift, Everything.swift. Notes, Calendar, Tasks and Everything already call workspaces.admits (Notes.swift:135, Calendar.swift:981/992, Tasks.swift:172, Everything.swift:139); the room composes onto that same query. Inbox.swift:42-45 keeps its stated no-lens rule verbatim.; Workspace.swift — WorkspaceModel, LivTerms, the stamp, admits, refreshLens through liv_query_ids_at; the room's predicate is area:<name> or no:area (services/src/search.rs:125, TermOp::No — already in the grammar) ANDed onto the same query. No FFI change.; WorkspaceSwitch.swift's New-workspace form and lens rows — reused as the Rooms card's 'New room…' form and the room's edit form.; Navigate.swift Feature, inOrder, makes; LivReturns as the typed capped stack with its forward stack, re-typed over Address with the one-policy push.; Chrome.swift chromeAway/chromeHomeAgain, surfaceCleanup, requestFocus/adoptCapture, open/openAsCard, createSomething; Desk.swift's createHere/catchText, the glass top keys, EntityTabBody, the note-verbs menu, trash chip and undo, the surfaceFoot.; Menu.swift LivMenu / livSheet(from:) — including the from-the-top card recipe at Menu.swift:292 that the workspace card already uses; Rows.swift, Kit.swift (LivName, nameless-row-says-its-kind), Glyph.swift (LivArea marks — promoted to the title and the Rooms card), Theme.swift, Surface.swift markers.; Panel.swift's ViewCounts — rewritten in place as PlaceCounts: the same single pass, filling rooms × lenses instead of six numbers, with a launch-flag shape self-check in suites.sh.; Search.swift, Detail.swift (properties), History.swift, Links.swift, Settings.swift, Trash.swift, Camera/Scan, Routes.swift (route table re-aimed at addresses), Notify.swift, Catch.swift and the share extension, Box.swift (every liv_* call, unchanged), Furnish.swift.; drive.sh, suites.sh and the harness — with two new checks, address (reads the title's rendered text) and room (a room narrows Notes/Calendar/Tasks/Everything and does not narrow Today/Inbox); the 'Notes lists more than open tabs' guard still holds for the Notes lens; the areas check is unchanged.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift's LibraryPanel (its rows, the Notesnook row builder, the foot), PanelDrag.swift (the window-level recognizer), Glyph.swift:590 PanelMark, Theme.swift:243 LivPanel (width, peek, wash, deskRadius), Chrome.swift libraryShown/libraryDrawn/setLibrary/panelProgress/deskShift/panelOut, and Desk.swift's square-leading-corner mask and wash overlay for the pushed desk — the library panel and everything that pushed the desk.; App.swift:267's fullScreenCover for the switcher — the same body rises as a card.; WorkspaceSwitch.swift as a separate top sheet hanging from the panel's foot — folded into the Rooms card (its form survives).; Chrome.swift go(feature), goToRoot, showList, land(.state)/(.document), openDocument's `state = .notes`, the `state == .notes` guard on openDoc (Chrome.swift:184), `here` computed from state, the special case that made a 'Notes' tap deselect a document — replaced by go(to: Address).; Feature.notes as the render state for documents; DeskHost choosing EntityTabBody vs NotesList by openDoc (Desk.swift:48-66) — the sheet is its own address component drawn over any lens.; adopt(workspace:) landing on .notes and clearing returns (Chrome.swift:678-690); DeskPlanes(workspace:) and the per-workspace desk (desk.v3.<ws> as live keys) — one desk per box; WorkspaceModel.activeKey's meaning becomes the Room.; Everything.swift:144's Swift `area == nil` test for the Unfiled slice — the slice asks the core the same no:area question the Unfiled room asks (one test, rule 4).; liv.overlay.library, liv.overlay.tabs, liv.overlay.workspace and drive.sh's panel and workspace checks — replaced by rooms/lenses/desk overlays and the address/room checks.; NOT thrown away, withdrawn from the first draft: the › Forward key, LivReturns.forwards and goForward; the TabCard grid and the Inactive shelf; the workspace stamp on catches from outside; the 2026-08-05 exemption for Today and Inbox.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- RESOLVED — 'Notes is two things': Notes is only a lens; a sheet renders as address.doc over any lens; the desk has a state of its own.
- RESOLVED — 'two doors on two edges': the Notes lens (a place, via the title at the top) and the Desk card (via Open at the thumb) are named differently and one lists what LIVES here while the other holds what is LIFTED; the edges differ because the things differ.
- RESOLVED — 'Open N counts an invisible container': the title prints the place; Open counts sheets and the card it raises is titled by that count; nothing else on screen is ever called open.
- RESOLVED — 'Notes means three surfaces': the room and the lens are printed; the sheet is a third thing with its own chrome (room chip, Properties, •••).
- RESOLVED — 'same note, different side effects by route': one verb, one push policy, every route lands at an address that ‹ can pop and › can retrace.
- RESOLVED — 'back key is a document-history key': ‹ undoes the last move of any kind; › retraces it; both keys keep their slots and finally mean what their glyphs say.
- RESOLVED — 'goToRoot is a patch': deleted; a lens tapped again is a no-op in all six; laying a sheet down is its own verb, visible as the title's lens word.
- PARTLY — 'positions vs documents unseen': a lens resuming shows in its own hero (the month, the day, the slice) and a sheet shows in the Desk card and the filled Open key; still nothing says 'parked' about a position.
- NOT RESOLVED — '+ is local or navigating by kind': a note still lifts a full sheet and a task a half card; only the address under them now stays put.
- RESOLVED — 'switcher is a mode and the only way back': the desk is a card over the room; ‹ also reaches a laid-down sheet from any lens because laying it down pushed.
- RESOLVED — 'panel fill answers state not surface': no panel; the title prints room and lens, the Open key shows the desk is up, a card is a card.
- PARTLY — 'Inbox is a queue, Notes a shelf': the Lenses card draws Today and Inbox as the 'Everywhere' group and the four room-lenses as 'In <room>', so the two kinds of surface are visibly different; the Inbox is still listed as a sibling lens.
- On the 18 questions: the desk has its own state (yes); the title, not a panel, says where you are and never says Notes for a sheet; Notes names the lens, Open names the desk, the list is the lens; the grid lives as the desk and the list lives as the lens, and they stop competing because they are named as different things; the number counts sheets and the card shows them; ‹ undoes any move and › retraces; one push policy; the root-of-view rule is gone; resuming shows in the hero, open shows in the card; Open is a card, not a mode; + labels its kind and stays put (the kind still decides the container — unresolved); the six rows are six lenses of one room, drawn in two groups; a note you are done with is laid down where you stood; the shape keeps records as cards and notes as sheets with no bar of views; the go-somewhere control stays at the top, by the owner's own state-up/actions-down ruling and the measured bar; the home is Everywhere › Today and a room change keeps the lens; the two doors are honest because they are two things; and it must be mocked first (three screens drawn above) and proven by a drive.sh assertion on the title's rendered text.

**Changed after the judges.**
- THE ADDRESS LEFT THE BAR. Measured: the capsule is 306pt at 390pt (LivBar.sideInset 42 ×2), five 61pt slots; a two-word pill wide enough for 'Learning › Calendar' pushes four keys under the 44pt touch floor, and 'Family & Friends › Everything' fits in no bar. It also broke the owner's own separation (state up, actions down, 2026-08-17) and his literal bar (2026-08-23, 2026-09-05). The address is now a 44pt glass chevron title at the top-left where the door was, at label size — Notion Calendar's idiom, which the Calendar already uses — full on a lens (~358pt available), mark + lens on a sheet (~254pt available). Three judges' 'measure the pill / too small / split pill fails touch' are answered by numbers, not prose.
- THE BAR IS UNTOUCHED AND THE RETENTION IS OWNED. Five keys, Forward kept (with every move pushing, it works for the first time), the numbered box kept: the owner ruled this set literally, twice. The cost judge asked to replace the two Obsidian lifts or own them; owned, with the reason.
- THE 2026-08-05 EXEMPTION IS RESTORED. Today and Inbox list the whole house in every room; the room narrows the four lenses the workspace lens narrows today; in every lens the room means exactly two things (what + stamps, where Route files). The Lenses card draws the two groups so the rule is furniture. 'My day is missing' cannot happen.
- CATCHES FROM OUTSIDE KEEP THE CURRENT STAMP. The first draft's 'lands Unfiled' was a silent change for a person who lives in one room; withdrawn, the existing rule stands.
- THE DESK CARD IS THE GRID, NOT A STRIP. The switcher body (TabCard, Inactive shelf, footer, New note bottom-right) is hosted in a card instead of a cover; 'tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box' is kept in content and order; the one container change is put to the owner as one sentence. The strip — the element five directions drew and the owner removed on 2026-08-23 — is gone from this one.
- EVERY NEW GESTURE IS DROPPED. No pill swipe, no Open-key swipe, no lens paging, no pull on the title: the hand judge's 'three swipes in one bar' and the cost judge's 'pick pill-only' are both answered by taps only, and rule 4's one door per thing holds.
- UNFILED IS ONE LINE, NOT AN FFI VERB. The core grammar already has no:<key> (services/src/search.rs:125, TermOp::No); the Unfiled room lenses no:area at the seam, and Everything's Swift-side `area == nil` slice test routes through the same query (rule 4). No owner-gated core change.
- ONE WALK, ONE SHAPE CHECK. PlaceCounts replaces ViewCounts with the same single pass filling a rooms × lenses table (two tables in the first draft, two walks); it ships with a launch-flag shape self-check in suites.sh, honestly labelled as Swift-side.
- 'ALL' IS RENAMED 'EVERYWHERE' so the room and Everything's All slice stop sharing a word; the Inbox no longer means a different list per room, so that collision is gone too.
- THE ROOMS CARD'S TWO GROUPS ARE DEFINED: a room is a place with a stamp; the furnished six stamp area:<name>; the person's rooms are today's workspaces. The word still stretches (conceded), but the grouping is a rule, not a drawing.
- THE TAP TABLE IS WRITTEN OUT, and the keyboard-up state is answered: the title does not retire for a keyboard, so a sheet with the keyboard raised still has a way somewhere.
- THE + STAMP IS KEPT, MADE LOUD, AND ARGUED rather than assumed: the alternative loses the new note from the list it was made in. It is listed under conceded and put to the owner on the mockup.
- A CODE/DOC DISCREPANCY WAS SURFACED: Today.swift applies the workspace lens although the 2026-08-05 doc says it ignores it; the direction follows the doc and flags the four call sites for the owner.
- survives_in_code / thrown_away were re-checked against the tree: Forward, the grid, the workspace-stamp rule and the exemption move from thrown to kept; Menu.swift:292's top-card recipe, Workspace.swift's admits seam, App.swift:267's cover and Everything.swift:144's slice test are cited by line.

**Conceded.**
- + in an area room stamps that room. It is filing-after-walking, not filing-after-thinking; the July finding is honoured only by making Everywhere the home and the stamp visible and reversible. It needs the owner's word and this direction cannot remove it without breaking 'a surface named after a thing contains it'.
- The go-somewhere control is at the top, a regrip from a right thumb — no worse than the door it replaces, and much wider, but the diagnosis asked for the thumb zone and the answer is no.
- A wrong filing is a wrong room. Browsing by area exposes the 57% ambiguity the lens chip used to hide; only measured retrieval can say whether the map pays for itself, and nobody has measured it.
- Rooms are an axis the owner did not ask for. He asked to rethink navigation, tabs and views; this adds the areas as the map because the product doc calls them the product, and that is an argument he has not yet heard.
- Crossing the grid — room AND lens — costs four taps, a cost that did not exist because rooms did not exist.
- No drag replaces the panel's drag-from-anywhere; only the system pull on a card remains, and the panel's big-target gesture that people found is lost.
- The Desk card still changes the switcher's container (a card, not a cover) and needs his word; the fallback is the cover, unchanged.
- The Inbox's leading circle files into the room you stand in — one control whose consequence depends on the printed room. Smaller than the first draft's mode error, but a mode all the same.
- The address is printed only while the top chrome is up; after a scroll the app says where you are only once you scroll back. Same as the door today, and still a gap.
- The printed room does less in Today and Inbox than in the other four lenses; the Lenses card's two groups explain it, and a first-day user will still notice.
- The room counts are checked by a Swift-side shape self-check, not a Rust cost test, because the walk lives on the shell's side of the snapshot.
- Vagueness 9 stays: + lifts a sheet for a note and rises a card for a task; the container still follows the kind.
- It stacks on revs 54–61, which are unverified on a device, and its two new harness checks do not exist yet.

**Open questions.**
- The + stamp in an area room (filing-after-walking): the owner's word, on the mockup, on 'a note made in Work › Notes is a Work note'. The alternative loses the note from the list it was made in.
- The Desk card versus the cover: the grid, order, shelf and footer are as they appeared; only the container changes. One sentence from him; the direction survives either answer.
- Today's list: design/today-inbox-study.md rules that Today ignores the lens, but Today.swift:584/604/687/716 apply it today (the changelog calls the last gap 'closed'). This direction follows the doc (Today global in every room). The owner should confirm which the code should do; four `admits` calls change either way.
- Should opening a sheet walk you into its room (the title changes to where it lives) instead of laying it over where you stand? The seed argues yes; this proposal chose no to keep 'a thing opens over where you stand'. One line to flip.
- 'Everywhere' as the launch room's name (in place of 'All', which Everything's slice already uses). Alternatives: the box's own name; 'Home' is taken by an area.
- Workspaces as rooms: a tag-only workspace is a room that is not an area. Alternative: keep workspaces as a separate group titled by the person's name for them, or retire the concept into 'New room… (Area, Tags)'.
- Whether the address title should also be the one place the room is printed, or the lens heroes should carry the room's mark too (loudness versus 'a fact printed once').
- Which drive.sh assertions change: tour reads the title's rendered address; panel becomes rooms/lenses; grid becomes desk; a new room check proves a room narrows four lenses and not two; each broken once on purpose before its green is trusted.
- The PlaceCounts shape self-check lives in suites.sh (Swift side of the snapshot); whether the owner wants a Rust cost test for the query the seam composes (liv_query_ids_at over no:area) as well.
- liv://<area> and a Room in the route table — trivial, unasked; not added.
- The unverified revs 54–61 sit under this; the first build.sh + drive.sh on a simulator should run before any of this is built.

### 6. The Line

*The whole app is one line at the foot of the screen — type into it to find or make, read it to know where you are, ‹ at its head to go back — and one row of worded chips beneath it to go somewhere: the open note by name, the six views, the areas, the lenses; no drawer, no five-key bar, no full-screen mode.*

**1. The navigation concept.** THE NAVIGATION CONCEPT. Liv becomes a box you talk to through one line. The foot of every surface is a single glass capsule — the Line — and beneath it one row of glass chips on the canvas — the Tray. Nothing else is persistent: the top-left is empty on every surface (the library door, the panel, the five-key bar, the numbered box, the switcher cover and the › key are gone; the ‹ moves into the Line).

The Line is one field with three jobs and one key at each end. (a) FIND: focus it and type; the existing Search list (Box.search → liv_search_at, kind groups, facet chips, workspace lens, 'Showing N of M') appears above it, read top-down like every other list in the app — the field moved to the foot, the list did not turn over. (b) MAKE: the words are the name. A 'New <kind> "words"' row is PINNED between the hits and the field, always visible, one tap. Return opens the FIRST hit when there are hits; Return creates only when there is nothing to open. So find-or-create is the standing gesture and never mints a near-duplicate by accident (the duplicate-after-failed-search metric is the one it must not move). The kind it makes is the kind the place holds (Feature.makes): a task on Today/Tasks due the shown day, an event on the shown Calendar day, a note everywhere else. Trailing key: + (tap = make what this place holds, nameless, caret in the name — today's one-tap +, unchanged; hold 0.45s = the New menu); while words are in the field the trailing key is ✕ (clear). (c) SAY WHERE YOU ARE: at rest on a view the Line reads what it makes here ('Find or add a task'); at rest on a document it prints the document's title in full ink — a document has a name, a view has a chip. Leading key: ‹ Back whenever LivReturns has somewhere to go, otherwise the magnifier (an ornament, not a key). Rename is not in the Line: the title is the note's first line and the properties card's LivName field, one grammar; tapping the Line always means find.

The Tray is one 44pt row of glass chips with WORDS, `label` (16) type, at LivRow.touch. Pinned at its leading edge: the DESK chip — the active document's title and the count ('▢ Kitchen rebuild · 3'; absent when nothing is open). After it, scrolling: the six views in Feature.inOrder as worded chips (Today · Notes · Inbox 2 · Calendar · Tasks · Everything), the current one in the panel row's soft fill (the 2026-08-17 'highlighted row', never accent); a divider; the AREAS as chips on the views that wear a lens (Notes, Calendar, Tasks, Everything — the furniture, at the thumb, one tap to see one area); saved filters (lit when active); the workspace chip 'Name ▾' (the existing card from the bottom, which also holds New filter); ⚙ Settings (Trash is a card inside it). The row auto-scrolls so the current chip is on screen on arrival. Places on the left, lenses on the right; every chip is a lens over the same box; nothing in the row is a verb — the only verbs in the app are in the Line. Measured on 390pt: '▢ Kitchen re… · 3' + Today + Notes + Inbox are visible at rest and Calendar is cut mid-word, which is the scroll's own affordance; Tasks and Everything are a thumb-flick away.

The desk gets its own state. `state` becomes `.view(Feature) | .desk`; a document renders only in `.desk`; `openDoc` is the active tab iff state == .desk. 'Notes' is therefore ONE thing: the view whose root is every note by recency. The desk's face is the pinned chip: from any view, one tap on the name returns to the active document, no keyboard, no mode. Its root — tap the chip while you stand on the desk, or pull the Line up from anywhere — is the desk SHEET: the existing TabSwitcher grid (TabCard with title and excerpt, the New-note card bottom-right, the Inactive fold, Close all) in a livSheet from the bottom, sunk to the thumb, the surface behind it washed. Swiping the Line sideways ON THE DESK steps through open documents in MRU order (a title swipes; a field does not — on a view the Line does not swipe).

Going somewhere is one policy: every arrival — chip, hit, link, swipe, sheet pick, notification, liv:// — pushes onto LivReturns; nothing clears it but a workspace switch. Back is the ‹ in the Line or the left-EDGE swipe (iOS's own; PanelDrag's recognizer with a 20pt edge zone, the previous surface following the finger). There is no forward.

Cold launch: Today, the Line at rest reading 'Find or add a task' with the magnifier (nothing to go back to), the Tray with the desk chip if anything is open and Today filled. Records (task, event) open as cards over where you stand and minimise to the pill above the Line — unchanged.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS are the desk, and the desk has a NAME on every surface. The data model is DeskPlanes verbatim (one app-wide desk per workspace, entity tabs, lastUsed stamps, the inactive predicate). What changes is where it is SEEN: (1) the Tray's pinned first chip prints the active document's title and the count — '▢ Kitchen rebuild · 3' — and it wears the fill while you stand on the desk; when nothing is active it prints the most-recently-used tab; when the desk is empty it is absent. (2) On a view, tapping it opens that document (state = .desk, the view pushed) — 'the note I was in' from Calendar is one tap on a name, not a count, and no keyboard. (3) On the desk, tapping it opens the desk SHEET — the desk's root, the same rule as tapping the view chip you stand on. The sheet is the switcher the owner asked to keep ('tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box'): TabCard cards in two columns with title and excerpt, the active card ringed, ✕ per card, New note bottom-right, 'Inactive · 4' folded at the head with Close all inside (LivTabs predicate unchanged; Close all asks nothing, tabs are device state). What changes is the container: a livSheet from the bottom over the washed surface, not a fullScreenCover; it covers the Line as the workspace card covers the bar (2026-09-08); a tap on the wash or a pull-down closes it; a card tap → state = .desk, the tab focused, the surface pushed as the way back.

     ┌──────────────────────────┐
     │ (Calendar, washed)       │
     │ ▸ Inactive 4   Close all │
     │ ┌─────────┐ ┌─────────┐  │
     │ │Q4 plan  │ │kitchen- │  │
     │ │Three ri…│ │plan.pdf │  │
     │ └─────────┘ └─────────┘  │
     │ ┌─────────┐ ┌─────────┐  │
     │ │Kitchen ●│ │ + New   │  │
     │ │rebuild  │ │   note  │  │
     │ │Get thre…│ │         │  │
     │ └─────────┘ └─────────┘  │
     │ 3 open                   │
     └──────────────────────────┘

(4) Pulling the Line upward from any surface opens the same sheet without a keyboard (the thumb-only shortcut). (5) Focusing the EMPTY Line still shows the sheet as the empty state of find (keyboard up) — a third door, kept because it costs nothing, but not the taught one. (6) Swiping the Line left/right while on the desk cycles the open documents in MRU order. (7) A document is drawn under its own state `.desk`: the Line prints its title, no view chip is filled, the desk chip is, and the top-right carries the document's own two keys (Properties, •••) as today. The full-screen cover, the numbered box, the '+ | N open | Done' footer and the Inactive shelf-as-a-screen die; the cards, the excerpt and the grid survive.

THE NOTE LIST is the Notes VIEW and nothing else: one of six chips, root = every non-trashed, non-archived note by the log's recency through the workspace lens (NotesList unchanged), row tap → the note lands on the desk (state = .desk, tab minted or reused, pushed). It never draws a document, never needs goToRoot, and tapping its chip while you are already on it scrolls to the top. The Notes list is the SHELF (what exists, 134 rows); the desk sheet is what is OPEN (2–6 cards); the desk chip is the one you are in. Three things, three names, one row at the thumb — the diagnosis's 'two doors on two edges to the same room' collapses into one line.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** The six views are six worded chips in one scrolling row: Today · Notes · Inbox · Calendar · Tasks · Everything (Feature.inOrder, one declared order). Each is a lens over the same box with one remembered position (`spots`: month/day, day/piles, filter/groups, slice, lens — unchanged); arriving restores it. They are peers in the Tray, but the row has a grammar the flat panel lacked: the DESK chip is pinned at the left edge and names a document because the desk is not a view; the Inbox chip carries its count as an ink digit when non-zero because it is the one place that must be emptied (a queue, drawn as a queue); Today is home (cold launch, workspace switch); the divider separates places from lenses; the AREAS sit first among the lenses on the four views that wear one, so the furniture is on the surface, not in a picker — 'arrive furnished' answered by chips, not by empty-state prose. Today and Inbox show no area chips (they ignore lenses by rule; Route asks WHERE there). Tapping a chip = `go(.view(f))`, which pushes and never deselects a tab; the desk keeps its active document, and the pinned chip says so by name in every view. Tapping the filled chip you stand on = its root (scroll to top; for the desk, the sheet) and does not push. The Tray retires on the first 8pt of downward scroll and the Line at 44pt with the existing hysteresis; both return on scroll-up or at the top; neither retires on an empty surface. Records are not in the hierarchy: a task or event opens as a card over the chip you stand on and minimises to the pill above the Line. Workspaces and filters are chips at the tail of the row — 'workspaces are picked, not typed' and 'a filter is not a workspace' both hold: area and filter chips toggle in place and intersect with the workspace lens; the workspace chip opens the existing card (edge = bottom, where its button is). A workspace switch swaps the desk (per-workspace planes kept), clears the way back, and lands on Today.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against each resemblance the references reader listed: (1) THE BOTTOM CAPSULE with five browser keys in Obsidian's order and proportions — gone; what remains is a text field that creates tasks by place with one ‹ at its head, which is Safari's pill with 'aA' and reload in it, not Obsidian's bar. (2) THE NUMBERED BOX (Obsidian's daily-note key with a count) — gone; the desk is a chip that prints the document's NAME and the count, and one tap opens the named document. (3) THE TAB SWITCHER 'verbatim in structure' as a full-screen cover behind a key — the cards survive, the mode does not: they live in a half sheet over the surface you stand on, and the taught door is a name. (4) LEFT DRAWER = LIBRARY with Obsidian's foot (name, count line, gear) — deleted entirely: no drawer, no edge to swipe it from, no wash, no pushed desk; the workspace is a chip. (5) TOP-LEFT SIDEBAR TOGGLE + TOP-RIGHT ELLIPSIS — the top-left is empty on every surface; the top-right pair appears only on a document and is Liv's (Properties + •••, ruled 2026-09-07). (6) EDGE-SWIPE FOR THE DRAWER — the edge swipe is iOS's own back. (7) BACK/FORWARD OVER A HISTORY STACK, 'the most Obsidian thing in the app' — one ‹, one meaning (the last arrival of any kind), inside the field; forward deleted. (8) THE THREE-REGION MODEL — gone: there is a surface and a line. (9) RECENTS LIST + TAB GRID as two answers to 'get me back' — the list is the shelf, the sheet is the desk, the chip is the one you are in; none is a mode. (10) FIND-OR-CREATE, 'Obsidian's quick switcher' — the one resemblance the design inverts rather than removes: Obsidian's switcher is a modal behind a key that only makes notes; here it is the standing surface and makes what the place holds, and it opens before it creates. (11) The kinds-are-peers diagnosis — 'one kind got the main surface and the rest got a drawer' — has no purchase: no drawer, no main kind; a document is something behind the Line, Today is the face, and the areas are chips on the surface.

What remains shared with Obsidian is editor-level and owner-ruled (inline title, tap-follows-link, monochrome glyphs, grey ground with one accent) plus chrome-hiding on scroll. None of it is navigation.

Against the three rejected shapes, honestly: with words on all six chips the Tray is CLOSER to a tab row than the glyph version was, and the owner rejected the four floors on sight. The differences are real but must be seen to be believed: the four floors put five keys — three views and two verbs — in one capsule and deleted the library; the Tray is a scrolling row of eleven-plus glass chips of one grammar (places, then lenses, then settings), mixes in no verb, names the open document by name, and sits under a field that is the only action. It is the Tasks view's own chip row promoted to the app's foot, not ClickUp's tab bar. One-list-three-arrangements and views-as-tabs are not touched. The grid-as-Notes-root is the opposite of this: Notes is the full list; the grid is a sheet. Nearest living relatives are Arc Search's pill (the field) and Todoist's filter-chip row (the Tray) — neither a notes app, neither with a drawer, and neither makes typed words into a dated task by where you stand.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- It is the nearest living relative of the rejected four-floor bar, and with words on all six chips it is nearer than before. The defences (homogeneous scrolling chips, no verb in the row, the desk named, lenses in the same grammar) are real, but the owner rejects on sight; this needs a mockup on the simulator, not a paragraph.
- The foot grows: Line 44 + gap 6 + Tray 44 = ~94pt at rest against the bar's 66 — 28pt worse on Calendar, where the bar was already 'always taking up space'. The Tray leaves on the first 8pt of scroll (then 50pt, then 0 at 44pt), which the bar never did; at rest it is simply worse.
- With words at `label` 16, the Tray is ~610pt long on a 390pt phone: Today, Notes and Inbox are one tap; Tasks and Everything are a flick and a tap; with the area chips the lens tail is a longer flick. The panel put all six one tap after the door; this puts four of them one tap and two of them two.
- The pinned desk chip eats ~120pt of the Tray on every surface while anything is open, and a long title is truncated to ~10 characters plus the count — the name is better than a number, but it is a short name.
- The Line carries two keys (‹ at the head, + / ✕ at the tail) and a field between them; on a document it prints a title that is not tappable for rename. Three targets in one capsule is more to learn than Safari's pill, though Safari's has the same count.
- Forward is deleted. The owner ruled 'a browser's set, literally' on 2026-08-23; this keeps Back with one meaning and drops Forward, the numbered box and the full-screen switcher — one reversal to put to him, not four footnotes.
- Every find raises the keyboard. The desk has two keyboardless doors now (the named chip, the pull-up), so 'my open notes' costs no keyboard — but the empty-Line-shows-the-desk door still exists and a tap-only person will meet it.
- Return opens the first hit; creating the exact words when a near miss exists is one tap on the pinned New row. Safer for duplicates, one tap slower for the person who meant to create. The metric decides whether the extra tap is felt.
- Results read top-down with the field at the foot, so the FIRST hit — the one Return opens — is the farthest row from the thumb; Return is the way to it, the thumb is not.
- The desk sheet shows four cards in its half height (two columns, sunk to the thumb); with six or more open it scrolls or is pulled to full height. The full-screen switcher showed eight.
- Back is one key and one edge swipe, no longer a drag from anywhere: discoverable, and it no longer collides with the editor's selection drag or Calendar's day pager, but the from-anywhere drag the panel taught since 2026-08-08 is gone.
- Trash and Settings move one door deeper (a gear chip at the Tray's tail; Trash inside Settings), and the panel's per-view counts vanish (only Inbox keeps one).
- The furnishing study says navigation should dominate search once Everything exists; a field as the app's face may push the mix the wrong way. The Tray with worded places and area chips is the counter-weight, and the mix must be measured, not asserted.
- The + ruling 'one tap makes what the place holds' survives only because a + is drawn INSIDE the capsule; a purist reading of 'one field' would drop it and cost a tap.
- While the editor's keyboard is up the Line and Tray retire (2026-08-02 stands): inside a note, while writing, there is no navigation — the same cul-de-sac every direction has; the keyboard's own ⌄ is the way to the Line.
- Mockup-first and NOT verified: nothing here has been drawn on a device; drive.sh tour/panel/bar/grid all need rewriting before a green means anything, and revs 54–61 beneath it are themselves unverified.

**Wireframes.**

*Cold launch — Today, the Line at rest, three notes open*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▪▪▪ │
│                            │
│ Tuesday 9 September        │
│  M   T   W   T   F   S   S │
│  7   8  [9]  10  11  12  13│
│                            │
│ ○ Call the plumber    Work │
│ ○ Renew passport      Home │
│ ○ Draft Q4 plan       Work │
│                            │
│ ▸ LATE 2                   │
│ ▸ Done 3                   │
│                            │
│ 4 captured today · Route   │
│                            │
│ ╭────────────────────────╮ │
│ │ ⌕  Find or add a task + │ │
│ ╰────────────────────────╯ │
│ ▢Kitchen…3 [Today] Notes Inb│
│            ───             │
└────────────────────────────┘
```
No top-left door, no top-right key. The Line is the bar's glass (LivBar renamed LivField, height 44): leading magnifier because there is nothing to go back to; placeholder 'Find or add a task' because Today makes tasks (Feature.makes); trailing + = one-tap make, hold = New menu. The Tray is glass chips on the canvas at `label` 16, touch 44: pinned '▢ Kitchen re… · 3' (the active document by name, the count after it), then Today in the soft fill, Notes, 'Inbox 2' (ink digit), Calendar cut at the edge — the row scrolls; Tasks, Everything, the divider, the areas (none on Today), saved filters, 'All ▾' and ⚙ are off to the right. Tap the desk chip → Kitchen rebuild, no keyboard. Scroll 8pt → the Tray leaves; 44pt → the Line follows.

*The Line focused — typing 'kitchen' on Today*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▪▪▪ │
│                            │
│ NOTES                      │
│ ≡ Kitchen rebuild     Home │
│ ≡ Kitchen — paint     Home │
│ FILES                      │
│ ▣ kitchen-plan.pdf         │
│ TASKS                      │
│ ○ Kitchen tiles quote      │
│                            │
│ Type ▸ note · task  Area ▸ │
│ ○ New task “kitchen”     › │
│ ╭────────────────────────╮ │
│ │ ⌕  kitchen|           ✕ │ │
│ ╰────────────────────────╯ │
│ q w e r t y u i o p        │
│  a s d f g h j k l         │
│   z x c v b n m  ⌫         │
│ 123      space      return │
└────────────────────────────┘
```
Search's list, unchanged, top-down: hits grouped by kind in Search's order, the workspace lens applied, 'Showing 200 of M' when capped. Below it the facet line (the existing constraint chips: tap a value → Only / Hide / Any in the bottom menu; the field holds only words). Pinned above the field: 'New task "kitchen"' — a task because we stood on Today (due the shown day; 'New note' on Notes/Inbox/Everything/the desk, 'New event' on Calendar) — always visible, one tap. Return opens the FIRST hit (Kitchen rebuild); it creates only when the list is empty. The trailing key is ✕ while words are in the field. The Tray is hidden under the keyboard. Tapping a note lands it on the desk; tapping a task raises its card over Today. Dismiss = tap the washed surface above the list or pull the sheet down.

*A document on the desk*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41            (⚌)  (•••) │
│                            │
│ Kitchen rebuild            │
│                            │
│ Get three quotes before    │
│ the end of the month.      │
│ Tiles from [[Ola's place]] │
│ — ask about the grey ones. │
│                            │
│ - [ ] call the plumber     │
│ - [x] measure the wall     │
│                            │
│                            │
│                            │
│                            │
│ ╭────────────────────────╮ │
│ │ ‹  Kitchen rebuild    + │ │
│ ╰────────────────────────╯ │
│ [▢Kitchen…3] Today Notes In│
│            ───             │
└────────────────────────────┘
```
state == .desk. The Line prints the document's title in full ink; its head is ‹ because Today is on the stack (this note was opened from screen 2's hit list); tap ‹ or swipe from the left edge → Today. The desk chip wears the fill; no view chip does. Tap the desk chip here → the desk sheet (its root: the TabCard grid, sunk to the thumb, over this note washed). Swipe the Line left/right → next/previous open document, MRU. Tap the Line's middle → find, words empty, the sheet shows the desk as its empty state. Top-right: the document's own Properties and ••• glass circles, ruled 2026-09-07 — they belong to the note and exist on no other surface. The + makes a second note. While the editor's keyboard is up the Line and Tray retire, as the bar does today.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- THE LINE — one Liquid Glass capsule at the foot (LivField in Theme.swift: LivBar's material renamed; height 44 = LivRow.touch; corner = capsule). Head (44pt zone): ‹ Back while LivReturns has a place, else the magnifier glyph (ornament). Middle: placeholder naming what it makes here on a view ('Find or add a task' / 'Find or add an event' / 'Find or write a note'), the document's title in full ink on the desk, or the typed words. Tail (44pt zone): + (tap = make here, hold = New menu) at rest; ✕ (clear) while words are in the field. Retires at 44pt of scroll and under the editor's keyboard; never retires on an empty surface.
- THE TRAY — one 44pt band beneath the Line (gap 6), glass chips on the canvas, `label` (16) text, chip height 32 in a 44 touch (LivTray tokens in Theme.swift), horizontally scrolling: PINNED first, the desk chip '▢ <active title, truncated> · N' (only when N ≥ 1; filled on the desk) · then scrolling: six view chips with words in Feature.inOrder, the current one in the panel row's soft fill, Inbox carrying its count as an ink digit when non-zero · a 1pt divider · area chips (only on Notes, Calendar, Tasks, Everything; lit when active) · saved-filter chips (lit when active) · workspace chip 'Name ▾' · ⚙ Settings. Retires at 8pt of scroll and under the keyboard.
- LivTopScrim — status-bar-only scrim on every surface (kept). Top-left: EMPTY on every surface.
- DOCUMENT-ONLY, top-right: Properties (slider.horizontal.3) and ••• 'Note actions' glass circles — the document's own keys, not chrome; appear under state .desk only; retire with the Line on scroll.
- MINIMISED RECORD PILL — one capsule above the Line holding a swiped-away card's glyph and title (kept; travels with the Line).
- TRANSIENT (not chrome): the Line's sheet (Search's list / the desk grid), the record card, the properties card, the history card, the workspace card, Settings, Trash (inside Settings), the camera cover, LivMenu cards from the bottom. Deleted: the library door and panel, the wash and pushed desk, the › key, the Search key, the Open key and its numbered box, the full-screen switcher cover, the Inactive shelf screen.

**Gestures.**
- Tap the Line's middle → focus; keyboard up, Tray hidden; the empty sheet shows the desk; typing shows Search's list top-down with the 'New <kind> "words"' row pinned above the field.
- Return → opens the first hit; with no hits, creates the words as what the place holds. Tap the pinned New row → creates regardless of hits.
- Tap ‹ at the Line's head → back one place on LivReturns (present only while there is one; otherwise the magnifier). Left-edge swipe (20pt zone, PanelDrag's recognizer with the pagerZone veto) does the same with the previous surface following the finger. No forward.
- Tap + at the Line's tail → make what the place holds, nameless, caret in the name (createHere, unchanged); hold 0.45s → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text). While words are in the field the tail is ✕ → clear.
- Tap the pinned desk chip on a view → the active (or MRU) document, state = .desk, pushed. Tap it on the desk → the desk sheet (the desk's root).
- Drag the Line upward from any surface → the desk sheet without a keyboard; pull it down or tap the washed surface → close.
- Swipe the Line left / right ON THE DESK → previous / next open document in MRU order. On a view the Line does not swipe; the Tray scrolls.
- Tap a view chip → go there (pushed); tap the filled chip you stand on → its root / scroll to top (Notes = the list, always). The Tray scrolls to keep the current chip on screen on arrival.
- Tap an area chip or a saved-filter chip → toggle it (a lens over the view, intersecting the workspace lens); tap the workspace chip → the workspace card from the bottom (New filter lives inside it); tap ⚙ → Settings (Trash is a card there).
- Scroll down 8pt → the Tray retires; 44pt → the Line retires (existing hysteresis); scroll up or reach the top → both return. Neither retires on an empty surface.
- Record card: swipe down → minimises to the pill above the Line; tap the pill → restore; ✕ → drop (unchanged).
- In the editor, '[[' → the Line's sheet rises over the note in picker mode with the typed seed; a pick writes [[id|Name]]; the New row births the scrap and links it (one search, two endings, kept).

**Capture.** Inside the app there is one capture door and it is the Line. WORDS + RETURN: if the list has a hit, Return OPENS the first one — a task raises its card, a note lands on the desk — and nothing is made. If the list is empty, Return makes the words. The pinned 'New <kind> "words"' row makes them regardless, one tap, always visible above the field — create-new is behind a door and the door is one row high. On Today or Tasks → box.createTask with the words as its name, due desk.contextDay ?? today at LivDue.defaultHHMM (09:00), stamped by the workspace → the record card rises over the view you stand on with the caret at the end of the name; the Line clears; you never left. On Calendar → box.createEvent on the shown day → card, same. On Notes, Inbox, Everything or the desk → liv_capture_at(words) (the same verb the share sheet and Search's Create row use) → stamped → the desk: state = .desk, a tab minted, the editor open with the caret at the end of the first line, the place you came from pushed as the way back; the desk chip now prints its name. That note IS the capture the Inbox lists until it is routed (unchanged rule). BARE + (tap): the view's kind, nameless, caret in the name — exactly today's one-tap + (createHere). HOLD +: the New menu. Nothing is parsed: 'due tomorrow' typed into the Line is a title, never a date (2026-08-09 refusal honoured); dates and filing stay chips, the card and the Inbox's Route. Typed words match THINGS only — never a view, an area or a workspace; a 'Calendar' row for 'cal' is typing where to go and rule 5 forbids it; places are chips. Capture from OUTSIDE is unchanged and deliberately different: the share extension spools to the App Group, the app drains at foreground through liv_capture_at, stamps, and does NOT open or focus — the Inbox chip's digit ticks up, which is the whole UI of it; liv://capture?text= does the same; liv://capture (bare) = the + tap for a note. The instant after any in-app capture: the Line is empty again and the Tray shows where you now stand (the desk chip filled and named if you landed there).

**Back, links and catches.** ONE POLICY. `here` is `.desk(activeId)` or `.view(feature)`. EVERY arrival pushes it: a chip tap, a hit, a [[link]], a Line swipe to another document, a desk-sheet pick, the desk chip, a notification, a liv:// route, a Notes-list row. Only a workspace switch clears the stack (it lands on Today with the incoming workspace's desk). Cap 20 (LivReturns.cap). No forward stack: `forwards`, stepForward, goForward, desk.forward and the › key are deleted. Back has one visible key — ‹ at the Line's head, drawn only while `desk.back != nil` (the magnifier otherwise; a dim key is a grey sentence) — and one gesture, the left-edge swipe, which is iOS's own and which every first-day user already knows. Both call `goBack` → `land`: `.view(f)` sets state and restores the tool's spot; `.desk(id)` sets state = .desk and focuses (re-mints) the tab. Re-arriving at the document already on screen is not a step (kept). The Notes list → note → ‹ lands on the LIST (state .view(.notes)), which today's `.state(.notes)` could not express — the dead ‹ is gone by construction. Tapping the filled chip you stand on is a root/scroll-to-top and does not push. This reverses half of 2026-08-23 ('a browser's set, literally'): Back keeps its glyph and gains a browser's meaning; Forward, the numbered box and the full-screen switcher go. That is one question for the owner and it is listed as one.

LINKS AND ARRIVALS. liv://<view> → go(.view(f)) (pushed); Today's 'Route them' footer calls the same door. liv://entity/<id> → box.refresh if unknown, then desk.open(id): a record → card over where you stand; a document → the desk (pushed). liv://capture?text= → catch, not opened; bare liv://capture → the + tap for a note; liv://capture/photo → the camera flow. A notification tap → desk.open(id), same rules. Every arrival still runs surfaceCleanup (sheet closed, menu nil, properties/history/settings/workspace/camera closed) so nothing lands behind something. A [[link]] tap in a note → the linked document on the desk, the current one pushed; a link to a record → its card. Editor '[[' → the Line's sheet in picker mode over the note (a pick writes the link; nothing navigates).

**Survives in code.** DeskModel and DeskPlanes (Plane.swift 481) verbatim: one app-wide desk per workspace, entity tabs, lastUsed stamps, open(entity:) reuse, setActive, close/closeInactive, the UserDefaults keys and every legacy fold, dropRecordsAndStrangers on first snapshot.; `spots` and Positions.swift (370): one token per tool (EverythingLens, InboxLens, TasksPosition, TodayPosition, CalendarPosition) and park(_:at:) — restored on every arrival exactly as now.; Tabs.swift's BODY: TabCard (title + excerpt), the two-column grid sunk to the foot, the New-note card bottom-right, InactiveTabs and its fold, Close all, LivTabs.isInactive and LivTabs.days (Tabs.swift:30-57) — re-hosted in a livSheet(from: .bottom) instead of a fullScreenCover.; LivReturns (Navigate.swift:86-137) as the typed, capped stack — with `forwards`/stepForward deleted and push-on-every-arrival as the policy; LivPlace becomes .desk(id) | .view(Feature); Chrome.swift's `back`, `goBack`, `land` survive.; Feature (Navigate.swift: inOrder, makes, title, glyph) — makes now labels the Line's placeholder and decides what Return/+ create; the words on the chips are Feature.title.; Record.swift (375): RecordCard, RecordCardHost, openAsCard, minimiseRecord/restoreRecord, MinimisedRecordPill, TabShape.of — Option C untouched.; Search.swift (671) almost whole: Box.search over liv_search_at, SearchTerm/LivTerms.term at the seam, the facet row and the Only/Hide/Any term menu, the kind grouping and rank, offersCreate, totalHits/'Showing N of M', the lens intersection, picker mode (onPick, seed) for '[[' and the Links section, the top-down list — re-hosted as the Line's sheet with the field moved from its own `pill` (Search.swift:324) to the Line.; createHere / createNote / catchText / createRecord (Desk.swift:622-727), createSomething / adoptCapture (Chrome.swift:486, 895) — the + tap, the hold menu and the capture verbs; FileImport, Camera/Scan flows.; Bar.swift's `key(_:_:on:)` builder and LivBar's material and glyph numbers (Theme.swift:491-512) — the ‹ and + inside the Line are drawn by the same builder; LivBar renamed LivField; LivTopScrim; chromeAway / livHidesChrome / chromeHomeAgain (Chrome.swift:47,133; Navigate.swift:218) with a second, lower threshold for the Tray; KeyboardWatch (Chrome.swift:907); LivMotion.nav as the one lateral easing.; PanelDrag.swift (280): the UIKit recognizer (latch at |dx|>18 && |dx|>2|dy|, the pagerZone veto, touch cancel under the finger) — repurposed as the left-edge back swipe with a 20pt edge zone added to the latch.; All six view bodies (Today, NotesList, Inbox with Route/Tidy and the route card, Calendar, Tasks, Everything) and their markers liv.surface.*; EntityTabBody/NoteEditor/FileBody and liv.surface.document; the properties and history cards and the note-verbs ••• menu; the trash chip with Undo; Today's 'Route them' footer (now calling the one door, go(.view(.inbox))).; WorkspaceModel, saved filters (snap.views), the workspace lens, WorkspaceSwitcher's card (livSheet from .bottom) with the New-filter form; adopt(workspace:) with per-workspace desk swap; the area facet the core already sends (the area chips are LivTerms.term('area', name) through the same seam as a facet tap).; LivMenu / LivMenuHost / livSheet(from:), LivListRow, LivName, LivKind.of, the Theme tokens, the Glyph table (minus day(n) and PanelMark), Settings, TrashView's body, Routes.swift, Notify, Catch.swift's Spool drain and the share extension.; drive.sh's method (geometry and rendered text, one surface at a time) and its untouched checks — goto, tour's assertions per marker, desk (rewritten to assert the desk chip's rendered title in all six views), lens, facets, chrome, rows, workspace, history, routes, spool, create, areas, vault, cycles, quiet.

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift (461) entirely: LibraryPanel, its six rows and ViewCounts (except the Inbox count, which moves to the chip), the filter rows, Trash row, the pinned foot (workspace name, count line, gear circle) — and in Chrome.swift libraryShown/libraryDrawn/setLibrary, panelProgress, deskShift, panelOut, the desk mask/wash/square-corners, and the PanelMark door glyph (Glyph.swift:590).; Bar.swift (160) as a bar: the five-key capsule, the words under keys, LivBar.disabledInk, the Open key and LivGlyph.day(n) (Glyph.swift:219), the Search key, the › key; Feature.makes.word as a bar label. The ‹ and + move into the Line.; Tabs.swift's container: TabSwitcher as a fullScreenCover (Tabs.swift:64-70), the '+ | N open | Done' footer and its Done key, InactiveTabs as an overlay-within-a-cover, desk.switcherShown as a cover flag (it becomes the sheet's), desk.newTab(); the -desk.boot switcher|inactive|library|grid flags.; SearchView's cover: the fullScreenCover, the Cancel key (Search.swift:146), its own field `pill` (Search.swift:324), searchShown as a cover flag — the list is hosted by the surface under the Line.; The `openDoc` guard on state == .notes (Chrome.swift:183-186), `state = .notes` inside openDocument (853) and adopt (683), DeskHost's `openDoc == nil` branch (Desk.swift:48-66), go() (698), goToRoot() (731), showList() (746) and the special case they exist for; `here` as .state/.document (760).; LivReturns.forwards, stepForward (Navigate.swift:108,131), goForward, desk.forward (Chrome.swift:766-775); every returns.clear() except the workspace switch (Chrome.swift:681, 701, 748).; The DeskTab `.position` case (Plane.swift:70; legacy folded on read — fold once more on first launch, then delete the case).; The overlay markers liv.overlay.library and liv.overlay.tabs (Surface.swift:76); drive.sh panel, bar and grid checks in their current form (replaced by field, tray, sheet, back).; The wash-takes-every-touch and 'the bar is inert under the panel' machinery, and LivPanel.width/peek (Theme.swift:243-252) — no panel to be under.; The drag-from-anywhere as a navigation gesture: PanelDrag's recognizer keeps its latch but gains an edge zone; a rightward drag in the body of a surface no longer navigates.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- RESOLVED — 'Notes is two things wearing one name': the desk gets its own state (.desk); a document is never drawn by borrowing Notes; Notes is one view whose root is the list, and the special case goToRoot exists for is gone.
- RESOLVED — 'the list from the panel, the documents from the bar': both are in one row at the thumb — the Notes chip (shelf), the desk chip (the one you are in, by name; its root is the open grid), the typed words (everything).
- RESOLVED — 'Open N counts an invisible container': the chip prints the active document's NAME and the count, appears only when non-zero, wears the fill while you stand on the desk, and tapping it goes to that document; the a11y label ('3 documents open, Kitchen rebuild active') and the printed words finally agree.
- RESOLVED — 'Notes means three surfaces and the + word changes': Notes means the view; the make-word moves into the Line's placeholder where it labels the field that makes, not a view.
- RESOLVED — 'panel row and Open key lead to a note with different side effects': one push policy for every arrival; no route clears the stack.
- RESOLVED — 'the back key's meaning changes with state': one ‹, one meaning — the last arrival of any kind — drawn only when it can act; forward gone rather than dim.
- RESOLVED — 'the root-of-view rule is a patch': tapping the Notes chip always means the list because a document cannot be drawn under that state; tapping the filled chip you stand on is a root in all seven (six views + the desk, whose root is the open grid), no branch.
- PARTLY — 'positions and documents are two invisible kinds of memory': the desk is now visible by name on every surface; a tool's resumed position is still shown by nothing on arrival.
- NOT RESOLVED — '+ stays where you stand or moves you depending on kind': Return/+ still raise a card on Today/Tasks/Calendar and move you to the desk elsewhere; the placeholder names the kind, not the consequence. Records-as-cards and documents-as-desk are both rulings this design keeps, so the asymmetry stays.
- RESOLVED — 'the switcher is a full-screen mode and the only door back': the way back to a note from Calendar is a named chip, one tap, no keyboard; the grid is a half sheet over the surface you stand on; the Line swipe and ‹ are two more doors of one kind.
- RESOLVED — 'the panel's fill answers state, not surface': a view fills its chip, the desk fills the named chip, and the Line prints the document; a card fills nothing because a card is a layer, which is honest.
- PARTLY — 'the Inbox is a queue, Notes a shelf, presented as flat peers': the Tray's grammar (desk pinned left, lenses right, the Inbox alone carrying a digit, the areas as chips on the views that wear a lens) says which one must be emptied and where things are filed; the six are otherwise still six equal words.

**Changed after the judges.**
- Words on all six view chips at `label` 16 in a scrolling Tray; the current one wears the panel row's soft fill instead of a widened glyph. Why: five glyph-only marks failed 'discoverable without labels' and the 2026-09-07 'tiny and subtle' correction (all three judges). Measured: ~610pt of chips on a 390pt frame, so Tasks and Everything are a flick away — recorded as a trade-off, not hidden.
- The desk chip prints the active document's NAME and count ('▢ Kitchen rebuild · 3'), is pinned so it never scrolls out of reach, and tapping it on a view opens that document with no keyboard. Why: judges 2 and 3 — 'a count chip is the numbered-box problem with a word', 'a number is never as good as a name', and the keyboardless door had to be the visible one.
- The desk's root is the existing TabCard grid in a half sheet (title + excerpt, New note bottom-right, Inactive fold, Close all) instead of two-voice rows. Why: it keeps 'tabs as they appeared before when you clicked the numbered box' as far as a sheet can, keeps Tabs.swift's body, and settles the excerpt question. Only the full-screen cover and the numbered box go.
- Return opens the first hit; it creates only when the list is empty; the 'New <kind> "words"' row is pinned above the field and always one tap. Why: all three judges — Return-on-near-miss minted duplicates and moved the metric the wrong way.
- Search's list stays top-down; the field moves to the foot. Why: judge 3 named the upward inversion of SearchView (671 lines) as the expensive half hidden in 're-hosted', and judge 1 noted it inverted the app's reading direction. The trade (the first hit is far from the thumb, Return reaches it) is recorded.
- A visible ‹ inside the Line's head, drawn only while there is somewhere to go back to; the from-anywhere drag replaced by the left-edge swipe with a 20pt zone. Why: 'back is a gesture with no key' from all three; and the from-anywhere drag collided with editor text selection (real) — though not with Inbox/Tasks rows, whose swipe actions are trailing-only (Inbox.swift:297; Tasks has none), which the judge got wrong and I checked.
- The Line swipes only on the desk (a title swipes; a field does not). Why: judge 2 — swiping a search field on Today jumped into a document unasked; and the cross-cutting rule that a bar element must either scroll or swipe, not both: the Tray scrolls, the Line swipes only where it prints a title.
- Tapping the Line on a document always means find; rename stays in the editor's first line and the properties card's LivName. Why: judge 2 — one capsule, one tap meaning.
- Typed words match THINGS only, never places, areas or workspaces. Decided off. Why: judges 1 and 3 — 'cal' → Calendar is typing where to go; rule 5.
- The Line drops to 44 (LivRow.touch), the Tray is plain canvas with glass chips (only the Line is a capsule), and the Tray retires at 8pt of scroll before the Line at 44. Why: ~100pt of foot chrome on Calendar; this is now ~94 at rest and 50 after the first scroll — honest: still worse than 66 at rest, recorded under conceded.
- The areas join the Tray as chips on the four views that wear a lens. Why: judge 1's cross-cutting note — no direction but `areas` put the furniture on a surface; 'arrive furnished' was answered by prose. This is the cheapest way this direction can answer it with a chip instead of a sentence; it lengthens the Tray, recorded.
- Trailing key is ✕ while words are in the field. Why: the + had no meaning mid-search and Safari's pill sets the precedent; also the only dismiss besides tapping the wash.
- The reversal of 2026-08-23 (Forward, the numbered box, the full-screen switcher) is put as ONE question at the head of open_questions, not spread over footnotes. Why: all three judges' cross-cutting notes.
- survives_in_code / thrown_away corrected against the tree: Search's own `pill` is the field (Search.swift:324), not a record-pill copy; Tabs.swift's body survives, its cover does not; Bar.swift's key builder survives inside the Line; the Route-them footer is redirected, not deleted; LivPanel tokens and the position case named by line.

**Conceded.**
- The foot is ~94pt at rest against the bar's 66 and cannot be less while the Tray is a separate row with 44pt touch targets; folding it into the Line would make it the address pill of `areas`/`two` and stop being this direction. On Calendar at rest it is 28pt worse than the thing the owner already called 'always taking up space'.
- The Tray with words is a row of view chips at the foot of the screen; whatever its grammar, the owner may see the four-floors bar he rejected on sight. Nothing but a simulator mockup can settle that, and this direction may lose there.
- Search-first perception: a field as the face of an app whose retrieval is by navigation (furnishing study §4.8) is a product risk this direction cannot remove without ceasing to be the Line. The worded Tray and the area chips are the counter-weight; the navigation-vs-search mix must be measured.
- Forward is deleted and the numbered box and full-screen switcher are gone — a reversal of 'a browser's set, literally' (2026-08-23) that needs the owner's word and may not get it.
- Tasks and Everything are two motions from Today (flick, tap) where the panel made every view two motions (door, row) and the four-floors bar made three of them one; the order is Feature.inOrder and is not reordered to fix this.
- The Line has three targets (‹, field, +/✕) and, on a document, prints a title that is not the rename field; a first-day user must learn that a printed name is not an editable one.
- Inside a note with the keyboard up there is no navigation — the 2026-08-02 'bar retires under the keyboard' ruling stands and this direction does not design the keyboard-up state, like every other direction.
- The + asymmetry (vagueness 9) stays: a task rises as a card where you stand, a note moves you to the desk; the placeholder names the kind, not the consequence. Both halves are rulings this direction keeps.
- A tool's resumed position (Calendar on March) is still shown by nothing on arrival (vagueness 8, half).
- Nothing here is verified: mockup-first, no device, and revs 54–61 beneath it are unverified too; drive.sh tour/panel/bar/grid must be rewritten and one assertion broken on purpose before a green means anything.

**Open questions.**
- The one question for the owner, put as one: this deletes the five-key bar, Forward, the numbered box and the full-screen switcher (2026-08-23, 2026-09-05) and keeps Back and the tab cards. Yes or no to that trade decides whether this direction exists.
- Density on a device: do '▢ Kitchen re… · 3' + Today + Notes + Inbox 2 fit a 375pt phone at `label` 16 with Calendar cut at the edge, and does the cut read as 'scrolls' or as 'broken'? Only the simulator answers; drive.sh tray asserts the chip frames.
- Should the area chips also appear on Today (which ignores the workspace lens by rule) as a one-tap view of the day by area, or does the area count line under the date already do that job?
- Where Trash lives (inside Settings as drawn, or as a card at the end of the desk sheet), and whether Settings deserves a Tray chip at all or a long-press on the workspace chip.
- Whether a workspace switch should land on Today (as drawn) or stay on the surface you were on, re-lensed; and whether the per-workspace desk swap still earns its keep when the desk has a name on every surface.
- The Inbox digit on its chip: an ink digit, never a colour blip — and is Today's LATE count owed the same on its chip?
- Whether the [[ picker and the Links section should present the Line's sheet in place over the editor (as drawn) or the Line itself should focus with a seed — one component, which host.
- The desk chip's truncation: ~10 characters plus the count, or drop the count while the desk is on screen and print more of the name?
- drive.sh: `field` (the Line's rendered text per surface: placeholder on a view, title on the desk, ‹ present only with a stack), `tray` (chip frames and the filled chip per view; the desk chip's title in all six), `sheet` (the grid rises over the surface, cards = live tabs, the New card bottom-right), `back` (‹ tap and edge swipe both land on the previous marker), `return` (Return with hits opens the first; with none, creates), plus the Notes-lists-more-than-open guard kept.
- The retrieval-rate and navigation-vs-search proxies: which sessions count as 'navigated' (a chip) versus 'searched' (words) when both go through the Line's foot, and where the counter lives (the shell, not the core).

### 7. Native Shapes

*Five places on the platform's tab bar, a document as a leaf pushed on whichever tab you are standing in, and the app's existing bottom capsule reshaped into the desk's face — Search · a titled Open pill · + — sitting at the thumb above the tab bar, with the Open pill opening a Safari-style tab sheet; no drawer, no numbered box, no full-screen mode, drawn for iOS 17 (the app's real floor), not iOS 26.*
