**1. The navigation concept.** The app is a TabView with one NavigationStack per tab, which is the iOS 16+ shape every iPhone user already has in their thumb. The five slots are the five places you STAND in, in the order of a day: Today · Notes · Inbox · Calendar · Tasks — the first five of Feature.inOrder, and five because the platform's iPhone tab bar holds five before it grows a 'More' tab. Each tab's root is the view; a DOCUMENT is a leaf pushed on top of the tab you are standing in (Apple Notes, Files, Mail: a thing you open is somewhere you push into and pop out of), so Notes means exactly one thing — the list of all notes by recency — and a note is never 'in Notes'; it is on top of wherever you opened it. Global STATE (which place) is the tab bar; global ACTIONS stay on the bar, exactly the 2026-08-17 split with the panel's six rows moved onto the platform's own row of places. That bar is the STRIP: Bar.swift's existing glass capsule with the two browser keys removed and the numbered box widened into a pill that names the active document — three labelled keys, Search · [kind glyph · title · ▣ N] 'Open' · + with its kind word. It is each surface's own foot (safeAreaInset, the 2026-09-08 rule kept), present on every tab and every leaf, never hidden for an empty desk (its + is the way out of one), retiring only under the keyboard and — on iOS 18+ — with the top doors on scroll through the existing chromeAway. Tap the pill's title → the active document is pushed on the current tab; swipe the pill sideways → the neighbouring open document (Safari's pill, a fixed handle, the one power gesture); tap ▣ N → the DRAWER, a system sheet at medium/large holding the existing TabCard grid with the Inactive shelf as a collapsed section at its foot. The sixth view, Everything, keeps its name, its list, its pills, its marker, its route and its position token; it is the place the Search key pushes onto the current tab (Files' Recents → search): an empty field over the whole box, lensed; typed words narrow it. Records stay cards (a system sheet over the tab you stand in); a minimised card borrows the pill's slot until restored. There is no side panel: the panel's rows are the tab bar; its foot (workspace, count, gear) becomes the SCOPE button — the active workspace's name with a chevron, in the same top-left 44pt glass circle the library door occupies today — whose card (from the top, the edge its button is on) holds workspaces, saved filters, New filter, Trash and Settings. Roots keep NO navigation bar: the screen name stays a full-size title inside the scroll (2026-09-02), the top-right of a root holds nothing, and the 2026-07-31 'no persistent top bar' ruling holds on roots and leaves alike. Back is the platform's: the glass chevron top-left with its label suppressed, the left-edge pop, and re-tapping the selected tab pops to its root. A document is a leaf on at most ONE stack; opening it from another tab moves it there. iOS 26 changes materials (glassEffect through the existing LivGlass fork) and lets the tab bar minimise on scroll; it changes nothing else, so there is one drawing, not two.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS = the desk = DeskPlanes exactly as it is (one plane per workspace, entity ids, lastUsed stamps, the same entity never twice, records swept off on first snapshot), plus one new invariant the screen now enforces too: the active tab is the LEAF of the selected tab bar tab, and an entity is a leaf on at most one stack. How it is SHOWN: (1) the Open pill in the strip — Bar.swift's Open key widened: kind glyph · the active document's title · a stacked-squares glyph with the live count, the word 'Open' under it at caption like the other keys (2026-09-05, every key has a word); empty desk: 'Nothing open' (the grid's existing word) and the count absent. Tap the title → the active document is pushed on the current tab's stack (from Calendar you are one thumb-tap from the note you were editing; back = Calendar). Horizontal swipe on the pill → the previous/next live tab becomes active and, if a document is the current leaf, replaces it (Safari's pill; a fixed handle swipes, nothing scrolls, so it collides with nothing — and it is a power gesture: the drawer is the taught door). Tap ▣ N → the drawer. (2) The drawer — the existing TabCard grid (title, ✕, four excerpt lines, kind footer, active card outlined, dashed New-note card bottom-right) inside a .sheet with detents medium/large and a grabber, titled 'Open · N', 'Close all' trailing when the Inactive section is expanded; the Inactive shelf is a disclosure section at the foot of the same scroll, not an overlay inside a cover. Picking a card dismisses the sheet and shows the document as the leaf of the tab you stood in (replacing a document leaf, pushing over a root, and pulling it off any other tab's stack). LivTabs.choices [7,14,21,0], declared in Tabs.swift:37 and read by nothing, becomes a Settings row worded like Safari's 'Close Tabs: After a week / two weeks / three weeks / never'. THE NOTE LIST = the Notes tab's root, and nothing else: all notes by recency (the 2026-08-29 rule 'a surface named after a thing has to contain it'), lensed by the workspace, hero 'Notes', the strip's + makes a note. It is one of five peers and knows nothing of the desk, any more than Today does; the desk is the strip under all five. The 'two doors on two edges' collapse to two doors on ONE edge with two different jobs the platform already teaches: a tab (all notes, a place) and a pill (what is open, a state). A document opened from the list is pushed on the Notes stack; back pops to the list; the document stays on the desk and the pill still names it — Safari's rule, not Apple Notes'.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** Six roots become five tabs plus one pushed place. Today (tab 1, launch tab, the title is the date, day strip, LATE/Done piles; 'N captured today · Route them' selects the Inbox tab); Notes (tab 2, the recency list); Inbox (tab 3, the only tab with a badge — the unrouted count; Route/Tidy lens row and the route card unchanged; ignores the workspace lens by rule); Calendar (tab 4, timeline-first, month card behind the day title, tap-an-hour creates); Tasks (tab 5, chips and groups). Everything (the sixth view) is not a slot — the platform's bar holds five — but it is still a PLACE: the Search key pushes it on the current tab as a leaf with its own marker (liv.surface.everything, unchanged), its All/Upcoming/Unfiled pills, its workspace lens and its position token in `spots`; the search field sits at its head, empty by default, so 'show me the whole box' is one tap on any tab and needs no typing; typed words become ranked hits with facet chips and the Create row; a hit pushes over it, a record rises as a card; Cancel or back pops to where you stood. Each tab's root keeps its parked token in `spots` exactly as now and the platform additionally keeps its stack, so a tool visibly resumes where you left it — and a document you left is not 'gone', it is one pill-tap away. Hierarchy is two levels deep everywhere: root → leaf (a document, a chain of documents by [[link]], or Everything with hits over it); cards float over either. A record never enters a stack. The panel's other contents become the scope card (workspaces as rows, the active one filled; saved filters as a second group, toggled on tap; New filter; Trash; Settings at the foot), reached from the scope button of every root, which prints the active workspace's name ('All' when none). A workspace switch pops every tab to root, swaps the desk, and STAYS on the tab you were on — the app has one home (Today) and switching a lens no longer teleports you to Notes.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against the references reader's list, item by item, and revised where the critique moved things. (1) The bottom capsule measured off the Obsidian clip — the MATERIAL and the labelled-key form survive (they are Todoist's, 2026-09-05, and the owner's); the browser's key SET — ‹ › ⌕ + [n], 'literally' — does not: no Back, no Forward, and the box is a pill that names a document. Three keys is Safari's bottom (address pill, tabs) more than Obsidian's five. (2) The numbered box, LivGlyph.day redrawn from Obsidian's daily-note key — deleted; the count sits beside a title, Music's and Safari's furniture. (3) Chrome hiding on scroll copied from the Obsidian clip — the existing chromeAway hysteresis survives for the doors and the strip on iOS 18+ (it is already there and the tab bar needs none of it); nothing new is measured off Obsidian; on iOS 26 the tab bar minimises by the platform's own rule. (4) The tab switcher 'verbatim in structure' — the grid survives but is no longer a full-screen mode with a Done key; it is a sheet, Safari's tab-group picker shape, reached from a pill, not a box. (5) One desk of tabs per vault — structurally kept (it is right), but Obsidian's tabs are where you STAND; here a document is a leaf on a place's stack and the desk is a list you consult — the Files/Mail relation of open-to-place. (6) The left drawer with three zones — gone entirely: no drawer, no wash, no push, no peek, no drag-from-anywhere; the left edge is the platform's pop gesture. (7) The panel foot with name, count line and gear — gone; workspace is a scope button at the top of a root (Mail's mailbox title, Files' location), Settings a row in its card. (8) Top-left sidebar toggle + top-right ellipsis — the toggle is gone (the most Obsidian pixel, axis 7); top-left on a root is a workspace name, on a leaf a plain chevron; top-right on a root is empty. (9) Edge swipe for the drawer — gone. (10) No right drawer — unchanged. (11)–(13) Inline title, properties chevron, editor grammar — kept; the owner asked for them by name, and a tab bar app with Obsidian's editor is Apple Notes with a better editor, not Obsidian. (14) Find-or-create — kept, as the Create row inside Everything's search field, Files/App Store furniture. (16) Neutral greys, one accent — kept; a palette is not a structure. (17) Back/forward stack 'the most Obsidian thing in the app' — LivReturns deleted; back is the stack pop, forward does not exist, as on every Apple app. (19) 'One kind got the main surface and the rest a drawer' — no kind has the main surface: Today is home, Notes one tab among five, Calendar and Tasks tab-bar peers, which is the owner's 2026-08-16 wish in the platform's own words — and, stated on the artboard so it is not smuggled: this RELOCATES the kinds-are-peers question into a bar of kinds rather than answering it. (20) A recents list and a tab grid as two answers to 'get me back' — both remain with different questions: the list answers 'what do I have', the pill 'what am I in the middle of', and the grid is subordinate to the pill. Persistent-chrome set (axis 6): Obsidian's was header + nav bar + drawer edges; this is floating doors + strip + tab bar. Nearest relative now: Safari's two bottom rows crossed with Todoist's worded tab bar and Apple Notes' push. What this direction does NOT buy is a hand of its own: it is distinctive from Obsidian by being indistinguishable in skeleton from Apple's own apps, and its identity has to come entirely from the furniture (areas drawn in Liv's pen, kind-named rows, the unfiled count on the scope card), which the 2026-09-06 direction A already committed to. That is the point of a control: it shows what invention buys by spending almost none.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- It re-argues a rejected shape, and after the critique it is CLOSER to it: four floors was Today·Calendar·Tasks·Find·Open on one bar with the library deleted; this is five places on the platform's bar and Find·Open·+ on the strip beneath it. The defence is real but subtle — no verb on the row of places, notes a peer, Inbox a peer with a badge, the library's contents kept in a card, the strip is the bar the owner already has minus ‹ › — and the owner judged the last one on sight. The artboard must show this beside the four-floors screenshot with those five differences labelled, or it will be judged the same way. This cannot be argued away, only mocked up.
- Density: one row lost on every screen size and never regained on iOS 17/18. Measured at 393×852: unscrolled, today ≈ 641pt of content (852 − 59 clock − 52 door band − 66 bar − 34 indicator) → 11 rows of 56; revised ≈ 592 (a 49pt tab bar added) → 10. Scrolled with chrome retired: today 759 → 13; revised 710 → 12 (the tab bar stays on 17/18, minimises on 26). At 375×667 (SE-class): 8 rows → 7. The top costs nothing new — roots keep no navigation bar, the scope button sits in the door's existing circle — so 'three bands' is really the two the app has now plus the platform's tab bar.
- The kinds-are-peers question is relocated, not answered: Notes | Calendar | Tasks on a tab bar reads as three kinds of thing, which is the Capacities type-door cost and what the owner said an Obsidian clone does with a drawer. A task is still a card, tasks are still a kind list in a tab. Same-shaped answer, different container — stated on the artboard.
- Everything is a pushed place, not a peer slot. It keeps its name, list, pills, lens, marker, route and token, and is one tap away on every tab with nothing typed — but it is one hop deeper than the other five and its door wears the word 'Search', so a person who never types may not learn that the empty field IS the whole box. The alternative (a sixth slot) grows a 'More' tab on iPhone, which is worse.
- Forward is gone. The owner wired › deliberately ('shipping a control that can never work seemed worse'); there is no native forward, so the wiring and the key both go. A chain of [[link]] jumps is a stack you can pop through; you cannot re-advance. The 20-place way-back diary that survived panel moves is not reproduced because nothing native reproduces it.
- Back is the native pop: consistent, but shallow. From a document reached by three link jumps, back walks the chain; from a document reached from the pill it pops to the tab's root, never to the document you swapped away from (Safari's rule). Tab moves are not history.
- The single-stack rule buys one honest desk and costs one surprising pop: open Kitchen plan from Today, then open it again from Notes — it moves; re-tap Today and you are one leaf lower than you left it (its root). The desk never lies; a stack occasionally shortens behind your back.
- The panel's per-view counts vanish except Inbox's badge. Today's late count, the notes count, the events count were one glance in the drawer; now they are each tab's own hero. The foot's 'N items · M unfiled' moves into the scope card, one hop further.
- Workspace, filters, Trash and Settings all sit behind one card from a top-left button. Switching workspace was two taps (door, foot) and is two taps still; the filters toggle lost its always-visible row, and 'a filter is not a workspace' is now a grouping in a card, which the app has refused elsewhere ('no section labels') unless drawn as two groups with no caption.
- The 2026-08-24 ruling 'the old ‹ Notes should be gone' is honoured by suppressing the platform's back label and drawing the chevron in the existing glass circle; the labelled back is the platform default and the mockup shows the suppressed form so the owner rules on it, not on prose.
- Every interactive habit built for the panel (drag from anywhere, wash-tap closes, pagerZone veto, PanelDrag's UIKit recognizer) is deleted, along with the 2026-08-15 insight that the library is a PLACE you move to. In this model there is no place called the library; there are places and a lens.
- The strip names ONE open document at the sill. It is not a row of chips, but it is a bar-shaped thing that mentions tabs, and on 2026-08-23 the owner said 'Not a tab "bar", remove it.' Whether a titled pill is that thing is one sentence from him, and the direction depends on it.
- The strip's sideways swipe is unlabelled — the one hidden gesture in the direction. It is a fixed handle (nothing under it scrolls) so it collides with nothing, and the drawer does the same job visibly; it can be dropped without loss if it grates.
- drive.sh's tour, panel, bar, grid, desk and workspace checks are rewritten wholesale; there is no panel, the bar has a new shape, the grid is a sheet. The harness must learn to select tabs, read the pill and open the drawer sheet before any green means anything — and revs 54–61 are unverified on a simulator before any of this starts.
- On iOS 17 the 'least invention' evidence is thinner than first claimed: TabView, NavigationStack, .sheet with detents, .badge and .searchable are native; the strip is built (from the app's own capsule), retire-on-scroll is the app's own (iOS 18+), and 'tap the selected tab to pop' needs the five-line selection-binding idiom in SwiftUI (UIKit does it free; SwiftUI does not until the platform's own behaviour on 26).

**Wireframes.**

*Today (cold launch): root of tab 1, desk holds 3 documents*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▂▄▆ ▮ │
│ ⟨Work ▾⟩                     │
│                              │
│ Tuesday 9 Sept               │
│  M   T   W   T   F   S   S   │
│  ·   ●   ·   ·   ·   ·   ·   │
│                              │
│ ○ Call the plumber      Home │
│ ○ Draft Q4 plan         Work │
│ ○ Renew passport             │
│ ▸ LATE 2                     │
│ ▸ Done 4                     │
│                              │
│ 2 captured today · Route them│
│                              │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │  ⌕   ▤ Kitchen plan ▣3  + │ │
│ │Search      Open       Task│ │
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
│ ──────────────────────────── │

│ Today Notes Inbox  Cal  Tasks│
│            ▬▬▬               │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```
Top-left ⟨Work ▾⟩ is the scope button in the door's existing 44pt glass circle (active workspace; 'All' when none) → the scope card from the top edge. Top-right is EMPTY on a root (nothing inert). No navigation bar: the date is the screen's own large title inside the scroll, as today. The strip is Bar.swift's capsule with three keys: Search (pushes Everything on this tab, field empty), the Open pill (kind glyph · active document's title · ▣ 3 live tabs; tap the title → Kitchen plan is pushed on Today's stack, back = Today; swipe → next open document; tap ▣ 3 → the drawer sheet), and + with its kind word (tap = a task due today via Feature.makes, opened as a card with the caret in the name; hold = the New card from .bottom). Below it the platform's tab bar: five places, words under glyphs, selected item filled, Inbox wearing the one badge in the app (2 unrouted). On iOS 18+ the strip and the scope circle retire on scroll through the existing chromeAway; the tab bar stays (minimises on 26). Markers: liv.surface.today; the strip liv.chrome.strip.

*A document as the leaf of the Notes tab (same screen from Today; only the selected tab differs)*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 9:41                   ▂▄▆ ▮ │
│ (‹)                (☰) (•••) │
│                              │
│ Kitchen plan                 │
│                              │
│ Cabinets: pick the oak,      │
│ [[Plumber quote]] says       │

│ island again before we       │
│ order.                       │
│                              │

│ - [x] confirm the tiler      │
│                              │
│                              │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │

│ │Search      Open       Note│ │
│ ╰──────────────────────────╯ │
│ ──────────────────────────── │

│ Today Notes Inbox  Cal  Tasks│
│            ▬▬▬               │

```
No navigation bar and no title in it: the note's title is the first line inside the scroll (kept). (‹) is the platform's back with its LABEL SUPPRESSED, drawn in the glass circle the library door occupies today — this is the shown choice that honours 2026-08-24 and needs the owner's word; the left-edge swipe pops. (☰) is the Properties key, (•••) the note-actions LivMenu from .top — unchanged. The strip stays and its pill names the document you are in (Safari's pill does; an open question whether it should fold here). Swipe the pill → the leaf is REPLACED by the neighbouring open document; back still pops to the Notes list. Tapping a [[link]] pushes another leaf (a chain is a stack). Re-tapping the selected Notes tab pops to the list; the document stays on the desk and the pill still names it. When the keyboard rises, the strip retires (2026-08-02) and the tab bar hides; the top chevron stays, so the note with a keyboard up is not a cul-de-sac; the editor's own accessory toolbar rides the keyboard. Marker liv.surface.document; the tab bar's selected item names the PLACE you are standing in (Notes), the pill names the DOCUMENT.

*The Open drawer: a medium-detent sheet over Calendar (tab 4)*
```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ ⟨Work ▾⟩                     │
│ ‹  Tue 9 Sept  ›             │
│  9 ┃ Standup                 │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮ │
│ │           ━━━            │ │

│ │ │Kitchen   │ │Plumber   ││ │
│ │ │plan    ✕ │ │quote   ✕ ││ │
│ │ │area·Home │ │area·Home ││ │

```
Reached by tapping ▣ 3 on the pill from any tab. A system .sheet with a grabber, detents medium/large, surface background — the same recipe as the properties card and the record card, so the app has ONE 'over where you stand' shape for everything that is not a place. It covers the strip and the tab bar (a sheet is presented over the window), and Calendar's day title stays rendered above it — the geometry the drive.sh 'drawer' check asserts. Content is the existing TabCard grid unchanged (active card outlined in accent, ✕ closes, dashed New note bottom-right → a new note pushed on the current tab). 'Inactive · 2' is a disclosure at the foot (Chrome's shelf, kept); expanded, it shows 'Close all' trailing and the aged cards with 'NOTE · 24D' footers. Picking Plumber quote dismisses the sheet, pulls it off any other tab's stack, and pushes it on Calendar's: back = Calendar, exactly what 67801ae had to special-case. Swipe the sheet down = Done. Calendar's parked day is untouched. Marker liv.overlay.tabs.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- TAB BAR (the platform's; persistent on every root and leaf except a full-screen cover, a sheet and the keyboard): five items in day order — Today ☀, Notes ▤, Inbox ⌂ with the unrouted-count badge (.badge), Calendar ▦, Tasks ☑ — glyphs from Glyph.swift, words under them (Todoist's form, 2026-09-05; the label size raised through UITabBarAppearance to a Theme token, since the platform's 10pt is under the app's floor); selected item filled. iOS 17: the standard bar; iOS 26: glass and minimise-on-scroll for free. Replaces: the library door as a place-switch, the panel's six rows.
- THE STRIP (Bar.swift's capsule reshaped, LivBar height kept; each surface's own foot via safeAreaInset — 2026-09-08 kept; present on every root and leaf, never hidden for an empty desk; retires under the keyboard and, iOS 18+, on scroll with the top doors): three worded keys — Search · the Open pill [kind glyph · active title · ▣ N] 'Open' ('Nothing open' when empty; a minimised record borrows the slot) · + with the kind it makes here. Replaces: the Back and Forward keys, the numbered box (LivGlyph.day), the minimised-record pill above the bar.
- TOP DOORS (floating 44pt glass circles under the clock over LivTopScrim, no bar behind them — 2026-07-31 and 2026-08-17 kept): on a ROOT, leading = the SCOPE button (workspace name + chevron.down; a11y 'Workspace: Work'), trailing = nothing; on a LEAF (a document), leading = the back chevron with the label suppressed, trailing = Properties (slider.horizontal.3) and ••• — unchanged from today; on Everything, leading = the back chevron and the search field at its head. The band retires with the buttons (2026-09-07). Replaces: the library door and PanelMark, the panel foot.
- SCREEN NAMES: inside the scroll as full-size titles (2026-09-02) — Today's date, 'Notes', 'Inbox', 'Calendar' (its day title), 'Tasks', 'Everything'. No navigation bar titles anywhere.
- KEYBOARD ACCESSORY (editor only): the existing formatting toolbar above the keyboard; the strip and tab bar are hidden while it is up; the top chevron stays.
- SHEETS (transient, one recipe): the drawer (Open · N), the properties card, the history card, the record card, the scope card (workspaces / filters / new filter / trash / settings rows, from the TOP edge since its button is at the top), Settings, Trash — all system .sheet, medium/large, grabber. Cards asked as questions (New, Note actions, route, trash confirm) stay LivMenu from the edge their button is on — the New card from .bottom again.
- FULL-SCREEN COVERS (transient): the camera only. Search is no longer a cover; the tab switcher is no longer a cover.
- BADGE: one — Inbox's unrouted count. No other counts persist on screen.
- DELETED CHROME: the library panel and its wash/push/peek, the panel drag recognizer, the top-left door and PanelMark, the Back/Forward keys and disabledInk, the numbered box, the switcher's + | N open | Done footer, the Inactive band's 'Back to tabs', the pill above the bar, the search cover's own chrome, the desk's leading-corner mask and travel.

**Gestures.**
- Tap a tab → that place's root, position restored from `spots` and its stack from the platform; tap the selected tab again → pop to its root (the 'tap the view you are in → root' rule, now visible; on iOS 17 SwiftUI needs the selection-binding idiom to do it, on 26 the platform does).
- Left-edge swipe or the top-left chevron on a leaf → pop one step (a document chain pops step by step; a pill-picked document pops to the root).
- Tap the pill's title → push the active document on the current tab; swipe the pill left/right → the previous/next live tab becomes active (replaces a document leaf, pushes over a root) — a fixed handle, the one hidden gesture; tap ▣ N → the drawer sheet.
- Tap Search → Everything is pushed on the current tab with the field empty and focused-on-request (a second tap in the field raises the keyboard); type → hits; back or Cancel → pop to where you stood.
- Scroll down on a root (iOS 18+) → the strip and the scope circle retire through chromeAway; scroll up → they return; the tab bar stays (iOS 26: minimises by the platform's rule).
- Tap + → make what the place holds (task due the shown day / event on the shown day / a note pushed as a leaf with the caret in it); hold + (0.45s, or the a11y 'More' action) → the New card (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text) as a LivMenu from .bottom, the edge its button is on.
- Tap the scope button (workspace name) → the scope card from the top edge: Workspaces, Filters, New filter, Trash, Settings.
- Swipe a record card down → it minimises into the pill's slot (kind glyph + title; tap restores, ✕ drops); the desk's title returns when the slot empties.
- Swipe down on the drawer sheet or tap outside → Done; a card's ✕ → close that tab, landing rules unchanged (index neighbour, else freshest live).
- Keyboard up in a note → strip and tab bar hidden, the top chevron and the edge swipe remain the way out.
- Long-press a tab-bar item: nothing. No drag-from-anywhere, no edge-drag for a drawer, no bar element that both scrolls and swipes.

**Capture.** Capture never asks, and this direction adds no surface to it and moves its door back to the thumb. The strip's + is the last key, at the far right ('maybe have the + button at far right', 2026-08-16), always present, and one tap makes the view's own kind with no question — in Today/Tasks a task due the shown day (createTask + due span + workspaces.stamp), opened as a record card over the root with the caret in the name; in Calendar an event on the shown day, as a card; in Notes/Inbox and on any document leaf a note (liv_create_note_at, stamped, requestFocus) pushed as a leaf ON THE TAB YOU ARE STANDING IN with the caret at its start, the strip and tab bar hidden under the keyboard — back pops to where you were, the note stays on the desk and the pill now names it. In Everything the + makes a note too, and the Create '\"words\"' row is the second create door there, which pushes the capture over Everything. Hold + → the New card (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text) as a LivMenu from .bottom, the edge the button is on (the existing livSheet(from:) call, unchanged); Note pushes a leaf, Task/Event raise a card, File runs the fileImporter and pushes the last pick, Scan text presents the camera cover. The instant after a note is created: the editor is the leaf, the pill reads its (empty → kind-named) title and the count ticks up, the Inbox badge ticks up if the note has no kind cells yet (the note IS the capture the Inbox lists, Desk.swift:684). The instant after a task is created: the card is up, nothing behind it moved, the strip and tab bar are still there under the card. From outside — the share extension spools to the App Group and the app drains at foreground, liv://capture?text= calls catchText — a catch is saved through liv_capture_at, stamped and NOT opened: the only visible change is the Inbox tab's badge, the platform's word for 'something is waiting for you' and the first honest face the queue has had. A bare liv://capture pushes a new note on the currently selected tab. Routing is unchanged: Inbox → Route row → the route card asking WHERE (six areas, then 'Not a note…'). What got worse: nothing in the flow; what got better since the first draft: the reflex is a thumb tap again, not a regrip.

**Back, links and catches.** BACK is the platform's stack pop, per tab, and nothing else: LivReturns (both stacks, the 20 cap, `here`, `land`) is deleted and each tab owns a NavigationPath of entity ids (plus one case for Everything). Pushes happen on: a row tap in any root, a search hit, a [[link]] tap, a properties-card reference chip, a Links row, a notification, liv://entity, a pill tap, a drawer pick, a camera Done, a file import, a new note, the Search key. THE SINGLE-STACK RULE: an entity is a leaf on at most one tab's path. Pushing entity E on tab B when E sits in tab A's path removes E and everything above it from A (those leaves were reached through E) — A's next visit lands one leaf lower, usually its root; the desk (DeskPlanes) is untouched by this, its activeTabId simply follows the selected tab's leaf. A document chain (A → [[B]] → [[C]]) is three leaves; back walks it. A pill swipe or drawer pick while a document is the leaf REPLACES that leaf (path.removeLast then append), so back from a switched-to document pops to the root, Safari-style — switching is not a step. Re-tapping the selected tab pops to root. Forward does not exist. A workspace switch pops every tab to root. Tab selection is not on any stack: Today → Calendar → Tasks by the bar leaves no history, which is what every tab-bar app does and what the diagnosis's ‹ silently did anyway. Records never enter a path; a card closes with a swipe and pops nothing. LINKS: liv://today|notes|inbox|calendar|tasks → select that tab and pop it to root (position from `spots` intact); liv://everything → push Everything on the current tab with an empty field (its route survives with its meaning: the whole box); liv://entity/<id> → box.refresh if unknown, then a document is pushed on the CURRENT tab (the way back is where you were; the single-stack rule applies), a record rises as a card; liv://capture, liv://capture/photo as in the capture flow; unknown hosts dropped; a cold-launch route parks until the TabView is up. A notification tap = liv://entity. Every arrival still runs surfaceCleanup, now smaller: dismiss any sheet (drawer, properties, history, record, scope, settings, trash) and the camera cover — a deep link never lands behind something. A [[link]] to a trashed thing still opens the 'This was deleted.' leaf.

**Survives in code.** Plane.swift DeskPlanes wholesale (481 lines): per-workspace desk (tabs, activeTabId, lastUsed), `spots` and position(feature), open(entity:) reuse, close/closeInactive landing rules, dropRecordsAndStrangers, the v3 keys and every folded older key, adopt(workspace:). The desk model is right; only its face changes, and the single-stack rule is enforced in the tab host, not here.; Positions.swift (370) and every park(feature, at:) call in the six views; the LivPosition roots. Each tab's root — and Everything as a leaf — still restores its token on arrival.; TabShape.of(row), DeskModel.open(id, as:) as the one door, openAsCard, the record card (Record.swift RecordBody, medium/large sheet, recordCardHost hoisted to the tab host), minimise/restore/drop — the pill's VIEW slot replaces the old pill above the bar; the model verbs stay.; Bar.swift: the LivBar capsule, its glass (LivGlass with its existing iOS 26/17 fork), the worded-key form and metrics — reshaped to three keys. Thrown from it: the Back/Forward keys, disabledInk, the five-key layout.; Chrome.swift: chromeAway/chromeHomeAgain/livHidesChrome and LivChromeScroll (Navigate.swift:223, iOS 18-gated) — they retire the doors and the strip exactly as they retire the doors and the bar today; surfaceCleanup (smaller); openAsCard; the create verbs.; Desk.swift: LivTopScrim (line 80) and surfaceFoot (104, 363) — the strip is hosted where the bar is hosted now, so 2026-09-08's 'the bar is the surface's own foot' is kept, not reversed; createHere/createNote/adoptCapture; Desk.swift:684's rule that a note made here is an Inbox capture.; Tabs.swift: TabCard, the grid layout with New-note bottom-right, LivTabs.days and the inactive predicate, LivTabs.choices [7,14,21,0] (finally read, by a Settings row). Thrown: the fullScreenCover host, defaultScrollAnchor(.bottom), the fixed footer, the InactiveTabs overlay chrome.; All six view bodies: Today.swift, Notes.swift (NotesList as the Notes root), Inbox.swift with the route card, Calendar.swift/Month.swift, Tasks.swift, Everything.swift with its marker and pills — now the body under Search.swift's field as a pushed leaf. Feature enum and Feature.makes unchanged; Feature.inOrder unchanged (the tab host takes its first five, Everything is the sixth).; Search.swift's field, facet chips, constraint menu, grouped results, Create row, picker mode — re-hosted as a leaf with Everything as its empty state instead of a cover; the cover's own recordCardHost and pill go.; WorkspaceSwitch.swift (rows, edit/trash context menus, the inline New workspace / New filter forms) — becomes the body of the scope card, gaining the filter rows, Trash and Settings rows that Panel.swift used to own; livSheet(from: .top) unchanged.; Detail.swift EntityInspector + InspectorValueSheet, History.swift, Links.swift, Settings.swift (plus one row), Trash.swift, Camera/Scan, FileImport, Editor/EditorText/EditorStyle/EditorCost, Files.swift, Outbox.swift, Share.swift, Catch.swift — untouched.; Menu.swift LivMenu / livSheet(from:) for the New card, note actions, route card, trash confirm; the edge rule holds (the + is at the bottom, so its card comes from the bottom; the scope button is at the top, so its card comes from the top).; Theme.swift as the only home of sizes and colours (new tokens: the tab bar's label size, the pill's title width; nothing typed in a view); Glyph.swift's kind, area and view glyphs; LivListRow / LivMenuRow; Kit.swift's kind-named nameless rows; Surface.swift's liv.surface.* and liv.overlay.* markers (one added: liv.chrome.strip; Everything's marker unchanged).; Box.swift as the one FFI file — this direction adds no verb and touches no core/services/ffi code.; Routes.swift host handling (the Everything and entity hosts change what they push, not what they parse); Notify.onOpen; the share extension and the spool drain in App.swift — unchanged, now visible through the Inbox badge.; drive.sh's discipline (assert geometry or rendered text, break one on purpose) and its unchanged checks: rows, lens, facets, create, routes (re-targeted), history, spool, areas, vault, cycles, quiet, chrome (re-targeted at the strip).

**Thrown away.** Panel.swift (461 lines: LibraryPanel, the rows, the foot, ViewCounts' render walk) and PanelDrag.swift (280: the window-level recognizer, pagerZone veto). No drawer exists.; Bar.swift's Back and Forward keys, disabledInk, the five-key layout and the Obsidian-measured key SET; LivGlyph.day(n) and PanelMark in Glyph.swift.; Navigate.swift LivReturns (places, forwards, cap, here/land), goBack/goForward, the `.document`/`.state` LivPlace enum. Each tab owns a NavigationPath instead.; Chrome.swift: `state: Feature` → `selected: Tab` plus five paths; `openDoc`'s `state == .notes` guard (the active document is the selected tab's leaf); go/goToRoot/showList and their stack-clearing; openDocument's `state = .notes`; deskShift/panelProgress/panelOut/setLibrary and the panel travel; surfaceCleanup shrinks to 'dismiss every sheet and cover'.; Desk.swift: DeskHost's choice between NotesList and EntityTabBody by openDoc (each tab's NavigationStack draws root vs leaf); the leading-corner mask, wash and offset; the top doors' library-door branch; the pill above the bar in surfaceFoot.; Tabs.swift: TabSwitcher's fullScreenCover, defaultScrollAnchor(.bottom) and the fixed footer; InactiveTabs as an overlay with its own band and 'Back to tabs'.; App.swift: the switcher and search fullScreenCovers, the search cover's own recordCardHost and pill, the -desk.boot library|grid flags (no panel to open); 'switcher' and 'search' boot flags become 'drawer' and 'everything'.; The 2026-08-15 'library is a PLACE' model, the 2026-08-23 panel geometry (LivPanel.width, peek 100, the wash) and the bar's browser key set — superseded by there being no panel and no browser.; Everything as a tab-bar slot: it is the sixth view still, pushed by the Search key; its -desk.boot flag boots it as a leaf over Today.; drive.sh checks panel, bar, grid, desk and workspace as written, and the 'goto notes may not answer document' guard (documents are leaves, never Notes' answer) — replaced by tabs, strip, drawer and scope checks, with `desk`'s count-across-views assertion absorbed by `strip`.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- Does the desk have a state of its own? Yes: the pill is its state and its face, present on every tab, independent of which place is selected; a document is drawn as a leaf on the selected tab's stack, never by borrowing Notes; and an entity is a leaf on at most one stack, so the desk and the screen agree.
- What does the 'where am I' indicator say while a document is on screen? The selected tab says the place you opened it from (Today, Notes, Calendar…); the pill says which document. Two questions, two honest answers.
- Is 'Notes' a view, a container, or a list? A list — the Notes tab's root — and only that. The container is the pill/drawer ('Open'); a document is a leaf and has no place-name.
- Are the Notes list and the switcher grid one surface or two? Two, each demoted to one job: the list is 'what I have' (a place), the grid is 'what I am in the middle of' (a state, behind the pill). Rule 6 is satisfied by deleting the full-screen mode, the footer and the panel row instead.
- What does the number count, and can the user see it? Live documents, and the pill shows the thing itself — its title next to the count — instead of an a11y label saying 'documents'.
- Is ‹ the undo of any navigation or of document opens only? Neither: it is the stack pop of the tab you are on, and tab moves are not navigation (the platform's rule). It wears Apple's chevron in Apple's slot, not a browser's glyph in a browser's bar.
- Why does go clear the stack while openDocument pushes? The question dissolves: one stack per tab, pushes are opens, pops are back, tab selection is orthogonal. LivReturns is deleted.
- Is 'tap the row of the view you are in → root' visible? Yes: re-tapping the selected tab pops to root, an iOS convention every user already has, the same on all five tabs (Notes included: it pops the document leaf and 'deselects' nothing invisible).
- What shows that a tool resumes while a document is only open? Each tab visibly resumes (its stack and its parked token); a document you left is visibly still on the pill. Two remembered things, two faces.
- Is Open [n] a destination, a mode or the desk? The desk, as a pill you can act on without leaving where you stand; its grid is a sheet, never a full-screen mode, and the way back to a note from any tab is one thumb-tap on the pill.
- Does + stay or move you, and what should its word say? Still two consequences: a record stays (card), a note moves (pushed leaf). The word stays the kind, as ruled, and the key is back at the thumb. What improves is that 'moving' is the platform's push on the same tab with the platform's back, so the consequence is legible even if it is still two consequences. Not resolved.
- Are the six rows peers, or different kinds of thing? Five are places on the bar; Inbox is marked as a queue by its badge; Everything is a place you push, not a slot; the desk is not a row at all. The bar shows which is which by what it badges and what it leaves off — and, stated plainly, it makes three of them read as kinds.
- Where does a note opened from Today/Calendar/Search belong when you are done? Back where you came from (pop), and on the desk (pill) until it ages out or is closed. Nowhere else.
- What shape makes tasks and calendar central without a bar of view keys? This direction is honest that it IS a bar of view keys — five places only, no verbs on that row, notes a separate peer, the actions on the strip beneath — and argues that the 'sloppy ClickUp' rejection was of a bar that mixed places with Find and Open and deleted the library. It cannot promise the owner will see the difference; the artboard must show both side by side.
- Thumb zone or top-left? Everything you DO is at the thumb: the place switch (tab bar), the desk (pill), Search and + (strip). Only the lens (scope) is top-left, in the circle the door already occupies, and it is a name, not a toggle.
- Why land on Notes after a workspace switch? It no longer does: a switch pops every tab to root and stays on the selected tab; the home is Today and only Today.
- Which of the two doors to a note survives? Both, on the same edge: the Notes tab (all notes) and the pill (open notes); the numbered box and the panel row are the doors that die.
- Can it be settled without a mockup, and which assertion proves it? No — mockup-first, beside the four-floors screenshot with the differences labelled. On a device: drive.sh 'tabs' (select each of the five, assert its surface marker; Search from each, assert liv.surface.everything), 'strip' (open a note from Today, pop, assert the pill's rendered title equals the note and the count is identical on all five tabs), 'drawer' (assert the sheet's frame stops at the medium detent with Calendar's day title still rendered above it), 'scope' (the workspace card's rendered rows from the top-left button, the card's top edge under the clock), 'move' (open A from Today, open A from Notes, re-tap Today, assert Today's root is rendered), and the existing 'lens', 'rows', 'chrome', 'create', 'routes'.

**Changed after the judges.**
- Deployment floor checked in the tree: MinimumOSVersion 17.0 in both plists and `-target arm64-apple-ios17.0-simulator` in build.sh; the only #available forks are iOS 26 (LivGlass) and iOS 18 (LivChromeScroll). Every iOS 26-only piece of the first draft — tabViewBottomAccessory, tabBarMinimizeBehavior, the search-role tab, the strip folding into the bar — is removed. One drawing for iOS 17; iOS 26 changes materials and minimise, nothing structural. The 'least invention' claim is restated at its true size (all three judges).
- The + and Search move to the thumb. The first draft put + top-right, 600pt away; all three judges called it the direction's worst placement of its most important act. The strip is now Bar.swift's own capsule with ‹ › removed and the box widened into a titled pill — Search · Open pill · + — at the far right where the owner put it on 2026-08-16. Roots have an empty top-right. The New card comes from .bottom again, the edge its button is on.
- Everything restored as a named place, not 'search's empty state': it keeps its marker, its pills, its lens, its position token and its liv://everything route, and is pushed on the current tab by the Search key with nothing typed. It is not a tab-bar slot because the platform's iPhone bar holds five before growing 'More' (all three judges; the owner's 'keep the six views').
- The single-stack rule adopted and the pop specified: an entity is a leaf on at most one tab's path; pushing it elsewhere removes it and everything above it from the old path; the desk's activeTabId follows the selected tab's leaf (judges 2 and 3).
- Bands measured instead of asserted: roots keep no navigation bar (2026-07-31 honoured rather than reversed), the scope button lives in the door's existing circle, so the only new band is the platform's 49pt tab bar — one row of 56 lost at 393×852 and at 375×667, unscrolled and scrolled, never two (judges 1 and 2).
- Rulings reversed reduced: 2026-07-31 (no top bar) is now kept on roots and leaves; 2026-09-08 (the bar is the surface's own foot) is kept because the strip is hosted in surfaceFoot; chromeAway and LivTopScrim survive rather than being replaced by platform behaviour that does not exist on 17. survives_in_code / thrown_away corrected accordingly: Bar.swift is reshaped, not deleted; Desk.swift's scrim and foot stay.
- The labelled back is drawn suppressed in the leaf wireframe and named as a shown choice needing the owner's word (judges 1 and 3).
- The four-floors comparison made an explicit artboard requirement with five labelled differences, and the honest admission that the revised shape is CLOSER to four floors than the first draft was (judge 1's and 3's first must_fix).
- The kinds-are-peers relocation stated on the artboard and in conceded rather than argued past (judges 1 and 3).
- drive.sh costed against the real script (23 subcommands read): tour/panel/bar/grid/desk/workspace die or are rewritten as tabs/strip/drawer/scope/move; chrome, create, routes re-targeted; rows, lens, facets, history, spool, areas, vault, cycles, quiet untouched (judge 3).
- The keyboard-up state answered (judge 2's cross-cut): the strip and tab bar hide, the top chevron and edge swipe remain, so a note with a keyboard up has a way out.
- The pill's sideways swipe kept but demoted to a power gesture under the 'a fixed handle swipes, chips scroll' rule, with the drawer as the taught door and an open question on dropping it (judge 2's collision class).
- Re-tap-to-pop noted as needing SwiftUI's selection-binding idiom on iOS 17 (the platform gives it free only in UIKit and on 26), so the 'platform teaches it' claim is exact.

**Conceded.**
- It re-argues a rejected shape and is now closer to it: five places on a bar with Find, Open and + beneath. The distinctions are real and the owner may not see them; that is the direction's largest risk and only a mockup beside the four-floors screenshot can test it.
- The kinds-are-peers question is relocated into a bar of kinds, not answered; a task is still a card and Notes | Calendar | Tasks reads as three kinds of thing.
- One row of content is lost on every screen size and, on iOS 17 and 18, never regained on scroll.
- It has no hand of its own: the skeleton is Apple's (tab bar, push, sheet), the strip is Safari's bottom crossed with Music's bar, the tab bar is Todoist's; identity comes entirely from direction A's furniture. As a control that is the point; as the product the owner's 'a collage of other people's apps has no hand of its own' applies.
- Everything is one hop deeper than the other five and its door wears the word 'Search'; the platform's five-slot bar leaves no honest sixth slot.
- Forward is gone, and with it the 20-place way-back diary that survived panel moves; back is per-tab and shallow.
- The library-as-a-place model (2026-08-15) and all its mechanics are deleted with nothing equivalent; there is no place called the library, only places and a lens.
- The strip names an open document at the sill, which is within reach of the 2026-08-23 'not a tab bar' ruling; the direction needs the owner's sentence on it and cannot pre-empt it.
- The single-stack rule produces one surprising pop (a tab shortens behind your back when you re-open its leaf elsewhere).
- The pill's swipe is the one unlabelled gesture; a manual-less person will not find it, and does not need to.
- On iOS 17 the 'least invention' evidence is thinner than first claimed: the strip is built from the app's own capsule, retire-on-scroll is the app's own iOS 18 machinery, and re-tap-to-pop needs a SwiftUI idiom. What is native for free: TabView, NavigationStack, .sheet detents, .badge, the pop gesture.
- + still has two consequences (a card for a record, a push for a note); the word says the kind and the key is at the thumb, but vagueness 9 is not resolved here.

**Open questions.**
- Will the owner see five places on a tab bar as the rejected four floors? The distinctions (no verbs on the row of places, notes a peer, Inbox badged, library contents kept in a card, the strip is his bar minus ‹ ›) are real but subtle; only the labelled side-by-side mockup can say.
- Is a pill that names one open document 'a tab bar' in the sense of 2026-08-23 ('Not a tab "bar", remove it')? It is one title and a count, not chips, but it is at the sill and it is about tabs — one sentence from him settles every 'the desk gets a face' claim in the field, this one included.
- Everything as a pushed place behind a key worded 'Search': does the whole-box view lose its name for people who never type, and should the key's word be 'Everything' (honest about the place) or 'Search' (honest about the field)? Or should Unfiled become the Inbox tab's second lens?
- The labelled back suppressed (2026-08-24) versus the platform default; and roots without a navigation bar (2026-07-31 kept) versus the platform's large-title bar — both are drawn the app's way and need the owner's word.
- Does the Inactive shelf survive as a disclosure section, or does Safari's 'Close Tabs after…' Settings row replace it outright (the first reader of the [7,14,21,0] choices either way)?
- Where saved filters sit in the scope card, and whether the active filter needs a face on the root (a LensChip under the title, as Search already draws) now that the panel's lit row is gone.
- Should the pill fold while the leaf IS the active document (Music's bar does not show the song you are looking at; Safari's pill does show the page)? Folding gives a note one more row; keeping it gives the swipe a home.
- The single-stack rule's pop: is 'coming back to Today lands on its root because you re-opened the note from Notes' acceptable, or should a re-open from another tab simply SELECT the tab that already holds it (Files' behaviour, no move, two stacks)?
- Should the strip's sideways swipe exist at all? It is the one unlabelled gesture; the drawer does the same job visibly.
- iOS 17 is the verified floor (Info.plist MinimumOSVersion 17.0; build.sh targets ios17.0). Is the owner willing to let the design gain the platform's minimise and glass on 26 without drawing for them, or should the floor be raised so the tab bar can carry more of the work (search-role tab on 18+, accessory on 26)?

## 8. Near-duplicates, kept for the record

- **The Desk** — `axes` upside down — documents as the ground and tools on a slide-in wall is Obsidian's assignment of the axes renamed, which the direction itself concedes; last on all three lenses, so it is retired to the appendix.

### 8. The Desk

*The open documents are a vertical fan of paper cards on a desk that is the ground of the app; the tools hang on a wall that slides in over the desk from the left at the panel's ruled width — and the wall on Today is what you face at cold launch; every note is in a drawer (the Notes view) that pulls up from under the fan; one three-key glass capsule sits at the foot of every surface — the wall's handle · Search · + kind; there is no library, no numbered box, no switcher and no back stack, because where you are is a place you can see and back is the move you made, reversed.*

**1. The navigation concept.** Three places, arranged like a real desk, plus one bar that is the same everywhere. THE DESK (centre, the ground): what you are working on — the open tabs, drawn as paper. Two zoom levels of one surface: the SPREAD (every open card fanned vertically by last use — the active card on top with its first three lines, the rest as 56pt title bands; stale cards fold into one 'N stale ▸' band at the foot of the fan, Chrome's shelf drawn as a fold instead of a second screen) and a LIFTED card (the top card filling the screen: today's editor, Properties and ••• top-right, the deck edge above the bar naming the card beneath). Lifting is a tap on a band; dropping is a tap on the deck edge. THE WALL (left): the five tools. It is the library panel's own mechanics — PanelDrag from anywhere, the desk pushed, rounded and washed, LivPanel.peek = 100 exactly as ruled 2026-08-23 — with the content swapped from a menu of doors to ONE TOOL at its parked position (Calendar on March, Tasks on its filter; the existing spots). Which tool: the bar's first key. The wall is what you face at cold launch, on Today (2026-08-18 kept: planning is what this one is for); slide it off and the desk is beneath. THE DRAWER (below): the Notes view. Every document in the box by log recency (today's NotesList, lensed), cards already on the desk marked 'on desk' — a surface named after a thing contains it — under a field that holds only words (today's SearchView); type and the same drawer shows hits across every kind. It is reached by pulling its lip at the bottom of the spread (resting) or by the bar's Search key (keyboard up), the same drag-or-key pair the library panel has today. THE BAR is one capsule of three worded keys on the desk, the lifted card and the wall, each slot with one meaning: slot 1 is THE WALL (on the desk it reads the wall's parked tool — 'Today' — and slides the wall in; on the wall it reads 'Today ▾' and raises the tool picker, a LivMenu from the bottom with the six views in Feature.inOrder as Notesnook rows with their counts, the current one soft-filled; hold it from the desk for the picker directly, the same tap/hold idiom the + already has); slot 2 is SEARCH (the drawer, field focused, from anywhere); slot 3 is + <kind> (the place's kind in one tap, the New menu on hold — unchanged). Above the spread a one-line strip of glass chips is the wall seen from the desk: the date, then only the counts that are non-zero — 'due', 'late', 'caught' — and the workspace name with a chevron; each chip is a shortcut onto the wall at that tool, the workspace chip opens the workspace card. Records stay cards: a task or event rises as today's half-height sheet over whichever place is in front and minimises to the same pill. Nothing replaces anything: the wall slides over the desk and slides off; the drawer slides up and down; a card lifts and drops. The app has one navigation verb — move — and every move is undone by moving back.

**2. Note Tabs and the Note List.** NOTE TABS are the cards on the desk. The active tab (planes.activeTabId) is the top card, drawn with its first lines; lastUsed is the fan order, top to bottom; the count is the visible length of the fan, so no key prints it. lastUsed is stamped on OPEN and on DROP, never while you flip — so the fan is a stable, visible stack while a card is lifted, which is what lets the deck edge be a real ‹ ›: ‹ is the card beneath by name, › is the card above once you have walked down. Close is the List's swipe-left on a band (Mail's form: partial reveals the ✕, full swipe commits; a diagonal flick scrolls); the note goes back into the drawer at its recency position — nothing is lost, a tab is device state. The same entity is never two cards (planes.open(entity:) reuses). A card past LivTabs.days does not leave the desk: it folds into the 'N stale ▸' band at the foot of the fan, which unfolds in place like LATE does on Today; the threshold stays the picker in Settings. The fan is not reorderable: last-used is the order, and the reorder verb from the first draft is dropped (the owner asked for one thing). THE NOTE LIST is the drawer, and the drawer is the Notes view: every document (note, file, capture) in the box by log recency, lensed, Trash as its last row; rows already on the desk carry 'on desk' as their fact and tapping one lifts that card rather than minting another. The drawer's field holds only words; facets are chips under it; a non-empty field turns the drawer into Search.swift across all kinds (a record hit raises a card over the drawer; a document hit lifts onto the desk and the drawer drops; the Create row births a scrap and lifts it). The drawer remembers its words and its scroll like a tool — Notes' position token in spots — so returning to a search is a pull. So the two lists that today compete for 'get me back' — the switcher grid and NotesList — become one continuous stack: the fan on top, the drawer beneath, one lip between them; and 'Notes' names exactly one thing again, the drawer, which is the view whose root is every note.

**3. The views in the hierarchy.** Not a hierarchy — a floor plan with the six views intact (Feature.inOrder unchanged: Today · Notes · Inbox · Calendar · Tasks · Everything). CENTRE: the Desk, the ground, two zoom levels (spread, lifted). LEFT: the Wall, holding FIVE of the views as tools — Today, Inbox, Calendar, Tasks, Everything — each singular, each resuming its parked position (spots), one at a time, chosen by the picker card from the bar's first key. Today and Inbox still ignore the workspace lens; Calendar, Tasks, Everything wear it. BELOW: Notes, the sixth view, as the drawer — every document plus search, lensed. The picker lists all six; its Notes row slides the wall off and pulls the drawer, so the six views have one declared order and one picker even though one of them is a drawer and five are hooks. ARRIVAL: cold launch is the wall on Today with the spread beneath; liv://<view> slides the wall in on that view (Notes pulls the drawer); liv://entity/<id> lifts a document or raises a record card; liv://capture?text= and the share sheet capture, stamp and do not open — the strip's 'caught' chip rolls. OVER EVERYTHING, from the edge their button is on: the record card, the properties card, the history card, the create menu, the note-actions menu, the route card, the tool picker (bottom, from slot 1), the workspace card (from the strip's chip, now carrying the saved filters and 'New filter' below the workspaces and Settings at its foot). Workspaces: one desk per workspace (DeskPlanes already keyed so); switching swaps the fan, the drawer and the wall's spots and lands on the incoming SPREAD, never on a view. The six view bodies (Today.swift, Inbox.swift, Calendar.swift, Tasks.swift, Everything.swift, NotesList) survive unchanged in content; only their container changes — five sit in the wall at the panel's width, one sits in the drawer.

**4. Why it is not Obsidian.** Against the references reader's list, item by item, after the critique. (1) The bottom capsule with the browser's five keys in Obsidian's order and proportions: gone; the bar is three worded keys — the wall's handle, Search, + kind — the same three on every surface, no ‹ › slots, no numbered box. (2) The numbered box drawn as Obsidian's daily-note glyph: gone with the count; the count is a fan you can see. (3) The tab switcher 'verbatim in structure' — full-screen cover, two-column grid, ✕ per card, dashed new card, 'N open | Done' footer: gone; the spread is not a cover you enter and leave with Done, it is the ground, and 'new' is the bar's +. (4) The left drawer as a menu of doors with a pinned foot: gone; nothing to the left of the desk is a list of links — the wall IS the tool, full height, at its parked position, and the workspace name has moved to the strip where it names the desk you are at. (5) The top-left sidebar toggle, 'the most Obsidian pixel on screen': gone; the wall's handle is a worded key at the thumb, and the spread's top-left is the date chip, which is content. (6) 'No home; open on the last tab': the app opens on the day, on the wall, over a desk it never opens INTO. (7) The Recents-list-plus-tab-grid duplication: collapsed into one stack — fan over drawer, one lip. (8) One workspace = one vault with the switcher hanging from a foot: the storage is kept; the switcher hangs from the desk's name on the strip. (9) Edge-swipe for a drawer: the swipe survives but reveals a place with content, never a list of links; Obsidian's drawer has icon tabs across its top — the wall has no tabs at all, its picker is a card from the thumb that exists only while asked for. (10) 'Documents are where you stand; everything else is a drawer you visit' — this is the one item the direction KEEPS, on purpose, and says so: the desk is the ground and the tools are a place you slide over it. What makes it not Obsidian's assignment in practice rather than in name: you arrive at the wall, not the desk; the wall is a whole tool at its parked position, not a menu; the pile (due · late · caught) is printed on the desk itself; and the tool is one tap from the thumb, not a hamburger. What it does not do is make a task and a note the same kind of object on the same surface — see conceded. What deliberately still resembles Obsidian is below navigation and was ruled by the owner: the title inside the editor, tap-follows-link, find-or-create, the ••• as a bottom sheet of grouped cards. The structural signature is the thing no reference app has: a phone whose ground is a fan of paper, a wall on one side, a drawer underneath, one three-key foot, and one navigation verb.

**5. Trade-offs.**
- WORSE: the wall is the panel's width (screen − 100pt), so every tool loses 100pt against today's full-screen views — Today's seven-day strip, Calendar's timeline and Tasks' rows all draw at 293pt on a 393pt phone, beside a washed desk. This is the price of not reversing 'Panel should not be full screen!' (2026-08-23); if the owner rules that a TOOL is not a panel, LivPanel.peek becomes 28 in one line and Calendar gets its width back.
- WORSE: cold launch is Today on the wall with the pushed, washed desk beside it — the app opens looking like a panel is open. Honouring 'launch on Today' (2026-08-18) inside this geometry means the first thing seen every morning is a tool at panel width. The owner may reject that on sight; the alternative (launch on the spread) reverses the ruling and is not proposed.
- ‹ › leave the bar. On the desk and the wall there is no back key at all — back is spatial (drop, slide off, pull down). On a lifted card ‹ › survive as the deck edge, worded (‹ Dentist notes · 2 more ›), walking the fan rather than a stack. This reverses the 2026-08-23 'browser's set, literally' ruling for two of three surfaces and must be put to the owner as the headline, not a footnote.
- View selection moves from a sidebar (2026-08-17: 'global STATE and view selection on the left') to a picker card at the thumb, which is the owner's own 2026-08-16 idea ('a menu for different views like calendar… and also notes') that he superseded a day later. It is one door, not two — the library is gone — but it reverses a standing ruling and he chose the Notesnook rows over it once already.
- From the desk, the tool the wall is parked on is one tap (slot 1) or one drag; any OTHER tool is a hold or two taps (slot 1 → picker → row), the same count as today's library door → row. Tool-to-tool on the wall is two taps, the same as today. Nothing gets faster except 'back to the wall's last tool' and 'Search from anywhere'.
- The strip is four glass chips at caption 14 on one line when the pile is full — date, due/late, caught, workspace — which is the 'so many blips crammed into rows' risk on the app's front surface. Zero counts are omitted, so on a quiet day it is two chips; on a bad day it is the pile, in your face, which is the product's point and the owner's taste risk in one.
- The + on Inbox and Everything still makes a note and therefore moves you: the wall slides off and the sheet lifts on the desk. The one fix that would stop it — a note typed into a card over the wall — is the capture sheet the owner reverted on 2026-08-18. Kept as a known fault (see conceded).
- Two rightward gestures on a lifted card: the wall drag (PanelDrag's latch) and text selection — the conflict the app already carries, unchanged, with the bar's slot 1 as the taught door so nobody has to find the drag. The deck edge no longer swipes; ‹ › on it are taps.
- The drawer lists cards that are also on the desk, marked 'on desk'. That is a duplicate by construction, chosen so that Notes contains every note; the cost is that the drawer's top rows are usually the fan again, one pull away from where you just saw them.
- Records are still not on the desk. A task is a card, a note is paper; the strip counts them and the wall shows them one tap away, but the diagnosis's root — one kind got the main surface — is kept, not answered (conceded).
- The spread costs vertical room: top card 144pt, bands 56pt, the drawer lip 44pt pinned above the 66pt bar; about eight bands fit under the strip on a 6.1-inch phone and the fan scrolls past that. A desk with fifteen cards is a messy desk; the stale fold keeps it honest, not pretty.
- drive.sh: tour survives (six goto targets, Notes landing on liv.surface.notes with more rows than open tabs), panel becomes wall, grid becomes fan, bar is rewritten for three keys, and flip is new. It is a real day of harness work and must land with the change or the change is unverified — as revs 54–61 already are.

**Wireframes.**

*Cold launch — the Wall on Today, desk washed beside it*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ Today                ▏╭───╮│
│ Tuesday 9 September  ▏│Kit││
│ Mo Tu We Th Fr Sa Su ▏│che││
│  7  8 [9] 10 11 12 13▏╰───╯│
│                      ▏╭───╮│
│ ○ Call dentist 10:00 ▏│Den││
│ ○ Send Q4 plan 14:00 ▏╭───╮│
│ ○ Fix the tap        ▏│Q4 ││
│ ▸ LATE 1             ▏╭───╮│
│ ▸ Done 2             ▏│Rea││
│                      ▏╰───╯│
│ 2 caught · Route     ▏▒▒▒▒▒│
│                      ▏     │
│ (Today ▾  Search  +Task)   │
└────────────────────────────┘
```
Today.swift unchanged inside, at LivPanel.width (screen − 100). The 100pt sliver on the right is the spread's card edges, washed 50%, taking only the tap or drag that slides the wall off — the library panel's own geometry, content swapped. The bar is the wall's foot: 'Today ▾' raises the tool picker (LivMenu from the bottom, six rows in Feature.inOrder with counts, current soft-filled); Search opens the drawer with the keyboard; + makes the tool's kind as a card. A note row lifts that note onto the desk and the wall slides off; a task row raises a record card over the wall. On a fresh box Today's empty state already names the six areas — the first open arrives furnished. Markers liv.overlay.wall + liv.surface.today.

*The Desk (spread) — the wall slid off, four cards open*
```text
┌────────────────────────────┐

│ [2 caught]        [Work ▾] │
```

The strip is the wall seen from the desk, as glass chips: the date → wall on Today, 'due/late' → Tasks, 'caught' → Inbox (omitted at zero), the workspace chip → workspace card (filters and Settings inside). The top card is the active tab with its first lines; bands beneath are the other tabs by last use with their area word at the right (the furniture as signature; monochrome — single-kind surface); the last band is the stale fold. The hatched lip pinned above the bar is the drawer: 'Notes', every document. Bar: slot 1 reads the wall's parked tool and slides it in (hold: the picker); Search; + Note. Empty desk: the six areas printed on the blotter in Liv's pen, no button — the + is the way out. Drawn on two strip lines here for 30 columns; one line on the phone. Marker liv.surface.desk.

One surface, the Notes view: with an empty field it is every document by recency (NotesList, lensed) with 'on desk' as the fact on rows already up there and Trash as its last row; with words it is Search.swift's grouped hits across all kinds, facet chips under the field, the Create row at the end. A document row lifts onto the desk and the drawer drops; a record row raises a card over the drawer; an 'on desk' row lifts that card. The strip stays above — the desk is still where you are. Opened resting by the lip, with the keyboard by the bar's Search key (the drag-or-key pair the library already has); the bar retires under the keyboard as today. Marker liv.surface.notes — the existing 'goto notes' and 'more rows than open tabs' checks keep working.

*A lifted card — the top card, full screen*

Today's editor, unchanged: title inside the scroll view, Properties and ••• top-right in glass; top-left is empty (no door, no hamburger — the wall's handle is in the bar). Above the bar the deck edge: a 44pt band naming the card beneath and counting the rest; tap its title to drop this card onto the spread, tap ‹ to lift the card beneath without leaving the editor, › appears once you have walked down. It is the browser's ‹ › with words, walking a stack you can see. Chrome retires on scroll as today; the deck edge goes with the bar. The rightward wall drag still works here, with the bar's 'Today' as the taught door. Marker liv.surface.document.

**Chrome — every persistent element.**
- THE STRIP (spread and drawer only, one line of glass chips under the clock): the date; 'N due · N late' and 'N caught' only when non-zero (a fact drawn only when it changes); the workspace name with a chevron at the trailing edge. Every chip is a shortcut onto the wall at that tool; the workspace chip opens the workspace card. Replaces the top-left library door and the panel's foot.
- PROPERTIES KEY and ••• (lifted card, top-right, 44pt glass): unchanged. The lifted card's top-left is empty.
- THE DECK EDGE (lifted card, a 44pt band above the bar, inside the surface's foot): '‹ <card beneath> · N more' and '›' when there is a card above; taps only. Replaces the Open [n] key, the switcher and the bar's ‹ ›.
- THE DRAWER LIP (spread, a 44pt hatched band pinned above the bar): 'Notes'. Replaces the panel's Notes row.
- THE BAR (every surface, one glass capsule, the surface's own foot, three worded keys in fixed slots): slot 1 the wall — the parked tool's name on the desk (slides the wall in; hold for the picker), the same name with ▾ on the wall (the picker); slot 2 Search; slot 3 + <kind> (Note on the desk; Feature.makes on the wall). Retires under the keyboard and on scroll, travels and washes with the desk under the wall. Replaces the five-key bar.
- THE TOOL PICKER (LivMenu from the bottom, over the wall or the desk): six rows in Feature.inOrder — monochrome glyph, name, count through the lens — the current tool soft-filled; the Notes row pulls the drawer. Replaces the panel's view rows.
- THE DESK SLIVER (wall out, LivPanel.peek = 100pt at the trailing edge): the fan's card edges, washed, taking only the tap or drag that brings the desk back. Unchanged geometry.
- THE MINIMISED RECORD PILL (above the deck edge or the bar, whichever is in front): unchanged.
- LivTopScrim under the status bar on every surface: unchanged.
- DELETED: the library door and PanelMark, the library panel and its foot, ‹ › as bar keys, Open [n] and LivGlyph.day, the tab switcher cover, the Inactive row and shelf, the Notes hero, the search cover (its body is now the drawer's).

**Gestures.**
- SPREAD: tap a band → lift that card (active, stamps lastUsed).
- SPREAD: swipe a band left (List swipe action; partial reveals ✕, full swipe commits) → close; the note is in the drawer at its recency.
- SPREAD: tap the stale fold → it unfolds in place (LATE's fold on Today); tap a stale band → lift it (which un-stales it).
- SPREAD: pull the drawer lip up, or tap it → the drawer, resting (Notes).
- BAR slot 1, desk or lifted card: tap → the wall slides in on its parked tool; hold 0.45s → the tool picker.
- BAR slot 1, wall: tap → the tool picker (six rows, Feature.inOrder, counts through the lens); the Notes row slides the wall off and pulls the drawer.
- BAR slot 2 anywhere: tap → the drawer with the field focused and the keyboard up.
- BAR slot 3 anywhere: tap → the place's kind in one move (desk: a note lifted with the caret; wall: Feature.makes); hold → the New menu (Note / Task / Event / File / Scan text). Unchanged.
- ANY PLACE: rightward drag from anywhere (PanelDrag's latch, |dx|>18 && dx>2|dy|, vetoed inside the Calendar pager) → the wall slides in on its parked tool. Unchanged mechanics.
- SPREAD strip: tap the date chip → wall on Today; 'due/late' → Tasks; 'caught' → Inbox; the workspace chip → workspace card.
- LIFTED: tap the deck edge's title → drop the card onto the spread (it stays the top card, lastUsed stamped); tap ‹ → lift the card beneath; tap › → the card above. No swipe on the deck edge.
- LIFTED: scroll down 44pt → keys, deck edge and bar retire; scroll up → return (today's chromeAway).
- WALL: drag left, or tap the desk sliver → the wall slides off onto the desk exactly as you left it (a lifted card stays lifted; the spread stays the spread).
- WALL: tap a note row → the wall slides off and the note lifts; tap a task/event → record card over the wall.
- DRAWER: drag down, or tap the strip above it → close, keeping words and scroll; type → hits; tap a facet chip → constraint chip (Only / Hide / Any in the bottom menu, ✕ drops it). Unchanged.
- CARDS (record, properties, history, picker, menus): swipe down → dismiss (a minimised record goes to the pill; tap the pill → restore). One dismissal for every sheet; the lifted document is not a sheet and drops by its key.

**Capture.** On the desk, tap + Note: liv_create_note_at → workspaces.stamp → a fresh card lifts on top of the fan with the caret in it (adoptCapture as now); it is both on the desk and in the Inbox's Route until filed (Desk.swift's rule that a note made from the bar IS an Inbox capture stands). Drop it and it is the top card of the spread; the strip's 'caught' chip rolls up by one — the desk telling you the pile grew. On the wall, + makes the tool's kind: Today/Tasks a task due the shown day, Calendar an event on the shown day (record cards over the wall, caret in the name); Inbox/Everything a note — the wall slides off and the sheet lifts on the desk (the one key that moves you; conceded). Hold + anywhere → the New menu; File → fileImporter, the last pick lifted; Scan text → the camera cover, Done lifts the last scan. liv://capture (bare) → a lifted new note; liv://capture?text=&url= → liv_capture_at, stamped, NOT opened; the share extension → spool → drained at foreground → liv_capture_at, stamped, NOT opened — in both cases the only visible event is the 'caught' chip rolling on the strip (or Today's own footer if the wall is in front), and the thing waits in the Inbox one tap away, where Route asks WHERE (the six areas) and the clerk's 'Work?' chip rides the row. The drawer's Create row births a scrap and lifts it. A calendar empty-grid tap creates an event as a card. Nothing asks; nothing types a grammar; nothing opens that did not ask to be opened.

**Back, links and catches.** There is no back stack: LivReturns and LivPlace are deleted, and the bar carries no ‹ › on the desk or the wall. Back is the reverse of the move you made — a lifted card drops to the spread; the wall slides off onto the desk exactly as you left it (lifted stays lifted); the drawer slides down keeping its words and scroll. The one place a ‹ › survives is where there is a stack you can see: on a lifted card the deck edge names the card beneath (‹) and, once you have walked down, the card above (›); because lastUsed is stamped on open and drop and never on a flip, the fan holds still while you walk it. Following a [[link]] lifts the target on top of the fan; the source is the card beneath, named on the deck edge — one tap back, one tap forward, a chain of four is four cards in order. A search hit lifts a card; to return to the search, pull the drawer — its field is still filled (Notes is a tool, its position survives). A note opened from Today lifts on the desk with the wall retracted; Today is one tap on slot 1 (or one drag) away, on the day you had. A properties-card reference chip, a Links row, a notification tap, liv://entity/<id>: a document lifts onto the desk (surfaceCleanup: every card and menu closed, the wall and drawer retracted), a record raises a card over whatever is in front. Re-opening the lifted card is not a move. What is lost: a way back across VIEWS (Today → Calendar → Today by a key) — which today's ‹ does not do either, since the panel's go clears the stack; the direction says so rather than pretending the browser's key was ever wired that way.
