Glyph.swift PanelMark (590) and LivGlyph.day.; The Notes hero; the a11y 'N documents open'; 'Open' as a printed word.; The -desk.boot flags library|grid|switcher|inactive|search (replaced by desk|lifted|notes; the six view names stay and mean 'the wall on that view', notes meaning the drawer; open and desk keep their meaning) and the drive.sh checks panel and grid (replaced by wall and fan).; The 2026-09-09 goToRoot special case and the 'a position survives, a document does not' rule — not because it was wrong but because no view can be wearing a document any more.; From the first draft of this direction: the reorder-by-drag verb, the 28pt sliver, the five-hook wall foot, the date chip as a wall handle on a lifted card, the deck-edge swipe, the title-band drag, and silent stale-to-drawer.

**What it answers in the diagnosis.**
- 1 Notes is two things wearing one name — RESOLVED. Notes is one thing: the drawer, the view whose root is every note. The desk is the container, and a document is drawn on the desk, never by borrowing a view's state.
- 2 The list from the panel, the documents from the bar — RESOLVED. One stack: the fan on top, the drawer beneath, physically continuous, one lip between them; the bar's Search key and the lip are the drag-or-key pair the library already has.
- 3 'Open N' counts an invisible thing — RESOLVED. The desk is visible; the deck edge names the next card and counts the rest; no key prints a count of a thing you cannot see.
- 4 'Notes' means three surfaces, '+ Note' in three views — RESOLVED for the word (one Notes, the drawer); PARTIAL for the +: it still changes its word by place, which the metaphor explains (the desk makes sheets, the wall makes what hangs there) but does not remove.
- 5 Two routes to a note with different back side effects — RESOLVED by deletion: no stacks, no push/clear policy; every route lifts the same card on the same desk and back is the same move.
- 6 The back key is a document-history key in a browser's slot — RESOLVED by moving it to where its meaning is true: ‹ › live on the lifted card's deck edge, worded, walking the visible fan; nowhere else.
- 7 goToRoot is a patch with two branches — RESOLVED. The picker's row for the tool you are on does nothing on every tool, because no tool can be wearing a document; the Notes row pulls the drawer, which is its root.
- 8 Positions and documents are two remembered things the user never sees as different — RESOLVED. A tool visibly resumes on the wall; a document visibly sits on the desk; the drawer visibly remembers its words. 'A document is plural, a tool is singular' has a face.
- 9 + stays or moves depending on kind — PARTIAL, conceded. On the desk + always stays; on the wall a task/event stays (a card) while a note takes you to the desk. The only fix is the capture sheet the owner refused.
- 10 The switcher is a full-screen mode and the only way back to a note from five views — RESOLVED. The desk is the ground and is one slide from every tool, onto the card you left lifted; there is no mode.
- 11 The panel's fill answers 'which state' while the screen answers 'which surface' — RESOLVED. There is no panel; where you are is which place is in front, and the picker lights only the tool the wall is showing.
- 12 The Inbox is a queue and Notes a shelf, presented as peers — PARTIAL. Notes leaves the peer row for a drawer of its own; the strip differentiates the rest by count (due / late / caught); but the picker is still six flat rows of words, as the panel was.
- THE TRIANGLE — RESOLVED at the root: the desk has a state of its own (the place in front, and a lifted flag), so no view is overloaded as its render surface; the panel vertex is deleted rather than repaired. NOT RESOLVED: kinds-are-peers — a task is still a card and a note is still paper, and the note kind still holds the ground (conceded).

**Changed after the judges.**
- Cold launch is now the wall on Today, always — one rule, no first-of-day variant — so 2026-08-18 ('the day is the home') is kept rather than reversed; the desk is the ground you slide the wall off to reach, not the first frame. (All three judges.)
- The wall's five-hook foot is deleted. View selection is a tool picker — a LivMenu from the bottom with the six views as Notesnook rows and counts — raised from the bar's first key; there is no row of view words anywhere. The strip's chips are shortcuts, not the only door. (Judges 1, 2, 3: 'the rejected four floors in a different room'.)
- The bar is ONE capsule of three keys with fixed slot meanings on every surface — the wall's handle · Search · + kind — instead of two key sets; the wall's handle is at the thumb, not only the top strip. (Judge 2: slot 1 meant two things; the wall's only labelled door was top-of-screen.)
- The wall opens at the ruled width: LivPanel.peek stays 100, the wall is screen − 100, the panel's mechanics reused verbatim; the 28pt sliver is withdrawn and the 100pt cost to every tool is listed as a trade-off with the one-line change if the owner re-rules. (Judges 1, 3: 'Panel should not be full screen!'.)
- Notes stays a view: the drawer IS the Notes view, holds every document (cards on the desk marked 'on desk'), keeps liv.surface.notes and the existing 'more rows than open tabs' check, and sits in Feature.inOrder as the picker's sixth row. (Judge 1: the owner said keep the six views; 'a surface named after a thing has to contain it'.)
- ‹ › are kept where a visible stack exists: the lifted card's deck edge is a worded ‹ (the card beneath) and › (the card above), taps only; lastUsed is stamped on open and drop and never on a flip so the fan holds still while walked. The deck-edge swipe and the title-band drag are deleted; every sheet dismisses by swipe-down and the lifted document drops by its key. (Judge 2: three horizontal gestures with no handles; two 'in front' idioms with two dismissals. Judge 1: state the ‹ › loss.)
- Closing a band is the List's swipe action (partial reveals ✕, full swipe commits) rather than a bare swipe-left on a scrolling fan. (Judge 2: accidental close on a diagonal flick.)
- Stale cards fold into an 'N stale ▸' band at the foot of the fan instead of sliding silently into the drawer — Chrome's shelf as a fold, LivTabs kept whole. (Judge 1's note on the Inactive reversal.)
- Reorder-by-drag and its new DeskPlanes verb are dropped; last-used is the order. (Judge 3: the owner asked for one thing.)
- Fan overflow and empty-desk geometry are specified: the drawer lip is always pinned above the bar, the fan scrolls under it (top card 144, bands 56, lip 44, bar 66); an empty desk prints the six areas on the blotter with no button. First open is Today's furnished empty state on the wall. (Judges 1, 3: arrive furnished; specify the pinned state.)
- The strip is redrawn as glass chips (which look tappable without a sentence), the date kept as content and shortcut, zero counts omitted, the workspace chip at the trailing edge. (Judges 2, 3: 'bold is not loud' tightrope.)
- The reversal list is now the first open question, as one question to the owner, with what is kept beside what is reversed. (All three judges' cross-cutting notes.)
- survives_in_code / thrown_away re-checked against the tree: LivReturns is Navigate.swift:97–146 (LivPlace 86–89), PanelMark is Glyph.swift:590, the switcher and search covers are App.swift:267 and :273, LivPanel.peek is Theme.swift:249; the first draft's claims that were wrong (28pt peek edit, a reorder verb, 'Panel.swift whole') are corrected.

**Conceded.**
- The axis assignment is Obsidian's, renamed: documents are the ground and the tools are a side surface you slide in. The direction's identity depends on it, so it is kept and said plainly rather than argued away. The kinds-are-peers root — one kind got the main surface — is not answered here; the wall being what you arrive at, and the pile being printed on the desk, soften it and do not resolve it.
- The + on Inbox and Everything still moves you from the wall to the desk. The only construction that keeps it in place — a note typed into a card over the wall — is the capture sheet the owner reverted on 2026-08-18 and asked not to be rebuilt without evidence; so vagueness 9 stays partial.
- Every tool draws 100pt narrower than today's full-screen views while the ruling stands, and the app's first frame every morning is a tool beside a washed desk. Both are the cost of keeping 2026-08-23 and 2026-08-18 inside this geometry; both may be rejected on sight.
- ‹ › are gone from the bar on the desk and the wall; a way back across views by a key does not exist (today's does not either, but the slot did). This reverses the owner's most literal ruling and needs his word before a line.
- The picker card at the thumb is the Go-to key of 2026-08-16 in the app's own menu recipe, and the owner chose sidebar rows over it the next day. It is one door, not two, but it is still a reversal.
- The drawer duplicates the fan at its top, marked 'on desk'; the fan and the drawer are two lists that overlap by design so that Notes contains every note.
- The strip can be four chips at caption size on a busy day; the spread with many cards is a messy desk; the picker is six flat rows of words like the panel was (vagueness 12 stays partial).
- Cost is the highest of the mid-tier: a new ~250-line spread, Notes and Search merged into one drawer body, Chrome's state model replaced, Panel and Tabs mostly deleted, Bar rewritten, the harness's panel/grid/bar/desk checks rewritten and flip added — about 22 files — on top of revs 54–61 that are unverified on a device. A one-week simulator slice (a static fan plus the repurposed panel as the wall, at the ruled width) proves the part most likely to be rejected, which is the honest thing to prove first.

**Open questions.**
- THE HEADLINE for the owner: this direction deletes ‹ › from the bar on two of three surfaces, the numbered box, the grid and the library, and moves view selection from the sidebar to a picker card at the thumb — reversing 2026-08-23 (the browser's set, literally), 2026-08-17 (state on the left) and 2026-08-15 (the library as a place). It keeps 2026-08-18 (launch on Today), 2026-08-23 (panel not full screen), 2026-08-10 (Inactive, as a fold) and 2026-08-28 (+ makes in one tap). Is that trade one he will make? One sentence from him decides whether this is drawn further.
- Is a TOOL a panel? The wall is drawn at the ruled width (screen − 100) and every tool loses 100pt. If a tool may take the screen minus a tappable sliver, LivPanel.peek = 28 is one line; if not, the Calendar at 293pt needs a mockup before anyone believes it.
- Does the wall's picker — six Notesnook rows in a bottom card from the bar's first key — read as the sidebar he asked for at the thumb, or as the Go-to key he superseded on 2026-08-17? A mockup of the picker alone, over the wall, is the cheapest question in this direction.
- Cold launch shows Today on the wall with the washed desk beside it, every morning. Is a pushed desk an acceptable first frame, or does 'launch on Today' inside this geometry need the wall to open flush on launch (a 'sometimes' the direction refuses to add unasked)?
- The bar's first key names the wall's PARKED tool ('Calendar' after you left it there), so the word changes with a state you cannot see from the desk. The alternative — the key always reads 'Today' and other tools need the picker — costs a tap for Calendar and keeps the word still. Which?
- Drawer contents: every document with desk cards marked 'on desk' (drawn; Notes contains every note) versus only what is not on the desk (the first draft's seed). The second is cleaner as a picture and fails 'a surface named after a thing has to contain it'.
- Stale cards: a fold at the foot of the fan (drawn — Chrome's shelf as a fold) versus a silent slide into the drawer (the first draft). The fold keeps LivTabs whole and the owner's 2026-08-10 shape.
- Does Everything belong on the wall (drawn: the fifth tool) or in the drawer as a slice (All / Upcoming / Unfiled over every kind)? In the drawer it would make Notes hold records, which the name refuses.
- Which drive.sh assertions prove it: desk (the fan's bands == liveTabs, the stale fold == the inactive count), wall (goto <view> lands liv.surface.<view> inside liv.overlay.wall with the desk sliver at x ≥ width − 100), notes (the drawer's rows ≥ every admitted document, the existing check), bar (three keys, the same three words on desk and wall bar slot 2 and a kind in slot 3), flip (a deck-edge ‹ tap changes liv.surface.document's entity and lastUsed does not), and the record card over each of the three places.
- The first slice, if any of this is built: the invisible three-file change every judge named — give the desk its own state on DeskModel, drop openDoc's .notes guard, delete goToRoot/showList — keeps today's chrome, is provable with the existing goto notes and desk checks, and resolves vaguenesses 1, 5, 7 and 11 before a pixel of the fan exists. It is the same first step for this direction as for any other.
